# Vaiṣṇavānandinī (Canto 1) \[BS\]

## Maṅgalācaraṇa

I offer respects to inconceivable, faultless Govinda, the cause of everything, unlimited, conscious and eternal, endowed with shining form praised by Brahmā, Śiva and others. With folded hands I worship he who appeared in Bengal and simultaneously destroyed all darkness, who was effulgent, pale, golden in complexion. May sober Vyāsa, who manifested the Brahma-sūtras, and its commentary, the twelve volumes of Bhāgavatam, who brought to life all the devotees with a shower of prema, protect me, his worshipper. I bow down to Śukadeva, chief of renunciates, whom Vyāsa manifested in order to sing the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa, filled with great sweetness, which describe pure bhakti. The feet of the great devotees who explained Śukadeva’s Bhāgavatam, should decorate my head constantly. May this commentary on Bhāgavatam, clear in meaning, though limited in size, give joy to the devotees and crush the Māyāvādīs.

## Chapter One - The Questions of the Sages at Naimiṣāraṇya

### 1.1.1

**Перевод:**

**Let us meditate upon the Absolute Truth, Kṛṣṇa, who is the cause of creation, maintenance and destruction of this universe, as the material and efficient cause, since he is the only knower of all objects and the only independent being; who revealed the Vedas to Brahmā within his mind; who is difficult to understand even for the learned; whose body is thought to be made of matter due to illusion just as one mistakes fire, water and earth for each other; and who negates all false arguments regarding his existence by possessing a transcendental, inconceivable form, and by the strength of devotees’ experiencing him through his svarūpa-śakti.**

**Комментарий:**

Though Bhagavān Vyāsa, son of Parāśara manifested the Brahmasūtras which determine the svarūpa and qualities of Bhagavān, not experiencing an increase of bliss, at the instruction of Nārada, he manifested the work Bhāgavatam, to realize this bliss, which is a commentary on the Brahma-sūtras and which showers the sweetness of the Lord’s pastimes. In this verse he shows the auspiciousness indicating meditation on the Lord.

The word yataḥ (from which) indicates a later substance arising from a previous substance. The word param indicates the Supreme Lord, since Vaijayantī-kośa says that para indicates an enemy or Paramātmā. Let us meditate on Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa. The plural form of the verb negates the concept of vivartavāda, which gives life to the idea that only one jīva exists (eka-jīva-vāda), for it is said:

By hearing Bhāgavatam, bhakti for the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa appears. (SB [1.7.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.7))

The word param means Kṛṣṇa since his name is praised as the moon in the sweet ocean of Śukadeva’s words. The plural indicates a group of students. Param is further described as satyam: unchanging in the three portions of time. He eradicates all influence of matter (nirasta-kuhakam) at all times (sadā) by the power of his superior śakti (dhāmnā) belonging to himself (svena). Thus the form of the Lord who is the object of meditation is not touched by māyā. It is shown that his possession of śakti which is intimately related to himself destroys material influence and that he has a form untouched by māyā. Sadā (always) can also modify dhīmahi. This means that, like the object of meditation, the meditation also is eternal. Both meanings are pleasing. And by the mention of meditation, the main method which is pure bhakti is indicated. The satya nature of the Lord is further described by kaimutya: yatra trisargo ’mṛṣā. Within the Lord (yatra) the creation of the three guṇas (trisarga), composed of elements, senses and devatās is not false (amṛṣā) as shown in the following. He is omniscient, controlling the minds of all, the best and independent. Such a Lord has created real objects eternally. (Īśopaniṣad [8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/iso/8)) Yāthātathyato means true or real according to Halāyudha. An example of not being false is given with the phrase tejo-vārī: the combination (vinimaya) of earth (mṛdā), water (vāri) and fire (tejo) is not false. Śruti also says: The Lord deliberated. “Let me enter the elements as the jīva, who is my body and let me create names and forms. Let me make each element threefold.” (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [6.3.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/6.3.2)) Know from me how the three elements become threefold. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [6.3.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/6.3.4)) The nature of fire that is seen to be red is the form of tejas before fire is combined in three parts. The nature of fire that is seen to be white is the form of water before fire is combined in three parts. The nature of fire that is seen to be black is the form of food (earth), before fire is combined in three parts. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [6.4.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/6.4.1)) All this indicates that the object of meditation is the highest reality. Śruti also says: He is called the unchanging among the unchanging. The jīvas are unchanging. He is the chief unchanging entity among them. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad [2.3.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/2.3.6)) Prāṇa means the jīvas. The Lord is a superior reality (satya) compared to the jīvas since he lacks the jīvas’ nature of apparent transformation such as contraction and expansion of dharma-bhūta-jñāna. The reason for the reality of tri-sarga is explained. Because of the Lord (yataḥ) creation, maintenance, and destruction (janmādi), etc., of the material realm with millions of universes occurs. How the creation takes place from the Lord is explained by anvaya and itara. Anvaya means effect, that which follows. This means the material cause, upādāna, connected with the activity which is to be done. It is formed by adding a vowel which follows formation of nouns from verbs like pac. Itara means other than the material cause—the efficient cause, nimitta. Clay is the material cause of a pot. The potter is the efficient cause. From the material cause with śakti of pradhāna and the kṣetra-jña and from the Lord endowed with superior śakti, creation, maintenance and destruction of the universes takes place. This indicates the greatness of the Lord, the object of meditation, the cause of the meditation. “The material cause can be observed but the efficient cause is described as satyam jñānam anantam and vijñānam ānandam. What is this object?” He knows all objects (artheṣv abhijña), which are visible transformations of prakṛti. He shines with his own senses (svarāṭ). kavir manīṣī: The Lord is omniscient. (Īśopaniṣad 8) The Lord is omniscient in general and specifically. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad [1.1.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/1.1.9)) The Lord manifests his constituents. Of what does the Lord consist? He consists of consciousness, power and śakti. (Madhva’s Brahma-sūtras-bhāṣya 2.2.11) We describe that the Lord is endowed with limbs, subsections, mind and intelligence. He has intelligence, mind, limbs and subsections. (Madhva’s Brahma-sūtrasbhāṣya 2.2.11) Thus time is rejected since it is an instrument of the Lord and material. The Lord’s omniscience is described. He taught Vedas (brahma) to Brahma (ādikavaye) in his heart. Kṛṣṇa created Brahmā and preserved knowledge of the eighteen-syllable mantra for him. Those who desire to give up everything surrender to Kṛṣṇa the revealer of all knowledge, who is endowed with all pastimes. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad [1.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/1.22)) Thus the Lord is not Hiraṇyagarbha, the master of the material elements. “Is the tattva of Kṛṣṇa hard to describe?” Those who are learned (sūrayaḥ) are bewildered concerning Svayam-rūpa Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa. This is explained in the pastimes of Brahmā stealing the calves and the festival of Govardhana. In this scripture, the Lord, the jīva, prakṛti and time—these four items are said to be eternal. The Lord, prakṛti, jīva and time are eternal. (Bhallaveyaśruti) The first item, the Lord, is independent. The others are dependent on him. O brāhmaṇa! The elements, karma, time, svabhāva and the jīva are not different from Vāsudeva. Nothing but he exists in truth. (SB [2.5.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/5.14)) Karma, which is without beginning, because of following the actions of the jīvas during creation, and which is destructible, is also dependent on the Lord. It is the fifth object. The Garuḍa Purāṇa states that Bhāgavatam is the meaning of the Brahma-sūtras and Gāyatrī mantra with artho’yam braham-sūtrānāṁ and gāyatrībhāsya-rūpo’ sau. As for Brahma-sūtras, its first four sūtras are explained. It says at the beginning athāto brahma-jijñāsā: now inquire about Brahman. Its explanation is satyaṁ paraṁ dhīmahi: let us meditate on the supreme truth Kṛṣṇa. After association with knowers of truth (atha), because of determining that Svarga, the result of karmas, is temporary and Brahman produces unlimited, indestructible bliss (ataḥ) one should desire to know and meditate on Brahman (jijñāsā). Janmādyasya yataḥ (second sūtra: from that Brahman arises creation, maintenance and destruction of this universe) is explained by the first line of the Bhāgavatam verse. Tene brahma hṛdā ya ādikavaye explains the next two sūtras: The Lord cannot be known by reasoning because the source of understanding the Lord is the Upaniṣads. But Viṣṇu is the conclusion because of the agreement of other scriptures as well. He imparted knowledge of the Vedas, which are understood in truth by himself. The meaning of śāstra-yonitvāt is Viṣṇu who is the knower of the Vedānta. The meaning of tat tu samanvayāt is he is the object of knowledge of all the Vedas. The meaning of the Gāyatrī is explained. Janmādyasya yataḥ means praṇava is the source of everything, since it is explained that oṁ possesses the śakti of creation, etc. Yatra trisargo’mṛṣā means the three vyāhṛtis (bhūr bhuvaḥ svaḥ part of Gāyatrī) since the intention is to explain the nondifference of the śaktimān from the three worlds (through Gāyatrī). Svarāṭ refers to the power which reveals savitṛ (savituḥ vareṇyam). Tene brahma hṛdā means pracodayāt: what inspires the intellect to prayer. Dhīmahi: this is directly taken from the Gāyatrī mantra. The ten characteristics of a mahā-purāṇa are also understood in this verse. Sarga, visarga, sthāna, and nirodha are indicated with janmādyasya yataḥ. Manvantara, īśānukatha are included in sthāna. Poṣaṇa is indicated by tene brahma hṛdā. Ūti is indicated by muhyanti. Mukti is indicated by dhāmnā. Āśraya is indicated by satyam param. Those many types of illusion concerning the sūtras, which have been composed with bad intentions should be rejected since Govinda-bhāṣya is the basis.

### 1.1.2

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord becomes immediately captured in the heart of the accomplished devotees from the moment they desire to hear Bhāgavatam. This does not occur in other works. In the Bhāgavatam alone, created by the Lord himself, is presented the real, permanent object which can be understood by those without selfish intentions, and which bestows auspiciousness and release from the material world of miseries. In the Bhāgavatam alone is presented the process for attaining that highest object, devoid of all material goals and liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

Two verses (2-3) explain the great excellence of Bhāgavatam to make the listeners favorable, who have been confused by hearing many scriptures and by many sādhanas and goals. It does this by excellence of topic expressed (Kṛṣṇa) and excellence of the revealer (Vyāsa). The highest most excellent dharma is defined in Bhāgavatam, which is most attractive (śrīmat) because of the subject and placement of words. That dharma refers to agnihotra, etc., and to hearing and chanting, etc. In order to indicate the highest dharma, the word \`dharma is modified by projjhita. Projjhita means to completely reject. This work completely rejects dharma which seeks material results (kaitavaḥ). It rejects results such as Svarga, etc., which are different from Kṛṣṇa and the happiness and powers of such places. One seeks out the result in the form of Kṛṣṇa who purifies the heart. The phrase Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam should not be criticized for the fault of avimṛṣṭa-vidheyāṁśasince it is the name of the scripture. Sometimes it is called Bhāgavatam, just as Satyabhāmā is sometimes called Bhāmā. Substance (vastu) and reality (vāstavam) are defined. An unchanging substance is free of transformation of quality and svarūpa because it is said: O King! What substance is there which undergoes transformation, but with a change of time does not go by a different name? (No substance) (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 2.13.96) The substance (vastu) has eternal vibhūtis. The spiritual sky called Gokula and the associates there are real (vāstavam). What is its nature? It is known by those who are devoid of ahaṅkāra (nirmatsarāṇām). Those who have ahaṅkāra are the chief of those who are not qualified. It is further described. It gives auspiciousness and uproots the three miseries such as ādhyātmikā. Meditating upon it, it destroys them completely and bestows the Lord himself who is most auspicious. It bestows the highest puruṣārtha. What type of work is it? That is described. The Lord, becoming a sage (muni), began to contemplate. Mahā-muni means Bhagavān according to śruti. Bhāgavatam was produced by Nārāyaṇa in summary form and in detail by Vyāsa. “Why are sādhya and sādhana not defined in Bhāgavatam as in other works?” What is the use of sādhanas mentioned in scriptures which give only temporary results (kiṁ vā paraiḥ)? For those who desire to hear Bhāgavatam, what to speak of those who actually hear it, Kṛṣṇa (īśvaraḥ), who is the controller of all beings and the shelter of all beings, is immediately (tat-kṣaṇāt) brought under control (avarudhyate) in the heart by ropes of prema. Or, because of joy of Kṛṣṇa (tat-kṣaṇāt), he is controlled in the heart filled with prema. Producing the highest bliss, he is like a bee in the lotus bud. If that is so, why would everyone not hear Bhāgavatam? It is heard by persons skillful at taking association of devotees (kṛtibhiḥ). Without that association, it is rarely attained. One should not worry that the word atra is repeated three times since it makes clear the meaning. Three statements relate only to Bhāgavatam and exclude other works. It contains the highest dharma. From it, the vastu can be known. It reveals bhakti which controls the Lord. It is excellent with the great sage as its revealer and speaker. Because of this, it should be heard by everyone.

### 1.1.3

**Перевод:**

**O knowers of rasa! O fortunate souls! Constantly drink from the mouth of Śukadeva the Bhāgavatam, the fruit of the tree of the Vedas, which has dropped from the tree to this earth, which is Text 3\] The Questions of the Sages at Naimiṣāraṇya 13 immortal, liquid, which is the essence of sweetness and which includes all types of liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

Bhāgavatam has its own svarūpa. O rasikas! Drink the Bhāgavatam, the fruit of the desire tree of the Vedas. From the breathing of the great Lord arose the Ṛg Veda, Yajur Veda, Sāma Veda, Atharva Veda, the Itihāsas, Purāṇas, Upaniṣads, smṛtis, sūtras, commentaries and explanations. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad [2.4.10](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/2.4.10), [4.5.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/4.5.11)) I studied Ṛg Veda, Yajur Veda, Sāma Veda, Atharvana—the fourth Veda, itihāsas-purāṇa or Mahābhārata—the fifth. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [7.1.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/7.1.2)) Nigama means the Vedas with six parts. Since it gives all results, it is called a desire tree. What is its nature? It has fallen (galitam) to the ground: it is attained on earth by the succession starting with Brahmā and Nārada. This shows that Bhāgavatam depends on sampradāya. Aho indicates that it is a rare attainment. “How can the fruit be drunk?” It is pure juice without skin, like the juice of a sweet such as amṛta-keli. Thus it can be drunk. The sweet liquid is attained from the mouth of Śuka, like honey from the sun.It becomes very tasty by the sweetness of being spoken by Śuka’s independent voice filled with affection. This was spoken by Vyāsa previous to Śukadeva’s speaking, since Vyāsa is omniscient, just as Vālmīki speaks of Rāma’s pastimes in the future. How much should one drink? One drinks up to liberation (ālayam): even in liberation when one embraces (laya) the feet of the Lord. Sāma Veda says, tad viṣṇoḥ paramaṁ padaṁ sadā paśyanti sūrayaḥ: the devotees always see the supreme feet of Viṣṇu. (Ṛg Veda 1.22.20) It cannot be given up because of the taste due to hearing about bhakti beginning with chanting and not being able to give it up on account of extreme taste. Thus the writer of the sūtras says: One should worship the Lord up till liberation and also after liberation because that is stated in the scriptures. (Brahma-sūtras [4.1.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/4.1.12)) Here it should be understood that even though all the Vedas are knowledge and bliss in their combination of syllables since they are the breathing of the Lord, the truth disappears because of dealing with insignificant matters. The statements of this portion of the Vedas however manifest as the fruit full of rasa since the subject matter is the greatest. Bhāgavatam is suitable for stories about kings of great puṇya and fame. One attains the same mood by studying them just as one becomes salty on entering a salt mine. O persons with auspicious occupations (bhāvukā)! This indicates exclusion of karmīs involved in violence to animals. The women, śudras and dvija-bandhus were not qualified to hear the Vedas. (SB [1.4.25](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/4.25)) By this statement the śūdras are qualified for this portion of Itihāsas and Purāṇas, a form of the Vedas. It is like the following example where the low born can light the sacrificial fire: The carpenter should light the sacrificial fires in the monsoon season. By these three (verses), the viṣaya, sambandha, prayojana and adhikārī of Bhāgavatam have been shown. Satyaṁ param shows viṣaya. Vastavaṁ vastv atra vedyam shows the sambandha, relation in the form of vācya-vācaka (the described and the description). Śivadaḥ tāpa-trayonmūlanam ālayam shows that liberation conforming to bhakti is the prayojana. Nirmatsarāṇāṁ satāṁ vedyam shows the adhikārī (qualified person). Thus the anubandhacatuṣṭayam has been mentioned. There are three types of adhikārīs: saniṣṭha, pariniṣṭhita and nirapekṣa. The saniṣṭha devotee is he, who follows his prescribed dharmas while thinking of it as a worship of the Lord and who desires to see various lokas. The pariniṣṭhita devotee is engaged in bhakti to the Lord while performing his dharmas with a desire to teach people. The nirapekṣa devotee is he, who is only absorbed in the Lord and who is pure by truthfulness, austerity and japa. The first two have āśrama, and their karmas do not involve animal sacrifices. The nirapekṣa devotee is without āśrama. This will be made clear in the appropriate place.

### 1.1.4

**Перевод:**

**At the place called Naimiṣāraṇya, the place of Viṣṇu, the sages headed by Śaunaka applied themselves to a sacrifice lasting a 16 Vaiṣṇavānandinī \[Ch.1 thousand years for attaining the planet of the Supreme Lord (or for going to the heavenly planets).**

**Комментарий:**

In the first chapter of the first canto, the joyful sages headed by Śaunaka ask six questions concerning the highest good, etc. After getting the attention of the listeners by showing the excellence of Bhāgavatam, the place suitable for the conversation between Sūta, the chief sage, and Śaunaka is shown. Om is uttered for auspiciousness. As well it indicates that this scripture is a form of oṁ. The sages such as Śaunaka engaged in sacrifice at Naimiṣāraṇya. A sacrifice which engages ṛtvikas as the sponsors is called a satra. They performed (āsata) a sacrifice. Roots have many meanings. At this place, the rim (nemi) of the wheel which Brahmā created in meditation shattered. This becomes nemiṣa (Pāṇini 3.2.101) and then naimiṣa. Vāyu Purāṇa describes this: “This mental wheel created by me will be released. The place where the rim shatters will be favorable for performing austerities.” Having said this, and having created the wheel brilliant like the sun in his mind and offering his respects to it, Brahmā released that great deity, the wheel. The rejoicing brāhmaṇas, offering respects to the lord of the universe, then journeyed to the place where the rim of wheel shattered. That forest became known as Naimiṣa and is worshipped by the sages. The Lord says in Varāha Purāṇa concerning Naimiṣāraṇya: evaṁ kṛṭvā tato devo muniṁ gaura-mukhaṁ tadā vāca nimiṣeṇedaṁ nihataṁ dānavaṁ balam araṇye ’smiṁs tatas tv etan naimiṣāraṇya-saṁjñakam bhaviṣyati yathārthaṁ vai brāhmaṇānāṁ viśeṣakam Having done that, the Lord then spoke to the sage Gauramukha. “The powerful demon was killed in that forest (araṇya) quickly in a moment (nimiṣa). Thus this place is called the Naimiṣāraṇya. It will be very suitable for brāhmaṇas in the future.” (Varāha Purāṇa 11.108-109) One should seek out that place where the mind stops moving about, where lust and other anarthas are destroyed and residing there, should meditate on the Lord. This forest is such a place. The place is further described. It is the place of Viṣṇu, who constantly sees, without blinking (animeṣa-kṣetre). Why did they perform sacrifices? They performed sacrifice for Viṣṇu (svargāya). Since Viṣṇu is praised in Svarga he is called svargāya. Svargāya lokāya means they performed sacrifice to attain Viṣṇu’s planet. The sacrifice was to be performed for a span of thousand years to attain the Lord by purifying the heart and by giving opportunity for discussions.

### 1.1.5

**Перевод:**

**Once, the sages, having offered oblations into the sacrificial fire in the morning, with great respect inquired from Sūta who had been received properly and was seated.**

**Комментарий:**

The conversation of the sages is described. They had offered oblation after oblation into the fire. This indicates that they had a wealth of nitya and naimittika karmas. They asked the topic (idam), which will be described.

### 1.1.6

**Перевод:**

**The sages said: O sinless Sūta! Certainly you have not only studied but also explained the Purāṇas and the dharma-śāstras along with Itihāsas such as Mahābhārata.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to hear the real truth from Sūta, the sages in three verses speak of his great knowledge. Itihāsa means Mahābhārata. Not only did you study all the dharma-śāstras but also explained them. Uta means api.

### 1.1.7-8

**Перевод:**

**O Sūta Gosvāmī! Because you are submissive, by the mercy of the gurus, you know all matters in truth which the Supreme Lord Vyāsa, best among knowers, and other sages who know Kṛṣṇa have understood. The gurus should speak the secret to the student who has affection for the gurus.**

**Комментарий:**

Parāvara means the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa who has eternally  liberated souls as servants. Those who know Kṛṣṇa (vidaḥ) know (viduḥ) what Vyāsa knows. You also know everything by the mercy of Vyāsa (tad-anugrahāt), because of having knowledge (tattvataḥ). The gurus should speak this secret to the student who has affection (snigdhasya) for guru. Śruti says: To the great soul who has great bhakti for the Lord and guru, the meanings of what was spoken are revealed. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [6.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.23)) O Sūta (uta)! By the mercy of gurus, you know the secret of scriptures. Therefore, we ask you.

### 1.1.9

**Перевод:**

**O long-lived sage! You should tell us at once in easy terms, and thoroughly, the highest object for mankind that you have discerned in all the scriptures.**

**Комментарий:**

“Should I speak everything?” You should speak what you have determined to be the best for human beings by the honesty of the scriptures (anjasā). This is the first question. Ekāntataḥ has a nominative sense (ekāntaḥ - highest good).

### 1.1.10

**Перевод:**

**O worthy sage! In Kali-yuga people are generally short-lived, lazy, unintelligent, devoid of the good fortune of devotee association, and afflicted by illness.**

**Комментарий:**

We do not desire to hear all the scriptures that you have studied. O worthy sage (sabhya)! In Kali-yuga people have generally short life spans. Even if they have a long life, they are lazy to hear about the supreme tattva (mandāḥ). Even if they are alert to hear, they are dullwitted. Even if they are not lazy, and even if they have intelligence, they are devoid of the good fortunate of associating with devotees who know the truths (manda-bhāgyā). Even if they have that good fortune, they are overcome with illness, etc., (upadrutāḥ). Or they are short-lived, lazy, unfortunate and afflicted with illness.

### 1.1.11

**Перевод:**

**O learned sage! For the welfare of all living beings, please, after extracting the essence of scriptures using your wisdom, describe the practices worthy of hearing which include many actions, by which our intelligence will be satisfied.**

**Комментарий:**

There are many sādhanas that should be heard in which many actions are undertaken. Extracting by intelligence, the essence that can be undertaken by the people of Kali-yuga, speak that to us. O great devotee! By that essence being heard, the intelligence (ātmā) becomes pleased. This is the second question.

### 1.1.12

**Перевод:**

**O Sūta! Blessings to you! You know for what purpose the Lord, master of the Yadus, appeared in Devakī, the wife of Vasudeva.**

**Комментарий:**

The essence is chanting and hearing concerning Kṛṣṇa. That is expressed in this verse. They give blessings out of eagerness by saying, “All auspiciousness to you.” Sātiḥ means for the purpose of happiness. Sāt means Kṛṣṇa by the rules of Pāṇini 3.1.138 and 3.1.94. Sātvata means his devotee, those who worship him. Sātvatām patiḥ is the Lord of the devotees, Kṛṣṇa. You know by which desire he appeared in Devakī’s womb.

### 1.1.13

**Перевод:**

**O Sūta (aṅga)! You should describe that Lord to us, who are eager to hear. The Lord made his appearance for the liberation and material prosperity of all beings.**

**Комментарий:**

Therefore, you should describe this and other auspicious activities. Kunti says: Some say that you have appeared in this world to help those suffering due to their actions arising from material desires, which in turn arise from ignorance, by engaging them in hearing and remembering about you, so that they can attain prema. (SB [1.8.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/8.35)) Relieving the earth of its burden is an accompanying result, possible by his giving mercy. Many Yātudhānas were destroyed by Parāśara and Dhruva. What is the result of hearing this? We will make ourselves successful. The last line and the next three verses answer. His avatāra is for liberation (kṣemāya) and prosperity (bhavāya) of all beings. What to speak of when Svayam Bhagavān appears. This is the third question.

### 1.1.14-16

**Перевод:**

**What person, desiring satisfaction of his intelligence, will not hear the glories of the Lord, whose actions are praised by reputed persons; whose name, which fear personified fears, immediately liberates helpless persons afflicted by the terrors of material existence; and whose devotees, having taking shelter of his lotus feet and having fixed their mind in the Lord, immediately purify others of all sins just by their thinking of the devotees, whereas the waters of the Gaṅgā purify only by contact and direct service.**

**Комментарий:**

Persons such as Ajamila who are overcome by saṁsāra and helpless are immediately freed from saṁsāra by chanting his name, since from one name alone time itself (bhayam) is afraid. What to speak of its vibhūti Yamarāja or his servants. Those who take shelter of the Lord’s feet, on being remembered, purify the person of the contamination of avidyā. Simply from remembering you, men’s houses even become immediately purified. What to speak of seeing, touching, washing your feet and giving you a seat! (SB [1.19.33](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/19.33)) How much more result there will be from serving those feet (upaspṛṣṭāḥ)! Water of the Gaṅgā brought from far away, purifies by serving it continually. There is lack of grammatical ending on āpas because of the rule of exceptional case at the end of a compound. Those waters give liberation and great bliss when seen and used for bathing, for it is said concerning Gaṅgā, muktis tad-darśanād eva na jāne snāna-jaṁ phalam: liberation comes from seeing it; I do not know the result of taking bath. Water coming from the Lord’s feet purifies by continuous service, but devotees who take full shelter of the Lord’s feet immediately purify. Of the two, the devotees are superior. These devotees have taken such shelter (ayanāḥ) because of intelligence fixed intensely on the Lord (praśamāyanāḥ). It is said śamo man-niṣṭhatā buddheḥ: śama means intelligence fixed in me. (SB [11.19.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/19.36)) Who, desiring to be free of avidyā (śuddhi-kāmaḥ) would not hear the fame of Kṛṣṇa (tasya), famous for great qualities, whose actions are praised by Nārada and others (puṇya-śloka)? He liberated Pūtanā as a child, defeated many enemies during paugaṇḍa period and performed pastimes such as the rāsa dance during his kaiśora period.

### 1.1.17

**Перевод:**

**Please tell us, full of faith, about the activities of the Lord who supports the forms of all avatāras by his will—activities of killing the demons and bestowing the highest bliss to his devotees, which are glorified by the sages.**

**Комментарий:**

Please tell us about the great activities, such as creation of the universe (karmāṇi), which are glorified by the brāhmaṇas (sūribhiḥ), performed by Kṛṣṇa, whose greatness was just described, and who supports expansions (kalā) vibhūtis such as Brahmā. This is the fourth question.

### 1.1.18

**Перевод:**

**O intelligent sage! Narrate the non-material stories of avatāras of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa, who performs his eternal pastimes independently by his own will.**

**Комментарий:**

Describe the stories of the Lord’s avatāras such as Varāha, stories which expand their greatness. The Lord manifests (vidadhataḥ) pastimes in these forms such as lifting up the earth, independently (svairaṁ), by his own will (ātma-māyayā). Ātma-māyā means by his will according to Śabda-mahodadhi. This is the fifth question.

### 1.1.19

**Перевод:**

**We are not fully satisfied with the exploits of the Lord whose fame is the highest, because with every word those exploits become more relishable for the hearers who have knowledge of rasa.**

**Комментарий:**

The sages express their eagerness. We are never fully satisfied with the exploits of Kṛṣṇa, praised (śloka) by the great devotees like Nārada whose ignorance is gone (uttama). Let others be satisfied. We do not have sufficient intelligence to hear everything. The reason is stated. Hearing those pastimes, they become more relishable with every word. There are three types of intelligence (which is satisfied): by filling the stomach, etc., by lack of knowledge of rasa and by lack of special taste. But in the case of those who hear, the ear holes cannot be filled since the exploits do not have a form. For persons who know rasa, there is no satisfaction. Animals are satisfied since they have no knowledge of rasa. In every nominal and verbal form of word (pade pade), it is relishable. There is no cessation due to lack of special taste, like in the case of a chewed sugar cane.

### 1.1.20

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord Keśava, whose intentions remain concealed because he is deceptive to mankind, performed pastimes with Balarāma.**

**Комментарий:**

They clarify the pastimes. These actions could be performed by humans of that time even if they are very strong (atimartyāni). The Lord cannot be understood even by forms of Viṣṇu, since Kṛṣṇa is deceptive (kapaṭa). In order to help the devotee, he disguises himself as a brāhmaṇa and begs from men (mānuṣaḥ) like Jarāsandha. The locative meaning of mānuṣaḥ in the compound (deceptive to men- -mānuṣa-kapaṭaḥ would be normal) is irregular as in words like gaḍu. (Pāṇini 2.2.35).

### 1.1.21

**Перевод:**

**Knowing that the age of Kali has arrived, taking the opportunity, we have seated ourselves at this place sacred to Viṣṇu on the pretext of a prolonged sacrifice in order to hear about the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

What is the opportunity for hearing about vastu for persons absorbed in karmas? At Naimiṣāraṇya (kṣetre), there is opportunity and great festival (sa-kṣaṇā) for such discussions because of performing a long sacrifice, in order to avoid other actions.

### 1.1.22

**Перевод:**

**We desire to cross the insurmountable ocean of Kali, the destroyer of all good qualities. Like the pilot of a ship, you have appeared before us by the grace of the Lord to guide us over that ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

Dhātrā means “by the Lord.” Karṇa-dhāraḥ means the pilot of ship.

### 1.1.23

**Перевод:**

**Please tell us where dharma has taken shelter, now that Kṛṣṇa, master of all yogas, devoted to the righteous, and the protector of dharma, has entered his abode.**

**Комментарий:**

Dharma-varmaṇi means the protector of dharma like an armour. Yogeśvare means capable of protecting. Brahmaṇye means he is merciful. Kāṣṭām means his dhāma. This is the sixth question. By these, the complete meaning of scriptures is asked.

## Chapter Two - The Stages of Bhakti

### 1.2.1

**Перевод:**

**The son of Romaharṣaṇa (Sūta or Ugraśravas), delighted at the questions of the sages, respecting their words, began to speak.**

**Комментарий:**

In the second chapter there is a description of three items: the process (abhidheya), bhakti; the goal (prayojanam), prema; and the object of worship (viṣaya), the Supreme Lord son of Devakī. The son of Romaharṣaṇa was Ugraśravas (also called Sūta). Joyful at the questions previously shown, praising their words (pratipūjya), he began to speak.

### 1.2.2

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: I offer my respects to the sage Śukadeva, who has entered the mind of all beings, and to whom Vyāsa, his father, cried out with pain of separation, “O son!” when Śukadeva, without even undergoing saṁskāras, left home and could not be brought back. Even the trees, being attached to Śukadeva, called out, “O son!”**

**Комментарий:**

Śaunaka and others, inquisitive about their ātmās, were sitting in front of him. “Their hearts purified by niṣkāma-dharmas, have become full of the means of knowing the Supreme Lord, which is the wealth of that dharma. Thus I will speak Bhāgavatam which describes in truth the svarūpa, qualities, vibhūtis and pastimes of the Supreme Lord to them?” Thinking thus, Sūta offers his respects to the speaker of Bhāgavatam, his guru Śukadeva and to its devatā, and teaches how to offer respects to them before beginning to speak. He does this in two verses (2-3). I offer respects to the sage, Śukadeva (tam munim). He is described. He renounced and wandered off (pravrajantam). He did not establish close relationships with his father (anupetam). He rejected all karmas (apeta-kṛtyam). Vyāsa called out in pain “O son!” Vyāsa was pained by his leaving (viraha-kātaraḥ) because the son, suitable to study Bhāgavatam, went off somewhere. This was disturbance arising from prema, related to the Lord, and not a material bewilderment. Not only did his father show affection for him, the trees, their hearts attracted to Śukadeva, facing him, called out in the form of echoes “O son!” Everyone has affection for the devotee. The universe is satisfied with the person who worships the Lord. All living beings moving and non-moving are attracted to that person. (Padma Purāṇa) This is described in the verse with the words sarva-bhūta-hṛdayam (he attracted the hearts of all beings.) This is because Śukadeva had performed bhakti to achieve samādhi on ātmā and by the mercy of his father, that bhakti later became pure. Śukadeva, by his samādhi manifested the quality of satya-saṅkalpa (his desire coming true). He answered his father through the echoes of the trees (whom he had attracted). This shows the greatness of Śukadeva.

### 1.2.3

**Перевод:**

**I surrender to the son of Vyāsa, the incomparable guru of all the sages, who mercifully spoke the Purāṇa full of hidden meanings, the essence of all the scriptures, the essence of hearing, for all the people of this world, even in the future; who spoke the Bhāgavatam, which revealed the excellence of rasa to Śukadeva, and which is the revealer of ātmā for those desiring to cross dense ignorance with ease.**

**Комментарий:**

He offers respects, speaking of his guru’s mercy. I surrender to the son of Vyāsa. He is described. He is the guru of the sages assembled such as Nārada, Parāśara and Vyāsadeva. The Purāṇa became as if newly produced by the sweetness of his voice, and by the independent explanation. Thus he became their teacher. He spoke the secret Purāṇa for the people in saṁsāra by his mercy. Making Parīkṣit the cause, he desired to deliver the living beings in the future. The Purāṇa is described. It has extraordinary (sva) authority (anubhāvam). It immediately brings the Lord under control in the heart for those who desire to hear submissively. Or, since Śukadeva (sva) had great power (anubhāvam), by his recitation the scripture became great. It is the chief (ekam) of all scriptures because it is the essence of all scriptures. It is a lamp, revealing special qualities of the Lord and jīva (adhyātma). The people in saṁsāra are described. They desire to easily cross (atititīrṣatām) great ignorance (andham tamaḥ).

### 1.2.4

**Перевод:**

**After offering respectful obeisances unto the presiding deities Nara-Nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi, unto the subject of the work Kṛṣṇa, unto the śakti of the work Sarasvatī, and unto the sage entrusted with the work Śrīla Vyāsadeva, one should speak the scripture which destroys ignorance.**

**Комментарий:**

He offers respects to the devatās. He offers respects to Nārāyaṇa and Nara, protectors of Bhārata-varṣaand to Kṛṣṇa (narottamam), who is explained in this scripture. Sarasvatī is wife of the Lord and is his śakti, bestowing knowledge. Keśavasya priyām: Sarasvatī is dear to Kṛṣṇa (Skanda Purāṇa). He offers respects to the son of Parāśara, Vyāsa, the sage of scriptures. After that he speaks the scripture which conquers ignorance (jayam). Speaking the scripture himself, Sūta instructs others. Tataḥ means the speaker, one who spreads (tanoti) or explains the meaning with dexterity. It is formed by the rule tani-mṛṅbhyāṁ kicca. (Uṇādi sūtra 3.88)

### 1.2.5

**Перевод:**

**O sages! You have asked good questions about what is beneficial for the world, because you have asked questions all about Kṛṣṇa, by which the intellect is immediately satisfied.**

**Комментарий:**

It was previously mentioned that he praised the sages (pratipūjya). These are words of praise for the sages. I have been nicely asked by you because this is auspicious for the world-- since your question concerns Kṛṣṇa by which the intelligence (ātmā) is pleased.

### 1.2.6

**Перевод:**

**The supreme dharma for all human beings is that by which bhakti to the Lord arises, which is not caused by anything other than itself, cannot be obstructed, and which satisfies the intelligence completely.**

**Комментарий:**

This answers the first question about what is most beneficial for humanity. The best dharma (paraḥ dharmaḥ) characterized by agnihotra., etc, prescribed for humans is that from which bhakti to the Lord arises; i.e. taste for his topics arises. Results like Svarga are inferior. What is that bhakti? It is devoid of desire for results (ahaitukī). There should be no result in this bhakti except the object of worship. Because of its powerful nature it cannot be defeated by obstacles (apratihatā).

### 1.2.7

**Перевод:**

**Bhakti dedicated to Lord Kṛṣṇa, endowed with special moods, quickly produces, detachment from material goals and knowledge of the Lord, devoid of the desire for liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

“Without jñāna and vairāgya, bhakti cannot be steady.” This verse responds. Vasanād eva vāde ca vāsudeveti śabdyate: The word vāsudeva means “he dwells in words.” (In Hari-vaṁśa, Śiva speaks to the Lord) Kṛṣṇa is to be known by discussion (vāda). Vāda means discussions which desire the truth. When performing sva-dharma with this intention and vairāgya and jñāna sprout, the bhakti yoga that arises makes them blossom. But this type of jñāna and vairāgya is the conclusion of the Upaniṣads and cannot be approached by dry logic (ahaitukam).

### 1.2.8

**Перевод:**

**Varṇāśrama-dharma of the human being, which does not produce attraction for topics of the Lord, is only wasted effort.**

**Комментарий:**

The opposite is stated. Ratiḥ means taste for hearing the topics of Kṛṣṇa. Hi indicates result like Svarga, etc., are not the result. They are a wasted effort because they are temporary.

### 1.2.9

**Перевод:**

**Material results are not suitable as the goal of one’s prescribed karmas. Attainment of material assets is not suitable as real gain for person who is dedicated to the highest dharma.**

**Комментарий:**

Those involved in material enjoyment think that the result of dharma is artha. Artha’s result is kāma. And kāma’s result is pleasure of the senses. Thus one again performs the sequence beginning with dharma. From performance of dharma comes artha and kāma. Can artha not be served? (Mahābhārata 18.5.49) This idea is rejected in this verse. Wealth (arthaḥ) is not suitable as a result (arthāya) of one’s prescribed karmas (dharma). What is the nature of dharma? It is for attaining a wealth of niṣkāma bhakti (apavarga) according to the Fifth Canto: All these destinations are prescribed for the self, according to the quality of their actions, as indicated in the Vedas. Apavarga is then achieved. (SB [5.19.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/19.19)) Āpavargya means “apavarga.” The intelligent do not say (smṛtaḥ) that material enjoyment (kāma) is the real gain (lābhāya) of the persons fixed in such dharma (arthasya dharmaikāntasya).

### 1.2.10

**Перевод:**

**For one who desires apavarga, sense pleasure attained from enjoying sense objects is not the goal as long as one lives. The goal of life is inquiry into the highest truth. What is accomplished by prescribed duties is not the goal.**

**Комментарий:**

Sense pleasure (indriya-prītiḥ) does not result (lābha) in happiness of the senses, such as the skin of a person experiencing touch by the skin (kāmasya), since it is very temporary. O King! In touching, in tasting, seeing, smelling and hearing, pleasure is said to last just for the time it takes an arrow to hit the ground. (Mokṣa-dharma) But as long as one lives, one attains enjoyment. The person in knowledge of matter should accept as much as he needs to maintain his life. He falls from the spiritual goal by accepting too much or too little. (Nāradīya Purāṇa) Inquiry about the truth by undertaking niṣkāma-dharma which purifies the heart is the goal of life (jīvasya). What is famous as artha such as animals, sons and Svarga by karmas is not the goal since it is destructible. The following should be understood. Those who desire liberation perform actions for detachment, for it is said: The person dedicated to me engages in karmas without desire and gives up karmas for material results. The person completely fixed in yogārūḍha does not care for nitya or naimittika karmas. (SB [11.10.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/10.4)) Agni-hotra, darśa, paurnāmāsa, cāturmasya are without animal offerings. I am nonviolent, strive for truth, devoid of lust and anger, equal in suffering and happiness, peaceful and laugh at death like an immortal. I, contemplating, am engaged in yajñas for peace and am situated in brahma-yajña. I will become the sacrifice of words, mind and actions during the northern course of the sun. How can I perform animal sacrifices with violence? The wise person by temporary sacrifices in holy places is like a witch. (Mokṣa-dharma in a discussion between father and son) Darśa, paurṇamāsa, agnihotra and cāturmāsya are actions of the wise. Among these actions yajña is eternal. Persons not engaging in projects, having determination and cleanliness called brahma. The brāhmaṇas, with a desire for immortality, satisfy the devatās. (Mahābhārata, Śānti-parva, conversation between Kapila and Syūmaraśmī) These sacrifices without violence are for the saniṣṭha devotees. The pariniṣṭhita devotees, fixed in bhakti, perform them only to teach others. These are the main activities of these pariniṣṭhita devotees since they are mixed with śānta. Jyotiṣṭoma sacrifices and fighting include violence and are for restricting persons qualified for rājadharma, who are much inclined to material enjoyment and are for restricting their enjoyment. When the enjoyment is minimized, peace and purification of the heart takes place. These are their secondary activities.

### 1.2.11

**Перевод:**

**The knowers of truth call this truth advayam-jñānam, the supreme conscious being, who is called Brahman by the jñānīs, Paramātmā by the yogīs and Bhagavān by the devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

It was said that the goal of life is inquiry into truth (tattva). This verse shows that tattva. That tattva is advaya-jñāna. Advayam means that which is different from (a) the two (dvaya)—prakṛti and jīva. Nor is tattva like the complete absence of these two which are the form of the universe, since it is impossible for knowers of the truth (tattva-vidaḥ) to speak concerning nonexistence when the speakers exist and there can be no proof if the speakers do not exist. As well, their existence is inferred even in mokṣa by saying ālayaṁ pibata (O jīvas! Drink Bhāgavatam in the state of liberation). Jñāna is self-revealing. It is not contaminated at all even by the scent of matter. Nor is it without qualities or nirviśeṣa just because the word jñāna is used in the sense of action (i.e. knowing).The first verse says this tattva is abhijña (full of knowledge) and in this verse the tattva is described as Brahman, Paramātmā and Bhagavān, which are viśeṣaṇas (descriptive terms). Though this jñāna indicates knowing, it appears as a knower because of viśeṣa, just as revelation is a quality of the sun by its quality of being a revealer. Viśeṣa resembles difference but is not difference. It causes perception of difference even in non-differentiated objects. One can make statements such as “Existence exists,” “The distinction is different,” and “Time exists at all times.”Such distinctions are made by the wise. Without such distinctions, the state of qualities and object in which the quality inheres (viśeṣaṇa-viśeṣya) could not exist. “Existence exists” is not an error of intelligence since it is never negated, just like an existing pot. It is not a projection or imposition like “Devadatta is not a lion” since the usage “existence does not exist” is never found. It is not that the word “exists, etc.” can be used with “existence, etc.,” (when saying “existence exists”) even when there is nothing apart from existence, etc., simply because of nature of things. Nature is included in the mention of the word viśeṣa itself. It is indisputable. Otherwise, meaning could not be perceived. The person who sees difference in the Lord, who is everywhere, falls into saṁsāra, like rain water on top of the mountain flowing in various streams. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad [2.1.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/2.1.14)) In the above verse, having spoken of the qualities of Brahman, difference in it is forbidden. Without viśeṣa, one cannot speak of the Lord’s many qualities and the relationship of dharma and dharmī (possessor of qualities). This is rejected of course by persons who proclaim the sole existence of consciousness. Because of the purity of the words, different explanations concerning existence of only one spiritual entity is not approved. If it were, it would mean that the concept is false. If viśesa did not exist, in the perception of the spiritual consciousness, there would be no perception of oneness. Even if the perception were not contrary to the illusion of difference, it would be contrary to perception of oneness. How could one make distinctions in this? Viśeṣa is not different from the object and is self-supporting. Thus it does not lead to infinite regress (anavasthā). The dharmī’s (Lord’s) ability to conceive is caused by the Lord’s possession of a form of “desiring to know.” This tattva is called Brahman when it is illuminated by viśeṣa. This is the meaning in later verses also. Iti is mentioned three times. It indicates the prominence of these three—Brahman, Paramātmā and Bhagavān. Brahman means possessing infinite qualities and greatness. The word Brahman is derived as follows: atha kasmād ucyate brahmeti bṛhanto hy asmin guṇā Why is he called Brahman? In him are infinitely expanding qualities (bṛh=to make big). One cannot say that the Lord’s qualities are different from himself since he is unlimited, since by the word Brahman, he includes everything. Therefore, the Brahman is a descriptive of Viṣṇu. From him arise unlimited qualities of others. (Brahma Purāṇa) brahma-dṛṣṭir utkarṣāt The Lord should be seen as Brahman since this is best for meditation. (Brahma-sūtras [4.1.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/4.1.5)) In other places the use of the word is secondary (gauṇa). Paramātmā is the Lord who possesses Śrī who is called parā. She is superior and intimately related to the Lord. It is said that he has various superior śaktis. He has intrinsic knowledge (saṁvit), strength (sandhinī) and action (hlādinī). (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8) Bhagavān however is characterized by many qualities such as affection for the devotees, etc. The syllable bha means the bestower and the maintainer. The syllable ga means the giver of prema, creator of qualities in the devotee and the cause of going to Vaikuṇṭha. The six bhagas are complete control, influence, good qualities, wealth, knowledge, and renunciation. The syllable va means the indestructible Lord in whom all beings dwell and who dwells in all beings. The word bhagavān means that he is complete with power of mind (omniscience), power of senses, power of body, influence, control, and beauty, and is devoid of all inferior qualities. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 6.5.73-75,79) These three forms are different in tattva from the jīvas such as nitya-muktas, etc. The word śabdyate indicates that the tattva can be described. The author of the Brahma-sūtras says: Brahman is not inexpressible because scriptural statements to the contrary are seen in the Upaniṣads. (Brahma-sūtras [1.1.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/1.1.5)) It is argued that the tattva is describable albeit indirectly (lakṣyam) according to the following śruti: yato vāco nivartante aprāpya manasā saha The Lord cannot be expressed by words. He is not attained by the mind. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad [2.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.4)) But that is not so. If all words cannot express him, lakṣaṇa is also impossible. But śruti says that he is not expressible completely. O best of the Kurus! I have related to you these narrations of the pastimes of Nārāyaṇa, the creator of this world and the abode of all living entities, in a brief summary. Even Lord Brahmā himself is incapable of describing them entirely. (SB [12.4.39](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-12/4.39)) He can be described because of inconceivability in the power of words. Without that, there could be no understanding of the pure meaning, since it would be without basis. Thus the tattva with two other different forms (jīva and prakṛti), is a personified form possessing unlimited auspicious qualities.

### 1.2.12

**Перевод:**

**The faithful sages realize that Absolute Truth in the heart by rendering devotional service which is first heard and then practiced.**

**Комментарий:**

This tattva is attained by bhakti with its accompaniments. The sages see by bhakti, this tattva (tat), who is the Lord full of bliss and knowledge(ātmānam), situated in their hearts (ātmani). They realize by eyes of prema, the final state, with a taste of the Lord’s topics. Some excellent person, with senses withdrawn from external objects, desiring immortality, sees the ātmā within. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad [2.1.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/2.1.1)) Meditating on the Lord who is undivided, becoming pure hearted by the mercy of the Lord, one sees the Lord. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad [3.1.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/3.1.8)) How do they realize? First by hearing and then by understanding (śruta-gṛhītayā). It is served by jñāna and vairāgya arising from ātmā. This solidifies the bhakti. The writer of the sūtras says: When there is intense bhakti, the Lord can be perceived by the senses, since this is stated in śruti and smṛti. (Brahma-sūtras [3.2.24](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.2.24))

### 1.2.13

**Перевод:**

**O best of the brāhmaṇas! The result of dharma, practiced without fault, according to divisions of varṇāśrama by men, is pleasure of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

That bhakti is also obtained from sva-dharma employed in satisfying the Lord. Thus satisfaction of the Lord is the main result. That is explained in this verse. Saṁsiddhiḥ means result. The result of svadharma practiced without fault (svanuṣṭhitasya) is satisfaction of the Lord.

### 1.2.14

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, with mind dedicated only to bhakti, devoid of karma and jñāna, one should constantly hear about, glorify and meditate upon the Supreme Lord — the master of the devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

If the result of sva-dharma, which pleases the Lord, is taste for the Lord’s topics, then bhakti is the only duty of those who take its shelter, what is the use of inclination for sva-dharma? The following question was asked: O learned sage! For the welfare of all living beings, please, after extracting the essence of scriptures using your wisdom, describe the practices worthy of hearing which include many actions, by which our intelligence will be satisfied. (SB [1.1.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/1.11)) Now the answer is given. With full concentration on the Lord (ekena manasā), with no inclination for sva-dharma, one should perform śravaṇa and kīrtana of the Lord’s names, form and qualities, meditate on the Lord’s form and worship the deity.

### 1.2.15

**Перевод:**

**Who will not develop attraction for topics of the Lord, remembrance of whom, like a sword, will cut the knots of karma?**

**Комментарий:**

Faith is the cause of qualification for bhakti. This is produced by the qualities of meditation. Those with discrimination (kovidaḥ), who have controlled minds (yuktāḥ), cut the bondage of the karma which ties the knot of thinking oneself to be the material body, with the sword of remembrance, meditation on the Lord. Who would not develop taste for topics of the Lord? The bondage of the knot refers to prārabdha-karma compared to provisions for a journey according to some. The body remains by the will of the Lord, like a burned piece of cloth.

### 1.2.16

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇas! Attraction for topics concerning Kṛṣṇa will arise by service to the great devotees, followed by faith, by surrender to the feet of the pure guru, and by the desire to hear.**

**Комментарий:**

The method of developing taste for the topics is explained. By service to the great devotees (mahat-sevayā), produced by great accidental mercy, by taking shelter of the feet of guru who cuts all doubts (puṇya-tīrtha-nisevanāt), taste for topics of Vāsudeva will arise for a person whose firm faith has awakened (śraddadhānasya) and who is eager to hear. Tīrtha means guru according to Amarakośa.

### 1.2.17

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa, who purifies by the processes of hearing and chanting, who is the benefactor of the devotees who hear about him, enters the hearts of the devotees and destroys their sins.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa, who becomes situated within by hearing topics – becoming the object of thought (antaḥ sthaḥ), destroys the inauspiciousness situated in the heart, through topics of his pastimes.

### 1.2.18

**Перевод:**

**As the impediments to bhakti become generally destroyed by constant service to the devotees and Bhāgavatam, the stage of niṣṭhā bhakti to Bhagavān, who is praised by the greatest sages, becomes established.**

**Комментарий:**

By service to devotees and Bhāgavatam, when anarthas in the form of offenses to the name are almost destroyed, steady (naiṣṭhikī) bhakti for the Lord appears.

### 1.2.19

**Перевод:**

**The mind becomes unaffected by lust, greed, anger, hatred and illusion which arise form rajas and tamas. Then the mind becomes fixed in the form of the Lord at the stage of āsakti and becomes satisfied.**

**Комментарий:**

The heart becomes unaffected (anāviddham) by lust and greed as well as anger, illusion, pride and envy and agitation and lethargy which arise (bhāvāḥ) from rajas and tamas. Taste for material objects becomes a taste for hearing and chanting. Then, the mind, steady (sthitam) in the Lord, the form of viśuddha-sattva (sattva), becomes satisfied.

### 1.2.20

**Перевод:**

**With a purified, detached mind, realization of the Lord manifests from bhakti-yoga.**

**Комментарий:**

By the mentioned method, for the pure mind (prasanna-manasaḥ), detached (mukta-saṅgasya), protected by bhakti-yoga to the Lord, direct realization (vijñānam) of the tattva of Bhagavān (bhagavat-tattva) as mentioned in vadvanti tat tattva vidaḥ (SB [1.2.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.11)) arises.

### 1.2.21

**Перевод:**

**Ignorance is cut and all doubts are destroyed. On seeing the Lord, all karmas are destroyed.**

**Комментарий:**

The accompanying results of directly realizing the Lord, who is the actual result, since he is form of the highest bliss, are described. Identification with the body (hṛdaya-granthiḥ) and all doubts are destroyed. On seeing the Lord (ātmani), all these are destroyed for the person who is dedicated to hearing about the Lord, etc. Doubts such as thoughts as “It is impossible to understand the Lord by hearing. By contemplation one will have thoughts contrary to the Lord and by seeing, qualification of being a knower is destroyed.” All karmas are destroyed. This means that by the Lord’s will, an ābhāsa of karma remains. The reflexive verbs (i.e. bhidyate, chidyante, & kṣīyante) indicate that these results are not sought by the devotee. Here, the attainment is shown by the path of mercy of devotees, serving them, faith, taking shelter of guru, desire for bhakti, bhakti, destruction of anarthas, niṣṭhā, āsakti and prema.

### 1.2.22

**Перевод:**

**Thus the wise constantly perform bhakti - which purifies the mind - to Lord Kṛṣṇa with great joy.**

**Комментарий:**

Having shown the proper conduct, now everything is summarized. The wise perform bhakti, which purifies the mind (ātmāprasādinīm). It must always be done (nityam). They perform bhakti with the greatest joy (paramayā mudā). This means bhakti is a form of happiness. O best of the brāhmaṇas, may you have undeviating bhakti for me. That bhakti is the crest jewel of all auspiciousness and full of bliss eternally. (Nāradīya Purāṇa)

### 1.2.23

**Перевод:**

**The one supreme puruṣa, accepting the guṇas of prakṛti known as sattva, rajas and tamas, for creation, maintenance and destruction of the universe, is called Viṣṇu, Brahmā and Śiva. The best results for the devotees will come from Viṣṇu with śuddha-sattva body.**

**Комментарий:**

It was said that the wise perform bhakti to Vāsudeva. This is further explained. The supreme person, the svāṁśa of Kṛṣṇa, Garbhodaśaya, one entity, for the purpose of creation, maintenance and destruction of the universe (asya) in pastimes of the eka-pādavibhūti (iha), endowed with sattva and other guṇas and stimulating them individually, takes (dhatte) the names of Viṣṇu, Brahmā and Śiva. The sandhi of harā with iti is poetic license. Brahmā, Śiva, and jīvas with great puṇya are endowed with śakti of creation and destruction and instigate rajas and tamas. I make whomever I desire into Śiva; I make whomever I desire into Brahmā or a sage. (Ṛg Veda 10.125.05.2) I create Brahmā and Śiva. They do not know me. My māyā is bewildering. They are the best of the devatās and arise from my pleasure and anger. Shown the method, they engage in creation and destruction. (Mahābhārata) The Lord himself instigates sattva from a great distance. Lord Hari, however, has no connection with the material modes. He is the Supreme Lord, the all-seeing eternal witness, who is transcendental to material nature. One who worships him becomes similarly free from the material modes. (SB [10.88.5](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/88.5)) Among the three, dharma, artha, kāma and mokṣa (śreyāṁsī) will arise for men from Viṣṇu, who distributes sattva (sattva-tanoḥ). By the other two, covered by rajas and tamas, dharma, artha and kāma arise to some degree.

### 1.2.24

**Перевод:**

**Smoke is superior to dull wood, and fire, sacred to the Vedas, is superior to smoke. Similarly rajas is superior to tamas, and sattva is superior to rajas, since it is favorable for realizing the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

This is illustrated with an example. Wood is devoid of manifestation of activity. It is not like smoke which by a portion (of heat) is related to fire. Superior to material wood (dārunaḥ), kindling for producing the sacrificial fire, is smoke which by a portion is related to fire and which has a portion of heat and activity as its nature. It is deeply connected with the karmas mentioned in the Vedas (trayīmayaḥ), since it produces desire for the karmas. Superior to smoke is the fire, also connected with the Vedas (trayīmayaḥ), since it directly produces the karmas. Superior to tamas, which is filled with mental inaction, is rajas which gives a slight vision of Brahman. It comes near to Brahman because it is a cause of knowledge in practical dealings. But sattva gives a vision of Brahman. Sattvāt sañjāyate jñānam: from sattva, knowledge arises. (BG [14.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/14.17)) Sattva is a cause of direct vision of Brahman by self-manifesting knowledge. Therefore Viṣṇu, Vāsudeva (sattva-tanuḥ) should be served. Brahmā and Śiva are called non-different from Viṣṇu because they are empowered by him.

### 1.2.25

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the ancient sages worshipped Supreme Lord, beyond the material senses, composed of viśuddha-sattva. Those who follow the sages attain auspiciousness in this world.**

**Комментарий:**

The correct conduct in bhakti is shown. Because of this (atha), sages like Sanaka worshipped the Lord, the form of viśuddha-sattva. Those who follow the sages (tān) attain auspiciousness (kṣemāya). One should not think that viśuddha sattva is material sattva devoid of rajas and tamas, since the Lord is devoid of contact with material sattva. The guṇas, tamas, rajas and sattva, are invariably mixed with each other at all times and all places. There are many scriptural statements showing that the Lord is untouched by māyā. He is a form of self-revealing knowledge according to some statements. He is a knower, a form of saṁvit śakti. This becomes clear later: O Lord, cause of all causes, if your transcendental body were not beyond the modes of material nature, one could not understand the difference between matter and transcendence. (SB [10.2.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/2.35))

### 1.2.26

**Перевод:**

**Rejecting the frightful forms of Śiva or other devatās, persons desirous of the Lord, without criticizing those devatās, worship the avatāras of Nārāyaṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

It is seen that some worship Bhairava and other devatās. That is true. They have material desires. Those devoid of material desires do not worship others. Those who, having given up degraded saṁsāra, desire the Lord full of bliss (mumukṣavaḥ), worship the avatāras (kalāḥ) of the Lord. They give up the lord of bhūtas. Worshipping kalās of the Lord, they give up those other forms and become peaceful (śāntāḥ). These are ferocious forms (ghora-rūpān). But the devotees do not criticize the devatās (anasūyavaḥ).

### 1.2.27

**Перевод:**

**Desirers of progeny and power along with wealth, having natures of rajas and tamas, corresponding to the natures of their deities, worship the pitṛs, Śiva, Brahmā and others.**

**Комментарий:**

“Why do they worship others, since a person with material desires obtains all results from worshipping Viṣṇu?” Persons who have natures similar to the pitṛs, etc., having natures of rajas and tamas, who desire sons and power along with wealth (śriyā) worship those deities. They do not desire liberation.

### 1.2.28-29

**Перевод:**

**Vāsudeva is the purport of the Vedas. Vāsudeva is the object of all sacrifices. Yoga, varṇāśrama, knowledge and austerities are all dependent on Vāsudeva. Dharma is dependent on Vāsudeva. Liberation is dependent on Vāsudeva.**

**Комментарий:**

Because it gives liberation, bhakti to Vāsudeva is recommended. This is also the purport of all scriptures. Thus bhakti to Vāsudeva is suitable. That is described in two verses. The Vedas have Vāsudeva as their subject (parā). Viṣṇu whom all the Vedas glorify. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad [2.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/2.15)) I am to be known by all the Vedas. (BG [15.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/15.15)) All the Vedas make Viṣṇu known, directly and by paramparā. Though one sees recommendation for sacrifices in the Vedas, those statements are made from the point of view of tattva-jñāna. Sacrifices (makhāḥ) indicate Vāsudeva. Karmas (kriyāḥ) such as ritualistic bathing, sandhyopāsana, etc., indicate Vāsudeva. Dharma in the form of offering one’s enjoyable objects to suitable candidates indicate Vāsudeva. By purifying the heart, these actions lead to Vāsudeva. Yogā means aṣṭāṅga-yoga which stops mental functions. Tapaḥ means austerities in serving the five fires, etc. They indicate Vāsudeva by control of the senses. Jñāna, meaning learning grammar, leads to the Lord by entering into the meaning of the Upaniṣads. Liberation (gatiḥ) leads to the Lord since it involves bhakti also.

### 1.2.30

**Перевод:**

**The powerful Lord alone, devoid of material guṇas, previously created this universe by his material energy composed of material guṇas and endowed with cause and effect.**

**Комментарий:**

“Should not the Vedas indicate that Vāsudeva is supreme as the creator of the universe, entering it and controlling it?” Four verses answer. This verse answers the question tasya karmāṇy udārāṇi brūhi: describe the Lord’s great activities (SB [1.1.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/1.17)). Bhagavān Vāsudeva created this universe by his māyā, made of the three guṇas, with gross and subtle aspects (sad-asad-rūpayā). The Lord is devoid of guṇas.

### 1.2.31

**Перевод:**

**The Lord entered into the jīvas covered by the guṇas, manifested by material māyā. He appears to be in contact with the guṇas, but he is strengthened by his spiritual potency.**

**Комментарий:**

Entering and controlling the jīvas are described. The Lord remained as a controller (antaḥ-praviṣṭaḥ) in the jīvas (eṣu) endowed with bodies and senses made of guṇas (guṇeṣu) and which were made objects of pastimes (vilasiteṣu) by māyā (tayā). He appeared to be overcome by the guṇas, becoming ignorant, but he was not. He remained effulgent (ābhāti). He remained powerful (vijṛmbhitaḥ) by his cit-śakti (vijñānena), untouched by the guṇas. It will be said: This is the power of the Lord: though he is situated in prakṛti, he is not affected by the guṇas which are situated in him, just as the intelligence of the devotee remembering the Lord is not affected by the guṇas. (SB [1.11.38](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/11.38))

### 1.2.32

**Перевод:**

**Just as one fire, situated in pieces of wood as its natural place of manifestation, blazes forth as many fires, the one Supreme Lord, the soul of the universe, Paramātmā, situated in all living beings, manifests as many.**

**Комментарий:**

Though the Lord is one, he has many forms. One fire, always situated in various woods which are its source (yoniṣu), shines variously. Similarly, the one ātmā of the universe, Vāsudeva, shines in all beings (bhūteṣu). He is non-different from all these forms, while being differentiated since they are controlled by him. eko ’pi san bahudhā yo vibhāti: Though he is one, he manifests as many. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad [1.19](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/1.19)) Just as fire becomes manifested by rubbing the wood, and burns wood, by meditation, the Lord becomes manifest and burns up the jīva’s upādhis.

### 1.2.33

**Перевод:**

**The Lord as Paramātmā, having entered all the bodies with material sense objects, senses and mind, which have been created by the Lord, makes the jīvas enjoy the sense objects colored by the guṇas.**

**Комментарий:**

Nourishing the jīvas is described. The ātmā of the universe (asau) enters the living beings (bhūteṣu) of four types by birth from eggs, etc. composed of mind, ahaṅkāra, mahat-tattva (ātmabhiḥ) as well as elements (bhūta), tanmātras (sūkṣma) and senses such as the ear (indriyāṇi). He makes the jīvas enjoy (bhuṅkte) objects suitable to them (tad-guṇān). Bhuṅkte here has a causative meaning. Bhuṅkte can also mean “nourishes.” Ātmanepada is poetic license. Bhujo ’navane: bhuj is used in ātmanepada except when it means to protect. (Pāṇini 1.3.66)

### 1.2.34

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, creator of the universe, absorbing himself in the forms of various līlāvatāras as devatās, animals and humans, protects the worlds through his role as the controller of sattva-guṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

This answers the question sūta jānāsi (SB [1.1.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/1.12)): “You know for what purpose the Lord, master of the Yadus, appeared in Devakī, the wife of Vasudeva.” The Lord (eṣaḥ) protects (bhāvayati) the worlds (lokān) by sattvaguṇa controlled by himself since he is the creator of the universe (loka-bhāvanaḥ). It is understood from Kuntī’s prayers that Kṛṣṇa avatāra is the goal, equivalent to all avatāras. He becomes absorbed (anurataḥ) in his forms as līlāvatāras in forms of devatās, etc.

## Chapter Three - List of the Avatāras

### 1.3.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: First of all, the Supreme Lord accepted the form of the eternal first puruṣa endowed with his sixteen śaktis, for creating the universes from mahā-tattva and other elements.**

**Комментарий:**

In the third chapter, topics of the auspicious appearance and activities of the puruṣa and other avatāras, which the sages requested previously, are glorified. The sages asked: Please relate topics of the Lord’s avatāras. (SB [1.1.18](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/1.18)) As an answer, the avatāras are described in order, staring with the puruṣāvatāras in five verses (1-5). The Lord glanced and created the worlds. (Aitareya Upaniṣad [1.1.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/aitareya/1.1.1)) By transformations of prakṛti, agitated by his glance, with a desire to create lokas (loka-sisṛkṣayā) by ahaṅkāra, tanmātras, etc., with a desire, “May the worlds be created,” the Lord accepted the form of the puruṣa (pauruṣam), or the form called the puruṣa. This means he engaged that form which already existed for creation of the planets, just as one takes a pot which already exists. To understand this, there is a further explanation. The form was highest (sam) real existing being (sambhūtam). Bhūtam means living being according to Nānārtha-varga. The puruṣa was endowed with sixteen kalas: such as Śrī and Vimalā. The first puruṣa is Saṅkarṣaṇa, Kāraṇodaśaya.

### 1.3.2-3

**Перевод:**

**After Garbhodakaśāyī-viṣṇu lay on the water and went into trance, Brahmā, lord of the universal creation, appeared from the lotus in the water of his navel. The expanse of planets is imagined to be situated at various places on his limbs. This form of the Lord is pure sattva, unmixed with rajas and tamas.**

**Комментарий:**

Brahmā arose from the lotus in the lake of the navel of the form lying in the Garbhodaka ocean (ambhasi), the water within the created shell (garbha), in a sleep which was samādhi (yoga-nidrām). Brahmā was the father of created beings (viśva-sṛjām) such as Dakṣa. The lokas from Pātāla to Satyaloka (loka-vistaraḥ) were arranged according to the configuration of the limbs of his form, beginning with the feet (avayava-saṁsthānaiḥ), due to their similarity. Pātāla is the sole of his foot. (SB [2.1.26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.26)) By such statements, steadiness of the mind is taught for persons of gross intellect. Definitely (vai) that form was pure, devoid of any particle of matter and characterized by his self-manifesting śakti (sattvam). It was strong (ūrjitam). Sat means that it negates prakṛti.

### 1.3.4

**Перевод:**

**With spiritual eyes, the siddhas see this amazing form with thousands of legs and arms, thousands of heads, ears, eyes and noses, shining with thousands of crowns, earrings and clothes.**

**Комментарий:**

Siddhas see that form by bhakti. They see with eyes which are purified by bhakti. Adabhram means not scant. Mauli is a head ornament. The rest is clear.

### 1.3.5

**Перевод:**

**He is the indestructible source of various avatāras. His expansion is Brahmā and Brahmā’s expansions are Marīci and others. Through them the Lord creates the devatās, animals and human beings.**

**Комментарий:**

This form is the source of all the avatāras that will be described. He is the aṁśī (nidhānam) of the avatāras. Though he is the seed, no seed is equal to him: he is eternal (avyayam). Devatās and others vibhūtis are created by his aṁśas such as Brahmā, and Brahmā’s aṁśas such as Marīci. This is the second puruṣa, Pradyumna, Garbhodaśaya. The third puruṣa Aniruddha, was described in the verse sattvaṁ rajas tamaḥ (SB [1.2.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/2.23)). These three forms starting with Saṅkarṣaṇa are explained in Brahmā-saṁhitā. The first is the antaryāmī of prakṛti. The second is the antaryāmī of Brahmā. The third, the size of a pradeśa, is the antaryāmī of the jīvas like us. The puruṣa is described in Sātvata-tantra: The wise know that the puruṣa consists of three forms of the original Viṣṇu–Saṅkarṣaṇa. The first is the creator of the mahat-tattva. The second is situated within the universe. The third is situated within each living entity. Knowing these three, one becomes liberated. “In Nārāyaṇīya (Mahābhārata) it is said: The fourth form, creator of indestructible Śeṣa is called Saṅkarṣaṇa. He manifested Pradyumna. From Pradyumna appears Aniruddha, myself. This happens repeatedly. From Aniruddha, Brahmā appears on a lotus. (Mahābhārata Śānti-parvā 326.68-69) Aniruddha, who, equipped with power of manifestation, created Brahmā, who is called the great soul of the worlds. (Mahābhārata Śānti-parvā 327.26) It is said here that Aniruddha produces Brahmā. But that is resolved by saying that he is nondifferent from Pradyumna. Otherwise it would contradict verse two of this chapter.

### 1.3.6

**Перевод:**

**Garbhodakaśāyī first made his appearance in the Kumāras. Becoming brāhmaṇas, they undertook continuous, severe vows of brahmacarya.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the activities of Sanat Kumāra and other avatāras will be discussed. Garbhodakaśaya (saḥ), a svāṁśa of Kṛṣṇa, his master, manifested (āśritaḥ) in the Kumāras (kaumāraṁ sargam). Becoming brāhmaṇas (brahmā), they taught the people unbroken brahmacarya. The four kumāras are considered one avatāra. Words like “first” indicate not the actual order of their appearance but just numbering.

### 1.3.7

**Перевод:**

**Secondly the Lord of sacrifice, lifting up the earth from Rasātala, for its welfare, took the form of the boar.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord manifested (upādatta) the form of a boar. For the auspiciousness (bhavāya) of the universe (asya), he will lift up the earth which had fallen to Rasātala during pralaya. The activity of Varāha is described. In these verses, each avatāra and his actions will be described. He appeared during Svayambhuva manvantara from the nostril of Brahmā.

### 1.3.8

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, making his appearance among the sages as Nārada, produced the Pañcarātra scriptures from which one learns how devotional activities free one from the bondage of karma.**

**Комментарий:**

The third avatāra is described. The Lord accepted manifestation (sargam) among the sages (ṛṣi) as Nārada (devarṣitvam). He produced Pañcarātra scriptures (sātvataṁ tantram) from which the bondage of karma is destroyed (naiṣkarmyam), from offering karmas to the Lord, like purifying mercury of its poison.

### 1.3.9

**Перевод:**

**Fourth, appearing in the wife of Dharma, he became Naranārāyaṇa, and performed severe austerities which give peace to the soul.**

**Комментарий:**

As the fourth avatāra (turye), the Lord appeared as Nāra-nārāyaṇa. Dharma-kalā, part of Dharma, refers to the wife of Dharma, since the śruti says ardho vā eṣa ātmano yat patnī: the wife is half of oneself. Manifesting in her (sarge), the Lord appeared as two entities. They are considered to be one avatāra. Elsewhere, it is stated that Hari and Kṛṣṇa were their brothers and non-different from them.

### 1.3.10

**Перевод:**

**Fifth, he became Kapila, best of the perfected beings, and spoke to Āsuri brāhmaṇa the philosophy of Sāṅkhya, which defines the various principles of existence, and which had become lost with time.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord as the son of Kardama (Kapila) spoke Sāṅkhya to his disciple named Āsuri. From Padma Purāṇa it is understood that a jīva of devious intellect born in the Agni dynasty spoke atheistic Sāṅkhya (a different Kapila).

### 1.3.11

**Перевод:**

**Being requested by Anasūyā, the wife of Atri, the Lord became her son as Dattātreya and taught the knowledge of soul to Alarka, Prahlāda and others.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes Dattātreya avatāra. He is the sixth. Requested by Anasūya, wife of Atri, the Lord accepted to be the son of Anasūya. This is described in the Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa in the story of the pativrata: Offering respects, Anasūyā spoke to the Brahmā, Śiva and Viṣṇu. If you are pleased with me, and consider me worthy of blessing, being merciful, you all should become my son. Ānvīkṣikīm means knowledge of the ātmā.

### 1.3.12

**Перевод:**

**Seventh, Yajña appeared as the son of Ruci in his wife Akūtī. He protected the reign of Svāyambhuva-manu with the devatās called Yāmas.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes the avatāra Yajña. He appeared as the son of Ruci (the father) in Ākutī, his mother. He protected the Svāyambhuva manvantara along with his sons, the Yāmas, who are devatās. At that time, he became Indra.

### 1.3.13

**Перевод:**

**Eighth, the Lord was born to Nābhi and his wife Merudevī as Ṛṣabha. He shows the path of the wise worshipped by all āśramas.**

**Комментарий:**

The eighth avatāra Ṛṣabha is described. He was born from Nābhi, son of Āgnīdhra, in the womb of Meru-devī. He showed the pāramahaṁsya state for the sober minded, which is respected by all āśramas.

### 1.3.14

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇas! Ninth, requested by the sages the Lord took the form of King Pṛthu. Because he milked herbs from the earth, he is considered the most desirable of the avatāras.**

**Комментарий:**
