The avatāra Pṛthu is described. The Lord took the form (vapuḥ) of Pṛthu, in the body of a king (pārthivam). He milked all substances (oṣadhīḥ) from the earth (imāṁ). Dugdha should actually be adugdha. Because of milking the earth, this avatāra is considered to be most attractive (uśattamaḥ).

### 1.3.15

**Перевод:**

**When there was an inundation at the end of the period of the Cākṣuṣa Manu, the Lord accepted the form of a fish and protected Vaivasvata Manu, placing him on a boat formed from the earth.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes Matsya. In Cākṣuṣa period, when there was a flood, the Lord accepted the form of Matsya. Placing Vaivasvata Manu in a boat in the form of the earth, he protected him. He was Satyavrata and later was called Vaivasvata Manu. There was a flood after the Cākṣuṣa manvantara. Viṣṇu-dharmottara says that there is flood after each manvantara.

### 1.3.16

**Перевод:**

**In the eleventh appearance, the Lord, in the form of a tortoise, held up on his back Mandara Mountain for the devatās and demons churning the ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

Kūrma avatāra is described. Situated between the devatās and demons churning the ocean to produce nectar, the Lord held up Mandara Mountain on his back, in the form of Kūrma.

### 1.3.17

**Перевод:**

**The Lord took the form of Dhanvantari as the twelfth avatāra and taking the thirteenth form as the woman Mohinī he let the devatās drink nectar and bewildered the demons.**

**Комментарий:**

Dhanvantari and Mohinī avatāras are described. Dhanvantari is the twelfth form. His activity was bringing the pot of nectar. The use of dvādaśamam instead of dvādaśam is poetic license. Taking the thirteenth form, he arranged for the devatās to drink nectar. What was his form? As the woman, Mohinī, he bewildered the demons (anyān).

### 1.3.18

**Перевод:**

**Taking the form of Narasiṁha as the fourteenth avatāra, he pierced the chest of the strong king of the demons with his nails just as a grass cutter breaks erakā grass.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes Nṛsiṁha avatāra. He pierced the strong king of demons, Hiraṇyakaśipu, with his fingernails (karajaiḥ), just as a wood cutter (kaṭa-kṛt) breaks erakā grass. Erakā is a type of long grass without joints.

### 1.3.19

**Перевод:**

**Taking the form of Vāmana as fifteenth avatāra, the Lord went to the sacrifice of Bali, begging three steps of land, but desiring to steal the heavenly kingdom from him.**

**Комментарий:**

Vāmana avatāra is described. He desired to take away (prayāditsuḥ) from Bali the heavenly kingdom.

### 1.3.20

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, appearing as Paraśurāma, the sixteenth avatāra, seeing the kings harassing the brāhmaṇas, became angry and twentyone times annihilated the kṣatriyas from the earth.**

**Комментарий:**

Paraśurāma avatāra is described. He killed the kṣatriyas twentyone times (triḥ-sapta-kṛtvaḥ). In regards to the suffix kṛtva, since the action is repeated many times, for multiplication the suffix suc is applied (for triḥ). (Pāṇini 5.4.17-18)

### 1.3.21

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, born from Parāśara in the womb of Satyavatī as the seventeenth avatāra, seeing the meager intelligence of the people, divided up the tree of the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyāsa avatāra is described. Seeing people of little intelligence (alpamedhasaḥ), he was born from Parāśara in Satyavatī. Vyāsa’s nature as Supreme Lord is shown by this action. Know that the compiler named Kṛṣṇa-dvaipāyana is Nārāyaṇa himself. Who else except the Lord could produce the Mahābhārata? (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 3.4.5)

### 1.3.22

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, taking the form of a king as Rāma, performed brave actions such as controlling the ocean with a desire to help the devatās.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes the avatāra Rāma. He took the form of a king (nara-devatvam) as Rāma. This is the eighteenth form (param). Controlling the ocean by building a bridge shows his great power. Thus it is mentioned as his main activity.

### 1.3.23

**Перевод:**

**The Lord appeared in the Vṛṣṇi dynasty in the two forms of Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa as the nineteenth and twentieth avatāras and relieved the burden of the earth.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes the avatāras Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa. The word should be viṁśatitame instead of viṁśatime, but the syllable ta is dropped for metrical reasons. The Lord became (prāpya janmanī) two manifestations by the names of Balarāma and Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.3.24

**Перевод:**

**Then when Kali-yuga starts, Buddha, the son of Ajina, will appear in the province of Gayā for bewildering the demons.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes Buddha avatāra. The son of Ajina will appear in Kīkaṭa in Gayā province, in the forest of dharma.

### 1.3.25

**Перевод:**

**At the junction of the yugas when the kings are almost criminal, the Lord of the universe Kalki will be born as the son of Viṣṇuyaśas.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes Kalki avatāra. He appears at the end of Kaliyuga (yuga-sandhyāyām). Kalki will appear from the brāhmaṇa named Viṣṇu-yaśas. Among the avatāras, the Kumāras, Nārada, Pṛthu, Paraśurāma and Kalki are great jīvas, aveśāvatāras. The Lord enters into the Kumāras and Nārada. (Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta [1.226](https://vedarama.com/en/books/lb/1.226)) āviveśa pṛthuṁ devaḥ śāṅkhī cakṛi gadādharaḥ The Lord holding the conch, cakra and club entered Pṛthu. (Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta [1.227](https://vedarama.com/en/books/lb/1.227)) O goddess! The activities of the great soul Parāśurāma, a śaktyāveśāvatāra of the Supreme Lord, holder of the bow, has been described. (Padma Purāṇa 6.241.79) At the end of Kali-yuga Vāsudeva enters into Kalki, a teacher of spiritual matters, and protects the world. (Viṣṇudharma) Other than these forms all are the Supreme Lord.

### 1.3.26

**Перевод:**

**Unlimited avatāras arise from the Lord, the treasure house of pure goodness, just as thousands of small rivers flow from an inexhaustible lake.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse summarizes all the avatāras since some like Hayagrīva and Haṁsa were not mentioned in this list. The avatāras of the Lord, an ocean of independence (sattva-nidheḥ) are countless. Sattvam means independent, and that refers to Kṛṣṇa and no one else. Know that all others are called asattvam because they are dependent, O Bhārata. (Mahābhārata) The avatāras of the Lord who is independent, appearing by his own will, are countless. An example is given. Countless avatāras arise from the Lord like thousands of small streams (kulyāh), natural torrents, flowing from an inexhaustible (avidāsinaḥ) lake. The root is das (avidāsinaḥ).

### 1.3.27

**Перевод:**

**All sages, Manus, devatās, powerful humans who are sons of Manu, are also considered to be vibhūtis of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Having described the avatāras, now the vibhūtis are described.

### 1.3.28

**Перевод:**

**All avatāras mentioned and not mentioned here, who are portions of Mahāviṣṇu or empowered jīvas, create happiness in the world whenever it is afflicted by the demons and their ideas. But Kṛṣṇa is the ultimate form of Bhagavān.**

**Комментарий:**

Having included Kṛṣṇa among avatāras like Matsya and Kūrma, because of general characteristics of appearing in the world, the author now remembers Kṛṣṇa’s true position. Those avatāras previously described (ete) and those not described (ca), are aṁśas and kalās of Garbhodaśaya Viṣṇu (puṁsaḥ). Matsya and others are aṁśas. Kumāras, and others are āveśas possessing portions of jñāna, bhakti or kriyā śaktis. The word tu indicates difference or restriction. Among those described, Kṛṣṇa is Svayam Bhagavān while others like Matsya are not the aṁśī. His position does not depend on any other form. Svayaṁ dāsās tapasvinaḥ: the austere people are their own servants. They are not dependent on others.Thus Kṛṣṇa’s nature as bhagavān is not dependent on others. Gopyas tapaḥ kim acaran: what austerity did the cowherds perform. (SB [10.4.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/4.14)) Regarding the cowherds’ austerity, it is concluded that they are self-perfected, and not dependent on others. This indicates that Kṛṣṇa is not the lord of Vaikuṇṭha as Vāsudeva. That would contradict the words “svayam.” This statement, a form of śruti (literal meaning), is strong because it does not depend on other statements. Statements that he is an aṁśa are sometimes seen but these are weak since they are contextual. śruti-liṅga-vākya-prakaraṇa-sthāna-samākhyānāṁ samavāye pāra-daurbalyam artha-viprakarṣātWhere there is a combination of literal meaning, interpretive meaning, altering the syntax, referring to other texts, philosophical stance, and etymological meaning, the later statements are considered progressively weaker in authority, because of the possibility of their producing contrary meaning. (Jaimini-sūtra 3.3.14) This is made clear by using the word aṁśa in the statement (in referring to other forms), which means they are similar. Moreover, at the beginning of the scripture, this chapter discussing the mystery of Kṛṣṇa’s birth is a sūtrabecause the statements concerning all the avatāras are indicated. Ete cāṁśa-kalā is the paribhāṣa because it makes a rule where a rule has not yet been applied. Wherever avatāras are described, they are understood to be aṁśas of the puruṣāvatāra. But Kṛṣṇa is Bhagavān Svayam. Though this is mentioned once, the statement spreads its meaning everywhere. Here Kṛṣṇa is proved to be Svayam Bhagavān, rather than Svayam Bhagavān becoming Kṛṣṇa because Dhvani-kṛtsays: anuvādam anuktvaiva na vidheyam udīrayetOne should not state a predicate before its subject, for it cannot thus stand without proper support. The relation of aṁśa and aṁśī is shown in the sūtras: prakāśādivan naivaṁ paraḥ The avatāras are not like jīvas but are the Lord, just as the sun and the firefly are called light but are different. smaranti ca The smṛtis confirm this. (Brahma-sūtras [2.3.44-45](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/2.3.44)) “How is this since Bhagavān is one tattva without difference?” One should accept that there are degrees of manifestation of śakti. A brāhmaṇa, sometimes quoting all scriptures, is said to be omniscient. Another time the same brāhmaṇa quoting one scripture, is said to be a little knowledgeable of scripture. Similarly, Kṛṣṇa manifests all śaktis at all times, whereas the aṁśas manifest some śakti as is neessary. Otherwise, what is ascertained about the aṁśī, understood in scriptures, would be shared by the aṁśas. This will be discussed more elsewhere. “But this would contradict the following. Mahā-varāha Purāṇa says sarve sarva-guṇaiḥ pūrṇāḥ sarva-doṣa-vivarjitāḥ: all the forms of the Lord are endowed with all qualities and devoid of all fault.” That is not so. That argument is defeated by the fact that Kṛṣṇa’s svarūpa is full of all spiritual qualities. And otherwise, the sūtra and statements that show the Lord’s various states would be contradicted. The goal of the ordinary avatāras is stated. The avatāras make happy (mṛḍayanti) the world which is disturbed by the demons, according to the occasions (yuge yuge).

### 1.3.29

**Перевод:**

**The pure person who chants with devotion the glories of the Lord’s appearance, which is mysterious, becomes free from all sorrows.**

**Комментарий:**

The results of glorifying the avatāras are described. Those who glorify (gṛṇan) the mysterious (guhyam) birth of the Lord become free of saṁsāra (duḥkha-grāmāt).

### 1.3.30

**Перевод:**

**This material realm, composed of the material guṇas and the elements from mahat-tattva to earth and with the Paramātmā as its basis, is a material form of the Lord who has a spiritual form of consciousness and is devoid of a material form.**

**Комментарий:**

“The universal form (i.e. virāḍ-rūpa) of the Lord has been recommended as the first type of worship in the Second Canto and other places with such verses as pātālam etasya hi pāda-mūlam: Pātāla is the base of his feet. (SB [2.1.26](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/1.26)) Why is this not considered an avatāra of the Lord?” This verse answers. This universal form is composed of the material guṇas and the elements from mahat-tattva to earth (mahadādibhiḥ) situated on the all-pervading svarūpa (ātmani) of the Lord who has a spiritual form of consciousness (cid-ātmanaḥ), and who is devoid of a material form (arūpasya). This form has been mentioned because it is for steadying the minds of people with material intelligence. In other words, since this universal form is composed of matter, it is not counted among the avatāras such as Matsya and Kūrma who are devoid of māyā.

### 1.3.31

**Перевод:**

**Just as unintelligent people attribute clouds to be the sky and particles of dust are attributed to be smoke, so unintelligent people impose material ideas on the jīva.**

**Комментарий:**

“How can one destroy saṁsāra, whose nature is experiences of happiness and suffering, just by hearing about the Lord’s birth?” Foolish people may imagine that groups of clouds in the sky are blue sky or that earthly dust particles in the air are smoke, since both are not visible. Similarly, foolish people impose on the jīva (draṣṭari), composed of knowledge, the visible qualities of body and antaḥkaraṇa such as “I am a human, pale, happy, sad.” This is like a piece of cloth that has become muddied. By hearing about the Lord, saṁsāra is washed away, as mud is removed by water, since the mud incidental.

### 1.3.32

**Перевод:**

**Superior to the gross universal form is that form which is invisible, devoid of the specific bodily parts, since it cannot be seen or heard. This is like the subtle body of the jīva, which is the instrument of rebirth for the jīva.**

**Комментарий:**

The jīva’s saṁsāra arises from being opposed to the Lord. Different from this gross form (ataḥ) is another subtle form (param avyaktam). It also is an imposition only. The reasons for being subtle is stated. It is devoid of a special form (avyūḍha) made of the guṇas, which are devoid of transformations in the form of hands, feet, etc. Because it is invisible, the jīva (saḥ) takes birth repeatedly (punar bhavaḥ). The reason for the imposition is given. It is unseen and unheard. The tattva of Bhagavān (vastu) is also unseen and unheard, since the jīva is opposed to the Lord without beginning. Thus it is said: In order to give mercy to the suffering people engaged in sin, who are under the control of karma and ignorant of Kṛṣṇa, the auspicious devotees of the Lord move in this world. (SB [3.5.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/5.3))

### 1.3.33

**Перевод:**

**When the gross and subtle material forms, which are impositions on the soul in ignorance, are removed by the realization of the devotees, one can realize Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

What is the nature of that Paramātmā whose glorification concerning his birth frees the jīva from all suffering (saṁsāra)? What is that knowledge of Paramātmā? From the meaning of the two verses 31 and 32, two facts emerge: the jīva (ātmani) is in ignorance and has gross and subtle bodies. When these two bodies (sad-asad-rūpe) are removed completely by one’s saṁvit śakti (svasaṁvidā), one realizes Brahman. Dhāmnā svena sadā: the Lord is beyond matter by his cit-śakti at all times. (SB [1.1.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/1.1)) The word iti means first, according to Amara-kośa. This is the jīva’s first knowledge (darśanam). Then, on understanding Paramātmā, who is devoid of māyā’s touch, who is the cause of the universe, who is glorious with beauty, and is condensed knowledge and bliss, the jīva becomes free from suffering by kīrtana about the Lord’s birth and pastimes, having become favorable to the Lord.

### 1.3.34

**Перевод:**

**When māya or ignorance weakens, the Lord bestows his mercy on the jīva. The jīva becomes endowed with this treasure. The wise understand that, this person is welcomed by the devotees in the spiritual world.**

**Комментарий:**

When does the jīva who has destroyed its opposition to the Lord, attain the Lord from glorifying him? When māyā, śakti of the Lord (devī), destroys the influence of avidyā, her function, and when the Lord, who knows the devotee’s inclination to him (vaiśāradī) desires, “May he attain my feet,” the devotee attains his feet. The wise understand this. tasya tāvad eva ciraṁ yāvan na vimokṣye’tha saṁpatsye Only as long as the body remains does he not become free. Then he achieves the Lord. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [6.14.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/6.14.2)) By the Lord’s desire, by the result of preaching of the sampradāya, this will happen gradually. Then one enters into the spiritual sky (sve mahimni) and is welcomed by the devotees (mahīyate).

### 1.3.35

**Перевод:**

**Thus rejecting the two bodies, the wise glorify the highest subject of the Vedas—the birth and activities of the Supreme Lord, who resides in the hearts of all beings, who has no material birth and no material activities.**

**Комментарий:**

By rejecting the gross and subtle forms in the above manner, the knowers of truth (kavayaḥ) describe the birth of the Lord without birth (ajanasya) and the activities of the Lord who has no material actions (akartuḥ). The lord is called ajana because he is devoid of connection with a body by karma. He takes birth, like the moon rising. Without birth, the Lord appears in many forms. (Mudgalopaniṣad 3.1) The Lord is himself the doer: He sees the Lord, creator of the world. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad [3.1.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/1.3)) The Lord is akartā: since he is devoid of performing action in combination with prakṛti. Because the ancient lord is not a doer who is dependent on matter, he is said to be the non-doer by the knowers of the Purāṇas. (Vāsudeva Adhyātma) He is the antaryāmī (hṛt-pateḥ). The meaning of this section is that the universal form is not included among the avatāras, since it does not have the same qualities as these forms.

### 1.3.36

**Перевод:**

**The independent Lord, who has spotless pastimes, who has entered into all beings, creates, maintains and destroys this universe. Being the master of the six opulences, he contacts the six sense objects (sound, touch, form, taste, smell and thought) without being affected.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord’s great powers that distinguish him from the jīva are described. The Lord (saḥ) creates, maintains (avati) and destroys (atti) this universe. In such (asmin) activities, he does not desire results (na sajjate). Then will his actions be useless? No. He is effective (amoghaḥ), since his actions are for the jīva’s enjoyment. He is situated within the living beings. He is the controller (īśaḥ) of the six senses (ṣaḍ-guṇa). The enjoyment is described. He experiences (jighrati) the happiness arising from his six powers (ṣāḍ-vargikam). By regulating the objects of the six senses, he experiences, but he does not desire any results for himself.

### 1.3.37

**Перевод:**

**The falsely intelligent person, with skill in jñāna or yoga cannot understand using their mind and words, the pastimes, names and forms of the Lord who distributes these whenever he descends, just as an ignorant person cannot appreciate the performance of an actor.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone devoid of bhakti and yet skillful at logic cannot understand the Lord. A foolish person cannot understand the pastimes (ūtīḥ) of the Lord who protects the world (asya dhātuḥ) by skillful intelligence fixed in dry logic (nipuṇena) and by mind and words, using that skillful logic. Who is this Lord? He spreads (santanvataḥ) his names and forms in this world. This is similar to an ignorant person who cannot understand gestures of an actor which indicate the moon, a lotus etc. The person in knowledge is not like this.

### 1.3.38

**Перевод:**

**He who worships the scent of the lotus feet of the Lord, sincerely, continuously, and with favorable attitude, knows the greatness of the holder of the cakra, the possessor of infinite prowess.**

**Комментарий:**

He is understood by the devotees. The Lord has unlimited powers (duranta-vīryasya). The affix dur means forbiddance according to Viśva-prakāśa. Thus duranta means with no end. He worships devoid of cheating (amāyayā), the scent of the Lord’s lotus feet continuously (santatayā) with devotion (anuvṛttyā).

### 1.3.39

**Перевод:**

**O men of knowledge! You are the most fortunate in this world, because when people concentrate their minds exclusively on Kṛṣṇa, the lord of all the worlds, by asking such questions, they cease painful repetition of birth.**

**Комментарий:**

An example is given to compare to the sages. You are fortunate since, being full of knowledge (bhagavantaḥ) you concentrate your minds on the Lord, in which state, the terrible repetition of birth and death will no longer take place.

### 1.3.40

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord Vyāsa wrote the greatest, most auspicious Purāṇa called Bhāgavatam, which bestows all human goals, is non-different from Kṛṣṇa, and describes the activities and qualities of the Lord, for the highest benefit of the world.**

**Комментарий:**

O Sūta! What is this attractive scripture not heard previously that you describe? It is equal to Kṛṣṇa (brahma-sammitam). Its svarūpa is described in Garuḍa Purāṇa: This work is most complete. It expresses the meaning of the Brahma-sūtras and Mahābhārata. It is the explanation of Gāyatrī and reinforces the meaning of the Vedas. It is the Sāma Veda among the Purāṇas and has arisen directly from the Lord. It has twelve volumes and a hundred divisions. It has eighteen thousand granthas. It is called Śrīmadbhāgavatam. Matsya Purāṇa says: It is called the Bhāgavatam, which describes the killing of Vṛtrāsura, and in which dharma is described in detail after referring to the Gāyatrī. The person who writes out the Bhāgavatam and then gifts it, placing it on a golden throne on the full moon of Bhadrā month, attains the highest goal. It is known as the Purāṇa with eighteen thousand verses. Skanda Purāṇa says: The scripture has eighteen thousand granthas and twelve volumes. In it, there is Hayagrīva-brahma-vidyā and the killing of Vṛtrāsura. It begins with the Gāyatrī mantra. This is known as Bhāgavatam. Padma Purāṇa says: O Ambarīṣa! Hear constantly the Bhāgavatam spoken by Śukadeva. Read it with your own mouth if you desire destruction of material life. One can see many such quotations elsewhere as well. The son of Parāśara (ṛṣiḥ), Vyāsa, manifested (cakāra) this work since it is said that he delivered it. Dhanyam means it brings pure devotees under its control. The rule is for the formation of dhanyam is found in Pāṇini 4.4.84.

### 1.3.41

**Перевод:**

**Vyāsa had his son Śukadeva, best of the self-controlled, study this scripture, which is the very essence of all the Vedas and histories.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyāsa had his son study this work, which extracts the essence of everything (sāram), like extracting butter from yogurt. The effort of extraction was successful. His son was the best of the ātmārāmas (ātmavatām). He developed greed for it since the taste was greater than the bliss of experiencing his own ātmā.

### 1.3.42

**Перевод:**

**Śukadeva spoke the Bhāgavatam to King Parīkṣit, who was surrounded by great sages, as he sat fasting till death on the bank of the Gaṅgā.**

**Комментарий:**

Śukadeva (saḥ) spoke it to Parīkṣit who was sitting, fasting till death (prāya). This indicates that he was most detached from the world. Thus he was qualified to hear. Prāya means not eating till death according to Medinī.

### 1.3.43

**Перевод:**

**Though Kṛṣṇa left Dvārakā, arrived at Prabhāsa, and then disappeared along with his six great qualities, this Purāṇa, another form of the sun, has now risen in Kali-yuga for those who have lost their knowledge.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse clarifies the statement purāṇaṁ brahma-sammitam: the Bhāgavatam is equal to Kṛṣṇa. This verse answers the question dharmaḥ kaṁ śaraṇaṁ gataḥ: where does dharma take shelter? (SB [1.1.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/1.23)) It will later be said: Kṛṣṇa, the sun, has set. (SB [3.2.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.7)) Lord Madhusūdana, the Supreme Lord, is eternally present in Dvārakā. (SB [11.31.24](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/31.24)) From these statements it is understood that when the sun, Kṛṣṇa, went to his abode, and when he could not be seen by people, but went to another manifestation of Dvārakā, along with his powers such as dharma and jñāna, this Purāṇa has risen like the sun for knowledge of the living beings who have lost knowledge (naṣṭadṛśām). The places are nondifferent.

### 1.3.44

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇas! I came and sat in the assembly, out of his great mercy. As I learned from the powerful Śukadeva, who recited it there, I will teach you, elaborating as necessary.**

**Комментарий:**

I learned this scripture from Śukadeva (vipraṛṣeḥ), effulgent with Kṛṣṇa who shone in his heart (bhūri-tejasaḥ), who recited Bhāgavatam in that assembly. Tejas means power or light according to Medinī. I came to the assembly and sat there, by his mercy. It will later be said: O best of the Kurus, the same Sūta Gosvāmī who is sitting before us will speak this Bhāgavatam to the sages assembled in the great sacrifice at Naimiṣāraṇya. This he will do when questioned by the members of the assembly, headed by Śaunaka. (SB [12.4.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-12/4.43)) As I learned it (yathādhītam), not from my imagination, I will tell you, elaborating what was told in summary (yathā matiḥ). Kīrtayataḥ and niviṣṭaḥ have two different subjects. There is no fault in this.

## Chapter Four - The appearance of Nārada to Vyāsa

### 1.4.1

**Перевод:**

**Vyāsa said: Among the sages gathered for the long sacrifice, Śaunaka, eldest by age, of high parentage and superior in Vedic knowledge, praising Sūta who had just spoken, began speaking.**

**Комментарий:**

In the Fourth chapter the excellence of the speaker and the hearer of the Bhāgavatam are declared. Also, Vyāsa’s pain in his heart in the absence of Bhāgavatam is described. Sūta had said (bruvāṇaṁ), “With pleasure, I will have you hear.” Among the sages, one who is the eldest should speak; and among the eldest, the head of the group; and among those are heads of groups, the knower of the Ṛg Veda (bahvṛcaḥ). With this qualification, Śaunaka, began speaking.

### 1.4.2

**Перевод:**

**Śaunaka said: O Sūta! O Sūta! Fortunate soul! Best of speakers! Please tell us the pure stories related to the Lord which the glorious Śuka spoke at the assembly of Parīkṣit.**

**Комментарий:**

He addresses Sūta twice out of respect. Yat stands for yām.

### 1.4.3

**Перевод:**

**In which yuga, in what place, and for what reason did the sage Vyāsa write this work? Who inspired him to write this work?**

**Комментарий:**

Taḥ is a sārva-vibhaktika (applicable to all cases of nouns). Kutaḥ means “by whom.” Kṛṣṇa refers to Vyāsa.

### 1.4.4

**Перевод:**

**His son, a great yogī, seeing all things equally, fully realized in Brahman, with mind concentrated on one goal, having dispelled ignorance, hid himself from the public and appeared to be a fool.**

**Комментарий:**

(SB [1.3.82](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/3.82) mentioned that Śukadeva spoke Bhāgavatam to Parīkṣit. Śaunaka describes Śukadeva as most renounced, in order to ask how he spoke Bhāgavatam. Śukadeva was fixed in samādhi (mahāyogī). He saw stones and gold equally (sama-dṛk). He was devoid of thinking, “That is mine and this is yours” (nirvikalpakaḥ). His mind ended in ātmā (ekānta-matiḥ). He had conquered sleep: his ignorance was destroyed(unnidraḥ). He was incognito (gūḍhaḥ). He was perceived (īyate) to be a fool.

### 1.4.5

**Перевод:**

**The young women playing in the water, on seeing the clothed Vyāsa, pursuing his naked son who had left home, became bashful and put on their clothing. Vyāsa was astonished that they were not embarrassed when his naked son passed by. When Vyāsa asked them the reason, they replied, “You make distinctions of male and female and he does not. He has pure eyes.”**

**Комментарий:**

The girls playing in the water, seeing Vyāsa fully clothed (anagnam) pursuing his son Śukadeva who was renouncing home, put on their clothing out of shyness. This indicates that Śukadeva was naked. They did not put on their clothing out of shyness in front of Śukadeva. Seeing this surprising response--that they were not embarrassed though young Śukadeva, naked, saw them—Vyāsa asked them why. When Vyāsa asked, they spoke. Because you are the Supreme Lord and think doubtfully, “These young girls examine the gaze of men,” you make distinctions between man and woman. Śukadeva is not like that since he is clearly a great yogī because he sees himself different from his body (vivikta-dṛṣṭeḥ). But Vyāsa is not inferior to Śukadeva, since he is eternally perfect as the Supreme Lord.

### 1.4.6

**Перевод:**

**After coming to the province of Kuru-jāṅgala, Śukadeva wandered in Hastināpura (the capital), like a madman, a mute or a fool. How did the people of the city recognize him?**

**Комментарий:**

Was he sought out by the people? He wandered first in Kuru \[Ch.4 jāṅgala provinces, and Hastināpura, the city named after elephant. Hasti was the name of a king. He built the city.

### 1.4.7

**Перевод:**

**How did the conversation of Parīkṣit with Śukadeva arise, through which this Vaiṣṇava scripture appeared?**

**Комментарий:**

Pāṇḍaveyasya refers to Parīkṣit. Sātvatī means “concerning the Lord (bhāgavatī)” Śrutiḥ means saṁhitā.

### 1.4.8

**Перевод:**

**The great devotee would wait in the houses of married couples only as long as it took to milk a cow, purifying the inhabitants by giving spiritual benefit.**

**Комментарий:**

Śukadeva must have remained a long time with Parīkṣit in order to explain this work. It could not be otherwise. But he would wait in a house for only the time it took to milk a cow. But he did not come for begging. He actually purified their houses. This shows the excellence of the speaker of Bhāgavatam.

### 1.4.9

**Перевод:**

**O Sūta! Parīkṣit is called the greatest of devotees. Please tell us about his astonishing birth and activities.**

**Комментарий:**

A description of the hearer, Parīkṣit is given in five verses (9-13). Gṛṇīhi means “please tell.”

### 1.4.10

**Перевод:**

**Why did this king, the glory of the Pāṇḍavas, neglect the wealth of kings and fast to death on the bank of the Gaṅgā?**

**Комментарий:**

Parīkṣit was the controller of all lands (saṁrāṭ). He gave up (anādṛtya) the wealth (śriyam) attained from Yudhiṣṭhira and others (adhirāṭ), who shine by ruling.

### 1.4.11

**Перевод:**

**Why did this brave young man, to whose feet enemies brought wealth and clearly bowed down for their own benefit, desire to give up such wealth, which is difficult to give up, along with his life airs?**

**Комментарий:**

Pāda-niketam means “foot-stool.” Ha means “clearly.” Enemies brought and gave wealth at his foot-stool. Parīkṣit was young, not old but desired (aiṣata) to give up all the wealth, along with even his life airs (asubhiḥ).

### 1.4.12

**Перевод:**

**Persons who are surrendered to the Lord live for the auspiciousness of the world: extinguishing repeated birth and death and supplying material needs, not for themselves. Why did Parīkṣit, becoming renounced, give up his body, which was meant as a shelter for others?**

**Комментарий:**

What is the use of wealth for the renounced? Devotees live for auspiciousness (śivāya) of the world. This is further described. They live for increasing jñāna and bhakti (bhavāya) and for wealth (bhūtaye). It is not proper to give up the body which is meant for helping others (parāśrayam) even if one has become indifferent to it.

### 1.4.13

**Перевод:**

**Please explain to us whatever I have asked you on this subject. I think that you are most capable of explaining all subjects, except the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

I think that you can speak correctly (snātam) on the meaning of the subject, except for Vedas, for which you are unqualified. “Since this work is a part of the Vedas, how can Sūta be the speaker?” Sūta is qualified for the Itihāsa and Purāṇa section of the Vedas as the rathakāra is qualified for the Vedic mantras of the agnyādhāna. The word nigama is used in (SB [1.1.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/1.3) Thus Bhāgavatam is not less than the Vedas. Rather it is the best of the Vedas, since it is said sarva-vedetihāsānāṁ sāraṁ sāraṁ samuddhṛtam: it is the essence of all Vedas and Itihāsas. (SB [1.3.41](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/3.41))

### 1.4.14

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Vyāsa was born in the womb of Satyavatī as a portion of the Lord when the third part of Dvāpara-yuga arrived in the passing of yugas.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyāsa’s birth is told in brief in order to reply to questions about the yuga. (SB [1.4.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/4.3)) In the progress (paryayaḥ) of many yugas such as Satya, in Dvāparayuga, in the third part, at the juncture, Vyāsa was born. Paryāya means passing according to Amara-kośa. Each yuga has three parts: sandhyā, yuga and sandhyāṁśa. This means Vyāsa was born in Vaivasvata Manvantara, twenty-eighth cycle, in the final portion (sandhyāṁśa) of Dvāpara yuga. He was born from Parāśara, the father, in the womb of Satyavatī, the mother (vāsavi), the daughter of Uparicara.

### 1.4.15

**Перевод:**

**At one time, Vyāsa, performing ācamana with water from the Sarasvatī River, being purified, sat alone in an isolated spot while the sun rose.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyāsa, having taken bath in the water of the Sarasvatī River, sat on the bank alone. The verb abhūt is missing. His sitting implies meditation. One should mediate while in a seated posture because then meditation becomes possible. (Brahma-sūtras [4.1.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/4.1.7))

### 1.4.16-18

**Перевод:**

**Vyāsa, who could see the past and the future, having pure vision, observed by his spiritual eye that the dharmas for the yugas had been destroyed on the earth, yuga after yuga, by the invisible force of time; that the bodies had decreased in ability; and that the people were devoid of faith, dominated by rajas and tamas, dull-witted, short-lived, and filled with misfortunes. He began to contemplate on what would be beneficial for all the varṇas and āśramas.**

**Комментарий:**

He knows the past and the future (parāvara-jñaḥ). Sitting there, he saw that there was disturbance of the dharmas of the yugas with time (yuga-dharma-vyatikaram). This verse 16 is connected with the verb vīkṣya in verse 18. Bodies (bhāvānam) had decreased in strength due to time (tatkṛtam). The people lacked determination (niḥsattvān) and were foolish (durmedhān). They were unfortunate (durbhagān).

### 1.4.19

**Перевод:**

**\[Seeing the purifying power of Vedic rites performed by the four Text 20\] The appearance of Nārada to Vyāsa 93 priests for the people at large who were not inclined for jñāna, or bhakti, he divided the one Veda into four for continuation of sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

The actions of Vyāsa for people’s benefit is described. The four priests are hotā, udgātā, adhvaryu and brahmā. The word hotram here indicates all of them. Seeing the purifying effect on the heart for people not suitable for jñāna or bhakti, of Vedic karmas of cāturhotram performed by the four priests, Vyāsa made one Veda into four so that the sacrifices would continue.

### 1.4.20

**Перевод:**

**He divided the Veda into the Ṛg, Yajus, Sāma and Atharva. Itihāsas and Purāṇas are called the fifth Veda.**

**Комментарий:**

He separated them (uddhṛtāḥ). The fifth Veda is so called because the Itihāsas and Purāṇas also emanate from the Lord’s breathing.

### 1.4.21-22

**Перевод:**

**For the Vedas, Paila became responsible for the Ṛg-veda, wise Jaimini chanted the Sāma-veda, Vaiśampāyana became learned in the Yajur-veda, the merciless sage Sumantu, inclined to incantations, became learned in the Atharva-veda and my father Romaharṣaṇa became learned in the histories and Purāṇas.**

**Комментарий:**

Kavi (Jaimini) means he wrote Dvādaśa-lakṣaṇī (Karma-mimāṁsa). Vaiśāmpāyana was the chief person due to being dedicated to Vyāsa (ekaḥ). Dāruṇaḥ means merciless. This is because Sumantu taught the Atharva-veda with many incantations for evil purposes in it.

### 1.4.23

**Перевод:**

**Each of these sages divided up his Veda into many divisions and these Vedas then developed branches as they were passed on to disciples, grand-disciples and great-grand-disciples.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyasyan means divided. These sages divided each Veda.

### 1.4.24

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord Vyāsa, merciful to the most fallen, thus divided the Vedas so that they could be understood by the foolish.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyāsa divided the Vedas which previously were understood by the intelligent, so that they could be understood by the unintelligent. He was merciful to the fallen (kṛpaṇa-vatsalaḥ).

### 1.4.25

**Перевод:**

**The women, śūdras and dvija-bandhus were not qualified to hear the Vedas. Thinking that karmas would be the best for the foolish in terms of the best sādhana, Vyāsa by his mercy wrote the Mahābhārata.**

**Комментарий:**

The dvija-bandhus are inferior among the three upper classes. Thinking that it would be best for the foolish to perform sādhana in the form of karmas (karma-śreyasi), he manifested the Mahābhārata.

### 1.4.26-27

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇas! Though he had engaged himself in this way for the welfare of all beings, his heart was not satisfied with all of that. Being not completely satisfied in heart, sitting in a solitary spot on the bank of the Sarasvatī River, the knower of dharma began to contemplate. He then spoke as follows.**

**Комментарий:**

Sarvātmakena means the same as sarvātmanā (by the whole). Ati means “praise” according to Viśva-prakāśa. This means his heart was devoid of excellent satisfaction. For this reason, he deliberated within himself and then spoke.

### 1.4.28

**Перевод:**

**I have respected the Vedas, the gurus and fire with sincerity and strict vows, and observed the rules.**

**Комментарий:**

I worshipped (mānita) the meters of the Vedas with sincere (nirvyalīkena) vows and observed the order of gurus (anuśāsanam).

### 1.4.29-30

*bhārata-vyapadeśena hy āmnāyārthaś ca darśitaḥ \| dṛśyate yatra dharmādi strī-śūdrādibhir apy uta \|\| tathāpi bata me daihyo hy ātmā caivātmanā vibhuḥ \| asampanna ivābhāti brahma-varcasya-sattamaḥ\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Through the Mahābhārata, in which dharma, artha, kāma and mokṣa for the women, śūdras, what to speak of the higher castes, is known, I have clearly shown the meaning of the Vedas. But still, my soul situated in the body, though powerful with austerity and knowledge by nature, appears incomplete and inferior, even though I am the best among the devotees endowed with power arising from studying the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Dṛśyate means “is known.” I have manifested the Brahma-sūtras and divided the Vedas as the job of my avatāra, but my ātmā, nondifferent from my body (daihyaḥ ātmā) though complete (vibhuḥ) in itself (ātmānā), seems incomplete (asampannaḥ). Daihya is derived from deha (body) with a similar meaning. His body and his ātmā were non-different. He was excellent (sattamaḥ) among those having power arising from study of the Vedas (brahma-varcasya).

### 1.4.31

**Перевод:**

**Perhaps bhāgavata-dharma pleasing to the paramahaṁsas has not been sufficiently described. And the elevated devotees alone are dear to the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The cause of dissatisfaction is described. In Mahābhārata, the bhāgavata-dharma is described by equating it with karma, etc, and in Brahma-sūtras it is described esoterically. He praises those dharmas. The devotees and the Lord are not pleased with me without describing them. How can I be satisfied?

### 1.4.32

**Перевод:**

**As Vyāsa was lamenting, considering himself most vile, Nārada approached the hermitage, which was previously described.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyāsa considered himself incomplete (khilam). Nārada approached the āśrama previously described, on the bank of the Sarasvatī. Since Vyāsa is the Lord, his ignorance and lamentation were caused by Kṛṣṇa in order to manifest Bhāgavatam.

### 1.4.33

**Перевод:**

**Understanding that Nārada, worshipped by the devatās, had quickly arrived, Vyāsa worshipped him as if he were Brahmā.**

**Комментарий:**

Sahasā means quickly.

## Chapter Five - Conversation Between Vyāsa and Nārada

### 1.5.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: The famous Nārada, with vīṇā in his hand, being seated comfortably, while slightly smiling, then spoke to Vyāsa, who was worshipping him.**

**Комментарий:**

In the Fifth chapter, speaking of the uselessness of crude jñāna and karma, Nārada speaks of the advantages of bhakti predominated by kīrtana. After being offered arghya, etc., and being seated comfortably, famous (bṛhac-chravāḥ) Nārada spoke to Vyāsa who was close by worshipping him. His smile indicated that he was pleased with Vyāsa.

### 1.5.2

**Перевод:**

**Nārada said: Great soul, son of Parāśara! If you think you are your body, is your body pleased with your body or not? Or if you think you are your mind, is your mind pleased with your mind or not?**

**Комментарий:**

He inquired about Vyāsa’s well-being. O fortunate one, an ocean of qualities related to your unlimited knowledge, strength and power! Are your body and mind, performing actions of your avatāra (ātmā), satisfied in their true nature (ātmanā)? Kaccit indicates a question according to Halāyudha. The Lord’s body and mind are non-different from his ātmā. With that understanding the words body and mind are used. There is no difference between the Lord’s body and his ātmā. (Kūrma Purāṇa) The body and mind are both himself. Śārīra and mānasa are alternatives for śarīra and manas with no change of meaning like prajñā and other words. Addressing Vyāsa twice indicates that it is not suitable for him to lament.

### 1.5.3

**Перевод:**

**You wrote the most astonishing Mahābhārata, complete with all instructions on dharma, artha, kāma and mokṣa. All that one desires to know is presented perfectly in this work.**

**Комментарий:**

You should not lament since you have accomplished your goal as avatāra. You have accomplished your goal in compiling dharma, etc., which is desirable to know. It is great by nature and most astonishing that you manifested Mahābhārata which is complete (paribhṛṁhitam) with meanings of dharmas, etc. (sarva).

### 1.5.4

**Перевод:**

**You have also investigated and realized the Brahman which is eternal. Then why should you lament that you have been unsuccessful?**

**Комментарий:**

You have investigated about the tattva of Supreme Brahman which is eternal (sanātanam) in Brahma-sūtrass, and have remembered and realized this (adhītam). Thus you have accomplished your work as avatāra. Then why do you lament as if not being successful, not accomplishing your goal?

### 1.5.5

**Перевод:**

**Vyāsa said: What you said about me is true. Yet my mind and body are not satisfied. I am asking the imperceptible reason for this from you because you were born from the body of self-born Brahmā and hence you possess great knowledge.**

**Комментарий:**

Being asked, Vyāsa answers. I ask you the reason (mūlam) for my dissatisfaction, which cannot be understood (avyaktam). You were born from the body of Brahmā (ātma-bhava). You can answer because of being born from Brahmā and because of your deep knowledge.

### 1.5.6

**Перевод:**

**The Lord is also you since you are an avatāra of the Lord. Certainly you know all secrets, because you worship the original person, the lord of cause and effect, who, remaining unaffected, creates, maintains and destroys the universe along with the guṇas by his will alone.**

**Комментарий:**

Agādha-bodham of verse 5 is explained. The cause of knowing all secrets is explained. You have worshipped the ancient person, Kṛṣṇa, who is the Lord of liberated and unliberated persons (parāvareśah), who creates the universe by his will alone (manasā), using the guṇas. He is untouched by the guṇas (asaṅgaḥ). You know the secret, being the son of Brahmā, and thus possessing great dharma, unlike me, having qualities as the son of Parāśara.

### 1.5.7

**Перевод:**

**You wander like the sun throughout the three worlds seeing everything and wander like the life air throughout the body as the witness of everyone’s intelligence. Though I have become very proficient in the Vedas and have realized the Brahman by proper methods, it is not enough. Please consider this.**

**Комментарий:**

By worship of the ancient Lord, you have attained great power. You see everything, like the sun wandering in the three worlds. You wander within all beings like the life air, by the power of yoga and witness the intelligence. You know the functions of intellect of all beings by your pervading wisdom. I have realized the Lord (pare brahmaṇi) by yoga (dharmaiḥ) and realized the Vedas (avare brahmaṇi) by study and rules (vrataiḥ). But that is insufficient. Please tell me.

### 1.5.8

**Перевод:**

**Nārada said: You have not sufficiently described the glories of the spotless Lord Kṛṣṇa. Because the Lord could never be satisfied with Vedānta, I think that writing the Vedānta-sūtras is insufficient.**

**Комментарий:**

Tell me what is lacking (nyūnam). You did not describe much about the glories of the Lord. “I revealed the Brahma-sūtrass, where the supreme tattva is described.” The Lord (asau) is not satisfied. I think that darśana to be insufficient (khilam) since it does not describe the Lord’s glories sufficiently. Otherwise, why would your heart feel dissatisfaction in manifesting this work?

### 1.5.9

**Перевод:**

**O venerable sage! You have not repeatedly described the glories of Vāsudeva Kṛṣṇa to the extent that you have glorified dharma, artha, kāma and mokṣa.**

**Комментарий:**

“But I glorified the Lord in Mahābhārata.” To the extent you have glorified dharma, etc., which are insignificant (ca meaning api), but which you made most prominent, you did not glorify the Lord. Ca means api. The Lord is somewhat glorified in some places. Mixing the Lord’s topics with dharma, etc., is like throwing a ruby into a pile of limestone pebbles. Thus you are dissatisfied. How can you be satisfied with Mahābhārata, a mixture of stories about vultures, jackals, cats and mice?

### 1.5.10

**Перевод:**

**Works whose attractive verses do not sing at all the glories of the Lord, which purify not only the author but the whole world, are considered by the devotees who have captured the mind of the Lord to be the place for crows, in which swans, living in pleasant lakes, take no enjoyment at all.**

**Комментарий:**

Poetic works devoid of glorification of the Lord are condemned. The words of poetry, though endowed with guṇa and alaṅkāra (citra-padam), but which do not at all have glorification of the Lord, which purifies both speaker and hearer, are considered to be holes where various left-over foods are strewn (vāyasaṁ tīrtham), since such places are desired by lusty people similar to crows. In those places the devotees who concentrate on the Lord (haṁsāḥ) do not enjoy. Sādhavo hṛdayaṁ mahyam: the devotees are dear to me. (SB [9.4.68](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-9/4.68)) The devotees have the feet of the Lord as their attractive (uśik) abode (kṣayāḥ). Kṣi (to live) becomes kṣaya by Pāṇini 3.3.56. The famous swans live in Mānasa Sarovara, and associate with attractive lotuses there (uśik-kṣayāḥ). They do not enjoy the hole with discarded food.

### 1.5.11

**Перевод:**

**The use of words in which, though not perfectly composed, there are the names of the Lord and descriptions of his glories which devotes hear, sing, and again receive, destroys the sins of all people.**

**Комментарий:**

If poetry does not contain guṇa and alaṅkāra, but has glorification of the Lord, it is purifying and should be served. The devotees after hearing and singing those creations of words which destroy the sins of living beings (janatāgha-viplavaḥ) up to ignorance, though containing imperfect meter or wrong words, or incorrect grammar, but which contain the names of the Lord, sing them and hear them again, not being fully satisfied.

### 1.5.12

**Перевод:**

**Even the stage of jñāna without the bondage of karma is not glorious if it is devoid of bhakti to the Supreme Lord. What is the use of having destroyed ignorance? What then to speak of sakāma-karma which causes suffering, both during practice and at the stage perfection, and niṣkāma-karma, when not offered to the Lord?**

**Комментарий:**

Not only are compositions made of words without glorification of the Lord useless, but jñāna and karma obtained in the Vedas devoid of prema for the Lord are also useless. Jñāna, whose subject is one’s ātmā, which is a method of liberation (naiṣkarmyam) if devoid of the Lord, does not shine. Prajāpati describes the svarūpa of the jīva in Chāndogya Upaniṣad: One should seek out and realize the jīva who is free of sin, free of old age, free of death, free of lamentation, free of hunger, free of thirst, who is fully satisfied and whose will becomes fact. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [8.7.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/8.7.1), [8.7.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/8.7.3)) Jñāna fulfills desires along with destruction of bondage of karma of the jīva endowed with eight qualities. If one knows the jīva as Brahman, and if he does not stray during his life, on giving up sin in the body, he enjoys all desirable objects. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.5) If a person understands that he is ātmā, “I am this,” will he make the body suffer for developing desire for material objects? (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad [4.4.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/4.4.12)) If such jñāna produced is devoid of prema for the Lord, it is useless (na śobhate). It does not produce the bliss of liberation. It is like a wife wearing ornaments when she is rejected by her husband. This is because it is devoid of attainment of the Lord (nirañjanam). Añju means beauty according to Dhātu-pāṭha. The Lord is attained by bhāva alone. Those who know the shining Lord, attainable by bhakti, who is known as having no material support, who is the creator and destroyer, who is most auspicious, and who creates the sixteen kalās, attain liberation. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [5.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/5.14)) If jñāna is like this, kāmya-karma, inauspicious during sādhana and inauspicious on attaining the result (śaśvad abhadraṁ) and even akāmya-karma (akāraṇam) certainly do not shine, when not offered to the Lord. “But knowledge of jīva gives the abode of the Lord, for it is said: Knowing that ātmā, most effulgent, beyond darkness, one surpasses saṁsāra. There is no other path for attaining liberation. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [3.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/3.8)) Does it contradict śruti? There is no contradiction since bhakti to the Lord is present as an accompaniment to one’s samādhi. Without bhāva, the attainment does not shine.

### 1.5.13

**Перевод:**

**Fortunate one! Therefore, since you have useful knowledge, spotless character, dedication to truth and determined vows, remember with attention the pastimes of the Lord which bring about liberation from all bondage.**

**Комментарий:**

“You have said everything that I have previously done is useless. Please instruct me what to do now.” You have fruitful knowledge of Brahma-sūtras (amogha-drk). You have pure fame (śuci-śravāḥ). You are dedicated to truth. You have firm vows (dhṛta-vrataḥ). Therefore, remember with concentrated mind (samādhinā), the pastimes of the Lord for the liberation of all beings from bondage. Meditation on the Lord’s form in various ages is proper because the Lord is all-pervading. The Lord, his associates and parts of action at a later time exist in the previous action because there is no difference between the Lord, his associates and the parts at various times. (Brahma-sūtras [3.3.10-11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.3.10)) These sūtras indicate the glory of childhood, etc., of Kṛṣṇa, who possesses unlimited powers. He has eternal, attractive pastimes which destroy bondage of living beings. You should meditate on all this and describe it. Anusmara is in the second person instead of saying “one should remember (anusmaratu)” (with bhavān) because of the distinctiveness of the statement.

### 1.5.14

**Перевод:**

**The intelligence of people desiring to speak on other subjects, who develop another vision, become disturbed by the words describing these other objects, and can never attain its proper object. It is like a boat attacked by the wind.**

**Комментарий:**

The harm of doing the opposite is described. The intelligence of a poet who merely desires to speak a little on subjects other than Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes and does not even speak, whose vision becomes separate because of his actions (pṛthag dṛśaḥ) becomes unsteady (duḥsthitā) by those forms and names produced by the desire to describe them and by the words and subject of the words, and does not attain a place at any time in any country. His intelligence sinks into ignorance just as a boat turned around by the wind sinks.

### 1.5.15

**Перевод:**

**You have created a great disturbance by teaching a condemned subject to people attached to material enjoyment in order to make them accept dharma. Thinking that what you have taught is real dharma, they do not consider giving it up.**

**Комментарий:**

“With the intention of having people renounce material desires, teaching kāmya-karmas and thus acknowledging the mind, I have presented many teaching of giving up those desires in conversations between father and son etc.” Vidura says: O sage! Your friend Vedavyāsa desiring to describe the qualities of the Lord, wrote Mahābhārata. In that work he attracted men’s attention to topics of the Lord through topics about material happiness. (SB [3.5.12](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/5.12)) By instructing kāmya-karma, condemned (jugupsitam) because it is mixed with violence, for instituting niṣkāma-karma (dharma-kṛte) to people attached to sakāma-karma by their natures, you have done something very improper. From your (yasya) words, convinced (sthitaḥ) that ‘this is dharma,’ those with desires (itaraḥ) do not accept the various statement of the wise or other śruti statements which negate those karmas such as:. Not by karmas, sons or wealth, only by renunciation, can one attain immortality. (Taittarīya-āraṇyaka 10.12.3, Kaivalya Upaniṣad 3) They consider that such renunciation is for the lame, blind and deaf. It is like throwing them in the middle of the river when they are already submerged at the edge of the river. What you have done is improper.

### 1.5.16

**Перевод:**

**The wise person, giving up material happiness, should realize the happiness of the Lord who is beyond measure and then take to bhakti, setting an example for others. Describe the pastimes of the Lord for those materialists who are pushed by the guṇas of matter.**

**Комментарий:**

“They do not consider the negative statements. However, since they lack qualification for hearing about the Lord, showing the Lord’s pastimes is useless.” A person skillful at understanding the real results of renunciation and enjoyment should know the happiness of Kṛṣṇa, lord of all controllers (vibhoḥ), who has no time or spatial limit (ananta- pārasya). He who knows the happiness of the Lord which is eternal and unlimited, while rejecting material happiness since it is temporary and limited, will hear the pastimes of the Lord, filled with his presence, and which give realization of him, rather than hear topics of a dull world. Whatever the leader does, others follow. (BG [3.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/3.21)) With the hope that others will become similar as illustrated in such verse, showing that path is not useless. Show the pastimes of the Lord for liberating the person devoid of discrimination (anātmanaḥ), who acts in the guṇas, from bondage. The sampradāya should spread. Knowing the nectar of the Lord’s pastimes, the person will not be greedy for the water in a trough, the meager pleasure of material happiness. Use of the second person (darśaya) with bhavān is poetic license.

### 1.5.17

**Перевод:**

**If a person having given up his nitya-karmas, begins the worship of the Lord’s lotus feet, and happens to deviate or not reach perfection, there is no misfortune for him at all in the future. But what does the person who follows all duties of varṇāśrama but does not worship the Lord gain?**

**Комментарий:**

“Giving up nitya-karmas because of absorption in the rasa of the Lord’s pastimes will become a sin. How can such a thing be taught by me, an ācārya of the Veda?” For a person, giving up one’s nitya-karmas (sva-dharmam) and worshipping the lotus feet of the Lord, though he does not obtain the result of bhakti (apakvaḥ) and dies (patet), or if he deviates from worshipping the Lord because of association with sense objects (patet), what inauspiciousness is there, in the form of falling from the Lord’s feet (abhadram)? No inauspicious is there because the seed of bhakti is indestructible. The Lord says in Gītā: O son of Pṛthā, this person does not fail to attain material or spiritual goals. One who has done the auspicious work of yoga is not deprived of the benefits, O my son. (BG [6.40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.40)) After attaining the worlds of the pious and living there for long years, the fallen yogī is born in the house of the religious and wealthy. (BG [6.41](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.41)) There is no need to worry, because like a renounced sannyāsī, he is not qualified for karma, since he has surrendered to the Lord’s lotus feet and relishes his pastimes. There is no insufficiency at all because the karmas are performed by representatives. The sages dedicated to the Lord perform all the karmas of those who give up all karmas and remember my names. (Ādi Purāṇa) One should not say that there is no need to worry about misfortune if one just performs sva-dharma. What value is there from svadharma for those who do not worship the lotus feet of the Lord (abhajatām)? There is no value since what is attained is destructible. Thus disregarding performance of karmas such as nitya-karmas, you should describe pure bhakti.

### 1.5.18

**Перевод:**

**The wise man strives for that happiness which is not attained by jīvas wandering from Brahma-loka to the lowest species. Material happiness is attained in all cases without endeavor, by the law of karma, just like distress, through the force of fast-moving time.**

**Комментарий:**

“Scriptures tell us about happiness of eating by worldly actions (laukika-karmas) like farming, and Svarga by vaidika-karmas such as jyotiṣṭoma. Giving up these actions which produce happiness, how can a person practice bhakti to the Lord?” The knower of real tattva (kovidaḥ) should strive for that object which is not attained by jīvas wandering from Brahma-loka (upari) to birth as grass (adhaḥ). Genitive (bhramatām) is used to indicate a general relationship. The feet of the Lord are full of pure happiness. Brahman is knowledge and bliss. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad [3.9.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/3.9.28)) Happiness from going to Svarga and eating, etc., like suffering, is attained by time. Because of previous karmas (anyataḥ) one attains births as animals, etc. Or happiness or otherwise (anyataḥ) suffering is attained. Just as one does not pray for misery, and it nevertheless comes to all creatures, in the same way happiness predominates over suffering. (Pañcatantra)

### 1.5.19

**Перевод:**

**Oh! The person who serves Mukunda will never under any condition return to the material world, unlike practitioners of other processes. Remembering the embrace of the Lord’s lotus feet, eager for that taste he has experienced, he will not desire to give up those feet again.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse further explains how there is no loss for the devotee in giving up karmas. A person serving the Lord never enters saṁsāra even by evil attractions, unlike persons fixed in sva-dharma (anyavat). An example of the opposite is given. Why? Remembering the embrace of Mukunda’s feet, the form of bliss, he does not desire to give them up since he has experienced a taste for those feet (rasa-grahaḥ). The Lords says in Gītā: He then strives again for complete perfection, O son of the Kurus. Definitely, he is attracted to that previous practice even without effort. (BG [6.43-44](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/6.43)) Others however with appearance of old karmas, enter low births and give up sva-dharma.

### 1.5.20

**Перевод:**

**This universe is the Lord but the Lord is also different from the universe for from him arises the maintenance, destruction and creation of the universe. You know this naturally since you are an avatāra of the Lord. Thus I have shown to you a small portion of bhakti and knowledge concerning the Lord’s power.**

**Комментарий:**

Nārada instructed Vyāsa to describe the pastimes of the Lord. Describing those pastimes is not possible without an understanding of the real nature of the Lord’s svarūpa and qualities. Thus he teaches this to him now. Just as the Lord is real, the world is real. Both are real states. It is said in Mahābhārata: Brahman is real. Austerity is real. Prajāpati is real. From the real, living beings are born. This produced world is real. (Mahābhārata, Āśvamedhika-parva) Previously it was said trisargo yatrāmṛṣā: this creation is not false. (SB [1.1.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/1.1)) Is Advaita philosophy approved? No. The Lord is also different from the world (itaraḥ). Since the world is a temporary combination of matter and spirit (jīva), the Lord, whose form is knowledge and bliss is different, and not touched by the world. This is an explanation of the Lord’s svarūpa. The real nature of the Lord’s qualities is then described. The places of this world arise from the three śaktis of the Lord. Thus his qualities such as maintaining the universe are spiritual (real). Since he is omniscient and produces the world, the statements indicating his creation of the world are true. His protection of the world is not imaginary, like thinking a rope is a snake. Statements about an unreal world are contradicted by the following: That person in knowledge attains the Lord who is pure consciousness and bliss, who is without material body and without veins, and who is pure, devoid of sin. The Lord, who is omniscient, wise, the controller of all beings and independent, has established all real objects eternally. (Īśopaniṣad [8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/iso/8)) Statements like brahma satyam (only Brahman is real) are thus contradicted. You know this without being instructed. I have shown you just a little (prādeśa-mātram) for protecting the meaning of The person having a teacher knows. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [6.14.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/6.14.2))

### 1.5.21

**Перевод:**

**O Vyāsa having useful knowledge! Know yourself by yourself, unborn, manifesting as a portion of the supreme person, Kṛṣṇa, for the benefit of the world. Therefore, please describe the great glories of the all-powerful Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

This is made clear in this verse. Seek out yourself by yourself (ātmanā). O Vyāsa having useful knowledge! The imperative tense (avehi) means present tense in the context: You seek yourself by yourself. Otherwise amogha-dṛk would be unsuitable in meaning. What is the nature of yourself? You are the unborn, manifested (prajātam) for the benefit of the world, since you are an unborn portion (kalām) or svāṁśa of the supreme person, Kṛṣṇa (parasya puṁṣaḥ), the supreme ātmā (paramātmanaḥ). The great glories of the aṁśi Kṛṣṇa (mahānubhāvasya) should be elaborately (adhi) described (gaṇyatām). Just as in that avatāra, the chief goal was to give pure bhakti, so you also should do the same.

### 1.5.22

**Перевод:**

**The unfailing cause of perfection from men’s austerity, study of the Vedas, performance of sacrifices, chanting of mantras, practicing jñāna and giving charity is defined by the wise as glorifying the qualities of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

It was said that, having given up sva-dharma and worshipping the Lord, one becomes successful. Some desire austerities, etc. These should also happen by worshipping the Lord. Śrutasya and other verbs in the list are taken as nouns (hearing, etc.). The unfailing cause (avicyutaḥ arthaḥ) of austerity, etc., of man is defined by the wise as describing the qualities of the Lord or bhakti. According to Viśva-prakāśa, arthaḥ means cause. The Lord will also say: Everything that can be achieved by karma, penance, jñānavairāgya, mystic yoga, charity and all other auspicious means of perfecting life is easily achieved by my devotee through bhakti. If somehow or other my devotee desires Svarga, liberation, or residence in my abode, he easily achieves such benedictions. (SB [11.20.32-33](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/20.32)) They all follow bhakti. Or arthaḥ means goal. When there is fruit, who will chew a tree?

### 1.5.23

**Перевод:**

**In another kalpa of Brahmā in a previous life, I was born as the son of a maidservant engaged by some persons studying the Vedas. Though a child, I was also engaged in serving yogīs during the monsoon season when they decided to stay in one place.**

**Комментарий:**

Pure bhakti from the influence of guru, which results in Kṛṣṇa, and was described, manifests by the mercy of devotee. To illustrate this, Nārada relates his own history. O sage Vyāsa (mune)! In the previous kalpa of Brahmā (purā) in past birth (atīta-bhave), I was born from a servant woman who attended persons who discussed the Vedas. I was engaged by them in serving persons who meditated on Kṛṣṇa (yogīnām), though I was a child, very fickle. They wanted to stay there during the four months of the rainy season (prāvṛṣi).

### 1.5.24

**Перевод:**

**Though the sages saw everything equally, they showed mercy to me, by which I, though a boy, developed sense control, gave up child’s play and all agitation, became obedient, served attentively, and spoke little.**

**Комментарий:**

Though the yogīs (te) saw everything equally, they showed mercy in the form of giving their remnants, etc., to me, a child, endowed with fickleness, who was accepted by causeless mercy. Thus though they saw everything equally, because they had bhakti for Kṛṣṇa by their natures, their causeless mercy had arisen first. Later special mercy appeared for me as I developed good qualities. Thus causeless mercy of devotees is the cause of bhakti. By this, mercy of devotees, service to devotees, faith, and taking shelter of guru are shown.

### 1.5.25

**Перевод:**

**One time only I happened to eat their food remnants with their permission. All obstacles to bhakti disappeared by that. Having developed an inclination for bhakti, being pure in mind, taste for devotional processes inevitably developed in my mind.**

**Комментарий:**

The special mercy is shown. One time, I ate the rice stuck to their plates with their permission. They said, “Eat this rice.” By eating that food, I became free of all the anarthas which obstructed bhakti (tad-apāsta-kilbiṣaḥ). Doing this daily, I developed a taste in my mind for hearing and chanting Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes (tad dharma) of these yogīs. Desire for bhakti, anartha-nivṛtti and ruci for bhakti are shown.

### 1.5.26

**Перевод:**

**By the mercy of the sages who were chanting about the attractive pastimes of Kṛṣṇa daily, I heard about Kṛṣṇa in that place with āsakti. Hearing constantly, I then developed rati for Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse speaks of āsakti and rati. I heard the topics of Kṛṣṇa (tāḥ) with bhakti (śraddhayā) and at every moment rati for Kṛṣṇa, famous as the producer of prema (priya-śravasi), developed. Me is connected with śraddhayā. Mama is connected with ratiḥ. Thus there is no fault of repetition of the word me and mama.

### 1.5.27

**Перевод:**

**O great-minded one! Having attained an excellent taste for the Lord, my intelligence did not waver from the Lord. By my intelligence, I directly experienced that my gross and subtle bodies created by the Lord’s material energy were fixed only in the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Having developed special taste, my intelligence became extremely (askhalitā) in Kṛṣṇa (priya-śravasi). By that intelligence I saw the gross and subtle body (sad-asat), present in me by māyā, who was engaged in her duty, to be engaged in Kṛṣṇa (brahmaṇi kalpitam). I saw the gross body with offering respects and the subtle body with ear, eye, mind, intelligence engaged in hearing, seeing and meditation. They were not engaged in material actions or enjoyable objects. The gross and subtle bodies which do not engage even with great effort were easily engaged. This is the greatness of his intelligence (matiḥ).

### 1.5.28

**Перевод:**

**In this way, for the two seasons of monsoon and autumn, I constantly heard the pure glories of the Lord chanted by the generous sages. I then developed prema, which destroys rajas and tamas in the jīvas.**

**Комментарий:**

He describes prema. For four months (two seasons), I constantly heard. I developed prema (bhaktiḥ). That prema destroys the rajas and tamas of the living beings (ātmā) who hear about that bhakti. By this path, he sees the Lord in the next chapter.

### 1.5.29-30

**Перевод:**

**Though I was a boy, I had developed prema for the Lord. I was humble, sinless, filled with faith, sense-controlled, and was willing to serve. The sages, most affectionate to the fallen, as they were about to depart, taught me the most confidential knowledge of pure bhakti, which was taught by the Lord himself.**

**Комментарий:**

“This child who has developed rati should not be cheated by scriptures like Sāṅkhya.” Thinking thus, wanting to leave, they taught him their siddhānta. This is explained in two verses. I was attached to them (anuraktasya), polite (praśritasya), and willing to serve (anucarasya). They taught scriptures which produce knowledge (jñānam) that is confidential: showing the svarūpa of the jīva, different from the body; more confidential knowledge of the Lord different from material world; and most confidential knowledge of the Lord controlled only by devotion. This knowledge was spoken by the Lord Kṛṣṇa directly to Brahmā, Uddhava and Arjuna.

### 1.5.31

**Перевод:**

**By this knowledge, I understood the influence of the mercy of creator Vāsudeva. By this understanding the devotees attain the Lord’s abode.**

**Комментарий:**

By that scripture (yena), I understood the influence of mercy (māyānubhāvam). Māyā means mercy according to Viśva-prakāśa. By this mercy the devotees go to the abode of the Lord.

### 1.5.32

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇa! They indirectly explained the destruction of the three types of miseries, in which karmas are offered to the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Introducing me to pure bhakti and scriptures indicating pure bhakti, they thought that I should not hear anything less than that. This indicates niṣkāma karma (which should not be heard). It was not directly spoken since it lacked my goal (saṁsūcitam). What is that bhakti? It is the cure for destroying the three miseries. And what is that? Karmas, prescribed according to one’s varṇa and āśrama, offered (bhāvitam) to the Lord.

### 1.5.33

**Перевод:**

**The substance by which a living being’s disease arises and which does not cure the disease, when combined with other substances in a medicine, destroys the disease.**

**Комментарий:**

“How can karmas, whose nature is to cause suffering, destroy suffering?” Sickness in living beings arises from ghee, etc. That same ghee, mixed with medicine (dravyam), does not maintain the disease, but destroys the disease. Here punāti means to maintain (rather than purify), since dhātus have many meanings.

### 1.5.34

**Перевод:**

**In the same way, all types of karma-yoga performed by men, which are normally causes of continued rebirth in this world, are able to destroy sufferings when offered to the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way, kriyā-yoga, beginning with nitya karmas till kāmya karmas (sarve), which are causes of misery (saṁsṛti-hetavaḥ) for men, when offered (kalpitā) to the Lord (pare), destroy the suffering fixed in the self (ātmā-vināśāya). By being infused with (bhāvita) the quality being offered to the Lord, like medicine infused in ghee, those karmas destroy the suffering fixed in the person. They produce satisfaction, like being in strong health. Some say that it is destroys the subtle body (ātmanaḥ).

### 1.5.35

**Перевод:**

**That jñāna which arises from karma, which is pleasing to the Lord because of being offered to him, is endowed with bhakti.**

**Комментарий:**

Manifesting jñāna and bhakti, those dharmas destroy suffering. Karmas performed for the satisfaction of the Lord produces knowledge concerning the Lord (tad adhīnam), like fibers in a lotus stem, since it aims for the Lord. Performed in that way, with bhakti-yoga, it produces knowledge of Lord and jñāna-yoga, since that knowledge is a cause of jñāna-yoga.

### 1.5.36

**Перевод:**

**When those engaged in karma continuously perform those activities according to the instruction of the Lord, they also chant and remember the qualities and names of Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

When people situated in offering karmas by chance associate with pariniṣṭhita devotees, then they, who are performing karmas according to instructions of Lord, chant and continually remember Kṛṣṇa’s names and qualities. The instructions are as follows: Whatever you do, whatever you eat, whatever you sacrifice, whatever you give, and whatever austerity you perform, offer it to me, O son of Kuntī. (BG [9.27](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.27)) Saniṣṭha devotees offer only Vedic karmas. Along with that the pariniṣṭhita devotees offer ordinary actions and bodily actions.

### 1.5.37

**Перевод:**

**I offer respects to you, Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa. Let us respect Vāsudeva, Pradyumna, Aniruddha and Saṅkarṣaṇa (Balarāma) in our minds.**

**Комментарий:**

Nārada now desires to teach a mantra attained from his guru. This is explained in two verses. Kṛṣṇa (bhagavate) contains the caturvyūha. The thirty-three syllable mantra describes in order the creator, maintainer and destroyer of the universe (Pradyumna, Aniruddha and Saṅkarṣaṇa).

### 1.5.38

**Перевод:**

**Using this mantra indicating the five forms, one worships Kṛṣṇa who is the subject of the dhyāna-mantra, the Lord worthy of worship. That person has attained real knowledge.**

**Комментарий:**

On seeing the person who worships Kṛṣṇa, the yajña puruṣa, worthy of being worshipped by the five names of those forms, others become successful (samyag darśanaḥ). Some say darśanaḥ means real knowledge. What is that form? He worships the form explained in the mantra (mantra-mūrtikam) or he worships the mantra as the form. He is not hard, but soft with mercy (amūrtikam) or is devoid of material body. Mūrti means hard and body according to Amarakośa.

### 1.5.39

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇa! The Lord, knowing that I had undertaken the highest instructions given by him, gave me realization of himself, then powerful siddhis, and finally prema for him.**

**Комментарий:**

I attained Kṛṣṇa’s great mercy as I chanted the mantra. Knowing that I chanted my mantra (sva-nigamam), the Lord was very pleased. He gave knowledge of himself and powers like aṇima, etc. Since I was not pleased, he gave me prema (bhāvaṁ). It is understood that, requested, Nārada taught his mantra to Vyāsa.

### 1.5.40

**Перевод:**

**O omniscient Vyāsa! Explain the glories of Kṛṣṇa, by which the curiosity of persons already having so much knowledge will be satisfied. The wise do not consider that there is any other way to destroy the afflictions of the jīvas tormented by suffering.**

**Комментарий:**

Teaching him the mantra, he then spoke. O one who has heard all scriptures (adabhra-śruta)! Explain the glories of the Lord by which the desire to understand of the learned is satisfied since they attain all desires by that. The knowers of truth do not desire another method of destroying suffering for persons afflicted with sorrow. A forest fire cannot be destroyed except by a shower.

## Chapter Six - Nārada Achieves Perfection

### 1.6.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: O brāhmaṇa Śaunaka! The powerful Vyāsa, son of Satyavatī, after hearing about Nārada’s birth and activities, again asked him questions.**

**Комментарий:**

In the sixth chapter, Nārada explains how he went to the forest to see Kṛṣṇa. Hearing his words, Nārada then attains a spiritual form. Niśamya means hearing.

### 1.6.2

**Перевод:**

**Vyāsa said: When the mendicants who had taught you that knowledge departed, being of young age, what did you do?**

**Комментарий:**

Vipravasite means “departing from there and going elsewhere.” Lack of samprasāraṇa(viproṣyate) is poetic license. What did you do? Since I am your disciple, I will do similarly.

### 1.6.3

**Перевод:**

**Son of Brahmā! How did you spend the rest of your life? In what manner did you give up that body as the son of the maidservant at the time of death?**

**Комментарий:**

How did you pass your later years (param vayaḥ)? Idam kalevaram (this body) refers to the body born from the maidservant. How did you give up that body?

### 1.6.4

**Перевод:**

**Time destroys everything. Why did time not destroy your memories from some previous day of Brahmā?**

**Комментарий:**

Esaḥ means the events of the previous day of Brahmā. Na vyavadhāt means “did not separate” but the implication is “time did not separate you from the memory and did not destroy the memory.” Vyavadhāt without the augment “a” (vyavādhāt) is poetic license.

### 1.6.5

**Перевод:**

**Nārada said: When the mendicant teachers who had given me knowledge left, even though I was of young age, I did as they instructed.**

**Комментарий:**

The verb form akāraṣam instead of akārṣam is for meter. It is said mūrdha-rephā vikalpyante chando-bhaṅga-bhayād iha: for fear of disrupting the meter, cerebral consonants and “r” are separated.

### 1.6.6

**Перевод:**

**My mother, a woman, uneducated, a menial servant, had only me as a son. She therefore had great affection for me, her only shelter.**

**Комментарий:**

She had only one son - myself (ekātmajā). She was a woman, and foolish. This was the cause of her affectionate attachment.

### 1.6.7

**Перевод:**

**Because she was dependent, though she wanted to protect me she could not do so. Just as a puppet master controls a wooden puppet, the Lord controls all people.**

**Комментарий:**

It was said she was a maid servant. This is further explained. Because she was dependent on others, she could not (na kalpā) pamper me nor maintain my life (yoga-kṣemam). The example of the wooden puppet shows her dependence.

### 1.6.8

**Перевод:**

**Only five years old, inexperienced with time, place and direction, I lived in a brāhmaṇa’s house, with the belief that she would never leave me.**

**Комментарий:**

Tad-avekṣayā means “with the expectation that she would not give me up.” I was ignorant of place and time since I was a child.

### 1.6.9

**Перевод:**

**One time, a snake, impelled by time, touched by her foot, bit my poor mother who had gone from the house to milk the cow at night and was walking along the path.**

**Комментарий:**
