Duhantīm means “to milk.” It indicates a cause (of the snake biting) by the rule Pāṇini 3.2.126.

### 1.6.10

**Перевод:**

**Considering that her death was the mercy of the Lord who is concerned for the welfare of his devotees, I departed immediately for the north.**

**Комментарий:**

Considering the death of my mother (tat) as the mercy of the Lord, to worship the Lord with no obstacles (śam), I departed (prātiṣṭham) to the north. Use of parasmaipada verb is poetic license.

### 1.6.11

**Перевод:**

**I passed through large populated areas, capitals, brāhmaṇa villages, cowherd villages, mines, farms, villages on mountain sides, gardens of flowers and betel, wild groves and plantations.**

**Комментарий:**

Four verses (11-13) are connected together with the following general structure: Passing through prosperous (sphītān) areas, etc., in the north, leaving behind the inhabited areas, I continued walking, and saw a large forest (’ham adrākṣaṁ vipinaṁ mahat verse 13). Janapada means area according to Amara-kośa. Pura means capital cities. Regarding grāma, Bhṛgu says: That place where brāhmaṇas and their servants live is called grāma. It also refers to a living place of śūdras. Vraja means cow sheds according to Halāyudha. Ākarān means mines, from which jewels are dug according to Amara-kośa. Kheṭa means a farming village. Regarding kharvaṭa, Bhṛgu says: When villages and towns situated on rivers or mountains become mixed together as one it is called kharvaṭa. Vāṭīḥ means gardens of flowers and betel. Vanāni refers to groups of trees and creepers growing together by themselves. Upavanāni refers to groups of trees which were planted.

### 1.6.12-13

**Перевод:**

**Passing by mountains colored with gold and silver, trees with branches broken by elephants, pools with fresh water, and lakes used by the devatās, beautified by bees wandering about, awakened by the sounds of birds; all alone, I saw a huge, repulsive, fearsome forest dense with reeds, cane, clumps of śara grass, kuśa grass and hollow bamboo which was the playground for snakes, owls and jackals.**

**Комментарий:**

There were mountains resplendent with silver and gold, and trees whose branches were broken by elephants. There were ponds with clear, tasty (śiva) water and lakes (naliṇīḥ). What type of lakes were they? The lakes were beautified with bees wandering here and there, roused by the sounds of birds (patrarathaiḥ). Patra-ratha means bird according to Amara-kośa. Stamba means clumps of grass. I saw a forest difficult to traverse, dense with grass, kuśa and bamboo. Amara-kośa explains veṇavaḥ kīcakās te sūrye svananty aniloddhatā: kīcaka is hollow bamboo which makes noise when blown by the wind. It was repulsive because of its fearsome form (pratibhyākāram). It was the playground (ajīram) of snakes, owls and jackals. I did not have surprise or fear in seeing those surprising and fearsome things because my mind was absorbed in the Lord at that time.

### 1.6.14

**Перевод:**

**Senses and body exhausted, thirsty and hungry, after bathing in a pool of a river, I performed ācamana and took rest.**

**Комментарий:**

Ātmā means body. I was completely overcome with thirst (tṛṭparītaḥ). I performed ācamana (upaspṛṣṭaḥ).

### 1.6.15

**Перевод:**

**In that desolate forest, sitting at the base of a pippala tree, I concentrated by using my intelligence on Keśava situated within my mind, as I had been taught.**

**Комментарий:**

Sitting (āśritaḥ) at the base of an aśvattha tree (pippalopasthe), by intelligence (ātmanā) I meditated on Keśava (ātmānam) situated in my mind filled with prema (ātmā-stham). Ātmā means mind according to Medinī.

### 1.6.16

**Перевод:**

**As I meditated on the lotus feet of the Lord with a mind conquered by prema, with tears in my eyes from longing, step by step the Lord made his appearance in my mind.**

**Комментарий:**

As I meditated with the mind (cetasā) conquered by prema (bhāva- nirjita), with eyes flowing with tears out of longing, the Lord appeared in my heart, gradually (śanaiḥ), revealing his form, then his ornaments, voice, and splendor.

### 1.6.17

**Перевод:**

**My limbs covered in distinct goose bumps out of excessive prema, filled with delight, I fainted out of bliss, and was not aware of myself or anything else.**

**Комментарий:**

My hairs on my limbs stood on end, mixed with intense prema. My body became like a kadamba flower. I was most blissful (atinirvṛtaḥ). Flooded with bliss, I fainted in happiness (līnaḥ). I was not aware of myself and anything else (ubhayam).

### 1.6.18

**Перевод:**

**Suddenly not seeing the attractive form of the Lord which destroys all lamentation, I became agitated from the sorrow of separation. I became despondent like someone who has lost a treasure.**

**Комментарий:**

For increasing his longing, the Lord then disappeared. Suddenly not seeing the form of Lord which destroys lamentation (śucāpaham), I became disturbed. Like a man who has lost a treasure, I became despondent (durmanā). Uttasthau is first person perfect tense.

### 1.6.19

**Перевод:**

**Desiring to see that form again, I fixed my mind in the heart. Though I looked intently, I did not see that form. Dissatisfied, I became like a diseased person.**

**Комментарий:**

Praṇidhāya means “having fixed.”

### 1.6.20

**Перевод:**

**As I endeavored to see him in that lonely place, the Lord, inexpressible by words, then spoke to me with affectionate words, which removed my grief somewhat.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord is beyond full description of words (girām agocaraḥ) since his qualities are unlimited. The Lord spoke words which removed the sorrow caused by not seeing his form. The word iva (somewhat) is used because his love in longing, caused by separation, had not been fully satisfied.

### 1.6.21

**Перевод:**

**Oh! In this body you will not be able to see me again. But lax practitioners who still have some contamination cannot see me at all.**

**Комментарий:**

What did he say? Oh! (hanta) This is an address made out of affection. In this birth, having the body of a practitioner (asmin janmani), you cannot see me, since I am invisible (durdarśaḥ) to those faulty practitioners of yoga (kuyoginām) whose contaminations such as lust have not been burned up. I am not visible to yogīs who meditate only on their ātmā. The intention here is to say, “But I showed myself to you. Therefore, you are not a faulty practitioner.”

### 1.6.22

**Перевод:**

**O sinless Nārada! I showed myself once to produce a desire in you to see me. The devotee so desiring me gradually becomes freed of all material desires.**

**Комментарий:**

“But mercifully just show yourself once more to me!” Seeing me only once is to increase your thirst for me (kāmāya). By lack of increase in longing, prema will not develop to the state of youthfulness. Without that, you cannot realize my form completely in a siddha-deha. The devotee who is thirsty for me frees himself from desires (hṛd chayān). This is a statement for ordinary people, not Nārada.

### 1.6.23

**Перевод:**

**By serving the devotees for even a short time, your intelligence became firmly fixed in me. When you give up this inferior planet, you will become my associate.**

**Комментарий:**

I can be attained by association with devotees. By serving the devotees for even a short time (adīrghayā), you developed strong intelligence in me. Giving up this low place (avadyam lokam), since it is temporary, you will become my associate (maj-janatām).

### 1.6.24

**Перевод:**

**Your intelligence being absorbed in me will never be destroyed. Even at the time of creation and destruction of the living entities, by my mercy, your memory of the previous kalpa will not be destroyed.**

**Комментарий:**

Your intelligence, concentrating on my qualities as the cause of everything, as untouched by māyā, endowed with concentrated knowledge, understanding me as the master and friend, bound to me by prema, will never be destroyed. Because I am eternal, your intelligence should be eternally fixed on me. After the destruction, your memory of the previous birth with all previous subjects will not be destroyed. The events of the previous kalpa will be remembered.

### 1.6.25

**Перевод:**

**Having spoken this, the Lord, whose words are the highest proof, whose words appeared in the sky, who was not visible to the eyes, and who was capable of bestowing mercy to the most fallen boy, stopped speaking. Receiving his mercy, I lowered my head to the greatest of the great.**

**Комментарий:**

Bhūtam means real since it is said tasya vā etasya mahato bhūtasya (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.4.10). Or it means the Lord speaks truthful words. The Lord became silent (upararāma). Since the Lord left a trace in the ether in the form of his speaking to Nārada, he is called (nabho-liṅgam). He is not known by senses devoid of bhakti (aliṅgam). Since he offered his feet even to me, the low born son of a maidservant, he is called the one Supreme Lord (īśvaram— one who is capable). I offered respects (avanāmam).

### 1.6.26

**Перевод:**

**Giving up shyness, I began to chant the names of the unlimited Lord, and to remember his most excellent, hidden pastimes. I wandered the earth with satisfied mind, without material desires, without pride or selfishness, waiting for that time.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛtāni refers to the Lord’s pastimes. Gām means the earth. Kālam pratīkṣan (expecting the time) means “When will that time come when I will become an associate of the Lord?” I was expecting that. I had no pride or envy since I always thought, “Who else would be as fallen as I?”

### 1.6.27

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇa Vyāsa! Concentrating only on Kṛṣṇa, not attached to material enjoyment and pure in mind, the time of receiving my spiritual body occurred simultaneously with that of giving up my material body, like lightning flashing simultaneously with lightning.**

**Комментарий:**

At the time of dissolving my body (kāle), the time (kālaḥ) which would make me an associate of the Lord made its appearance. I had waited a long time for this. Like the statement “Time exists at all times” by the power of viśeṣa, time is simultaneously both the subject and object (the time came, I waited for time.) An example is given. It was just like lightning manifesting with another bolt of lightning. Tāḍayati means to strike. Sudāmā means a crystal mountain. Lightning (saudāmanī) manifests on the mountains edge according to Pāṇini 4.3.112. The previous time destroyed his life. The next moment of time made him an associate of the Lord.

### 1.6.28

**Перевод:**

**Having been awarded a transcendental body befitting an associate of the Lord, the body made of five material elements, with karmas relating to the present body, fell away.**

**Комментарий:**

Becoming the Lord’s associate is described. The promise, “You will become by associate (tām),” given by the Lord was without deceit (śuddhām). Due to this promise, when the body produced by the parā śakti acting according to the desire of the Lord (bhāgavatīm) was being arranged by the Lord (prayujyamāne) on me, my material body fell away. The ārabdha karmas of that body, which remained by the will of the Lord, had been destroyed, as if by fire, like burned cloth. The destruction of the material body and attainment of the spiritual body was simultaneously, like “sitting down and eating.” Because of being related to the Lord, that body of an associate, pūrva-siddha (previously perfected), belonging to the glory of the spiritual sky, composed of knowledge and bliss, shines. That pūrvasiddha body joined with awareness of other objects as its quality (dharma-bhūta-jñāna) as one among many other forms. By the mercy of the Lord the liberated jīva attains the body of an associate. Kaṭha Upaniṣad says: He is attained by a person whom the Lord chooses. The Lord then reveals himself to that devotee. (Kaṭha Upaniṣad [2.24.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/katha-bs/2.24.2)) The body is pūrva-siddha (previously perfected) as mentioned in Chāndogya Upaniṣad: Shedding sins like a horse shaking his mane, and shedding the material body, like the moon freeing himself from Rāhu’s mouth, I, being successful, attain the eternal Lord. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [8.13.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/8.13.1)) In that place all men reside with forms like the Lord and they worship the Lord with unmotivated service. (SB [3.15.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/15.14))

### 1.6.29

**Перевод:**

**At the end of the kalpa when Brahmā withdrew the universe with his breathing, I entered into Brahmā who desired to sleep in Nārāyaṇa, who was lying in the water of the only ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

“If you attained an eternal body as an associate, then in this day of Brahmā during Svayambhuva manvantara, how were you born from Brahmā? It is said utsaṅgān nārado jajñe: Nārada was born from the deliberation of Brahmā, which is the best part of the body. (SB [3.12.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/12.23))” That is true. Just as the Lord for his particular pastimes enters the womb of Devakī, for the pastimes of being the son of Brahmā, at the end of the previous kalpa (Brahmā’s previous day), I entered the body of Brahmā. With this intention he speaks this verse: “When Brahmā withdrew the three worlds (idam ādāya), I entered along with his breathing into Brahmā who desired to go to sleep in Nārāyaṇa (śayāṇe), who was sleeping in the water of the only ocean (udantavaḥ).” Having appeared, Brahmā, entering the body of Viṣṇu, becoming one with Viṣṇu, then went into Viṣṇu’s spiritual sleep. (Kūrma Purāṇa)

### 1.6.30

**Перевод:**

**At the end of thousand yuga cycles, Brahmā awoke and Marīci, other sages and I appeared from the senses of Brahmā, who desired to create the universe again.**

**Комментарий:**

At the end of thousand yuga cycles means “at the end of the previous kalpa and the beginning of this kalpa.” Marīci and other sages (marīci-miśrā) and I were born from the prāṇas of Brahmā, who arose from Viṣṇu. Jajñire instead of jajñimahe is poetic license.

### 1.6.31

**Перевод:**

**With continuous worship of the Lord, by the grace of Mahā-viṣṇu, I travel outside and inside the universe with no obstacles at all.**

**Комментарий:**

Being a brahmacārī with unbroken vow(askanda-vrataḥ), I wander throughout the universe and in the spiritual world. These words describe his liberated state. Śruti also describes this state. sa svarāḍ bhavati. tasya sarveṣu lokeṣu kāmacāro bhavati. He will be self-ruling. He will wander in whatever worlds he desires. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad [1.25.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/chan-upa/1.25.2)) Marīci and others, not liberated, travel within the material world.

### 1.6.32

**Перевод:**

**Playing mūrchanas and ālāpas on the vīṇā given by the Lord, using the seven sacred notes of the scale, I wander about singing the glories of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Why do you wander everywhere? I do so for the auspiciousness of the worlds, by the order of the Lord. This is explained in four verses (32-35). The vīṇā was given by Kṛṣṇa (deva-dattam). This is stated in Liṅga Purāṇa. The vīṇā is decorated with the notes such as “ni” which indicate Brahman. The seven notes are thus svataḥsiddha. I play mūrchanas and ālāpas on the vīṇā.

### 1.6.33

**Перевод:**

**When I sing his glories, the Lord who makes any place that he touches holy, and who is attracted to those who sing his glories, quickly appears in my heart, as if being called.**

**Комментарий:**

He comes as if called. Even without calling, the Lord appears. He appears in the heart. This stands for other ways in which he could appear also. I do not reside in Vaikuṇṭha or in the heart of yogīs. O Nārada! I am situated wherever my devotees sing. (Mahābhārata)

### 1.6.34

**Перевод:**

**For persons whose minds are constantly afflicted with desires for enjoyment of sense objects, I have directly experienced that singing the glories of the Lord is the boat for crossing material existence.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse summarizes the topic being discussed. For persons whose minds are afflicted by the desire for experiencing (sparśāḥ) the sense objects (mātrā) like sound, touch, etc., by the ear, etc., the boat (plavaḥ) for crossing the material ocean is praising the Lord’s activities. I have directly experienced that (dṛṣṭaḥ). There is no proof necessary. Here kīrtana as the main aṅga of bhakti is indicated.

### 1.6.35

**Перевод:**

**The ātmā constantly afflicted by lust and greed will not be satisfied by aṣṭāṅga-yoga and other paths as much as by direct service to Mukunda.**

**Комментарий:**

The great strength of pure bhakti which has been approved is described. The ātmā afflicted by lust and greed is not purified by yoga mixed with bhakti as much as it is directly purified (addhā) by pure bhakti (mukunda-sevayā) which disregards yamas, etc. Starting with verse [1.5.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.8) and ending with the present verse, the process of sādhana bhakti has been taught. Pure bhakti of the nirapekṣa was taught in the following: If a person having given up his nitya-karmas, begins the worship of the Lord’s lotus feet, and happens to deviate or not reach perfection, there is no misfortune for him at all in the future. But what does the person who follows all duties of varṇāśrama but does not worship the Lord gain? (SB [1.5.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.17)) In another kalpa of Brahmā in a previous life, I was born as the son of a maidservant engaged by some persons studying the Vedas. Though a child, I was also engaged in serving them during the monsoon season when they decided to stay in one place. (SB [1.5.23](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.23)) The pariniṣṭhita’s bhakti touched with karma was taught by: When those engaged in karma continuously perform those activities according to the instruction of the Lord, they also chant and remember the qualities and names of Kṛṣṇa. (SB [1.5.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.36)) The saniṣṭha’s bhakti, mixed with yoga, consisting of offering karmas that include jñāna is described by: That jñāna which arises from karma, which is pleasing to the Lord because of being offered to him, is endowed with bhakti. (SB [1.5.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.35)) Thus Vyāsa should describe various types of bhakti.

### 1.6.36

**Перевод:**

**O sinless Vyāsa! I have explained all this, confidential knowledge about my birth and activities about which you have asked so that your mind will be satisfied.**

**Комментарий:**

Sarvam here refers to the appearance, growth, and result of bhakti; and to the devotee’s later activities, destruction of prārabdha-karma, the falling away of the body created by those prārabdha-karmas, and achieving the body of an associate. It is secret (rahasyam).

### 1.6.37

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Nārada, freely moving without material motive, having spoken to Vyāsa, the son of Satyavatī, and taking leave, departed while taking pleasure in his vīṇā.**

**Комментарий:**

Āmantrya means taking leave. Yādṛcchikaḥ means that Nārada moves about without a motive.

### 1.6.38

**Перевод:**

**Oh! Nārada is most fortunate because, singing and rejoicing in the glories of Kṛṣṇa with his vīṇā, he gives bliss to the suffering world.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse reveals astonishment. Tantryā means “with the vīṇā.”

## Chapter Seven - Punishment of Aśvatthāmā

### 1.7.1

**Перевод:**

**Śaunaka said: O Sūta! When Nārada departed, what did the powerful Vyāsa do, having heard the advice of Nārada?**

**Комментарий:**

In the seventh chapter it is described that Vyāsa sees the meaning of all scriptures while in trance. The withdrawal of the brahmāstra and the punishment of Aśvatthāmā are also told. Tad-abhipretam means having heard the advice of Nārada.

### 1.7.2

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: There is a hermitage for sages called Śamyāprāsa, favorable for many sacrifices, situated on the west bank of the Sarasvatī River where brāhmaṇas take shelter.**

**Комментарий:**

Brahma-nadyām can mean river related to the Vedas, of the brāhmaṇas, of austerity, of the Paramātmā. Amara-kośa says vedas tattvaṁ tapo brahma brahmā vipraḥ prajāpatiḥ: the word brahma can mean Veda, truth, austerity, the Brahman, Lord Brahmā, a brāhmaṇa, and a progenitor.

### 1.7.3

**Перевод:**

**In his hermitage surrounded by a grove of badari trees, Vyāsa sat down, performed ācamana, and began to reflect using his mind.**

**Комментарий:**

Nārada had instructed Vyāsa to remember the pastimes of the Lord continually in samādhi (SB [1.5.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.13)). Thus he sat and concentrated in samādhi using his mind. Nārada instructed samādhinānusmara tad-viceṣṭitam: remember the pastimes of the Lord in samādhi (SB [1.5.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/5.13)).

### 1.7.4

**Перевод:**

**In that mind which was undisturbed because it was pure from the performance of bhakti, he saw Lord Kṛṣṇa, complete with all his energies and spiritual expansions, and also saw māyā who took shelter behind the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

What did he see in samādhi? In his mind he saw the puruṣa. His mind was brought under complete control (samyak praṇihite) because it was purified (amale) by bhakti. That puruṣa was endowed with various vilāsa, vyūha and svāṁśa expansions, with his superior śakti, and with many attractive pastimes (pūrṇam). This indicates Kṛṣṇa, Svayam Bhagavān, because it will later be said kṛṣṇe paramapūruṣe (SB [1.7.7](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.7)). Instead of pūrṇam, pūrvam is also seen. He saw the puruṣa who was pūrva-siddha. pūrvam evāham ihāsam iti tat-puruṣasya puruṣatvam: It is said, “In the beginning I alone existed in this world.” Because the Lord was the only person (puruṣasya) existing previously (pūrvam), he is called the puruṣa. (Taittirīya Āraṇyaka 1.23.1) He saw māyā made of three guṇas. Māyā stood behind him since it was inferior. It is specifically stated that Vyāsa, in looking at the Lord, did not look at māyā.

### 1.7.5

**Перевод:**

**Bewildered by that māyā, the jīva, though separate from the three guṇas, considers himself made of the three guṇas and takes on material existence created by the guṇas.**

**Комментарий:**

“Seeing the Lord is necessary in order to describe the Lord’s qualities and pastimes. What is the purpose in seeing māyā?” I will reveal Bhāgavatam for the health of jīva. The disease of the jīva is māyā. Without seeing it, how can I prescribe the great medicine to destroy it? Thus Vyāsa saw both the Lord and māyā. Bewildered by māyā, the jīva, though separate from the three guṇas (paraḥ) thinks himself made of three guṇas. He attains material existence (anartham) produced by the guṇas and becomes afflicted with suffering.

### 1.7.6

**Перевод:**

**And Vyāsa saw bhakti-yoga to the Lord which effectively destroys jīva’s saṁsāra. Learned Vyāsa then wrote the Bhāgavatam for ignorant people.**

**Комментарий:**

He also saw the medicine for destroying the disease of māyā. He saw directly bhakti-yoga which destroys the sufferings created by the guṇas (anarthas). It is also said in the Atharva Veda, śraddhābhakti-dhyāna-yogād avehi: know the Lord through faith, bhakti, meditation and yoga. (Kaivalya Upaniṣad 2) People then learn about his qualities and svarūpa. Bhakti means service or worship of Kṛṣṇa. It means concentration of the mind on Kṛṣṇa without desires for enjoyment in this life or the next. It is devoid of all karmas. The form of Kṛṣṇa, filled with knowledge and bliss, resides in bhakti-yoga, which is also full of eternity, knowledge and bliss. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad [2.77](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/2.77)) Accordingly: In you who are the shelter of all spiritual qualities exist the energies of hlādinī, sandhinī and saṁvit. In you who are devoid of material qualities, the mixture of happiness and distress found in the material world does not exist. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.9.45) The Lord has three-fold svarūpa-śakti called parā. A portion favorable to the Lord, consisting of these two parts of hlādinī and saṁvit of the svarūpa-śakti, combines with the thirst for the Lord arising from jīva’s knowledge of the Lord like milk and water and, as bhakti-yoga, brings the Lord under control. Thus by knowledge of ātmā or by illusory knowledge and bliss composed of viśuddha-sattva, the Lord cannot be realized or brought under control. For the benefit of people having no knowledge of what he saw, Vyāsa manifested the twelve cantos which reveal all this (sātvata-saṁhitām). The sequence of the vision of what he had heard is this: first he saw Kṛṣṇa complete with vilāsa, vyūha and svāṁśa expansions, with his svarūpa-śakti and his pastimes. Then he saw Māyā hiding behind the Lord. He saw the jīva-śakti bewildered by māyā. And he saw bhakti-yoga which destroys the jīva’s illusion created by māyā.

### 1.7.7

**Перевод:**

**By hearing Bhāgavatam, bhakti for the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa appears. Hearing it also destroys lamentation, illusion and fear in the human being.**

**Комментарий:**

The topic (viṣaya) and the result (phala) of Bhāgavatam is explained. Hearing it, what to speak of hearing it and reciting it, prema (bhaktiḥ) for Kṛṣṇa appears. A secondary result is shown: it destroys lamentation, illusion and fear. It destroys their roots as well. Kṛṣṇa is the topic of Bhāgavatam and prema is its result. Liberation manifests as a byproduct.

### 1.7.8

**Перевод:**

**Having already made the Bhāgavatam, the sage Vyāsa revised it and taught it to his son Śukadeva who had realized Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

Having previously manifested an abbreviated version, after Nārada’s instructions, he enlarged with many sequential pastimes (anukramya). The abbreviated version was made previous to Mahābhārata and the detailed version after. The previous version is included in the later version. That agrees with this statement: Having written the eighteen Purāṇas, Vyāsa wrote the Mahābhārata which explains the meaning of the Vedas. (Skanda & Matsya Purāṇas) Since the shorter version is included in the eighteen Purāṇas and the longer version appeared after Mahābhārata, and since the two are considered one, the statement in Matsya Purāṇa on the characteristics and number of verses is justified. Because Śukadeva had destroyed other desires (nivṛtti-niratam), he was a candidate for learning Bhāgavatam. Śukadeva was inclined to contemplate ātmā (munim). Knowledge of the jīva is suitable for knowledge of Paramātmā. When the yogī realizes the Supreme Lord through realization of jīva’s position, similar to a lamp, knowing the pure, unborn, fixed Lord, who is endowed with all tattvas, he becomes free of all bondage. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [2.15](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/2.15)) The jīva is mentioned for another purpose (for knowing Paramātmā). (Brahma-sūtras [1.3.20](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/1.3.20))

### 1.7.9

**Перевод:**

**Śaunaka said: Śukadeva was fixed in the Brahman, indifferent to everything, and enjoying in the self. Why did he study this elaborate work?**

**Комментарий:**

Since he experienced the bliss of ātmā, he was devoid of desire for other things (sarvatropekṣakaḥ), why did he study this huge work of eighteen thousand verses (bṛhatīm).

### 1.7.10

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Some sages who are ātmārāmas, beyond the scriptures, false ego and rules, also practice unmotivated, pure bhakti to Kṛṣṇa, with a great sequence, since he possesses qualities attractive to even them.**

**Комментарий:**

He presents an answer. The ātmārāmas, having seen the eight qualities of their ātmā through samādhi, give up scriptures because of tasting bliss (nirgranthāḥ). As one who desires grains gives up grass, he gives up scriptures completely. (Amṛta-bindu Upaniṣad 18) However, when the ātmārāmas attain association with great devotees knowledgeable of the Lord’s infinite attractive qualities, they practice bhakti, incited by those qualities, for the Lord. That bhakti is devoid of desire for other results (ahaitukīm). To reject all objections, it is said ittham-bhūta-guṇo hariḥ. Kṛṣṇa has such sweet qualities that they forget the bliss of their ātmā. The Lord is described. Ātma-jñāna arises from scriptures, from that comes the bliss of experiencing one’s ātmā, from that they reject scriptures, then from hearing about Lord’s qualities they get bhakti and then they study the saṁhitā (Bhāgavatam) which describes the Lord’s qualities. In this way, there is a great (uru) sequence arising from him.

### 1.7.11

**Перевод:**

**Śukadeva, the son of Vyāsa, whose mind became distracted from trance by the qualities of Kṛṣṇa, and who became attracted to the Vaiṣṇavas, studied this great Bhāgavatam.**

**Комментарий:**

“How did Śukadeva, who was absorbed in samādhi in the forest, approach his father for knowledge?” Understanding some verses of Bhāgavatam sung by disciples sent by Vyāsa, his intelligence became disturbed by the Lord’s qualities and, attracted out of samādhi, became absorbed in bliss. He studied this great scripture because it revealed Kṛṣṇa’s qualities and he was serving his father. He became dear to the devotees or the devotees were dear to him for tasting of rasa in the stories. This answers the question tasya putro mahā-yogī (SB [1.4.4](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/4.4)), how Śukadeva, having such nature, became interested in this scripture.

### 1.7.12

**Перевод:**

**I will speak about the birth, activities and death of King Parīkṣit, and the death march of the Pāṇḍavas, which will give rise to discussions about Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the other question (in (SB [1.4.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/4.9)) of how Parīkṣit heard the Bhāgavatam while fasting till death and other topics will be explained. Vilāpanam means death. Saṁsthām means a walk till death. These topics give rise to talks about Kṛṣṇa, since they relate to the aim of the Bhāgavatam.

### 1.7.13-14

**Перевод:**

**After the warriors of both sides had attained either Svarga or liberation on the battlefield, and Duryodhana had his thigh broken by a blow from the club of Bhīma; when Aśvatthāmā, thinking that Duryodhana would be pleased, presented him with the severed heads of the sleeping sons of Draupadī, everyone condemned the horrible act, even Duryodhana.**

**Комментарий:**

A story is now told in order to show how Parīkṣit, even in the womb, was able to see Kṛṣṇa. When Aśvatthāmā (drauṇiḥ) beheaded the five sons of Draupadī, the mother began to weep. The sentence extends for three verses. Kaurava refers to Duryodhana’s party. Sṛñjaya refers to the Pāṇḍavas’ party since Dhrṣṭadyumna of the Sṛñjaya family was their general. Vīra-gatim means liberation or Svarga. Vṛkodarāviddha-gadābhimarśa means by the blow inflicted by the club thrown by Bhīma. Thinking that Duryodhana would be pleased (priyam), he presented the heads of the sleeping sons of the Pāṇḍavas to him. Actually, Duryodhana was not pleased with this action (vipriyam). After feeling joy on hearing that enemies had been killed, he began to lament since he understood through later contact that his enemies such as Bhīma had not been killed, that children had been killed, and that the Kuru dynasty had been destroyed. Thus he died with sorrow. Therefore, here it is said, “Everyone condemned this horrible act.”

### 1.7.15

**Перевод:**

**At that time, their mother hearing of the intolerable death of her youthful sons, pained, eyes full of tears, began to lament. Arjuna spoke to pacify her.**

**Комментарий:**

Arjuna is called kirīṭamālī because he had a garland and crown.

### 1.7.16

**Перевод:**

**O gracious woman! I will remove your sorrows when I bring the head of that lowest brāhmaṇa murderer using arrows released from my Gāṇḍīva bow and when you sit upon it, and bathe yourself after burning the bodies of your sons.**

**Комментарий:**

Śucaḥ means sorrows. I will remove (pramṛjāmi) your sorrows when (yat) I present to you using the arrows released from my bow the head of the lowest of brāhmaṇas (brahma-bandhoh), the aggressor who had a weapon in his hand (ātatāyinaḥ). According to the smṛti scriptures there are six aggressors: There are six aggressors: the arsonist, poisoner, holder of weapons, the stealer of wealth, the stealer of property, and the stealer of others’ wives. (Vasiṣṭha-smṛti 3.19) Having burned the bodies of your sons, stepping on his head, you will bathe.

### 1.7.17

**Перевод:**

**Thus pacifying her with many sweet words, Arjuna, whose friend and charioteer was Kṛṣṇa, holding his terrifying bow, with Hanumān on his flag, put on his armor and pursued Aśvatthāmā on his chariot.**

**Комментарий:**

Acyuta-mitra-sūtaḥ means who had Kṛṣṇa as his friend and charioteer. Daṁśitaḥ means “putting on armor.” He had Hanumān on his flag. He pursued (anvādravat) Aśvatthāmā.

### 1.7.18

**Перевод:**

**Seeing Arjuna pursuing on his chariot from far off, the killer of children became disturbed in mind. Desiring to save his life, he fled as fast as he could over the ground, just as the sun fled out of fear of Śiva.**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing Arjuna pursuing him, Aśvatthāmā, with trembling mind (udvigna-manāḥ), desiring to save his life (prāṇa-parīpsuḥ), not his fame, fled on the ground as far as it was possible to go (yāvadgamam). A demon named Vidyunmālī, a follower of Śiva, pursued the sun on a golden chariot given by Śiva. Due to the effulgence from the chariot, night disappeared. Seeing this, the sun became angry and melted the chariot with his heat and made it fall from the sky. Then fearing Śiva, who was angry, the sun fled and fell down at Vārāṇasī and became known as Lolārka (unsteady sun). This is famous in Vāmana Purāṇa.

### 1.7.19

**Перевод:**

**When he understood he had no one to protect him and his horses were exhausted, the son of the brāhmaṇa considered using the brahmāstra to protect himself.**

**Комментарий:**

He saw that he had no protector (aśaraṇam). “But fleeing should be his protection!” His horses were tired. He considered that the brahmāstra would be a method of protecting himself (ātma-trāṇam). The word dvijātmajaḥ (son of a brāhmaṇa instead of brāhmaṇa) indicates that he was short-sighted.

### 1.7.20

**Перевод:**

**Sipping water and concentrating his mind, he released the weapon without knowing how to withdraw it, because his life was in danger.**

**Комментарий:**

He performed ācamana (salilam upapṛśya). Samāhitaḥ means that he began meditating.

### 1.7.21

**Перевод:**

**Seeing that the fierce fire manifesting in all directions was a danger to life, Arjuna spoke to Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Jiṣṇuḥ is Arjuna.

### 1.7.22

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna said: O Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa! Mighty-armed Lord! Bestower of fearlessness in your devotees! You alone are the deliverer of all those who are burning in the fire of material existence.**

**Комментарий:**

You are the liberation (apavargaḥ) for living beings burning from saṁsāra. Therefore, what is it to deliver me from the fire of this weapon?

### 1.7.23

**Перевод:**

**You are the original Lord, the controller, beyond material energy. You are situated in your spiritual form, being one with your spiritual energy, and separate from the material energy.**

**Комментарий:**

“How do I give liberation?” You are without beginning (ādyaḥ), directly the Lord. You are Svayam Bhagavān with eternally manifested six powers (sākṣād īśvaraḥ). You are not touched (paraḥ) by māyā (prakṛteḥ). “How am I beyond prakṛti?” By your cit-śakti you reject (vyudasya) māyā. Parāsya śaktiḥ: you have your superior energy. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [6.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.8)) It is situated in your pure (kaivalye) svarūpa (ātmani). Kaivalya comes from kevala and means pure. Thus you are beyond prakṛti. Thus you can give liberation from prakṛti.

### 1.7.24

**Перевод:**

**Though you are non-different from your spiritual energy, you benefit all the jīvas bewildered by māyā through your power by giving them dharma, artha, and kāma.**

**Комментарий:**

You also give material benefits of tri-varga. The Lord is situated in his pure self, rejecting māyā, but, being the shelter of all jīvas overcome by māyā, gives dharma, artha and kāma by his own power.

### 1.7.25

**Перевод:**

**You appear in this world in this way, in your spiritual form, with a desire to relieve the burden of the earth and to give continuous happiness to your dedicated devotees by letting them worship you.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way, rejecting māyā, in your pure svarūpa (tathā), you appear in this world (ayam avatāraḥ) for worship by your followers possessing pure bhakti and some sādhakas as well. But relieving the earth of its burden happens by your will.

### 1.7.26

**Перевод:**

**O Supreme Lord! What is this? I do not know where it has come from. This most ferocious fire is spread out in all directions.**

**Комментарий:**

Having praised Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna then informs Kṛṣṇa of the matter at hand. Svit indicates conjecture. From where has this fire come?

### 1.7.27

**Перевод:**

**The Lord said: You know that what is displayed here by Aśvatthāmā is the brahmāstra. Seeing danger to his life, he has released it, though he does not know how to withdraw it.**

**Комментарий:**

“If he did not know how to withdraw it (na veda saṁhāram), why did he use it?” He used it to protect his own life.

### 1.7.28

**Перевод:**

**No other weapon can counteract this weapon. O knower of weapons! Destroy the profuse fire of this weapon with the fire of your brahmāstra.**

**Комментарий:**

“But the fire can be counteracted by the water weapon.” Nothing can stop this weapon (pratyavakarśanam), even another fire weapon. You must destroy the great (unnaddham) fire of this brahmāstra with your brahmāstra (astra-tejasā). Your weapon can counter it and dispel its power since you know about weapons.

### 1.7.29

*sūta uvāca— śrutvā bhagavatā proktaṁ phālgunaḥ para-vīra-hā \| spṛṣṭvāpas taṁ parikramya brāhmaṁ brāhmāyasandadhe \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Hearing the words of the Lord, Arjuna the slayer of hostile warriors, performing ācamana, circumambulated Kṛṣṇa and aimed his brahmāstra in order to counteract Aśvatthāmā’s weapon.**

**Комментарий:**

Apaḥ spṛṣṭvā means performing ācamana. Tam refers to Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.7.30

**Перевод:**

**Surrounded by arrows, the fires of both weapons, combining together like the sun in the sky and the fire emanating from Saṅkarṣaṇa’s mouth at the time of final destruction, increased in strength, covering heaven, earth and the space between.**

**Комментарий:**

The fires of the two brahmāstras combining (anyonyaṁ saṁhatya) increased by being surrounded with arrows, and filling heaven and earth and either (rodasī). It was like the sun combining with the fire emanating from Saṅkarṣaṇa’s mouth at the time of devastation arising from Pātāla-loka, creating increased heat.

### 1.7.31

**Перевод:**

**Seeing the great fire of their two weapons burning up the three worlds, all the citizens, afflicted by the heat, thought that this was the fire of final destruction.**

**Комментарий:**

Tayoḥ refers to Aśvatthāmā and Arjuna. Sāṁvartakam is the fire of final destruction according to Amara-kośa.

### 1.7.32

**Перевод:**

**Seeing the danger to the inhabitants of the universe and the destruction of the planets, and understanding the will of Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna withdrew the two weapons.**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing the destruction of the planets (loka-vyatikaraṁ), Arjuna withdrew the weapons. Vyatikaram means destruction.

### 1.7.33

**Перевод:**

**Eyes burning with anger, Arjuna quickly caught cruel Aśvatthāmā, and tied him up with rope like a beast.**

**Комментарий:**

Aśvatthāmā was the son of Kṛpī, who came from the Gautama gotra. Thus she is called Gautamī. The example shows the distinguishing feature of tying him up. He tied him up like priest ties up a sacrificial animal with rope.

### 1.7.34

**Перевод:**

**Seeing that Arjuna, who having forcibly bound him with ropes, wanted to bring him back to the camp, lotus-eyed Kṛṣṇa became angry and spoke to Arjuna.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa speaks to Arjuna, who was filled with lamentation and anger, to show the world Arjuna’s standard of justice. Śibirāya means to the royal camp. Kṛṣṇa’s anger was an external gesture. It is said aroṣaṇo hy asau devaḥ: the Lord is not angry. (Mahābhārata) Thus he is described as lotus-eyed.

### 1.7.35

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna! You should not release this fallen brāhmaṇa who murdered innocent, sleeping children in the night. Kill him!**

**Комментарий:**

He is a low brāhmaṇa (brahma-bandhum). The children were innocent, since they committed no offense (anāgasaḥ).

### 1.7.36

**Перевод:**

**The knower of dharma does not kill an enemy who is a drunkard, or who is inattentive, insane, sleeping, young, a female, immobilized, surrendered, without chariot, or afraid.**

**Комментарий:**

Mattam means intoxicated because of liquor, etc. Pramattam means inattentive. Unmattam means crazy because of planetary influences or disturbance of vāta in the body. Jaḍam means devoid of zeal. Prapannam means surrendered. Viratham means his chariot is broken.

### 1.7.37

**Перевод:**

**The merciless, cruel person who nourishes his own life by taking other lives should be killed; otherwise, that man goes to hell from that sin.**

**Комментарий:**

Aghṛnaḥ means merciless. Killing him as punishment is best for him (tad-vadhaḥ śreyaḥ) because it is an atonement. Smṛti says: rājabhir dhṛta-daṇḍas tu kṛtvā pāpāni mānavāḥ \| vidhūta-kalmaṣā yānti svargaṁ sukṛtino yathā \|\|Those who have committed sin, if punished by the king, become freed of that sin and go to Svarga like the pious. If he is not killed, because of that sin (yad), he goes to hell.

### 1.7.38-39

**Перевод:**

**I have heard that you promised to Draupadī, “O noble woman! I will present you with the head of the killer of your sons.” O warrior! This sinner, aggressor, killer of relatives, disgrace to his family, who has displeased Duryodhana, should be killed.**

**Комментарий:**

Pratiśrutam means promised. Bhartuḥ means Duryodhana.

### 1.7.40

**Перевод:**

**Sūta Gosvāmī said: Although Kṛṣṇa, who was examining Arjuna in religion, encouraged Arjuna to kill the son of Droṇācārya, Arjuna, a great soul, did not want to kill him, although Aśvatthāmā was a murderer of Arjuna’s family members.**

**Комментарий:**

Arjuna did not want to kill him, even though Aśvatthāmā had killed his son (ātma-hanam), because he was great (mahān) – he knew the opinion of Kṛṣṇa. “Kṛṣṇa is testing me, since it is forbidden to punish the brāhmaṇa.”

### 1.7.41

*athopetya sva-śibiraṁ govinda-priya-sārathiḥ \| nyavedayat taṁ priyāyai śocantyā ātma-jān hatān \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Arriving at his camp, Arjuna, whose friend and driver was Kṛṣṇa, offered Aśvatthāmā to his wife who was lamenting for her killed sons.**

**Комментарий:**

He offered Aśvatthāmā to her saying, “The killer of your sons has been brought.”

### 1.7.42

**Перевод:**

**Draupadī, of noble nature, looked with compassion upon wicked Aśvatthāmā, son of a guru, brought before her tied up like an animal, with downcast face because of his shameful crime, and also offered him her respects.**

**Комментарий:**

Tathā āhrṭam means “brought with disdain.” Karma-jugupsitena means “because of the repulsive act.” Because of that Aśvatthāmā lowered his head. Apakṛtam (wrongful act) should be apakāriṇam “the person who has done wrong.” She looked with compassion on him. Vāma means pleasing in actions. Draupadī (kṛṣṇā) offered her respects.

### 1.7.43

**Перевод:**

**Unable to tolerate him being brought in a bonded state, the noble Draupadī said, “Release him! Release him! The brāhmaṇa is always our guru.”**

**Комментарий:**

The word ca with uvāca and nanāma (in the previous verse) indicates “she spoke with reverence.” She is called satī or noble because she could not tolerate his bondage. She was thus more glorious than Arjuna.

### 1.7.44-45

**Перевод:**

**By the mercy of Droṇa, you learned the military arts with secret mantras, the method of throwing and withdrawing all weapons. Droṇa is standing before you in the form of his son. The other half of Droṇa, his wife Kṛpī, did not follow her husband when he died, but remains alive, because she has a son.**

**Комментарий:**

Sa-rahasyaḥ means “with secret mantras.” You learned how to throw and withdraw (upasaṁyamaḥ) weapons. The son is considered a representative of the father according to the saying ātmā vai jāyate putraḥ: one is born again as one’s son.The wife is considered half of the husband according to the śruti text ardho vā eṣa ātmano yatpatnī: the wife is half of oneself. (Kṛṣṇa Yajur-veda, Baudhāyanaśrauta-sūtra 29.89.381.2) She did not follow her husband to death because she had a son (vīra-sūḥ).

### 1.7.46

**Перевод:**

**O knower of dharma! Man of good character! You should not cause suffering to the family of your guru, because they are always praiseworthy and worthy of worship.**

**Комментарий:**

The relatives of your guru (gauravam kulam) should not attain suffering by you, because they are to be worshipped. Since you obtained the whole Dhanur Veda from him, you will show ingratitude by humiliating or injuring his son.

### 1.7.47

**Перевод:**

**Do not make Kṛpī, the mother of Aśvatthāmā and devoted to her husband, cry as I cry constantly with tearful face, pained by the death of my sons.**

**Комментарий:**

Mārodīt means na roditu “Let her not cry.”

### 1.7.48

**Перевод:**

**Angered by the uncontrolled warriors, the brāhmaṇas will quickly destroy the warrior class and their associates who then will become full of lamentation.**

**Комментарий:**

The angry brāhmaṇas will destroy the kṣatriya families. Sānubandham means “with their associates.” Śucārptiam means “spread with lamentation (śucā).”

### 1.7.49

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: O brāhmaṇas! Yudhiṣṭhira, son of Dharma, agreed with the words of his queen, which were according to scripture, reasonable, merciful, without desire to harm others, desirous of peace and great.**

**Комментарий:**

The six qualities of her words are seen in the previous six verses. Her words were faithful to dharma: release him because the brāhmaṇa is always our guru. (SB [1.7.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.43)) Her words were filled with correctness: his father taught you the military arts. (SB [1.7.44](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.44)) Her words were full of mercy: the wife is half the husband. (SB [1.7.45](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.45)) Her words expressed unwillingness to cause pain to others: do not cause suffering to the family of your guru. (SB [1.7.46](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.46)) Her words expressed sense of calmness in suffering: do not let her cry like me. (SB [1.7.47](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.47)) Her harsh words express greatness, desiring to benefit the hearer: the angry brāhmaṇas will destroy the kṣatriyas. (SB [1.7.48](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.48))

### 1.7.50

**Перевод:**

**Nakula and Sahadeva, Sātyaki, Arjuna, Kṛṣṇa, and the women, were also happy with her words.**

**Комментарий:**

They all agreed with her words. Yuyudhāna means Sātyaki.

### 1.7.51

**Перевод:**

**At that time angry Bhīma spoke. “He who has killed the sleeping children without purpose, without benefit to his master or to himself, should be killed.”**

**Комментарий:**

Tasya means Aśvatthāmā.

### 1.7.52

**Перевод:**

**Hearing the words of Bhīma and Draupadī, Kṛṣṇa with four hands looked towards his friend Arjuna while smiling and spoke.**

**Комментарий:**

Bhīma favored killing Aśvatthāmā and Draupadī favored releasing him. The Lord assumed four arms to keep the two parties apart. Kṛṣṇa smiled slightly as if saying, “O friend! Today I will test the sharpness of your intelligence.”

### 1.7.53

**Перевод:**

**The Lord said: One should not kill a fallen brāhmaṇa. But an aggressor should be killed. Please follow both of these instructions established in scripture made by me.**

**Комментарий:**

No brāhmaṇa should be killed. But Manu has said that if an aggressor approaches with the desire to kill, he can be killed, even if he is learned in Vedānta. Follow both of these instructions ordered by me, whose body is śruti and smṛti.

### 1.7.54

**Перевод:**

**You must fulfil your promise to Draupadī so that she is pacified. You must also act to please Bhīma, Draupadī, me and others.**

**Комментарий:**

You made a promise to bring Drauapadī the head of Aśvatthāmā. Thus you should cut off his head. That was your promise. You should also please Bhīma and kill him. You should also please Draupadī who does not want him killed. You should do both acts, since Bhīma and others are dear to me.

### 1.7.55

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Suddenly understanding Kṛṣṇa’s intention, Arjuna cut off the jewel on Aśvatthāmā’s head along with his hair.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord does not want me to kill him since he is a brāhmaṇa and is not an aggressor since he does not now have a weapon in his hand. (SB [1.7.36](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.36)) He actually meant, “Do not kill him but release him.” (SB [1.7.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.35)) “Killing him is good for him but not for the killer.” (SB [1.7.37](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.37)) “Let him be killed” (SB [1.7.39](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.39)) should be done related to his bondage. I will take away his head, but not cut it off. This will fulfil my promise. Doing this, everything will be fulfilled. Thinking thus he cut off the jewel on the forehead along with the hair. Since the hair is part of the head, he cut off his head in one sense. Thus he killed him and did not kill him.

### 1.7.56

**Перевод:**

**Aśvatthāmā, who had lost his effulgence by killing the children, was further deprived of his jewel and energy. Arjuna released him from the bondage of the ropes and led him from the camp.**

**Комментарий:**

Arjuna led him from the camp (nirayāpayat).

### 1.7.57

**Перевод:**

**The method of killing the fallen brāhmaṇa is by shaving his head, taking away his wealth, and taking away his living place. One should not physically kill him.**

**Комментарий:**

One should punish the fallen brāhmaṇa not physically but by shaving his hair, taking his wealth and banishing him from the village.

### 1.7.58

**Перевод:**

**Then all the Pāṇḍavas and Draupadī, lamenting for their sons, performed the death rites for their dead relatives and carried them for burning.**

**Комментарий:**

They took the bodies of relatives (svānām) for burning (nirharaṇa) and performed other rites.

## Chapter Eight - Kuntī’s prayers and Yudhiṣṭhira’s grief

### 1.8.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Then the Pāṇḍavas along with Kṛṣṇa, putting the women in front, went to the Gaṅgā to give water to the departed relatives who desired it.**

**Комментарий:**

In the Eighth Chapter, Kṛṣṇa protects the Pāṇḍavas and Parīkṣit in the womb from the brahmāstra, Kuntī offers prayers to Kṛṣṇa and Yudhiṣṭhira laments. After that (atha), the Pāṇḍavas went with Kṛṣṇa. There is a rule that the women should go in front during the funeral rites to give water.

### 1.8.2

**Перевод:**

**Offering water to the departed and lamenting intensely, everyone again took bath in the water purified by the dust from the lotus feet of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Ninīya means “having given.” Having bathed they offered water and then bathed again (punaḥ).

### 1.8.3-4

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa along with the sages then consoled Yudhiṣṭhira and his brothers, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Gāndhārī, Kuntī and Draupadī, lamenting the loss of their sons, all of whom were overcome with grief by the death of their relatives, who were all seated there, by showing the relentless movement of time upon all entities.**

**Комментарий:**

Kuru-patim refers to Yudhiṣṭhira. Sahānujam means “with Bhīma and his brothers”. With the sages, Kṛṣṇa consoled them.

### 1.8.5-6

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa had Yudhiṣṭhira established in his rightful kingdom stolen by cheaters, had the unrighteous kings whose lives were shortened because of grabbing Draupadī’s hair destroyed, and had Yudhiṣṭhira conduct horse sacrifices three times with best arrangements. Thus his pure fame spread in all directions like that of Indra.**

**Комментарий:**

The kings’ lives were destroyed by Duḥśāsana’s grabbing Draupadī’s hair and other sinful acts. The horse sacrifices were actually carried out later. Śatamanyu (hundred sacrifices) is Indra. Manyu means a sacrifice according to Amara-kośa.

### 1.8.7-8

**Перевод:**

**O Śaunaka! Having spoken to the Pāṇḍavas, having worshipped the sages such as Vyāsa and having been worshipped by them, Kṛṣṇa, desiring to go to Dvārakā, while seated on his chariot with Sātyaki and Uddhava, saw Uttarā, shaking with fear, running towards him.**

**Комментарий:**

Śaineyaḥ means Sātyaki, the grandson of Śini. Kṛṣṇa desired to go to Dvārakā.

### 1.8.9

**Перевод:**

**Uttarā said: O great yogī! O Supreme Lord! Master of the universe! Please protect me. I do not see anyone except you who is fearless in this world where each living being causes another’s death.**

**Комментарий:**

Other than you (tvad), I see no one who can give fearlessness. Death is mutual (parasparam) in the sense that one person causes another’s death, and someone else causes his death.

### 1.8.10

**Перевод:**

**O powerful lord! This arrow of burning iron is pursuing me. O Lord! Let it burn me up! But let it not kill my child!**

**Комментарий:**

Her fear is described. The arrow was hot iron. “Why should you live when you do not have a husband?” That is answered. Let it burn me up! But let it not kill my child!

### 1.8.11

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Hearing Uttarā’s words, Kṛṣṇa, affectionate to his devotees, understood that Aśvatthāmā had released a brahmāstra to rid the world of the Pāṇḍavas.**

**Комментарий:**

Aśvatthāmā wanted to make the world (idam) devoid of the Pāṇḍavas (apāṇḍavam). He was very angry because he was defeated.

### 1.8.12

**Перевод:**

**O best of sages! Seeing five flaming arrows headed towards them, the Pāṇḍavas then took up their weapons.**

**Комментарий:**

The approaching weapon with many heads is described. Pāṇḍavas alone saw this and no one else could.

### 1.8.13

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, seeing the danger to the Pāṇḍavas who were dedicated only to him, protected them with his own weapon - the Sudarśana.**

**Комментарий:**

Because it is difficult to counteract the five weapons that were coming by one brahmāstra released by Arjuna and unable to tolerate the delay in releasing the arrow, the Lord, though having no weapons, protected them by his Sudarśana. Thus he used Sudarśana. He protected devotees whose minds were dedicated to him fully (ananya-viṣayātmānām).

### 1.8.14

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, master of yoga, the soul dwelling within all beings, covered the embryo of Parīkṣit within Uttarā, in order to preserve the family of the Pāṇḍavas, using his yoga-māyā.**

**Комментарий:**

As the Paramātmā, Kṛṣṇa was situated within Uttarā already (antaḥsthaḥ), but he entered as Kṛṣṇa and covering the embryo, protected it. Because of his mercy (māyayā) to his own devotee (sva), Parīkṣit, Kṛṣṇa covered the embryo. Kuru here means the Pāṇḍavas since they were also in the Kuru family.

### 1.8.15

**Перевод:**

**O Śaunaka! Though the brahmāstra is effective and cannot be prevented from acting, on meeting the weapon of Viṣṇu, it became completely ineffective.**

**Комментарий:**

Vaiṣṇavam tejas means Sudarśana.

### 1.8.16

**Перевод:**

**You should not consider this so surprising for Kṛṣṇa who is full of all wonderful powers. He creates, maintains and destroys this universe by his power of Māyā-devī.**

**Комментарий:**

This counteracting the brahmāstra (etat) is not astonishing for Kṛṣṇa who is fixed (acyute), and is the abode of inconceivable, infinite śakti (sarvāścarya-maye). By his shining (devyā) desire (māyayā) he creates, maintains and destroys this universe (idam).

### 1.8.17

**Перевод:**

**The devotee Kuntī, along with her sons and Draupadī who were saved from the fire radiation of the brahmāstra, then spoke to Kṛṣṇa as he was about to depart for Dvārakā.**

**Комментарий:**

The devotee (satī) Kuntī along with Draupadī (kṛṣṇayā) spoke to Kṛṣṇa as follows.

### 1.8.18

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respects to you, the Supreme Person, the original one, the controller, beyond the control of prakṛti, unseen by material senses, and existing inside and outside of all living beings.**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing Kṛṣṇa’s powers at that time, Kuntī was unable to tolerate the agitation caused by the great respect that arose in her heart. Thus she began praising Kṛṣṇa. “But I am your nephew. Why are you offering respects?” But you are the first person (ādyam puruṣam). “But jīvas are also like this.” You are the Lord (īśvaram). “Well, Brahmā is like this.” You are beyond prakṛti. “No one speaks of me in this way.” You are invisible to all beings devoid of your mercy (alakṣyam). But you are visible to persons having your mercy. Entering Uttarā, you protected her embryo within and the Pāṇḍavas outside.

### 1.8.19

**Перевод:**

**I do not know you, covered by the screen of māyā, beyond the knowledge of the material senses, and unaffected, just as the puppeteer behind the screen moving the puppets is not understood by the eyes of the ignorant.**

**Комментарий:**

She expresses the idea, “Being ignorant, I cannot understand whether you are one or many, whether you are human in size or allpervading.” You are covered by a screen of my intelligence lacking such knowledge (māyā), just as clouds cover the sun. Māyā means intelligence according to Trikāṇḍa-śeṣa. Sense knowledge (akṣajam jñānam) is situated far beneath you (adhaḥ). This is the meaning of adhokṣaja. An example is given. An actor uses facial and eye movements to impersonate a young girl but this is not seen by the eyes of a fool, who does not know the scriptures on dramatics. Similarly, you are not known by me.

### 1.8.20

**Перевод:**

**How can we women hope to see you, the object of bhakti for the omniscient, liberated sages?**

**Комментарий:**

She indicates humility arising from the nature of bhakti. How can we know he whose goal is to produce (vidhāna) bhakti-yoga for those who have rid themselves of all contamination of performing karmas (amalātmanām)? You have appeared for giving a wealth of bliss through bhakti, by showing yourself to those anxious to see you without meditating on you like Śukadeva. We women are devoid of qualification as paramahaṁsas.

### 1.8.21

**Перевод:**

**I repeatedly offer respects to Kṛṣṇa, the son of Vasudeva, who gave joy to Devakī, who was the child of Nanda and lord of the cows.**

**Комментарий:**

Vasudeva and others are more fortunate than us. You are the son of Vasudeva, my brother. You give bliss to Devakī, being situated in her womb. By that relationship we are fortunate. More than them, Nanda and others are fortunate. Nanda is a cowherd, the king of Vraja. Ayājayad gosavena goparājam: the Lord had Nanda perform sacrifice by worshipping the cows. (SB [3.2.32](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.32)) You are Nanda’s son (kumarāya). You were enthroned by Indra as king of the cows (govindāya). Priyān na indro gavām: Govinda, the Lord of the cows and the destroyer of Indra’s false pride, be pleased with us. As lord of the cows, you have great wealth. Sacrifices take place because of cows. The devatās appear because of cows. The Vedas with their six aṅgas appear because of the cows. (Go-sūkta) They were most fortunate because Nanda and others experienced the sweetness of pastimes of your childhood.

### 1.8.22

**Перевод:**

**I offer respects to you, with lotus navel, wearing a lotus garland, having lotus eyes, and lotus feet.**

**Комментарий:**

Though I am not like those devotees, I see you and am successful. Thus I offer respects: namaḥ.

### 1.8.23

**Перевод:**

**O master of the senses! O Lord! Just as you protected Devakī imprisoned by evil Kaṁsa one time, you, my master, released me along with my sons repeatedly from even greater dangers, since I was afflicted with great suffering.**

**Комментарий:**

Moreover, you have shown more mercy to me than to your mother. Devakī, having a husband, desiring to have a son like you, was suddenly tormented by Kaṁsa one time. But I, a widow, with small children, was afflicted many times by enemies. You protected me. You are well known for being affectionate to the miserable.

### 1.8.24

**Перевод:**

**You saved us from Bhīma getting poisoned, from the burning house of lac, from the sight of rākṣasas like Hiḍimba, from the gambling den, from the hardships of living in the forest, from unlimited weapons thrown by great warriors in countless battlefields, and from the brahmāstra of Aśvatthāmā.**

**Комментарий:**

In this verse, she shows the variety of dangers. Viṣād refers to the giving of poison in sweets to Bhīma. Mahāgneḥ means “from the burning of the house of lac.” Puruṣāda means the rākṣasas like Hiḍimba. Asat-sabhāyāḥ refers to the gambling gathering.

### 1.8.25

**Перевод:**

**O guru of the universe! May we have dangers in such situations continually, because in those dangers we will see you, and by that we will gain release from this material world.**

**Комментарий:**

I consider these dangers are a great fortune to me. In all these dangers in different places and at different times, I saw you. From that (yat), I no longer see saṁsāra. This is anujñālaṅkāra. It is described as follows, duḥkhasya prārthanānujñā tatraiva sukhadarśanāt: one prays for suffering since that gives happiness.

### 1.8.26

**Перевод:**

**A man swelling with pride because of birth, power, learning or money, cannot chant your names. You are available to those who have nothing except you.**

**Комментарий:**

Abhidhātum means “to say the names of the Lord like Kṛṣṇa and Govinda.” You are attained by those who are devoid of wealth of the demons (akiñcana-gocaram).

### 1.8.27

**Перевод:**

**I offer my respects to he, whose treasure is unalloyed devotees, who is devoid of material interest, who is disinterested in those who are not devotees, who forgives his devotees, and helps the devotee desiring liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

She offers respects again to the Lord who is dear to the akiñcanas. They have no wealth except you. You are their wealth. Or you have them as your wealth. Why is this? You are detached from the functions of the guṇas. Having described Lord’s attachment to akiñcana-bhaktas, his indifference to others is described. You are ātmārāma. You do not become angry even if the devotee commits offense (śāntāya). You are the giver of liberation (kaivalya-pataye).

### 1.8.28

**Перевод:**

**I consider you to be destroyer of the offenders, the controller, without beginning or end, the most powerful, equal to all, moving everywhere in all living beings, and the abode of conflicting qualities.**

**Комментарий:**

You destroy the afflicter of the righteous (kālam). You are capable of destroying them (īśānam). You are a beginningless abode (anādinidhanaṁ) and by that form you are situated everywhere at all times (vibhum). You exist (carantam), showing that you are equal (samam) to the righteous and the wicked. There is contention of the living beings concerning you: are you a bound-up Lord or a liberated Lord? The first option is impossible (by definition). And you are not a liberated Lord since you do not show such proclivity. Thus accepting this is meaningless. Are you an impartial (equal to all) or a prejudiced Lord? You are not equal since it is seen that you give mercy and punishment to different people. You are not the prejudiced Lord because of your pacifying words. In this way there is quarrel.

### 1.8.29

**Перевод:**

**No one knows the intentions of the Lord who desires to hide himself in human form, who does not show favor or hatred to anyone, and about whom all men have different opinions.**

**Комментарий:**

“Who will know me amidst all these disagreements?” No one. O Lord! No one knows your intentions. The Lord is described. You conceal yourself (viḍambanam īhamānasya) from men by acting very much like a human though actually the Supreme Lord and for whom no one who is disciplined (kaścid) is dear and no one who is undisciplined (kaścid) is an enemy. The mercy and punishment are caused by their karma. The writer of the sūtras says: The Lord is neither prejudiced nor cruel because bodies are created according to karma. Scripture shows this. (Brahma-sūtras [2.1.34](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/2.1.34)) However, men have differing ideas about you. Thus no one knows you.

### 1.8.30

**Перевод:**

**O soul of the universe! You are unborn and perform no actions, but you take birth and perform activities in the forms of animals, men and aquatics. This is extremely deceptive.**

**Комментарий:**

“Since my actions follow those of this world, how is it deceptive to people?” O soul of the universe! You (ātmanaḥ), who have no birth (ajasya), by accepting astonishing body and senses, take birth, like the sun manifesting itself. You are not a doer by relating to matter, but act on your own (akartuḥ) with actions full of their own power (karma). Where does this happen? It happens among animals with forms like Varāha, among men with forms like Rāma, and among sages with forms like Vyāsa and among the aquatics (yādaḥsu) with forms like Matsya. It is a great derision to those beings, because in manifesting your actions like sniffing out the earth as a boar, by expanding your powers, they become useless.

### 1.8.31

**Перевод:**

**My dear Kṛṣṇa, Yaśodā took up a rope to bind you when you committed an offense, and your perturbed eyes flooded with tears, which washed the mascara from your eyes. And you were afraid, though fear personified is afraid of you. This sight is bewildering to me.**

**Комментарий:**

I cannot understand your pastimes. When Yaśodā, queen of Nanda’s kingdom, took rope to bind you for the offense of breaking the yogurt pot, you cried. That state bewilders me, though I understood you had powers. You lowered your face, and with emotions of fear, thinking, “She will beat me because of my offense,” you stood there (sthitasya), since she held you and you were not able to flee. Your face was covered (kalila) with tears, eyes covered with mascara (añjana) and were full of confusion (sambhrama). “It is fitting that I was afraid of my mother.” You were afraid, though fear personified is afraid of you. Thus both your fear and fearlessness are reality. This fact is the cause of my bewilderment.

### 1.8.32

**Перевод:**

**Some say that you, though unborn, have taken birth in the Yadu family to give fame to Yudhiṣṭhira, just as sandalwood, originating in the Malaya Hills gives fame to those hills.**

**Комментарий:**

Enough with deliberation! Your pastimes are relishable. Some say you appeared in the Yadu dynasty (anvavāye) to give fame to Yudhiṣṭhira and the Yadus (puṇya-ślokasya), like sandalwood appears in Malaya for the fame of the Malaya hills.

### 1.8.33

**Перевод:**

**Some say that you, though unborn, appeared as the son of Vasudeva in Devakī upon the request of Sutapa and Pṛśni, in order to protect the world and kill the demons.**

**Комментарий:**

Some say that, requested by Sutapa and Pṛśni, you were born as the son of Vasudeva in Devakī. Using third person (abhyagāt) instead of second person verb ending (abhyagāḥ) with tvam is poetic license. Another version with arbhatvam (to be their son) is easy to understand.

### 1.8.34

**Перевод:**

**Others say that you appeared at the request of Brahmā for lifting up the earth which had sunk like a boat overloaded with weight.**

**Комментарий:**

Another opinion is that you appeared mainly because of Brahmā’s request for removing the burden of the earth. The earth had sunk down by its great weight like a boat in the ocean.

### 1.8.35

**Перевод:**

**Some say that you have appeared in this world to help those suffering due to their actions arising from material desires, which in turn arise from ignorance, by engaging them in hearing and remembering about you.**

**Комментарий:**

This is her opinion of the reason for the Lord’s appearance. First there is ignorance, cause of saṁsāra (avidyā), then desire for enjoyment (kāma), and then actions (karmabhiḥ). Some say that you were born in order to perform pastimes which cause pure bhakti in the form of hearing and meditation, which destroy the suffering of the living beings suffering in this material world (bhave) from actions caused by desire which in turn is caused by ignorance.

### 1.8.36

**Перевод:**

**Persons who constantly hear, sing, speak, remember about your activities and by that prosper—they alone, without delay, are able to see your lotus feet which destroy the course of material existence.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse is the conclusion of that statement. They hear your pastimes (īhitam). They sing about those pastimes and obtain good fortune by that (nandanti). They and not others (eva) see for certain, without delay (acireṇa), not after a long time, your (tāvakam) lotus feet, not the lotus feet of one of your expansions. They directly see those lotus feet which destroy the course of saṁsāra, not just seeing Vaikuṇṭha. There are many ascertainments of beauty in those feet.

### 1.8.37

**Перевод:**

**O Lord! Do you, whose deeds are automatically accomplished, desire to reject us today, though we are your friends and depend on you? We, having created trouble with many kings, have no other shelter than your lotus feet.**

**Комментарий:**

She hints at his desire to go to Dvārakā. O Lord! Do you desire to go, giving us up today? Svit indicates a question. “I have necessary work there.” To this Kuntī answers, “But you are the person whose necessary works are already accomplished. Your intentions fulfil themselves automatically (sva-kṛtehita). Without your glance we will suffer greatly. We have no other shelter than your lotus feet. When you leave, other causes of suffering will arise. The Pāṇḍavas had created suffering for many kings by killing their fathers or relatives. They are everywhere. Therefore, stay here and protect us your friends.” The statement expresses pain at separation.

### 1.8.38

**Перевод:**

**Without your presence who are we, the Pāṇḍavas along with the Yadus with their fame and strength? We are like the senses without the jīva.**

**Комментарий:**

“Your enemies are dead. The Yādavas are your friends. Your sons are great warriors and shine with great dharma. Why do you speak like this?” When you do not glance upon us, then who are we, with fame and prosperity (nāma-rūpābhyām)? We are nothing. We are useless without you, just as the senses become useless without their controller, the jīva. Your separation will kill us.

### 1.8.39

**Перевод:**

**O holder of the club! When you go, this land, marked with the special signs on your feet, will not glow as it does now.**

**Комментарий:**

Moreover, when you leave from here, the land protected by us will not be glorious as it is now when it is glorious with the marks of your feet. The feet are described. They are marked with the signs of the flag, thunderbolt and goad not seen in others. We will be greatly saddened on seeing this land devoid of those marks.

### 1.8.40

**Перевод:**

**This thriving land, filled with ripe herbs and fruit-laden trees, forests, mountains and rivers, has grown prosperous by your glance.**

**Комментарий:**

The lands, without your glance will lack prosperity. They will cause us suffering.

### 1.8.41

**Перевод:**

**O Lord of all the universes! O life of all the universes! O form of all the universes! Please cut my strong bonds of affection for my relatives, the Pāṇḍavas and Yādavas.**

**Комментарий:**

Since leaving from here gives great pain to the Pāṇḍavas, and coming here gives great pain to the Yadus, live in many places. If that is not possible, cut my bond of affection to both my relatives – Pāṇḍavas and Yādavas. The many vocatives indicate his capacity to do this.

### 1.8.42

**Перевод:**

**Let my mind, with attention fixed on no other object, repeatedly carry its affection to you, chief of the Madhu dynasty, just as the Gaṅgā carries a full stream of water to the ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

“You request cutting bonds of affection for the Pāṇḍavas and Yadus. Do you also request cutting bonds of affection for me also?” I have said that I suffer from your separation. But now I request to you that my mind have prema (ratim) to you, who accompany those devotees. This is consistent with the next verse. This is so that I may not be separated from seeing you.

### 1.8.43

**Перевод:**

**O Kṛṣṇa! O friend of Arjuna! Best of the Yādavas! Destroyer of the kings who harmed the earth! Lord of undiminished strength! Lord of all the cows! Destroyer of the suffering of the cows, brāhmaṇas and devatās! Master of yoga! Guru of all people! O Lord! I offer respects to you.**

**Комментарий:**

Having prayed for continuous prema, remembering Kṛṣṇa as the cause of bliss to all, she offers respects. O Kṛṣṇa! O friend of Arjuna (kṛṣṇa-sakha)! Destroyer of dynasties of kings who afflicted the earth. You have undiminished strength (anapavarga-vīrya). O son of Nanda, having attained an unlimited wealth of desire cows (govinda)! O Bhagavān! I offer respects to you.

### 1.8.44

**Перевод:**

**Suta said: The Lord, whose greatness had been glorified by the sweet words of Kuntī, smiled softly as if bewildered by mercy.**

**Комментарий:**

He was praised by the words filled with sweetness (kala). Pariṇūta comes from the verb nū of the tud class with long vowel. It means “to praise.” The Lord was bewildered, by mercy (māyayā). The word iva is used in order to indicate that his bewilderment was natural to him. Or māyā means prema, not material māyā.

### 1.8.45

**Перевод:**

**Agreeing with her, Kṛṣṇa entered Hastināpura. Wanting to leave for his city, he took permission from Kuntī and the other women. He was prevented from leaving by Yudhiṣṭhira out his great love for Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

She prayed that her mind would carry prema for Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa agreed with her (tām). He entered Hastināpura from his chariot and stayed there. Later, taking permission from Kuntī, Draupadī and Subhadrā, he started to leave for Dvārakā but was prevented by the king out of prema. The king prayed, “Stay a little longer.” And he stayed. That was the great strength of the king’s prema.

### 1.8.46

**Перевод:**

**Though instructed through stories by Vyāsa and other sages who knew the Lord’s intentions, and though even instructed by Kṛṣṇa, who had performed the remarkable action of bewildering Yudhiṣṭhira at this moment, Yudhiṣṭhira became overcome with grief, and could not understand those instructions.**

**Комментарий:**

This is an introduction to the passing of Bhīṣma. Though enlightened by Vyāsa and others and by Kṛṣṇa, the king did not develop discernment (nābudhyata). Kṛṣṇa was capable of increasing the glory of his devotee. His intention was to enlighten the king by the words of Bhīṣma, while Kṛṣṇa along with his followers stayed with Bhīṣma when he was leaving his body. Vyāsa and others were instructed of his intentions (īhā) by his hints (īśvarehā-jñaiḥ), though they knew his actions. The phrase can also mean Vyāsa could not be understood since he was the Supreme Lord and thus omniscient. It is an atad-guṇa-saṁvijñāna bahuvrīhi compound. Though taught by Kṛṣṇa and by the sages, the king was not enlightened. Rather his confusion increased. Thus Kṛṣṇa’s action was astonishing (adbhuta-karmaṇā).

### 1.8.47

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇas! Yudhiṣṭhira, thinking of the killing of his friends, his mind overcome with bewilderment arising from affection, due to material thinking, then spoke.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes Yudhiṣṭhira’s loss of discrimination. He was overcome by bewilderment because his mind was affected by lack of discrimination regarding what is to be done, caused by prakṛti (prākṛtena ātmanā), a temporary mixture arising by the Lord’s desire. Ātmanā means by the mind.

### 1.8.48

**Перевод:**

**Oh! Look at the ignorance spread over my evil heart. Just for my body which is fit for the jackals’ food, I have killed many armies.**

**Комментарий:**

For my body which is food for dogs and jackals (pārakyasya), I have killed many akṣauhiṇīs. Akṣauhinīḥ should be in the nominative instead of accusative. Vyāsa has described the size of the akṣauhiṇī. O best of the brāhmaṇas! Those who understand counting know that an akṣauhiṇī consists of 21,870 chariots and the same number of elephants. It has 109,350 foot soldiers. It has 65,610 horses. (Mahābhārata, Ādi parva)

### 1.8.49

**Перевод:**

**Because I have killed children, brāhmaṇas, relatives, friends, paternal uncles, cousins, and gurus, I cannot be free from hellish punishments for ten thousand years.**

**Комментарий:**

Suhṛt here means relatives. Pitṛ means paternal uncles.

### 1.8.50

**Перевод:**

**There is no sin for the king who kills the enemy in a righteous war, protecting the citizens. This rule does not apply to me.**

**Комментарий:**

There is no sin for a king in killing the enemy during war. The instruction (śāsanam), the words of dharma scriptures, do not apply to me since those words are for the protector of the citizens. The meaning here is that for the king involved in a dharmic battle for protecting the citizens from enemies who harass the citizens, there is no sin. However, out of greed for a kingdom, I have killed Duryodhana and others, who were protecting their citizens. Why should I not incur sin?

### 1.8.51

**Перевод:**

**I cannot counteract by household rituals the injury I have inflicted on the women, whose husbands or sons I have killed.**

**Комментарий:**

In fighting enemies, let killing be dharma. Unintended violence has arisen to the women whose husbands I killed. I cannot remove that injury by agnihotra and other sacrifices prescribed for the householders. Thus I am sinful.

### 1.8.52

**Перевод:**

**Just as one cannot purify muddy water by using mud, or cannot purify what is contaminated with liquor by applying more liquor, it is not possible to purify oneself of killing even one living being by performance of animal sacrifices.**

**Комментарий:**

“Śruti says sarvaṁ pāpmānaṁ tarati brahma-hatyāṁ yo ’śvamedhena yajeta: he who performs a horse sacrifice is purified of all sins, even the sin of killing a brāhmaṇa. Therefore, you can purify yourself by a horse sacrifice. Your lamentation is not proper.” One cannot destroy the sin by horse sacrifices since, being mixed with great violence, they are a cause of sin. Just as thick mud cannot purify muddy water, and an object made impure by contact with wine cannot become pure by washing it with a lot of wine, one cannot be purified of killing even one living being accidently by the performance of many sacrifices which has intentional killing of animals as a major part. He discerns in this manner by manifesting logical arguments.

## Chapter Nine - Passing of Bhīṣma

### 1.9.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Being fearful because of having committed violence against the population, Yudhiṣṭhira, desiring to examine all dharmas, went to Kurukṣetra where Bhīṣma had fallen.**

**Комментарий:**

In the ninth chapter Bhīṣma sees Kṛṣṇa, and speaks on dharma, praises the Lord and attains the Lord. Vyāsa, others and I myself gave you instructions. But your confusion has not dissipated.
