Ask Bhīṣma, your great benefactor, who is the personification of knowledge of all dharmas. When Kṛṣṇa said this, the king went to Bhīṣma. Vinaśanam refers to Kurukṣetra.

### 1.9.2

**Перевод:**

**His brother and brāhmaṇas such as Vyāsa and Dhaumya followed behind with chariots decorated with gold and fine horses.**

### 1.9.3

**Перевод:**

**O Śaunaka! The Lord along with Arjuna also followed behind in his chariot. King Yudhiṣṭhira shone with his brothers like the god of wealth Kuvera accompanied by the Guhyakas, guardians of his treasures.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa also followed behind Yudhiṣṭhira, who shone with his brothers (taiḥ).

### 1.9.4

**Перевод:**

**Seeing Bhīṣma lying on the ground like a fallen devatā, the Pāṇḍavas offered respects to him along with their followers and Kṛṣṇa.**

### 1.9.5

**Перевод:**

**O Śaunaka! There the brāhmaṇa sages, the sages of the heavenly planets and the kings who were sages had gathered to see Bhīṣma, the best of the Bharata lineage.**

**Комментарий:**

Bharata-puṅgavam means Bhīṣma.

### 1.9.6-8

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇa! Parvata, Nārada, Dhaumya, Vyāsa, Bṛhadaśva, Bharadvāja, Paraśurāma, Vasiṣṭha, Indrapramada, Trita, Gṛtsamada, Asita, Kakṣīvān, Gautama, Atri, Kauśika, Sudarśana, along with their disciples, and as well many other pure sages such as Śuka, Kaśyapa and Bṛhaspati came along with their disciples.**

**Комментарий:**

Renukā-suta is Paraśurāma. Brahma-rāta is Śukadeva. Āṅgirasa is Bṛhaspati.

### 1.9.9

**Перевод:**

**The best of the Vasus, Bhīṣma, endowed with great qualities, knowledgeable of dharma as applicable according to place and time, seeing that they had gathered, worshipped them.**

**Комментарий:**

Vasūttama is Bhīṣma. He worshipped them suitably.

### 1.9.10

**Перевод:**

**Bhīṣma, understanding Kṛṣṇa’s powers, worshipped Kṛṣṇa, the lord of the universe, situated within the heart, who was seated there and who had come before Bhīṣma out of great mercy.**

**Комментарий:**

He who knew Kṛṣṇa’s great powers (tat-prabhāva-jñaḥ) worshipped Kṛṣṇa, whose form was brought close (upātta) to Bhīṣma’s eyes . by his mercy (māyayā). Māyayopātta-vigraham can mean Kṛṣṇa who had a conflict (vigraha) with Yudhiṣṭhira by covering up his discrimination with the three guṇas (māyayā). Vigraha can mean body or conflict according to Viśva-prakāśa. To say that Kṛṣṇa had a form made of matter is wrong. Kṛṣṇa has a form of sac-cid-ānanda. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad [1.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/1.35)) This universe appears real because it is manifested by the potency of illusion emanating from you, whose unlimited form is full of eternal happiness and knowledge. (SB [10.14.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/14.22))

### 1.9.11

**Перевод:**

**His eyes blinded by tears of love, he saw the Pāṇḍavas sitting there, filled with humility and love.**

**Комментарий:**

Abhyācaṣṭa means “he saw.”

### 1.9.12

**Перевод:**

**Having the shelter of brāhmaṇas, dharma and Kṛṣṇa, you should not live your life in suffering since that is dangerous and improper.**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing the Pāṇḍavas, he speaks of the cause of dangers, difficult to comprehend. O sons of dharma! You do not deserve to live in difficulty since you take shelter of brāhmaṇas, dharma and Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.9.13

**Перевод:**

**When the great warrior Pāṇḍu died, his wife Kuntī with young children constantly underwent many difficulties in raising you since you were young at that time.**

**Комментарий:**

“What was the suffering?” This verse describes it. Saṁsthite means he died. Kuntī’s miserable condition is described: Being alone with young children caused much suffering (bāla-prajā vadhūḥ). Though you are now grown up, she underwent great suffering with you sons constantly, since she had you as children (tokavatī).

### 1.9.14

**Перевод:**

**I consider that this, which is unwelcome, has all been done to you by time, which controls the world and its protectors just as the wind controls the clouds.**

**Комментарий:**

“You say that though you are omniscient, you do not discern the cause. Please tell us something of the cause.” Bhīṣma then speaks with a mixture of ordinary views. Though you are great devotees of Kṛṣṇa, the unfavorable situations were due to the power of time, which controls the world.

### 1.9.15

**Перевод:**

**There is misfortune where there is Yudhiṣṭhira, the son of Dharma, Bhīma, holder of the club, Arjuna holder of the bow Gāṇḍiva, and your friend Kṛṣṇa!**

**Комментарий:**

Time has done something most impossible. Kṛṣṇa means Arjuna, with his bow (astrī). Though the causes of prosperity existed, prosperity did not exist. It is the alaṅkāra of viśeṣokti.

### 1.9.16

**Перевод:**

**O King! No one can understand the plan of Kṛṣṇa because even those engaged in reasoning and scripture are bewildered by that inquiry.**

**Комментарий:**

He offers his own opinion. No person, even Brahmā, can understand the plan of Kṛṣṇa (asya). They do not know what he intends. “No one may know but at least there should be inquiry.” Yogīs (yuktāḥ) and scholars (kavayaḥ) are bewildered by inquiry. The following should be said. Your difficulties cannot be said to be produced from karma since the dedicated devotees of Kṛṣṇa have no influence of karma. Being mixed with some sin is contrary to the dharmic nature of Yudhiṣṭhira. The difficulties cannot be said to be the product of time since devotees are not under its influence. Śruti and smṛti say that karma arises from you: He controls time. He has all good qualities. He inspires all knowledge. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [6.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.2)) Kāla-kṛtam (verse 14) means by Kṛṣṇa, who possesses the śakti of time, because it will later be said sva-kāla-śakyā: by his time śakti (SB [10.1.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/1.22)). Therefore your difficulties are ordained by Kṛṣṇa: When a person realizes you, he no longer cares about his good and bad fortune arising from past pious and sinful acts, since it is you alone who control this good and bad fortune. (SB [10.87.40](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/87.40)) Inquiry is not proper, since even scholars are bewildered. Did he plan to give you sorrow, happiness or both? It cannot be the first, since that would destroy his affection for his devotees. It cannot be the second option, since we do not see that. It cannot be the last option since his friendliness would be negated.

### 1.9.17

**Перевод:**

**O master! O controller! O best of the Bharata lineage! Therefore, discerning that the suffering and happiness is dependent only on Kṛṣṇa, follow him and protect the helpless citizens.**

**Комментарий:**

Give up deliberating further. May the Lord act as he wishes. You should surrender. Deciding to be dependent on the Lord (daiva- tantram), following the Lord (tasya anuvihitaḥ), protect the citizens, devoid of a master (anāthāḥ). O master (nātha)! O Yudhiṣṭhira, lord capable of protecting them (prabho)!

### 1.9.18

**Перевод:**

**Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa, the original Nārāyaṇa, the puruṣa, who bewilders the world with his energy, secretly moves in the Yādava family.**

**Комментарий:**

“You should instruct us something more, since we are dedicated devotees of Kṛṣṇa. Teach us about the nature of the Lord?” He is the shelter of twenty-five tattvas (ādyaḥ nārāyaṇaḥ). He is directly Bhagavān svayam rūpa. He is the aṁśī of Nārāyaṇa. “But others do not say this.” He bewilders the world. Those who are fully dedicated say this.

### 1.9.19

**Перевод:**

**O King! Lord Śiva, Nārada, sage of the heavens, and Lord Kapila know the most secret powers of Kṛṣṇa, though not his intentions.**

**Комментарий:**

He shows himself to the devotees. They know Kṛṣṇa’s most secret great powers (anubhāvam), but they do not know his plan. Sometimes taking away everything, increases the devotee’s prema. Sometimes, he increases the devotee’s prema with no hardships. He is famous for this. They know directly (sākṣāt) his powers. Kapila is the Lord, son of Kardama.

### 1.9.20

**Перевод:**

**You think of Kṛṣṇa as your cousin, as a dear friend, as your selfless helper, and out of affection made him your counselor, messenger, and charioteer.**

**Комментарий:**

Sometimes he gives difficulties to his devotees in order to make them experience his steady friendship. He gives results to all beings. He is the controller. He is the shelter of all. He is the friend of all. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [3.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/3.17)) Who would be able to recognize him as the Supreme Lord without that hardship? He gives difficulties in order that the devotee experience bliss.

### 1.9.21

**Перевод:**

**His actions are not a mistake of his judgment for he is faultless, he is the soul of all beings, he sees all equally, he is without a second, and is without pride.**

**Комментарий:**

Becoming a messenger because of his friendliness is not a fault in him. He is the controller of all (sarvātmanaḥ). He sees all equally, thinking “they are all to be regulated by me.” He is different from jīva and prakṛti (advayasya). He has no pride (anahaṅkṛteḥ) because others are not comparable to him. His acting as a messenger, etc. (tat-kṛtam) has no sense of thinking in terms of dualism (mativaiṣamyam), thinking, “This person is qualified and this person is not qualified.” Rather his quality of being affectionate to his devotee is a great quality.

### 1.9.22

**Перевод:**

**O King! See his mercy to his dedicated devotees! By that mercy he has come before me directly when I am about to leave my body.**

**Комментарий:**

He shows the Lord’s friendship with his devotees. Though he is of this nature, full of the highest bliss, most pure, yet see his mercy to his dedicated devotees, which is full of great friendliness. By that mercy (yat), he has personally come to be visible to me at the time of my death, while I am pierced with arrows in all my limbs, in this disgusting place. What can one say about him?

### 1.9.23-24

**Перевод:**

**The yogī whose mind is absorbed in Kṛṣṇa, whose voice chants his name, becomes freed from all karmas on giving up the material body. May that Kṛṣṇa, lord of lords, with glowing lotus face, red eyes and pleasing smile, with four arms, the object of my meditation, remain before me while I drink his beauty and praise him, before giving up this body.**

**Комментарий:**

With those having bhakti mixed with yoga, he acts differently. I am surrendered to him alone. He is the doer of everything for me. He who is contemplated in the heart by meditation (dhyāna-pathaḥ) for others, is situated in front of me. “How long will he wait for me?” May he wait for me. “How long does he have to wait?” Till I give up (hinomi) my body. Giving up his body was by his own will. Drinking the nectar of his sweetness with my eyes, praising him as I place him in my mind, I proclaim him. Till that time, let him remain. Bhīṣma addressed him as a form with four arms because that was the form of Kṛṣṇa mentioned in the mantra he used during meditation.

### 1.9.25

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Yudhiṣṭhira, hearing what he had said, then asked Bhīṣma, lying on a bed of arrows, about various dharmas while the sages were listening.**

**Комментарий:**

Hearing his merciful words, Yudhiṣṭhira asked about dharma from Bhīṣma, who knew all material and spiritual truths like an amalakī in his hand, who did not feel pain from weapons by blessing of the Lord, as he lay on the bed of arrows.

### 1.9.26

**Перевод:**

**He described dharmas suitable for men according to their natures, according to varṇa and āśrama, which have qualities of renunciation and enjoyment, described according to a person’s detachment or attachment.**

**Комментарий:**

He described the ordinary dharmas for humans prescribed by their natures, according to their qualification (yathā) in varṇa and in āśrama. Yathā-varṇaṁ and yathāśramam are indeclinable phrases, which mean dharmas suitable to varṇa and āśrama. Furthermore, these dharmas were mentioned (āmnāta) as having upādhis of enjoyment or renunciation. They had these qualities of pravṛtti and nivṛtti. The meaning is this. It is a rule that all the āśramas, beginning with brahmacārī, need not be undertaken one after the other by all brāhmaṇas. If they have intense renunciation, from brahmacārī life they become sannyāsīs, and if they have attachment to sense objects, they should be situated as gṛhasthas. With the weakening of attachment, they can progress. After completing brahmacārī life one becomes a householder. Having finished household life one becomes a vānaprastha. Having completed vānaprastha, one becomes a sannyāsī. Or he may become a sannyāsī after brahmacārī life, or after household life. He may perform sacrifices or not. He may complete his brahmacārī life or not. He may maintain the fire or not. Whenever he becomes detached, he may take sannyāsa. (Jābāla Upaniṣad 4, Yājñavalkya Upaniṣad 1) He described them according to śruti.

### 1.9.27

**Перевод:**

**Within varṇāśrama, he described duties of charity, duties of the king, duties for attaining liberation, duties of women and bhagavad-dharma, in brief and in detail.**

**Комментарий:**

Mokṣa-dharma consists of control of senses and mind, and śravaṇa, manana, nididhyāsana. Bhagavad-dharmān means activities mentioned in Pañcarātra—in the form of bhakti, which pleases the Lord. He described them in brief and in detail (samāsa-vyāsayogataḥ).

### 1.9.28

**Перевод:**

**O Śaunaka! Bhīṣma, knower of truth, accurately described dharma, artha, kāma and mokṣa along with their methods, using various stories and histories as proof.**

**Комментарий:**

He described the dharmas according to qualification along with their sādhanas (sahopāyān), with stories which were historical.

### 1.9.29

**Перевод:**

**The time of uttarāyaṇa, which was desired by Bhīṣma, who had finished speaking on dharma and could die when he chose, then arrived.**

**Комментарий:**

Chanda-mṛtyoḥ means “of he who could die when he pleased.”

### 1.9.30

**Перевод:**

**At that time, withdrawing his words from other subjects, with eyes wide open, Bhīṣma, leader of a thousand chariots, concentrated himself, free of all material attachment, upon Kṛṣṇa, the original person, dressed in shining yellow garments, with four arms, standing before him.**

**Комментарий:**

Sahasraṇīḥ refers to Bhīṣma, who led (nī) or protected a thousand charioteers gathered for battle. Another version has sahasriṇīḥ which means “possessing a thousand treasures.” Withdrawing his words from other subjects (giraḥ upasaṁhṛtya), with eyes completely open without blinking, he completely absorbed his mind in Kṛṣṇa. He gazed at Kṛṣṇa with his eyes, though yogīs cannot approach him even with their minds. The Lord, from foot to head, entered his mind.

### 1.9.31

**Перевод:**

**As Bhīṣma, free of all inauspiciousness by his pure concentration, free of physical fatigue from fighting and free of wandering senses by Kṛṣṇa’s glance of mercy, left his body, he began to praise Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Tad-īkṣayā means by the glance of mercy of Kṛṣṇa. Vibhramaḥ means the various wanderings (vividha-bhramaṇa) of the senses. Janyam means the material body, or the material world.

### 1.9.32

**Перевод:**

**Bhīṣma said: At the end of my life I offer my thoughts to you, Bhagavān full of six qualities, best of the Yadus, superior to all other forms of the Lord, absorbed in bliss with your associates, and who, as a pastime, in the form of the puruṣāvatara, sometimes accepts māyā by your glance, which produces the material world.**

**Комментарий:**

At the end of my life (iti), my thoughts, fixed on Kṛṣṇa, Bhagavān, king of the Yādavas (sāttvata-puṅgave), eternal (vibhūmni), who has come to me out of great mercy, are offered as a gift to the Lord. The thoughts are for anything else except him (vitṛṣṇā). Kṛṣṇa has attained (upagate) bliss in pastimes (sukham) with his devotees (sva) the Yādavas and Pāṇḍavas. Sometimes, through his svāṁśas, he accepts prakṛti, glancing over it, because of which the sequence of creation takes place.

### 1.9.33

**Перевод:**

**Let me have pure prema for Kṛṣṇa, the friend of Arjuna, who possesses a body desired by all the inhabitants of the three worlds, which is clothed with intense yellow garments shining in the sun, whose complexion is dark like the tamāla tree, and whose lotus face is surrounded by locks of hair.**

**Комментарий:**

He prays for rati, being attached to the Lord’s form. May I have rati for Arjuna’s friend (vijaya-sakhe), who manifests (dadhāne) a body dark like a tamāla tree. The form of the Lord is not accepted or given up by him. Accepting and giving up a body actually means the Lord’s appearance and disappearance. (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) That body is desired (kamanam) by the three worlds, with cloth very yellow like the rays of the sun. That rati has no desire for anything except that form (anavadyā).

### 1.9.34

**Перевод:**

**May my mind concentrate on Kṛṣṇa, whose face was decorated with hair covered with the dust raised by horses, tossed all about because of the speed of his driving, and with perspiration because of his great effort in protecting Arjuna, whose armor shone brightly, being pierced slightly by my sharp arrows.**

**Комментарий:**

Absorbed in the sweetness of Kṛṣṇa’s face surrounded by locks of hair, Bhīṣma praises him. The face is decorated with locks of hair thrown all about because of the speed of the chariot and colored grey with the dust raised by the horses. The face is decorated with perspiration arising from effort. This indicates Kṛṣṇa’s efforts because of his affection for Arjuna. May my mind concentrate on that Kṛṣṇa. “You have prema for me, why do you shoot arrows at me?” This was for giving you happiness. Your skin was being pierced but not actually pierced by my sharp arrows. You cannot be deformed by arrows since you are sac-cid-ānanda. This was a stimulus for you to fight as a great warrior. I was not without prema in doing this. The woman wounds her lover with her fingernails not for displeasing him, but for pleasing him.

### 1.9.35

**Перевод:**

**May I have prema for the chariot driver of Arjuna, who placed the chariot between the two opposing armies immediately on hearing Arjuna’s request, and, situated there, by his glance, took away the lives of the opposing party.**

**Комментарий:**

O Acyuta! please station my chariot between the two armies, so I can view at the commencement of the war those situated with a desire to fight, and can see my companions in battle. (BG [1.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/1.21)) Hearing Arjuna’s words, he placed the chariot between the two armies. May my mind concentrate on that Kṛṣṇa, driving the chariot. Situated there, his friendship is described. On the pretext of showing the armies he took away the lives of the opposing troops headed by Duryodhana using his kāla-śakti.

### 1.9.36

**Перевод:**

**May I have prema for the feet of the Supreme Lord who, by giving knowledge of himself, destroyed the ignorance of Arjuna who, on seeing the heads of the opposing army at a distance, refused to fight because he thought it was a sin to kill his relatives.**

**Комментарий:**

He prays for rati to Kṛṣṇa, who mercifully manifested the Bhagavad Gītā for delivering the world. Vyavahita-pṛtanā-mukhaṁ nirīkṣya means “seeing Bhīṣma and others standing in front of the army at a distance.” Arjuna became disinclined to kill his relatives, which was his dharma, thinking it sinful. He threw down his bow and arrows and lamented. May I have prema for the feet of the Lord who dissipated the confusion of this Arjuna by ātma-vidyā. Just as he made Yudhiṣṭhira disturbed so that he could be merciful to me, so he confused Arjuna so that he could deliver the world through the Gītā. This is the Lord’s great mercy.

### 1.9.37

**Перевод:**

**Giving up his own promise not to fight, and making my vow to make him fight come true, Kṛṣṇa, situated on the chariot, quickly got down and holding the wheel of a chariot, ran towards me, like a lion coming to kill an elephant, while the earth shook and his top cloth fell to the ground.**

**Комментарий:**

His affection for his devotee is so amazing that Kṛṣṇa elevates his devotee to a higher position than his own. This is explained in two verses (37-38). Kṛṣṇa made a promise (sva-nigamam) that he would only assist Arjuna without using any weapon. He gave up that promise. I made a promise that I would make him take up weapons. To make sure that stronger promise would become true, Kṛṣṇa, situated on the chariot, quickly got down from the chariot in such a manner that no one could see his separation from the chariot. Or Kṛṣṇa, though he got down, remained on the chariot to protect it in another form. He took the wheel of the chariot and ran towards me to kill me, like a lion killing an elephant. This indicates intolerable strength. He caused the earth to tremble (calad guḥ) due to his running in extreme agitation. His cloth fell on the ground. The earth trembled in fear that the Lord, giving up his promise, would destroy the earth before pralaya. He calmed the earth by offering his cloth. This is the hint.

### 1.9.38

**Перевод:**

**Let my only goal be Mukunda, who, afflicted by my sharp arrows, armor broken, covered in blood, rushed towards me, his enemy, with great force, in order to kill me.**

**Комментарий:**

Afflicted by my sharp arrows, as I, the enemy, held weapons in my hand and smiled, he approached me and his armor became shattered. He became covered with drops of blood, because the earth was covered with rivers of blood from the slain warriors. He came with force, bypassing Arjuna who tried to prevent him, to kill me. May he be my refuge. Some say that because Bhīṣma and others acted as demons, Kṛṣṇa acted in this way. That is explained in the commentary on the following verse: O King! You should understand that the Supreme Lord’s appearance and disappearance, which resemble those of embodied conditioned souls, are actually a show enacted by his illusory energy, just like the performance of an actor. After creating this illusion he entered into it, played within it for some time, and at last destroyed it, withdrawing from it by his own power. Thus he remains. (SB [11.31.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/31.11)) Now that that absorption in anger had ended, Bhīṣma begged forgiveness.

### 1.9.39

**Перевод:**

**Desiring to die, may I have prema for the Lord who protected Arjuna’s chariot while holding a whip in his right hand, the reins in his left hand, whose beauty must be seen, and who bestowed liberation to those who died on the battle field after seeing him.**

**Комментарий:**

He prays for rati to Kṛṣṇa as the charioteer again. He protected (kuṭumbe) Arjuna’s chariot even at the cost of morality. He held a whip in his hand (ātta-totre). He held the reins of the horses. He was beautiful to see dressed as a charioteer by the characteristics described. May I have rati for that Kṛṣṇa, while desiring to die with these aspirations. There is a well-known saying ante yā matiḥ sā gatiḥ: whatever you think of at death, you attain that. May Kṛṣṇa of this nature remain before me. Proof is given using kaimutya. Those who having seen such Kṛṣṇa were killed in the war by Bhīma, Arjuna and others, attained their svarūpa manifesting eight qualities and the Lord’s svarūpa manifesting unlimited qualities like omniscience, etc. (svarūpa).

### 1.9.40

**Перевод:**

**The gopīs were worshipped by Kṛṣṇa’s expert actions, emotional displays, words and glances. Attaining those qualities, they responded in harmony to his most extraordinary displays of love, blinded by the madness of love. How amazing that these gopīs attained the nature of Kṛṣṇa himself.**

**Комментарий:**

Having prayed for rati to Kṛṣṇa as a charioteer, he now describes mādhurya pastimes dear to him, concerning the son of Nanda, which manifest to him by his omniscience given by that rati. His graceful movements (lalita-gati) were those in the rāsa dance. His vilāsa were special pastimes causing manifestation of qualities like dhīra-lalita, etc. He had an attractive (valgu) laugh suitable to the pastime. He had glances filled with prema (praṇaya-nirīkṣaṇa) which indicated his affection. The gopīs were greatly respected by these actions. Or they respected him by these actions. They were attracted to each other. They imitated his actions during the rāsa dance, etc., (kṛtam) since it is said that they danced with him. The gopīs were mad with prema (unmadāndhāḥ). They attained his nature. Agan instead of agaman is poetic licence. Prakṛti means nature according to Dhanañjaya. Or in separation they imitated his pastimes such as lifting Govardhana.

### 1.9.41

**Перевод:**

**The lord of my life, worthy of being seen, who received the worship of all the greatest sages and kings in the assembly during the rājasūya sacrifice of Yudhiṣṭhira, has become visible to my eyes.**

**Комментарий:**

Remembering the worshippable form, he prays for the form he should attain. Kṛṣṇa was seen with amazement by the sages exclaiming, “O what beauty! O what greatness!” In the midst of the gathering composed of the best sages and kings, during the rājasūya sacrifice of Yudhiṣṭhira, he received (upapede) worship. The antaryāmī of the universe (ātmā), Kṛṣṇa, full of knowledge and bliss, appears to my eyes. I have great fortune.

### 1.9.42

**Перевод:**

**Freed of the illusion of difference in the Lord’s various forms, I have attained the Lord who is one, though appearing to be many like the sun seen by many people, who is the charioteer, but who is also in my heart, the unborn and is situated in the hearts of all the jīvas, who create their own bodies.**

**Комментарий:**

You pray to me as the charioteer of Arjuna. Yogīs however meditate on the tattva measuring one pradeśa. Some yogīs meditate upon the Paramātmā measuring one pradeśa, who is residing in the heart within the body, and who holds the lotus, wheel, conch and club in his four hands. (SB [2.2.8](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-2/2.8)) What is the difference between these forms? That is described. I know (samadhigataḥ) the charioteer of Arjuna, who is also one pradeśa in size. He is that form by his svāṁśa. I have illusions of different forms dispelled (vidhūta-bheda-mohaḥ). Though one he manifests as many. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad [1.21](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/1.21)) The Lord is devoid of attaining a new body. He has an eternal form (ajam). He is situated (dhiṣṭhitam) as the regulator in the hearts of jīvas having bodies produced by themselves (ātma-kalpitam). Dhiṣṭhitam instead of adhiṣṭhitam is poetic license. Jīvas create their bodies. The śruti says, yathāgneḥ kṣudrā visphuliṅga vyuccaranti: the souls wander around like small sparks. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad [2.1.20](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/2.1.20)) sarva-bhūtāntarātmā: The Lord is within all beings. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad [6.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sveta-bs/6.11)) He is like the sun which appears to be many by each eye (prati- dṛṣam). In liberation, the difference between the Lord and the jīva is not denied. The pure jīva attains likeness to the Lord. (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad [3.1.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/mundaka/3.1.3))

### 1.9.43

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Thus Bhīṣma, absorbing himself in Kṛṣṇa, Bhagavān, the charioteer situated in his heart, using his mind, words and sight, stopped his breathing and ceased external functions.**

**Комментарий:**

He absorbed himself (ātmānam āveśya) in Kṛṣṇa, the charioteer, the Lord full of knowledge and bliss (ātmani) by his mental and other functions. Stopping his breath, he gave up his life. The prāṇas left since he was concentrating on the Lord. Being pure, he attained Kṛṣṇa in front of him. One should see the discussion between Ārtabhāga and Yājñavalkya and commentary on the following sūtra: This interpretation is correct because in one recension it is clearly stated that the prāṇas do not leave the jīva (who takes another body). (Brahma-sūtras [4.2.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/4.2.13)) The same explanation is given for Parīkṣit. Merging his life airs within, concentrating intently, situated in the spiritual realm, he placed Kṛṣṇa in his mind by his intelligence. (SB [12.6.9](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-12/6.9))

### 1.9.44

**Перевод:**

**The sages, who were like birds who become silent in the evening, understanding that Bhīṣma had attained Kṛṣṇa, became silent.**

**Комментарий:**

The sages and others, having heard Bhīṣma’s request, understanding that he had united with Kṛṣṇa (brahmaṇi), who wears a padakā ornament (niṣkale), became silent. Medinī says that niṣka means a chest ornament. Vayāṁsi means birds.

### 1.9.45

**Перевод:**

**Drums played by devatās and men sounded, the kings without hatred praised him, and flowers fell from the sky.**

**Комментарий:**

Sādhavaḥ rājñām means “those who were without hatred among the kings.” They spoke of his fame.

### 1.9.46

**Перевод:**

**O Śaunaka of the Bhṛgu dynasty! Yudhiṣṭhira had last rites of the departed Bhīṣma performed and became sad for a moment.**

**Комментарий:**

Having the rites performed for Bhīṣma’s body, which had given up life (sampretasya), Yudhiṣṭhira, taking shelter of normal custom, lamented. One should understand here that Bhīṣma attained Kṛṣṇa (sam – completely, para – Kṛṣṇa, itasya – attained). The Vasu who entered him, went to Svarga and at the end of his privilege attained liberation. yāvad adhikāram avasthitir ādhikārikāṇām: Those designated by the Lord remain in their posts on earth as long as that Lord chooses. (Vedānta-sūtra [3.3.33](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.3.33))

### 1.9.47

**Перевод:**

**The sages in joy praised Kṛṣṇa with his confidential names and then with Kṛṣṇa in their hearts departed again for their hermitages.**

**Комментарий:**

Tad-guhya-nāmabhiḥ indicates that they said, “O Kṛṣṇa, affectionate to the devotees, controlled by prema!”

### 1.9.48

**Перевод:**

**Then Yudhiṣṭhira went to Hastināpura with Kṛṣṇa and consoled Dhṛtarāṣṭra and Gāndhārī, who was overcome with grief.**

**Комментарий:**

Pitaram means Dhṛtarāṣṭra. Gāndhārī was overcome with grief because of loss of her sons (tapasvinīm).

### 1.9.49

**Перевод:**

**With the permission of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, and agreement of Kṛṣṇa, powerful Yudhiṣṭhira ruled the ancestral kingdom according to dharma.**

**Комментарий:**

Pitrā means “by Dhṛtarāṣṭra.”

## Chapter Ten - Kṛṣṇa goes to Dvārakā

### 1.10.1

**Перевод:**

**Śaunaka said: After killing the enemy desiring his rightful kingdom, how did Yudhiṣṭhira, the best of righteous men, while enjoying what he had won back, take up ruling the kingdom along with his younger brothers? What did he do?**

**Комментарий:**

The Tenth chapter describes how Kṛṣṇa after placing the Pāṇḍavas in the kingdom without obstacles went to Dvārakā and was constantly praised by the women. Not fully satisfied with the discussion of Kṛṣṇa and his devotees, Śaunaka inquires again. After killing Duryodhana and others because they stole their wealth (riktha), Yudhiṣṭhira enjoyed what was retrieved (pratyavaruddha) from the enemies.

### 1.10.2

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Kṛṣṇa, upon whom Śiva meditates, became pleased, having increased the Kuru family which was destroyed by the fire of anger in the family, by protecting Parīkṣit, and by installing Yudhiṣṭhira in his rightful kingdom.**

**Комментарий:**

The answer shows that he took up the ruling of the country only after seeing that Kṛṣṇa was pleased. Kṛṣṇa increased the Kuru family, which had been destroyed by the fire (of anger) in the forest of their family, by saving Parīkṣit. Just as the bamboo burns by the fire ignited by rubbing of the bamboos, the Kuru family was destroyed by a war which arose from mutual anger. Kṛṣṇa makes even Śiva (bhava) meditate on his pastimes (bhāvanaḥ). Hariḥ means he takes away the sorrow of his devotees.

### 1.10.3

**Перевод:**

**After hearing the words of Bhīṣma and Kṛṣṇa, and thereby gaining knowledge and removing ignorance, Yudhiṣṭhira, having taken shelter of Kṛṣṇa, ruled the earth extending to the oceans with the cooperation of his brothers, just as Indra, having taken shelter of Upendra, rules over heaven extending in all directions with the compliance of Upendra.**

**Комментарий:**

What did Yudhiṣṭhira do? The knowledge which appeared (pravṛtta-vijñāna) was that the world is dependent on the Lord, and is not independent. He was free of the illusion that “I am the independent doer (vidhūta-vibhramaḥ).” Gām means earth and heaven. Ajita refers to Kṛṣṇa. Paridhi means ocean and also all directions upwards as well (in the case of Indra). Anujānuvartitaḥ means with the compliance of his younger brothers, or with the compliance of his younger brother Upendra (in the case of Indra).

### 1.10.4

**Перевод:**

**The clouds rained sufficient water, the earth yielded all desired products and the cows with full udders in joy sprinkled milk in the cow pens.**

**Комментарий:**

The prosperity of the kingdom is described. Udhasvatī means full udders.

### 1.10.5

**Перевод:**

**The rivers, oceans, mountains, trees, shrubs, and herbs of that kingdom yielded products for the king in every season.**

**Комментарий:**

Anvṛtu means all seasons. Tasya means Yudhiṣṭhira’s.

### 1.10.6

**Перевод:**

**While Yudhiṣṭhira ruled, the living beings suffered no anxieties, sickness or inconveniences caused by nature, by other living beings or by themselves.**

**Комментарий:**

Ādhayaḥ means mental pain. Vyādhayaḥ means sickness such as fever. Kleśāḥ means lust, anger, greed, illusion, etc. Their causes are nature, other beings or the self. Daiva (devatās) causes are drought, etc. Causes from other beings are madness, etc. Causes from the self are leprosy, etc.

### 1.10.7-8

**Перевод:**

**Staying for several months in Hastināpura to console his friends and please Subhadrā, Kṛṣṇa, after taking permission to leave, addressing and embracing Yudhiṣṭhira, bid farewell and mounted his chariot, while being addressed and embraced by others.**

**Комментарий:**

These verses introduce his departure for Dvārakā. Svasur-bhaginyāḥ means Subhadrā. Taking permission from Yudhiṣṭhira, being offered respects by Kuntī, being embraced and with their consent (abhyanujñātaḥ), Kṛṣṇa mounted his chariot.

### 1.10.9-10

**Перевод:**

**Subhadrā, Draupadī, Kuntī, Uttarā, Gāndhārī, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Yuyutsu, Kṛpācārya, Nakula, Sahadeva, Bhīma, Dhaumya, Satyavatī and other women, falling into confusion, could not bear separation from Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Yuyutsu was Dhṛtarāṣṭra’s son by a vaiśya wife. He was a follower of the Pāṇḍavas and a great warrior. Gautama is Kṛpa. Matsya-sutā refers to Satyavatī.

### 1.10.11-12

**Перевод:**

**The intelligent person, freed of bad association by good association, cannot give up the pleasing qualities of Kṛṣṇa which have been recited, even if he hears them once. How could the Pāṇḍavas, who were absorbed in him while eating, sitting, sleeping, talking, touching and seeing, tolerate separation from him?**

**Комментарий:**

The two verses are connected. How could the Pāṇḍavas tolerate separation from the Lord, whose glories the intelligent person cannot give up? Rocanam means pleasing. The intelligent person is described as he, who has given up bad association by taking good association. Without good association, bad association which causes pride, hatred and other bad qualities will not disappear. Without the disappearance of bad qualities, the pleasing qualities of the Lord, difficult to give up, would not manifest. This is implied. The Pāṇḍavas placed their intelligence in Kṛṣṇa by seeing him, etc.

### 1.10.13

**Перевод:**

**Looking upon him with unblinking eyes, their hearts melted, they became filled with affection, and moved wherever he moved.**

**Комментарий:**

They looked at him with unblinking eyes. After looking (anu), their hearts melted. Then they followed him wherever he went.

### 1.10.14

**Перевод:**

**When Kṛṣṇa left the house, all the wives of his friends blocked the tears flowing from their eyes because of their attachment to him, thinking, “There should be no inauspiciousness for him.”**

**Комментарий:**

When he left the house, because of attachment to him, they stopped their flowing tears, because they thought there should be nothing inauspicious for him. With great effort they stopped the tears, but the tears still flowed. They hid those tears with the edges of their garments.

### 1.10.15

**Перевод:**

**Mṛdaṅgas, conches, bheris, vīṇas, paṇavas, gomukhas, dhundhuris, ānakas, bells and dundubhis began to sound.**

**Комментарий:**

These are various instruments.

### 1.10.16

**Перевод:**

**The Kuru women, climbing on the roofs of the palace to see, glancing at him to convey smiles, shyness and love, showered Kṛṣṇa with flowers.**

**Комментарий:**

Kusumaiḥ should actually be in the accusative case kusumāni. They conveyed smiles, shyness and love through their glances.

### 1.10.17

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna, conqueror of sleep and dear to the Lord, held a white umbrella decorated with pearls and a jewel-studded handle for his dearest friend.**

**Комментарий:**

Guḍākeśaḥ means Arjuna, who had conquered sleep. He held an umbrella. He was dear (priyaḥ) to Kṛṣṇa (priyatamasya).

### 1.10.18

**Перевод:**

**Uddhava and Sātyaki held astonishing fans. The lord of the Madhus appeared splendid on the road when sprinkled with flowers.**

**Комментарий:**

Madhupatiḥ means Mādhava.

### 1.10.19

**Перевод:**

**On the road, he heard the factual blessings uttered by brāhmaṇas, which were not appropriate for the Lord who is beyond all qualities of this world, but which were appropriate for the Lord who has spiritual qualities relished by his devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

On the various roads (pathi pathi) he heard blessings. The statements were factual (satyā) since all qualities, which were the subject of the blessings, exist in him always. But the blessings were both inappropriate (nānurūpāḥ) and appropriate (anurūpāḥ). The sandhi is poetic license. Blessings such as “Be happy!” are inappropriate for one who is not touched by the guṇas of māyā (nirguṇasya) and fixed in spiritual bliss. The same blessings are appropriate for a jīva like a king who experiences happiness and suffering and is conditioned by māyā. He was a mine of qualities (guṇātmanaḥ) connected to himself such as being devoted to brāhmaṇas, serving the elders, and being controlled by prema. The words were appropriate because the Lord accepted the blessings.

### 1.10.20

**Перевод:**

**The Kuru women, fully absorbed in Kṛṣṇa, conversed about him with words attractive to all ears and minds.**

**Комментарий:**

Their conversation was attractive to all ears (sarva-śruti-manoharaḥ). Another meaning is “their conversation was attractive to all the Upaniṣad scriptures personified. The śrutis were happy with the conversation.” Or the words stole the minds and ears of everyone.

### 1.10.21

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa is certainly that ancient puruṣa who alone existed without expansions before the agitation of the guṇas and during devastation, when all the jīvas along with their identities were merged within him, the soul of prakṛti.**

**Комментарий:**

Vai means for certain. Kila means it is well known. That puruṣa is this Kṛṣṇa, son of Devakī. He alone existed independently in his svarūpa (ātmani) when the jīvas (ātmani) situated in the universe during pralaya (niśi) previous (agre) to manifestation of mahattattva, etc., (guṇebhyaḥ) were merged in the Lord’s svāṁśa (īśvare), antaryāmī of prakṛti. We have heard this from Vyāsa and others. “What type of person was he at that time?” The Lord was most excellent, beyond whom there is nothing excellent (aviśeṣaḥ). Or at this time, as at that time, he performs pastimes with his associates. Jagad-ātmani is in singular form because it represents a class. It means all the jīvas of the universes. “Since the jīvas are eternal how can they be merged or destroyed?” Their bodies are upādhis. The bodies are destroyed. The jīva being destroyed is figurative. They sleep in prakṛti (suptāḥ śaktiṣu).

### 1.10.22

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, after manifesting the scriptures, and desiring to make names and forms for the jīvas, then followed prakṛti, who desired to create the universe, but who moves only by his power, and by his will alone bewilders the jīvas.**

**Комментарий:**

The son of Devakī is the creator of the universe. Kṛṣṇa, who existed previous to creation without difference in himself, followed prakṛti through his svāṁśa Saṅkarṣaṇa. Prakṛti is described. She is instigated by his glance (nija-vīrya-coditām). Sa aikṣata lokān nu sṛjā: he glanced and created the worlds. She bewilders the jīvas who arise from his śakti (sva-jīva-māyām). He followed her while being independent of her. She creates the mahat-tattva, etc., after being empowered by his glance (sisṛkṣatīm). Why does he follow her? He desires to make names and forms of devatās and humans, etc., for the jīvas devoid of names and forms. By this, the jīvas hankering for enjoyment fulfil their enjoyment by getting the bodies. What was the Lord’s nature in following her? Before glancing at prakṛti the Lord manifested the Vedas which reveal karma-yoga and bhakti tattva as his breathing (śāstra-kṛti).

### 1.10.23

**Перевод:**

**He is that person whose form, the sages who have controlled their senses by controlling their life air see with intelligence purified by intense bhakti. Certainly he can purify the intelligence completely.**

**Комментарий:**

One should not think that he appeared only at the end of Dvāparayuga and that he did not exist previously. He gave power of creation to Brahmā at the end of the parārdha. He who previously instructed Brahmā has now appeared here. In this world (atra) the Kumāras and others (sūrayaḥ), controlling their senses after controlling their prāṇas, see his feet by pure intelligence (ātmanā) by intense bhakti. One attains the lord by faith, bhakti and meditation. (Gopāla -tāpanī Upaniṣad) Pure intelligence is caused by his mercy, not by just yoga. That is certain (nanu). He purifies the heart (sattvam). The Lord himself says: The person who, after restraining all the senses, becomes devoted to me, will maintain steady realization of ātmā. He whose senses have thus been brought under control is sthita-prajña. (BG [2.61](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/2.61))

### 1.10.24

**Перевод:**

**O friend! He is Kṛṣṇa with human form, friend of Arjuna, who is the subject of pure topics sung by some who discern the secrets in the confidential scriptures, who, though being the one Lord, creates, maintains and destroys without being attached, as the soul of the universe.**

**Комментарий:**

“All do not speak of him in this way.” O friend! His topics are constantly (anu) sung by Durvāsa and others speaking secretly (guha-vādibhiḥ) in Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad, etc. (guhyeṣu vedeṣu). The glorification is described. He is one(ekaḥ) – unaided by others. I offer respects to Govinda the form of the universe, the cause of maintenance and destruction of the universe, the Lord of the universe, non-different from the universe. (Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad [1.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/gopala-tapani/1.35))

### 1.10.25

**Перевод:**

**When kings whose minds are affected by ignorance live by irreligion, then Kṛṣṇa, accepting forms for the welfare of the world in Vraja, Mathurā and Dvārakā, in every day of Brahmā, displays his six powers, truth, pleasing words, mercy and good qualities endowed with pure sattva.**

**Комментарий:**

The cause of Kṛṣṇa appearing as many avatāras is explained. When kings of demonic nature (tamo dhiyaḥ) maintain (jīvanti) their lives by adharma, then Kṛṣṇa, increasing sattva-guṇa (sattvataḥ) in the world, manifests forms such as Varāha for prosperity (bhavāya) of the universe and manifests his six opulences (bhagam), filled with (ṛtam) truthful words (satyam), mercy and fame.

### 1.10.26

**Перевод:**

**Oh! Most praiseworthy is the family of Yadu! Most purifying is Mathurā-maṇḍala, which Kṛṣṇa, the best of men, the Lord of auspiciousness, respects by taking birth there, moving about and performing pastimes.**

**Комментарий:**

When the Lord appears in person then there is an increase in great bliss in the world. Mador vaṇaṁ is Mathurā-maṇḍala. Here, while the word ślāghyatamam indicates perfection of the superiority of both Mathurā and Yadus, puṇyatamaṁ is mentioned separately due to Mahurā’s fame as most purifying. The words alam and aho indicate unlimited praise. The reason is given. The son of Devakī, an ocean of beauty, having unlimited qualities (eṣaḥ), worshipped (añcati) the place by walking there, and worshipped the Yadus by his pastimes with them. He is the husband of Rukmiṇī and lover of Rādhā (śriyaḥ patiḥ). Both Vasudeva and his followers and Nanda and his followers, arising in the Yadu dynasty, are situated in Mathurā-maṇḍala according to Gopāla-tāpanī Upaniṣad. Añcati is in the present tense indicating that. Even now he is present there. This is taught by Dvaipāyana Vyāsa: Kṛṣṇa is the one Lord, engaged in eternal pastimes. He is present in his devotees, within their hearts. (Puruṣa-bodhinī Upaniṣad)

### 1.10.27

**Перевод:**

**Oh! Dvārakā derides the fame of Svarga. It gives fame to purity on this earth because the inhabitants constantly see Kṛṣṇa with smiling face, who was sent here by mercy!**

**Комментарий:**

Having glorified Mathurā-maṇḍala, they now praise Dvārakā. O friend! Bata indicates astonishment. Dvārakā (kuśasthalī) derides the fame of Svarga and planets up to Satyaloka (svar-yaśasaḥ) since the devatās in charge of those places take shelter of Dvarākā for perfecting their work. It makes earth most excellent, surpassing Svarga, by manifesting on earth the many pastimes of the Lord which are most purifying. That is Dvārakā’s inconceivable potency. Its inhabitants are praised. The citizens see their master endowed with a smiling glance. His mercy is sought (anugraheṣitam). The Supreme Lord is always pleased with these inhabitants. This is not the situation in Svarga.

### 1.10.28

**Перевод:**

**O friend! The wives of Kṛṣṇa who drink the nectar of his lips constantly must have certainly worshipped him by austerities, bathing in sacred places and sacrifices in previous lives to attain their present status. But the women of Vraja whose minds were completely absorbed in that nectar, fainted in bliss simply because of remembering it.**

**Комментарий:**

Young women companions of Draupadī and others speak. O friend! Kṛṣṇa must have been worshiped by his wives in previous births because of which (hi) they always drink the nectar of his lips. The women of Vraja whose hearts were fixed on that nectar, fainted, on remembering in the morning, the drinking of that nectar at night. They had more prema. We would be successful just by drinking the nectar of his beauty.

### 1.10.29-30

**Перевод:**

**Rukmiṇī, Jāmbavatī, Nāgnajitī and others, who were taken away at the price of valor after Kṛṣṇa defeated strong kings headed by Śiśupāla and others in the thousands, who were accepted by him on killing Narakāsura, and who had been impure and had lost all auspiciousness, then became the best of women, because lotuseyed Kṛṣṇa, increasing the bliss in their hearts by bringing things to them, never left their houses.**

**Комментарий:**

Two verses elaborate on what has been said. Vīrya-śulkena means “by the price of valor.” The queens were brought by Kṛṣṇa by the price of valor. Śuṣminaḥ means “very strong.” Kṛṣṇa defeated strong kings headed by Śiṣupāla. Those who had Pradyumna, Sāmba and Āmba as sons refers to Rukmiṇī, Jāmbavatī and Nāgnajitī. “Others” includes Satyabhāmā. Thousands of others were taken on his killing Bhauma. He made their material (param) womanhood glorious (sādhu), since they had lost cleverness and independence (apāsta-peśalam) and purity (nirasta-śaucam). Peśala means cleverness according to Medinī. The cause of their becoming glorious is explained. He never left their houses. The extremely pure Lord was controlled by persons like us. He manifested prema (spṛśan) in their hearts by bringing various dear gifts like the Pārijāta tree. It is not that all women are impure, since women like Gaṅgā are purifying. This should be understood here: the supreme tattva endowed with the svarūpa-śakti called parā is Bhagavān and Lakṣmī. Appearing in the human world with body of a man and woman, they give good fortune to humans. But man and woman fixed in the supreme tattva are not equal to the Lord and Lakṣmī, since the Lord and Lakṁsī are the pinnacle of purity.

### 1.10.31

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Acknowledging the words of the women of Hastināpura who had spoken in this way, with smiles and glances, Kṛṣṇa departed.**

**Комментарий:**

By his glances he acknowledged the words of the city women who had knowledge of his svarūpa, and by his smiles he pleased those who had knowledge of his pastimes.

### 1.10.32

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira, worried about enemies, out of affection engaged an army of four parts for the protection of Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Yudhiṣṭhira, for protecting Kṛṣṇa, arranged out of affection an army of four parts: elephants, horses, chariots and foot soldiers. Affection was his nature. And though devoid of fear, he developed fear.

### 1.10.33

**Перевод:**

**Leaving the Pāṇḍavas who were most affectionate, who were afflicted by separation, and who had accompanied him for a long way, Kṛṣṇa went to Dvārakā with his dear followers.**

**Комментарий:**

Kauravān means the Pāṇḍavas. Priyaiḥ means Uddhava and others.

### 1.10.34-35

**Перевод:**

**O son of Bhṛgu! Passing through the provinces of Kuru-jāṅgala, Pāñcāla, and Śūrasena on the banks of the Yamunā River, Brahmāvarta, Kurukṣetra, Matsya, Sārasvata, and the deserts and arid regions, and then Sauvīra and Ābhīra, the Lord arrived at Ānarta with slightly tired horses.**

**Комментарий:**

The places are not listed in proper sequence. Maru is a place without water and dhanva is a place with a little water. He arrived at Ānarta, the area of Dvārakā with slightly tired horses (manāk śrānta vāhaḥ). O Śaunaka (bhārgava)!

### 1.10.36

**Перевод:**

**At all the places along the way, devotees offered gifts to the Lord. He arrived at Dvārakā in the afternoon. Then the sun entered the western ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

“When the Lord went to Dvārakā from Hastināpura, did the people of those places not greet him?” He was offered (pratyudyata) gifts (arhanaḥ) on the path by the devotees of those places in order to bring him to their places. He went to those places and stayed there a few days to fulfill his devotees’ desires. Then he resumed his journey on the road. He arrived at Dvārakā (paścād diśam) in the afternoon (sāyam). At that time the sun (gaviṣṭhaḥ) had entered the water (gām) of the western ocean. The word go (gām in accusative) means water and Svarga.

## Chapter Eleven - Kṛṣṇa arrives at Dvārakā

### 1.11.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Arriving at his own prosperous city of Dvārakā, Kṛṣṇa, somewhat allaying the suffering of the inhabitants, blew the best of conch shells.**

**Комментарий:**

In the eleventh chapter, after being praised by the people of Ānarta, Kṛṣṇa, with his relatives, arrives at Dvārakā, and attracted to his wives, is praised. Daravaram is his conch Pāñcajanya.

### 1.11.2

**Перевод:**

**The white-bellied conch, continually blown in his lotus hands, like a bellowing swan amidst a lotus grove, reddened by the touch of Kṛṣṇa’s lips, appeared most attractive.**

**Комментарий:**

That conch appeared beautiful (uccakāśe), being reddened by the redness of his lips. He continually blew it (dādhmāyamānaḥ). The conch held in his hands was like a swan in a group of lotuses (abjaṣaṇḍe) since he held it with four hands. This is guṇālaṅkāra. The color of the conch became pink because of his lips and palms of his hands. Tad guṇaḥ sva guṇa-tyāgād anyadīya-guṇa-grahaḥ: this ornament occurs when an object gives up its own quality and accepts some other quality.

### 1.11.3

**Перевод:**

**Hearing that sound of the conch, which gives fear to the dangers of material existence, all the citizens, desiring to see their master, went out to meet him.**

**Комментарий:**

The conch gives fear to the fear of this material existence.

### 1.11.4-5

**Перевод:**

**Offering him gifts like offering a lamp to the sun, faces blossoming in affection, they began to speak with words choked up with joy to Kṛṣṇa, who is always satisfied with his own qualities, who is satisfied with himself, and who is always their friend and protector, like a father protecting his children.**

**Комментарий:**

To Kṛṣṇa, their master (tatra), the women offered gifts (upanītāḥ balayaḥ), which were given out of adoration, though he did not need them, just like a sun worshipper offers a lamp to the sun. Ātmārāmam (self-satisfied) means he experienced his svarūpa full of knowledge and bliss. He was fully satisfied (pūrṇa-kāmam) with attainment of his superior śakti (nija-lābhena). They spoke to him, their protector (avitāram) like children to a father.

### 1.11.6

**Перевод:**

**O master! We continually offer respects to your lotus feet, the supreme shelter for those desiring the highest benefit, which are worshipped by Brahmā, the Kumāras, Indra, which cannot be influenced by time, though time controls even Brahmā.**

**Комментарий:**

Vairiñcyāh means the Kumāras. Param parāyaṇam means the supreme shelter. In the shelter of these lotus feet, time, the master of Brahmā and others (paraḥ prabhuḥ), do not have power.

### 1.11.7

**Перевод:**

**Protector of the universe! Work for our best interests! You alone are our mother, father, friend and master. You are our guru and supreme deity, by following whom we have become successful.**

**Комментарий:**

Bhavāya means “for welfare.” Exist for our welfare. Medinī says bhavaḥ kṣeme saṁsāre: bhava means welfare and material existence. Following you, we have become successful.

### 1.11.8

**Перевод:**

**In you, we have found our master. We have become successful, because we can see your form endowed with all good qualities, with a face smiling in love and glancing in affection, which is difficult to see for the devatās.**

**Комментарий:**

Traiviṣṭapānām means “of the devatās.”

### 1.11.9

**Перевод:**

**O lotus-eyed Lord! When you go to Hastināpura or Vraja to see your friends, one moment becomes like a trillion years for us, who belong to you, and who become like eyes without the sun.**

**Комментарий:**

O lotus-eyed Lord! When (yarhi) you give us up and go to Hastināpura (kurūn) or Mathura-maṇḍala (madhūm), desiring to see the Pāṇḍavas and Nanda, a moment becomes for us like a million years. We become blind, like eyes without the sun. Kurūn means Hastināpura and madhūn means Vraja, not Mathurā, because at that time none of his friends resided there. This is understood from the word “all” in the statement tatra yogaprabhāvena nītvā sarva-janaṁ hariḥ: the Lord by his power of yoga brought all the inhabitants of Mathurā to Dvārakā. (SB 10.50.57) He sent messengers back to the gopīs when he was leaving, saying, “I will come back.” (SB 10.39.35) And he sent Nanda back to Vraja saying, “I will come to see you.” (SB 10.45.23) He was obliged to fulfil those promises. It is also clearly mentioned in the Uttarakhaṇḍa of Padma Purāṇa, that he did return to fulfil his promise. Balarāma also went there. (Thus it is concluded that Kṛṣṇa returned to Vraja. This confirms what was stated above.)

### 1.11.10

**Перевод:**

**After hearing these words from the citizens, Kṛṣṇa, affectionate to his devotees, spreading mercy by his glance, entered the city.**

**Комментарий:**

He glanced mercifully at the citizens.

### 1.11.11

**Перевод:**

**He entered the city, protected by the Madhus, Bhojas, Daśārhas, Arhas, Kukuras, Andhakas and Vṛṣṇis, similar in strength to himself. The city appeared like the city of Bhogavatī protected by the Nāgas.**

**Комментарий:**

Dvārakā is described in five verses (12-16). They had strength equal to his because they were eternal associates. All the Yadavas are my associates. They are dear to me and possess qualities equal to mine. (Padma Purāṇa, Pātālakhaṇḍa, 89.22)

### 1.11.12

**Перевод:**

**The city appeared splendid with lakes surrounded by pleasure gardens and plantations, with groves of pious trees and creepers giving fruit and flowers of all seasons.**

**Комментарий:**

The city appeared splendid with lakes filled with lotuses (padmākara) surrounded by forests in which there were recreation areas, creepers and auspicious trees with a wealth (vibhava) of flowers and fruit of all seasons. The gardens (udyāna) had mainly fruit and the groves (upavana) had mainly flowers. The recreational areas (ārāma) were for pastimes.

### 1.11.13

**Перевод:**

**Festoons made with joy had been placed over the gates and doors. The multitude of colorful flags and banners with insignias and with auspicious patterns prevented the heat from entering.**

**Комментарий:**

Gopuras are city gates. Dvāras are doors to houses. The city had attractive festoons made of pearls and decorative arches (toraṇa) made with joy. The heat of the sun was stopped from entering within by the colorful flags. The flags (dvaja) were marked with Garuḍa and other symbols. Patākas indicted victory.

### 1.11.14

**Перевод:**

**The major roads, lanes, market roads and footpaths were sprinkled with scented water and spread with flowers, fruit, rice and sprouts.**

**Комментарий:**

Mahā-mārga means main road. Rathya are other roads. Āpaṇaka means shopping street. Catvara is a footpath. They were cleansed with water and scattered (uptām) with fruits, etc.

### 1.11.15

**Перевод:**

**The city was adorned with full water pots, incense, lamps, offerings, yogurt, rice, fruits and sugar cane stalks at the door of every house.**

**Комментарий:**

Balibhiḥ means “with offerings.”

### 1.11.16-19

**Перевод:**

**Hearing that their dear Lord was coming, liberal Vasudeva, Akrūra, Ugrasena, Balarāma of astonishing prowess, Pradyumna, Cārudeṣṇa, Sāmba, the son of Jāmbavatī, overlooking eating, sitting, and sleeping out of great bliss, overjoyed and enthusiastic, excited from love, putting the best elephants in front, went out with brāhmaṇas decorated auspiciously, accompanied by chanting of Vedic mantras and the sounds of conches and tūryas. Hundreds of beautiful dancing girls, eager to see the Lord, with glittering earrings lighting up their cheeks, also came on vehicles.**

**Комментарий:**

Hearing that Kṛṣṇa, dear to themselves (preṣṭham) had come, Vasudeva and others went. Out of joy they overlooked (ucchaśita) eating, etc. The verb śaś means to jump up. Vāraṇendraṁ means king of elephants. Sasumaṅgalaiḥ means holding auspicious items in their hands. Brahma-ghoṣeṇa means “with chanting of Vedic mantras.” Sādhvasam means zeal. Dancing girls (vāra-mukhyā) approached (pratyujjagmuḥ).

### 1.11.20

**Перевод:**

**The actors, dancers, singers, reciters, bards and eulogists sang about the astonishing activities of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Naṭas (actors) are those who are skillful at portraying rasa. Nartakas (dancers) are those who dance to the rhythms of the songs. Gandharvas are singers. Sūtas and others are described as follows: Sūtas recite Purāṇas. Māgadhas praise the lineage of families. Vandis of pure intelligence sing praises.

### 1.11.21

**Перевод:**

**The Lord met them all and suitably respected all friends, relatives, and citizens who had come.**

**Комментарий:**

Yathāvidhi means “as suitable.” Meeting with those who came (upasaṅgamya), he respected them (mānam).

### 1.11.22

**Перевод:**

**Removing all fear from fathers and gurus by bowing his head, greeting the elders of the Yadus with words, others with embraces, the touch of his hands, smiles and glances, he respected all, down to the outcastes with desirable gifts.**

**Комментарий:**

He greeted Vasudeva, Garga and others by bowing his head (prahvā). He greeted elders of the Yadu clan with words (abhivādana). He greeted equals with embraces and holding hands. To others he gave glances. Down to the outcastes (āśvapākebhyaḥ), he comforted all, giving them fearlessness. Varaiḥ means “by desirable gifts.”

### 1.11.23

**Перевод:**

**Receiving blessings from fathers and teachers, brāhmaṇas, elders with their wives, eulogists and others, he entered the city.**

**Комментарий:**

He was blessed by the elders and entered the city with praisers.

### 1.11.24-25

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇas! When Kṛṣṇa arrived at the main road, the women of Dvārakā, overjoyed on seeing him, climbed to the top of the palaces, because the eyes of the inhabitants of Dvārakā could not be satisfied even with constantly gazing upon Acyuta, whose limbs were the abode of beauty.**

**Комментарий:**

Viprāḥ means “O brāhmaṇas!” They went to the tops of the palaces because (yat) their eyes, even gazing at him constantly, were not satisfied. His limbs were the abode of beauty.

### 1.11.26

**Перевод:**

**His chest is the shelter of Lakṣmī, his face, the drinking vessel, is the shelter for the eyes, his arms are the shelter of the protectors of the world, and his lotus feet are the shelter of the bee-like devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

His chest is the shelter of Lakṣmī. His face, a cup full of sweet beauty, is the shelter for the eyes. His arms are the shelter for protectors of the worlds such as Indra. Taking shelter of their strength, the devatās live without fear. His feet are the shelter of those who sing (ga) the glories of the Lord (sāram), the devotees. This verse is connected to the previous verse describing Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.11.27

**Перевод:**

**On the road, furnished with umbrella and cāmaras, showered with flowers, wearing yellow cloth with a garland, he appeared to be a cloud with the sun, moon, rainbow and lightning.**

**Комментарий:**

He was decorated (upaskṛtaḥ) with white umbrella and cāmaras. Showered with flowers, Kṛṣṇa shone. He was like a cloud with the sun, moon, stars (uḍupa), rainbow (cāpa), and lightning. He was like a cloud. Above was the sun (umbrella). The cāmaras waved at his sides were like two moons with the stars (flowers). There were two rainbows in the form of a fixed garland along with immobile flashing lightning of his yellow cloth. In this way Kṛṣṇa appeared glorious. This is an example adbhūtopameya, astonishing comparison, since impossible elements become possible in this case. Some call this bimbānubimbopama where Kṛṣṇa is the ideal, to which objects are compared.

### 1.11.28

**Перевод:**

**Entering the house of his parents, embraced by seven mothers, headed by Devakī, he offered respects with his head to them.**

**Комментарий:**

Seven is only an indication. Vasudeva actually had eighteen wives and Kṛṣṇa offered respects to all of them with his head as they were equal to Devakī. Seven are especially respected due to being the sisters of Devakī.

### 1.11.29

**Перевод:**

**Hearts trembling with joy, milk flowing from their breasts, they put Kṛṣṇa on their laps, and moistened him with their tears.**

### 1.11.30

**Перевод:**

**He then entered his own incomparable quarters which had all enjoyments, where there were sixteen thousand one hundred and eight palaces for his queens.**

**Комментарий:**

Sva-bhavanam means his own excellent (anuttamam) quarters, which had all enjoyments (sarva-kāmam). Ca with sixteen thousand indicates an additional one hundred and eight.

### 1.11.31

**Перевод:**

**Seeing from a distance their husband arrive after absence, with great desire in their minds, with bent heads and shy glances, they quickly rose from their seats and their minds, along with their penances.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa entered each of the palaces of the queens simultaneously. Their mind became filled with great joy (sañjāta-mano-mahotsavāḥ). They desired to embrace him. Immediately and at distance (ārāt) they rose from their seats (āsanā) and from their hearts (āśayāt). Then they glanced at him from the sides of their eyes with bent heads and bashful faces (vrīḍita-locanānanāḥ). This means that they first gave up their seats to embrace their Lord, using the body. They then noticed an obstacle in the form of their bashfulness. They therefore gave up their minds, which were the dwelling place of that bashfulness, and embraced him with their souls. They rose along with their vratas. Yājñavalkya speaks of vratas: In absence from her husband the wife should give up recreation, decorating the body, seeing festivals in society, laughing and going to other houses. Though it was improper for them to be seen by their husband in a state of vrata, because they could not suddenly give up those vratas, they rose along with their vratas. Seeing them in an unkempt state because of separation, he became more affectionate to them.

### 1.11.32

**Перевод:**

**The queens first embraced the Lord with their eyes, full of desire, and then they embraced with their subtle bodies so that no one could see. O chief amongst the Bhṛgus, though they tried to restrain their tears because of embarrassment, they inadvertently shed some tears.**

**Комментарий:**

Giving up their hearts, they fainted. Regaining their hearts, they embraced their husband. Amara-kośa says makaradhvaja ātmabhūḥ: ātma-bhūḥ means Cupid. They saw him with intense desire stimulated by seeing him. First, they embraced him with their eyes filled with desire and then with their minds (antarātmanā) i.e., they enjoyed him with their eyes and their minds. Understanding that their husband understood their hidden (durantabhāva) intentions, they became embarrassed and closed their eyes, but tears slightly flowed beyond their control. O best of the Bhṛgu dynasty!

### 1.11.33

**Перевод:**

**Though Kṛṣṇa’s feet were next to them and in private, those feet appeared more and more beautiful at every moment. Which woman could give up those feet which Lakṣmī never gives up?**

**Комментарий:**

“When scenes like this were enacted many times, there should not be such thirst.” The pastimes were ever fresh. At every moment (pade pade) the feet appeared newer and newer to them. Kaimutya is used. If Lakṣmī, the embodiment of all wealth, finds those feet ever fresh, then what to speak of anyone else being able to give them up.

### 1.11.34

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, without weapons, by creating enmity between kings Text 35\] Kṛṣṇa arrives at Dvārakā 263 powerful with armies, who had created a burden on the earth, destroyed them by having them kill each other, just as the wind, by creating friction between bamboos, destroys them by fire.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to express the unimpeded nature of his love for the queens another type of activity lacking that excitement is described. Just as wind (śvasanaḥ) produces fire by rubbing the bamboos together and then destroys them by burning, the Lord, without weapons, by creating enmity between kings, who were powerful with their vast armies and who created a burden for the earth, destroyed them (uparataḥ) by having them kill each other.

### 1.11.35

**Перевод:**

**Having appeared on this earth the Lord enjoyed among the best of women by expanding himself through his yoga-māyā, according to his nature.**

**Комментарий:**

By his superior (parā) śakti (sva-māyayā), emanating from himself, situated simultaneously among his many wives, he enjoyed as if materially (yathā prākṛtaḥ). His enjoyment is beyond prakṛti. He is attached to it. The word ratna (jewel of women) indicates that they were Lakṣmī?s of the supreme avatāra.

### 1.11.36

*udbhaṭa-bhāva-piśunāmala-valgu-hāsa- vrīḍāvaloka-nihato madano ’pi yāsām \| sammuhya cāpam ajahāt pramadottamās tā yasyendriyaṁ vimathituṁ kuhakair na śekuḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Although the queens’ beautiful smiles and furtive glances were all spotless and exciting, and signified deep love, and although they could conquer Cupid himself by making him give up his bow in frustration, those who approached him with false smiles and glances could not agitate the senses of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The matter is clarified in this verse. Cupid’s weapons became useless by their smiles and bashful glances attractive and pure, which indicated their strong (udbhaṭa) love, when he saw their greatness. Bewildered by those glances, Cupid gave up his bow. As his lovers (pramada), these women were excellent (uttamāḥ) with prema. Still they could not disturb Kṛṣṇa’s senses in a special manner (vimathitum) by glances endowed with slight deception, with a type of prema having a desire to be equal to his best beloved (kuhakaiḥ). They could disturb him with actions based on their natural prema. It was previously said that he was absorbed in those wives: Having appeared on this earth the Lord enjoyed among the best of women by expanding himself through his yogamāyā, according to his nature. (SB [1.11.35](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/11.35))

### 1.11.37

**Перевод:**

**Ignorant people think of the Lord, though uncontaminated by matter, as a human being, one of themselves, contaminated by matter because of seeing unappealing behavior such as his compliance with Satyabhāmā’s attachment to getting the pārijāta tree.**

**Комментарий:**

Ordinary people ignorant of his qualities and svarūpa, think that Kṛṣṇa who is uncontaminated with matter (asaṅgam), who is filled with pure prema, to be material (saṅginam). They think he is a human like they are. The cause is explained. They think he is materially engaged in many acts like taking the pārijāta for Satyabhāmā. Thus they are ignorant of his real nature. The fool (abudhaḥ) devoid of discrimination of true and false thinks that prema is material attachment, just as a fool thinks that a sapphire is glass.

### 1.11.38

**Перевод:**

**This is the power of the Lord: though he is situated in prakṛti, he is not affected by the guṇas which are situated in materialists, just as the intelligence of the devotee remembering the Lord is not affected by the guṇas.**

**Комментарий:**

“It is impossible for the Lord to not be contaminated with matter. Since he is situated in the world of matter, he must associate with matter.” Though he is situated as the antaryāmī of the world, a transformation of prakṛti, he is not affected by the guṇas since that is the power (īśanam) of the Lord. The guṇas are described. They are situated in persons who are opposed to the Lord (asadātma-sthaiḥ). An example is given with kaimutya. The intelligence of the devotees (tad-āśrayā) whose subject is the Lord, is not affected by matter.

### 1.11.39

**Перевод:**

**Women, not capable of estimating the powers of Kṛṣṇa, considered him to be under the control of women following them to solitary places, just as foolish intelligence cannot know the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The ordinary men who think of the Lord as material were described. Now the thoughts of ordinary women are described. Women devoid of knowing the Lord’s svarūpa thought of the Lord as a person controlled by women, who followed them to a lonely place. They cannot know the extent of powers of the master of the universe (bhartuḥ), just as fish in the ocean do not know the extent of the ocean and just as the intelligence of Vaiśeṣikas calculate the Lord who has unlimited qualities to be limited in qualities.

## Chapter Twelve - Birth of Parīkṣit

### 1.12.1

**Перевод:**

**Śaunaka said: The embryo killed by the intense heat of the brahmāstra thrown by Aśvatthāmā was revived by the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The twelfth chapter describes how the King celebrated the birth of Parīkṣit, and hearing his future life from the brāhmaṇas, rejoiced. Sūta had promised to tell about Parīkṣit’s birth (SB [1.7.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/7.22)), but was sidetracked in reciting the sweet topics of how Parīkṣit was protected in the womb, the prayers of Kuntī, the passing of Bhīṣma, the Lord’s journey to and entrance into Dvārakā, and his pastimes with the queens there. Sūta became absorbed in those narrations. Śaunaka, desiring to hear about Parīkṣit’s birth, again asks about this specifically. Upasṛṣṭena means “being thrown.”

### 1.12.2-3

**Перевод:**

**We desire to hear about the birth, activities, passing away and destination of this highly intelligent devotee, to whom Śukadeva gave knowledge. Please tell us, full of faith, if you desire to tell this.**

**Комментарий:**

Pretya means giving up the body. “If you desire to tell us” means “Be merciful and tell us.”

### 1.12.4

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Yudhiṣṭhira, like his father, freed from personal desires by engaging in service to the Lord, satisfied and protected the citizens.**

**Комментарий:**

Apīpalat means “he protected.”

### 1.12.5-6

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇa! He had all wealth, sacrifices, people, queens, brothers, the earth, lordship over Jambū-dvīpa, and fame in Svarga, desirable even for the devatās. Did these things give joy to the King, whose mind was only fixed on Mukunda without deviation and nothing else?**

**Комментарий:**

The wealth and other items were desired by the devatās. Spārhāḥ means desire, coming from spṛha, by adding the internal vowel “a”. Did these things create happiness for the King? No. The reason is given. He was thinking of Mukunda. “Other things” includes sandalwood and garlands offered in honor. His wealth was for service to Mukunda. Other things like garlands were meant for satisfying a person after he has eaten food.

### 1.12.7

**Перевод:**

**O son of Bhṛgu! The courageous hero Parīkṣit in the womb of his mother, burned by the heat of the weapon, saw the form of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The topic of discussion now begins. Because of his natural courage (vīraḥ), he was not afraid of the fire of the weapon. He saw the Lord somewhat when in the womb. His eyes and mind became fixed on Kṛṣṇa, like Vāmanadeva.

### 1.12.8

*aṅguṣṭha-mātram amalaṁ sphurat-puraṭa-maulinam \| apīcya-darśanaṁ śyāmaṁ taḍid vāsasam acyutam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**That pure, indestructible, beautiful form was the size of a thumb, dark in complexion, wearing cloth flashing like lightning, with a shining gold crown.**

**Комментарий:**

Because the space everywhere in the womb is just that much, the size of thumb mentioned is figurative. Actually, by the inconceivable power of the Lord, he saw the Lord (normal size) as seen by Yudhiṣṭhira and others. Later on it is said: Because he constantly remembered the Lord whom he had seen in the womb and searched for him among all men, he was famous as Parīkṣit in this world. (SB [1.12.30](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/12.30)) He would not look among men to find that form if it had actually only been the size of a thumb. There is no human form that size. He wore a gold crown. Maulin is formed from mauli (crown) in the class of words like vrīhī. The form he saw was most beautiful (apīcya-darśanam). Apīcya is a Vedic formation as explained in bhave chandasi (Pāṇini 4.4.110). though it is seen in the Vedas, Bhāgavatam may be considered a part of the Vedas. Darśanam here means “the form seen”, formed from dṛś. He was dark in complexion and was wearing cloth shining like lightning. This indicates that his form, like a rain cloud ornamented with lightning, had suddenly appeared in the sky of Uttarā’s womb to save the young elephant Parīkṣit who was being burned by the forest fire of the brahmāstra.

### 1.12.9

**Перевод:**

**He had four long arms, bright gold earrings. His eyes were red from anger and in his hands he was clutching a club. He wandered everywhere, swinging the club, which shone like a meteor.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṣatajākṣam (blood eyes) means that his eyes became very red because of anger at the brahmāstra.

### 1.12.10

**Перевод:**

**Seeing that form nearby annihilate the brahmāstra with his club, just as the sun dissipates frost, Parīkṣit considered in his mind, “Who is that?”**

**Комментарий:**

Like the sun (gopatiḥ) destroys frost (nīhāram) he destroyed (vidhamantaṁ) the brahmāstra. He considered (paryaikṣata) “What person poised for battle is protecting me?” This sentence is connected with the next verse.

### 1.12.11

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, maintaining his reputation of being affectionate to his devotees, inconceivable in form, and also a thief, having destroyed the brahmāstra, disappeared at that spot from the sight of the child ten months in the womb.**

**Комментарий:**

Dharma-gup means “the Lord who maintains his quality of being affectionate to his devotee.” He was seen (miṣataḥ) by Parīkṣit who was just about to be born, being ten months in the womb (daśamāsasya). The Lord disappeared there, where he was situated, without going elsewhere, because he is all powerful (vibhuḥ). He entered the womb and, attracting Parīkṣit’s mind (hariḥ, attractor), he then disappeared.

### 1.12.12

**Перевод:**

**Then, with the ascendant filled with favorable planets, which would later manifest all good qualities, the maintainer of Pāṇḍu’s lineage, similar to Pāṇḍu in strength, was born.**

**Комментарий:**

Sarva-guṇā udarkaḥ means in the future all good qualities would manifest. When he was born, there was a conjunction of favorable planets in the ascendant.

### 1.12.13

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira had the brāhmaṇas such as Dhaumya and Kṛpācārya chant auspicious verses and perform the birth rites.**

**Комментарий:**

Jātakam means the birth rites.

### 1.12.14

**Перевод:**

**On the auspicious occasion of the birth, the best of kings, knowing the proper time to give charity, gave the brāhmaṇas gold, cows, land, villages, elephants, horses, and the best food.**

**Комментарий:**

Prajā-tīrthe means “at the auspicious time of a son’s birth.” Putre jāte vyatīpāte datte bhavati cākṣayam: what is given at the birth of a son or during calamity has indestructible results. Tīrtha-vit means the knower of time for charity, considering the following: Until the umbilical cord is cut, it is not considered birth. When the cord is cut, it is considered birth.

### 1.12.15-17

**Перевод:**

**The satisfied brāhmaṇas said to the humble king: O best of men! Though the pure dynasty of Puru was destroyed by unavoidable destiny, this son has been given as mercy to you by the powerful Viṣṇu. Therefore he will be known as Viṣṇu-rāta, “Gift of Viṣṇu,” and without doubt, in this world will be the best, the greatest devotee, O fortunate King!**

**Комментарий:**

Though the pure (śukle) dynasty of the Purus was destroyed (upeyuṣi) by fate, this son was given (rātaḥ) by Viṣṇu as his mercy. Because he was given by Viṣṇu he will be called Viṣṇu-rāta.

### 1.12.18

**Перевод:**

**The King said: O great souls! Will this son follow our lineage of reputed saintly kings with fame and good praise?**

**Комментарий:**

Having heard that Parīkṣit would be a great devotee, the astonished king inquired with joy. Api indicates possibility. Svit indicates a question. Anuvartitā means “follow the qualities of.” Will he be like them or not?

### 1.12.19

**Перевод:**

**The brāhmaṇas said: O son of Pṛthā! He will be the protector of the citizens. He will be exactly like Ikṣvāku, the son of Manu and Rāma, the son of Daśaratha. He will be charitable to the brāhmaṇas and true to his promise.**

**Комментарий:**

O Yudhiṣṭhira (pārtha)! Prajā avitā means protector of the people. Directly (sākṣāt), meaning without troops, he is like Īkṣvāku. Mānavaḥ means Manu’s son. Satya-sandhaḥ means “true to one’s promise.”

### 1.12.20

**Перевод:**
