**Like Śibi, from Uśīnara, he will be a donor and protector of those who surrender to him, and will spread the fame of sacrifices and family members just as Bharata, the son of Duṣyanta, did.**

**Комментарий:**

Śibi was the ruler of the province of Uśīnara. He protected a pigeon who had surrendered to him by offering his own flesh to a hawk. He will spread the fame of relatives (svānāṁ) and brāhmaṇas (yajvanām). Bharata was the son of Duṣyanta.

### 1.12.21

**Перевод:**

**He will be the best of archers like Arjuna and Kārtavīrya, as intolerable as fire and as insurmountable as the ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

The two Arjunas are the son of Pṛthā (Kuntī) and son of Kṛta-vīrya.

### 1.12.22

**Перевод:**

**He will be as courageous as a lion, honored like the Himālayas, tolerant like the earth and patient like a mother and father.**

**Комментарий:**

Mṛgendraḥ means lion. Like the Himālayas, he will be served by sages. He will be patient like a father and mother. Just as they tolerate the offenses of their son, he will tolerate the offenses of the citizens.

### 1.12.23

**Перевод:**

**He will be impartial to all beings like Yudhiṣṭhira, easily satisfied like Śiva, and the shelter of all living beings like Nārāyaṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Pitāmaha is Yudhiṣṭhira or Brahmā. Sāmye means that he has hatred for no one. Ramāśrayaḥ (shelter of Lakṣmī) means Nārāyaṇa.

### 1.12.24

**Перевод:**

**He will be famous for all good qualities like Kṛṣṇa, generous like Rantideva, and a follower of dharma like Yayāti.**

**Комментарий:**

He will be like Kṛṣṇa, sharing a portion of his glory, with abundance of good qualities.

### 1.12.25-26

**Перевод:**

**He will be as determined as Bali, and will be absorbed in the most excellent Kṛṣṇa, like Prahlāda. He will perform horse sacrifices and respect elders. He will have wise kings for descendents, will punish those who deviate from the law, and will punish Kali in order to destroy the suffering of dharma and earth.**

**Комментарий:**

Sad-grahaḥ means “properly absorbed in.” The persons mentioned had single good qualities. Parīkṣit however had all excellent qualities. That is the meaning of this section. Having spoken of Parīkṣit’s qualities, they then describe his activities. He will punish Kali in order to relieve the suffering of earth and dharma.

### 1.12.27

**Перевод:**

**Hearing that he will be killed by Takṣaka who was dispatched by a brāhmaṇa’s son, detached from all things, he will worship the feet of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Upasarjitāt means “being dispatched.” Hearing of his death from Takṣaka sent by the brāhmaṇa’s son, renouncing his kingdom (mukta-saṅgaḥ), he will worship the Lord’s feet.

### 1.12.28

*jijñāsitātma-yāthātmyomuner vyāsa-sutād asau \| hitvedaṁ nṛpa gaṅgāyāṁ yāsyaty addhākutobhayam \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**O King! Having inquired the truth about the Lord and himself from the son of Vyāsa, giving up his body on the bank of the Gaṅgā, he will certainly attain the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Jijñāsitātma-yāthātmyaḥ means “he who had inquired about the truth about the Lord and himself.” Idam refers to the body. Akutobhayam is Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.12.29

**Перевод:**

**Informing the king in this manner, all the brāhmaṇas, expert in astrology, after being worshipped, returned to their houses.**

**Комментарий:**

Labdhā apacitayaḥ means “those who were worshipped.”

### 1.12.30

**Перевод:**

**Because he constantly remembered the Lord whom he had seen in the womb and searched for him among all men, he was famous as Parīkṣit in this world.**

**Комментарий:**

The name Parīkṣit is explained. Remembering the person he had seen in the womb, he pondered, “Is this that person or not?” among all the people he saw (nareṣu iha). Thus Parīkṣit is famous in this world.

### 1.12.31

**Перевод:**

**Just as the waxing moon grows daily in phases to become the full moon, the prince quickly grew, nourished by his elders.**

**Комментарий:**

Just as the moon increases during the waxing phase and becomes full by the succeeding digits (kāṣṭhābhiḥ), Parīkṣit grew by the care and affection, being surrounded by his relatives such as Yudhiṣṭhira.

### 1.12.32

*yakṣyamāṇo ’śvamedhena jñāti-droha-jihāsayā \| rājālabdha-dhano dadhyāv anyatra kara-daṇḍayoḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira, desiring to rid himself of sin incurred by killing relatives by performing a horse sacrifice, began to consider whether there was an alternative to force or tax to attain wealth for the sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

The horse sacrifices described briefly previously are now described in detail. He considered that there was no alternative to taxes or force, because it was necessary to have abundant wealth for the sacrifice. Since the wealth gathered from taxes and by force would disappear in maintaining the dependents, he began to worry about gaining additional wealth.

### 1.12.33

**Перевод:**

**Understanding his intention, his brothers, inspired by Kṛṣṇa, brought abundant wealth cast off from the sacrifice of King Marutta in the northern direction.**

**Комментарий:**

Understanding the king’s intentions, Bhīma and others (bhrātaraḥ) brought gold vessels cast off (prahīṇam) from King Marutta’s performance of sacrifice. Wealth without an owner becomes the king’s wealth.

### 1.12.34

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira, the son of Dharma, collecting the ingredients for sacrifice by that wealth, worshipped the Lord by three horse sacrifices since he feared the sin of killing relatives.**

**Комментарий:**

Sambhṛta-sambhāraḥ means “having collected the ingredients of sacrifice.” Bhītaḥ means “fearing the killing of relatives.”

### 1.12.35-36

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa, called there by Yudhiṣṭhira, had the King perform the sacrifice with brāhmaṇas, and stayed there for some months to satisfy his friends. Taking the permission of the king, Draupadī and other friends, he then departed for Dvārakā with Arjuna, surrounded by the Yadus.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa and the king along with relatives and himself performed the sacrifice.

## Chapter Thirteen - Nārada explains the Disappearance of Dhṛtarāṣṭra

### 1.13.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Having learned about Kṛṣṇa, the goal of the jīva, from Maitreya while on pilgrimage, Vidura came to Hastināpura with a desire in going there.**

**Комментарий:**

The thirteenth chapter describes how by the words of Vidura, Dhṛtarāṣṭra leaves for the forest and by Nārada’s instructions Yudhiṣṭhira is relieved of grief. Having told about Parīkṣit’s birth, before telling about how Kali was punished by Parīkṣit, first the coronation will be described. But to describe the coronation, first the arrival of Vidura and the departure of Dhṛtarāṣṭra on the instruction of Vidura, the arrival of Arjuna, and the departure of the Pāṇḍavas are described in three chapters. He learned from Maitreya, who was taught by Kṛṣṇa, his shelter (ātamanaḥ gatim). Tayā avāpta-vivitsitaḥ means “he who had a desire to attain by going there.” Vivitsitaḥ comes from the verb vit, meaning “to attain,” in desiderative form.

### 1.13.2

**Перевод:**

**Having asked sufficient questions to Maitreya, Vidura ceased asking any more when he heard about pure devotion to Govinda.**

**Комментарий:**

Kauṣārava means Maitreya. He gave up questions which were avoided by the sage. He did not have any more desire to hear further, because all other questions are useless once bhakti has appeared.

### 1.13.3-5

**Перевод:**

**Seeing that their friend had returned, Yudhiṣṭhira and his brothers, Dhṛtarāṣṭra, Yuyutsu, Sañjaya, Kṛpācārya, Kuntī, Gāndhārī, Draupadī, Subhadrā, Uttarā, Kṛpī, the rest of the relatives of Pāṇḍu and their wives and sons became revived, as if their life airs had returned to their limbs, and according to suitability, joyfully mixed with his company, greeted and embraced him.**

**Комментарий:**

Tam refers to Vidura. Sūta means Sañjaya, Śāradvataḥ is Kṛpa. Jāmayaḥ means wives of the relatives. Seeing him, their life air which had almost left because of fainting, returned.

### 1.13.6

**Перевод:**

**Agitated by the longing created by separation, they shed tears of love. The king had him accept a seat and worshipped him.**

### 1.13.7

**Перевод:**

**After feeding him, letting him rest and sitting him down, the king with humility spoke to Vidura for all to hear.**

### 1.13.8

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira said: Do you remember us, because we, along with our mother, growing up under the shade of your wings, were saved by you from the poison and the fire and other dangers?**

**Комментарий:**

Just as birds raise their young with great affection under the shade of their wings, you raised us under your wings. Do you remember us? The protection (samedhitam) is described: you saved us from poison etc.

### 1.13.9

**Перевод:**

**As you wandered over the earth, what did you do for maintenance? What major holy places did you visit on the earth?**

**Комментарий:**

Vaḥ means “by you.”

### 1.13.10

**Перевод:**

**O master! Devotees like you are holy places incarnate. You purify the holy places because the Lord is situated within you.**

**Комментарий:**

In this verse he says that Vidura’s going to the holy places is good fortune for those places. Because of contact with contaminated people the tīrthas become contaminated. By Kṛṣṇa (gadā-bhṛtā) situated in your heart, you, a tīrtha make those places shine as tīrthas, places with power to purify.

### 1.13.11-12

**Перевод:**

**O father! Did you see or hear about our relatives and friends, devotees of Kṛṣṇa, the Yadus? Are they residing happily in Dvārakā? When Yudhiṣṭhira had thus spoken, Vidura then gradually described everything he had experienced, except for the destruction of the Yadu dynasty.**

**Комментарий:**

Api indicates a question. Do they reside happily in their city?

### 1.13.13

**Перевод:**

**A merciful person, unable to see others suffer, should not reveal an inauspicious event arising on its own which no human can tolerate.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse explains the reason according to nīti-śāstra, why he did not tell them about the disappearance of the Yadus.

### 1.13.14

**Перевод:**

**Respected as a god, Vidura stayed there for some time, pleasing all and giving the highest benefit to Dhṛtarāṣṭra.**

**Комментарий:**

Śreyas-kṛt means “to give benefit.”

### 1.13.15

**Перевод:**

**As long as Yama passed his life for a hundred years as a śūdra because of a curse, Aryamā performed his duties of punishing the sinful in an appropriate way.**

**Комментарий:**

“Since Vidura was inferior and a śūdra, why did he act as a teacher.” Aryamā is the chief of the pitṛs, a vibhūti of the Lord. pitṛṇām aryamā cāsmi: of pitṛs I am Aryamā. (BG [10.29](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/10.29)) He took the job of punishing the sinful (agha-kāriṣu) properly (yathāvat) as long as Māṇḍavya’s curse on Yama being a śūdra lasted for a hundred years. The story is told in Mahābhārata. One time the soldiers of a king were chasing some thieves. They caught them near the sage Māṇḍavya who was performing penance. Tying him up along with the thieves, they brought the sage to the king. By the king’s order, they were all impaled. But the king recognized the sage, and had him taken down from the spike, and pleased him. The sage went to Yama, considering him to be the punisher who had entered the king, and in anger spoke. Yama replied, “As a boy you pierced a grasshopper with the tip of a kuśa grass, as play.” Hearing this, Māṇḍavya cursed Yama, “Since you punished so severely though I was ignorant, you should become a śūdra.” Thus though Vidura was inferior and a śūdra, he could teach since he had realized the Lord because he was the king of dharma. Thus Bhāradvāja-saṁhita says that, though women and śūdras are forbidden to teach, one can learn from women and śūdras who have realized (sākṣāt kṛta) the supreme truth (para-tattva). Those born outside the varṇas, the defamed person, the fallen person, a person with many desires, the women and śūdras should never become givers of mantras for a person without material desires. Women, śūdras and others can teach what is beneficial and unbeneficial. They should be respected suitably and should not act as ācārya. Yogīs however are born in all wombs. One should not worry about family, etc., for those who have realized Paramātmā. (Bhāradvāja-saṁhitā 1.42-44)

### 1.13.16

**Перевод:**

**Having attained his kingdom, and seeing the heir to the dynasty, Yudhiṣṭhira along with his brothers who were like protecting devatās, became overjoyed with the greatest splendor.**

**Комментарий:**

Kulan-dharam means one who continues the dynasty.

### 1.13.17

**Перевод:**

**Insurmountable time overtook the lives of those attached to their houses and intoxicated with household affairs without their knowledge.**

**Комментарий:**

The word “life” should be supplied as object of the verb atyākrāmat (overtook). This verse is a criticism of people other than Yudhiṣṭhira and his family present at that time. It has already been said that he was unattached to everything except the Lord. kṣudhitasya yathetare: he was completely attached to the Lord and nothing else. (SB [1.12.6](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/12.6))

### 1.13.18

**Перевод:**

**Knowing that his time was approaching, Vidura spoke to Dhṛtarāṣṭra. “O King! You should leave immediately. See! Fearful time is approaching!”**

### 1.13.19

**Перевод:**

**O master! The supremely powerful time which no one can stop has approached us all.**

**Комментарий:**

“If one stays in the house one can counteract time.” This verse answers. By saying “all” Vidura indicates that time had approached even those who should be able to prevent the actions of time. One can counteract time by worshipping the Lord, renouncing these items.

### 1.13.20

**Перевод:**

**Attacked by time, a person is separated immediately from his beloved life airs, what to speak of other things like wealth.**

**Комментарий:**

Afflicted (abhipannaḥ) by time, in the form of death (yena), a person is separated from his life airs.

### 1.13.21

*pitṛ-bhrātṛ-suhṛt-putrā hatās te vigataṁ vayaḥ\| ātmā ca jarayā grastaḥ para-geham upāsase \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Your father, brothers, friends, and sons have been killed. Youth has departed. The body is afflicted by old age. You are living in another person’s house.**

**Комментарий:**

In seven verses (21-27) Vidura teaches renunciation.

### 1.13.22

*This verse is found only in a few editions and has not been commented on by the past ācāryas.*

**Перевод:**

**You were blind from birth. You are now deaf, and feeble witted. Your teeth are broken, your digestion is weak, and your body is full of mucus. Still you are attached.**

### 1.13.23

*aho mahīyasī jantor jīvitāśā yayābhavān \| bhīmāpavarjitaṁ piṇḍam ādatte gṛha-pālavat \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**So strong is the desire to live among all creatures that, like a dog, you take the food given by Bhīma!**

**Комментарий:**

Āvarjitammeans given. Gṛha-pālaḥ is a dog.

### 1.13.24

**Перевод:**

**What is the use of life maintained by food given by those whom you tried to burn, poison, whose wife you insulted, and whose land and wealth you stole?**

**Комментарий:**

Garaḥ means poison. What is the use (kiyat) of life (asubhiḥ) attained by the morsel of food given by them (tad-dattaiḥ)?

### 1.13.25

**Перевод:**

**Though wanting to live and not give the body up, your body, worn out with old age, is dying, beyond your will, like two pieces of cloth.**

**Комментарий:**

The body has a subtle and gross form. It is like two pieces of clothing. Paraiti means “it is wasting away.” Blindness and deafness indicate decay of the subtle body (since the senses are subtle) and wrinkles and grey hair are the decay of the gross body.

### 1.13.26

**Перевод:**

**He is called wise who gives up this body which has not been used to worship Kṛṣṇa, after rejecting wealth and sons and going where no one can find him, while practicing bhakti.**

**Комментарий:**

Gata-svārtham means “the goal of the body is unfulfilled because of not having performed worship of Kṛṣṇa.” Mukta-bandhanaḥ means giving up wealth and sons. Avijñāta-gatiḥ means “going where no one knows.” The words “gives up’’ should mean “gives up the body at a holy place while remembering the Lord.” Such a person is wise.

### 1.13.27

**Перевод:**

**He is the best of men who gives up the house after developing discrimination by self-endeavor or inspiration of others and becoming detached, while concentrating the mind on the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The best of men (narottamaḥ) however prepares his remedy before old age. His qualities are described. Getting knowledge himself, or getting instructions from others, he becomes fixed in discriminating ātmā from the body (ātmavān). But you are not the best of men.

### 1.13.28

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, go to the north, somewhere unknown to your relatives. Then time, the destroyer of most of man’s qualities, will come.**

**Комментарий:**

Therefore, become a narottama now. Without the Pāṇḍavas (svair) knowing where you are going (ajñāta-gatiḥ) go to the north because from now Kali will enter. That time, the destroyer of qualities like determination and mercy in men, will come.

### 1.13.29

**Перевод:**

**The King, born of Ajamīḍha’s dynasty, blind, enlightened by his younger brother Vidura, possessing the path shown by his brother, cutting the ropes of affection because of determination, left the house.**

**Комментарий:**

He was blind (prajñā acaksuḥ) but finally accepted bhakti to the Lord, which gave liberation (bodhitaḥ). He was born in the Ajamīḍha dynasty. Because of his mental determination (draḍhimnaḥ) he could leave. The path shown by his brother was the path of bondage and liberation.

### 1.13.30

**Перевод:**

**Chaste Gāndhārī, daughter of Subala, having good qualities and dedicated to her husband, followed him as he went to the Himālayas, which gives joy to those who have given up violence. She took the challenge like a warrior enjoying a good battle.**

**Комментарий:**

Gāndhārī was the daughter of Subala. She followed her husband who was going to the Himālayas. “How did she, a delicate woman, go to the Himālayas?” That place is a cause of joy for those who are nonviolent. An example is given. It is like a good (san) fight (samprahāraḥ) experienced by the warrior even if he is very young. Just as attachment to the bliss of Svarga is a stimulus for battle, so attachment to bliss of liberation is a stimulus for going to the Himalayas.

### 1.13.31

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira after performing sandhya rites, offering oblations in the fire and offering respects to the brāhmaṇas by giving sesame, cows, land and gold, entered the house to offer respects to his elders. But he did not see Vidura, Dhṛtarāṣṭra or Gāndhārī.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛta-maitraḥ means “having performed sandhya rites for Mitra (maitraḥ).” Offering respects to the brāhmaṇas, he worshipped them with sesame and other items. This is understood by ākṣepa (the necessary connection), just as the words praviśa (enter) and piṇḍīm (a lump of food) are completed by supplying gṛha (house) and bhuṅkṣva (eat). Pitarau refers to Vidura and Dhṛtarāṣṭra.

### 1.13.32

**Перевод:**

**Afflicted in mind, he asked Sañjaya who was sitting there, “O son of Gavalgaṇa, where is our father, my blind, aged uncle, his wife grieving for her slain children, and my uncle and friend Vidura?”**

**Комментарий:**

O Sañjaya (gāvalgaṇe)! He is without eyes (hīnaḥ netrayoḥ). Pitṛvyaḥ means Vidura.

### 1.13.33

**Перевод:**

**Has he thrown himself in the Gaṅgā along with his wife, out of sorrow, with all his children dead, while contemplating my foolish offense?**

**Комментарий:**

Was Dhṛtarāṣṭra depressed (āśaṁsamānaḥ) with the thought of offense (śamalam) committed by me, a foolish person (akrātprajñe), thinking, “Why should I live, when not even one of my sons remain?” Did he throw himself in the Gaṅgā with his wife? Or another meaning is: he desired (āśaṁsamānaḥ) as follows, “Let Yudhiṣṭhira also have the sin of killing me too.”

### 1.13.34

**Перевод:**

**When our father Pāṇḍu departed, these two protected all us children, who were their relatives, from dangers. From this place, where have they gone?**

**Комментарий:**

He remembers their assistance and repents. Pitṛvyau means Vidura and Dhṛatrāṣṭra.

### 1.13.35

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Sañjaya, pained by the separation, and not seeing his lord, overcome with compassion and the bewilderment of affection, did not reply.**

**Комментарий:**

Sañjaya (sūtaḥ), not seeing his lord Dhṛtarāṣṭra (ātmeśvaram), was overcome with melted heart, thinking, “What will become of these two old people without any shelter?” He was disturbed by affection caused by his relationship with his master. By that disturbance, he became weak from separation and felt pain.

### 1.13.36

**Перевод:**

**Wiping his tears with his hands, making his mind steady by intelligence, remembering the feet of his master, he replied to Yudhiṣṭhira.**

**Комментарий:**

Viṣṭabhyātmānam ātmanā means “creating steadiness in the mind by intelligence.” He remembered the feet of his master Dhṛtarāṣṭra (prabhoḥ).

### 1.13.37

**Перевод:**

**Sañjaya said: O joy of the dynasty! I certainly do not know about your uncles and Gāndhārī. O mighty-armed warrior! They have cheated me.**

**Комментарий:**

Vyavasitam means certainly. Muṣitaḥ means cheated. They left while I was sleeping.

### 1.13.38

**Перевод:**

**At that time, Lord Nārada, holding a vīṇā, arrived. Standing up, Yudhiṣṭhira, along with his brothers, after welcoming and worshipping him, spoke to him.**

**Комментарий:**

Out of lamentation, Yudhiṣṭhira worshipped him.

### 1.13.39-40

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira said: “O master! I do not know the destination of my uncles. Where did they go from here? Where did my austere aunt, grieving for her dead sons, go? You are like a pilot for crossing the ocean of grief; you can see the other side.” Then Lord Nārada the best of sages spoke.**

**Комментарий:**

Nāhaṁ veda should be na vedmi (I do not know). Tapasvinī means she who follows rules. She has pained with grief. Apāre means “in the ocean of grief.” You are all-knowing (bhagavān), and one who shows the way to cross the ocean of grief. Please tell me.

### 1.13.41

**Перевод:**

**Nārada said: O King! Do not lament for anything, because this world is dependent on the Lord. The Lord, to whom even the devatās carry offerings, joins and separates the living beings.**

**Комментарий:**

In the beginning, Yudhiṣṭhira would faint with lamentation on hearing what had happened. Therefore, first Nārada appeased his lamentation. Do not lament for anything (mā śucaḥ). The reason is given. Everything is dependent on the Lord. “I lament because of separation from them.” You cannot prevent separation because union and separation are under the Lord’s control.

### 1.13.42

**Перевод:**

**Just as cows are bound by ropes to the nose and the ropes are all attached to one longer rope, all humans, bound by designations of varṇāśrama derived from the Vedas carry out the orders of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Two examples in two verses (42-43) are given to strengthen the statements. Each cow has a rope tied to its nose, and all are tied to one longer rope. “But what is the connection between the ropes and the longer rope with the present discussion?” The Veda is the long rope. Humans are bound by the short ropes of the designations in the Vedas, such as brāhmaṇa, kṣatriya, etc., and brahmacārī, gṛhastha, etc. Their payment (balim) is following the order. They offer in the form of carrying out karmas prescribed for their varṇa and āśrama.

### 1.13.43

**Перевод:**

**Just as the items used in playing are brought together and separated by the will of the player, humans are brought together and separated by the will of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Krīḍopaskarāṇām refers to things used for playing.

### 1.13.44

**Перевод:**

**If one considers oneself to be the soul, the body, neither or both, in all these cases, the person, situated with discernment beyond material affection, should not lament for his relatives.**

**Комментарий:**

It has been just said that because the meeting and separation of living beings is under the control of the Lord, lamenting is no suitable in examining the nature of things. But when examining the real nature of objects, there is no opportunity for lamentation. You may consider yourself a fixed entity, as a jīva (dhruvam), an impermanent entity, as a body (adhruvam) or different from jīva (permanent) and body (temporary) by being Brahman. Considering these three types of perception, you should not lament for relatives. If you think you are a jīva, because it is permanent, you should not lament. If you think you are the body, you should not lament, since it is always temporary. If you think you are different from both, you should not lament since there is no object of lamentation in oneness. Why do people lament? It comes from affection generated from illusion, false knowledge of thinking of body and house in terms of I and mine. Such affection is the place of lamentation. The affection arising from illusion causes lamentation, but not the affection related to the Lord and his devotees.

### 1.13.45

**Перевод:**

**O King! Give up your agitation arising from ignorance, thinking, “How will they, suffering and without protection, live without me?”**

**Комментарий:**

Give up the disturbance of mind which thinks, “How will they live without me?”

### 1.13.46

**Перевод:**

**This body of five elements is dependent on time, karma and the material ingredients. How can a person, as if bitten by a snake, protect others?**

**Комментарий:**

No one can protect anyone else by giving maintenance. Time is the general cause. Karma is the cause of birth. Guṇa indicates the material cause. The body made of five elements is dependent on these. The body with these constituents is quickly subject to destruction. How can such a person protect others? He cannot. An example is given. A person bitten by a snake cannot protect others bitten by snakes.

### 1.13.47

**Перевод:**

**The humans are maintained by the animals and the animals are maintained by the plants. The big are maintained by the small. One living being is the maintenance for another living being.**

**Комментарий:**

The maintenance of all entities is established in the beginning by the Lord. The animals (ahastāni) are food for the humans. The plants (apadāni) are food for the animals. The small fish are food for the big fish. One entity is the natural food for another. This is arranged by the Lord. Maintenance through leaves and fruits made by the Lord is not forbidden. Your lamentation is not proper.

### 1.13.48

**Перевод:**

**O King! This universe is only the Lord. He is the one soul of all the jīvas. He is self-manifesting, not dependent on others. He is the jīva and the objects of enjoyment for the jīva. See the one Lord manifested as many by the jīva.**

**Комментарий:**

“You have said that the world is dependent on the Lord. Then how is the body dependent on time, karma and matter?” The one Lord is this world (idam). “How can the Lord full of knowledge and bliss be the insentient world?” He is the world by his śakti (māyayā). The knowers of the real meaning of words say that māyā means cit-śakti, knowledge, what is made of the three guṇas. (Śabda-mahodadhi) In that work, cit-śakti means the parā śakti and kṣetra-jña śakti since they are both non-different being cit (spiritual). Time is also his śakti. Sva-kāla-śaktyā kṣapayan: The Lord diminishes the burden of the earth by his time śakti. (SB [10.1.22](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/1.22)) The Lord is the antaryāmī (ātmā) of the jīvas (ātmanām). He is unperceived (antaraḥ). He is the form of the universe by the jīva called kṣetrajña, by time and by the three guṇas. You can see this by discerning vision.

### 1.13.49

**Перевод:**

**He, the Lord of Dvārakā, protector of all beings, has appeared in the form of time to destroy the demons.**

**Комментарий:**

“Where does this person with such powers exist?” He is living in Dvārakā. He is here (so ’yam). He has appeared on earth (asyām) for destroying the demons. Appearing to the demons as the form of time he destroys them. Appearing to the demons as time, he remains in Dvārakā with a form of knowledge and bliss.

### 1.13.50

**Перевод:**

**Having completed the actions for the devatās, he is waiting for the remaining activities. While he remains on earth, you should tolerate while he completes the remaining actions.**

**Комментарий:**

He is waiting only for the remaining actions, to cause the disappearance of the Yadu dynasty. Like Vidura, Nārada did not describe the arrangement for the destruction of the Yadu family, which he knew had already taken place. You should endure everything while the Lord carries out the remaining actions.

### 1.13.51

**Перевод:**

**Dhṛtarāṣṭra, with his wife Gāndhārī, and Vidura, has gone to a hermitage of sages on the south side of the Himālayas.**

**Комментарий:**

Having dispelled his lamentation by his teachings on tattva, he now tells the actual facts to the inquisitive Yudhiṣṭhira in six verses (51- 56). Dakṣiṇena means “in the southern direction.”

### 1.13.52

**Перевод:**

**That place where the Gaṅgā became seven by dividing into seven branches to please the seven sages is called Sapta-srota.**

**Комментарий:**

That which is famous as Gaṅgā (svardhunī) made herself into seven (saptadhā). “Why?” She did it to please the seven sages. That place is called by names like Sapta-srota or Marīci-gaṅgā, or Atri-gaṅgā.

### 1.13.53-54

**Перевод:**

**At that place Dhṛtarāṣṭra, bathing three times a day, performing sacrifice according to scriptural rules, drinking only water as food, controlling the mind, ridding himself of material desires, perfecting sitting postures and breathing, withdrawing the six senses, has destroyed the contamination of sattva, rajas and tamas by meditation on the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The process of aṣṭāṅga-yoga that he performed is described in four verses (53-56). The niyamas are bathing, sacrifice and taking water as food. Āsana, prāṇāyāma and pratyāhārā are described in the third line. Dhāraṇā and dhyāna are described in the last line.

### 1.13.55-56

**Перевод:**

**Meditating on merging the false ego into the mahat-tattva, merging the mahat-tattva into the jīva, merging the jīva into the Brahman, and merging the Paramātmā into Bhagavān, the supreme shelter, like merging the sky in the pot into the sky, he has destroyed the impressions arising from the guṇas of māyā, controlled the senses and mind. Stopping all enjoyment of the senses (or eating), he remains without movement like a pillar.**

**Комментарий:**

Merging the elements of his body into their cause the false ego, merging the false ego into the mahat-tattva (vijñānātmani), merging the mahat-tattva into the jīva (kṣetra-jñe), and merging the jīva into the Paramātmā (brahmaṇi) and merging Paramātmā into Bhagavān, the Lord (ādhāre), the āśraya-tattva, the aṁśī, he destroyed his material impressions. “It is well known that Paramātmā and Bhagavān are one. Why to mention them as two?” Difference is rejected through an example. When a pot is destroyed the air in the pot merges with the air in the sky. Similarly, at pralaya, when everything merges in Paramātmā, then Paramātmā merges into the all-pervading aṁśī. When he does not display his śaktis of monitoring the universe, Paramātmā becomes one with Bhagavān. His state previous to yoga perfection is described. He has the desire to destroy the results of the guṇas. Thus he controls the senses. Perfection is prevented internally by agitation of the guṇas and externally by agitation of the senses. He had not attained control of both. Thus he had not attained perfection.

### 1.13.57

**Перевод:**

**Do not be an obstacle to Dhṛtarāṣṭra who has renounced all actions! O King! Five days from now he will give up his body. He will turn himself to ashes.**

**Комментарий:**

Since Yudhiṣṭhira may try to bring him back Nārada says, “Do not be an obstacle (antarāyaḥ)!” Abhūḥ in this case should be bhūḥ with mā, but stays in that form for the sake of meter. Yudhiṣṭhira may still want to see him. Therefore, Nārada says five days from now (adyatanāt) he will give up his body. Yudhiṣṭhira may want to go to burn the body. Nārada says that he will burn himself up (svam).

### 1.13.58

**Перевод:**

**While his body is being burned along with the cottage, the noble wife, situated outside, will enter the fire to follow her husband.**

**Комментарий:**

“I will go to bring back Gāndhārī!” No, that is not possible. When the body of her husband burns along with the hut (sahoṭaje) by the fire of yoga and the gārhapatya fire, she, situated outside, will enter the fire following (anu) her husband.

### 1.13.59

**Перевод:**

**O joy of the Kurus! Seeing this astonishing event, Vidura, with joy and lamentation, will depart to visit holy places.**

**Комментарий:**

“I will go and bring Vidura back.” No, that is not possible. Seeing (niśāmya) this astonishing event, Vidura will be joyful on seeing him attain an excellent destination, but also lament out of affection for Dhṛtarāṣṭra. He will then go from that place to visit holy places.

### 1.13.60

**Перевод:**

**Saying this, Nārada with his vīṇā ascended to Svarga. Yudhiṣṭhira, taking those words in his heart, gave up his lamentation.**

## Chapter Fourteen - Yudhiṣṭhira’s Doubts on Seeing Omens

### 1.14.1-2

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: When Arjuna went to Dvārakā to see his friends and understand the activities and intentions of famous Kṛṣṇa, he did not return for some months. Yudhiṣṭhira saw inauspicious omens at that time.**

**Комментарий:**

In the fourteenth chapter, Yudhiṣṭhira understands the meaning of the inauspicious signs when he sees Arjuna arrive in distress. Jiṣṇu means Arjuna. He went to understand Kṛṣṇa’s activities and his intentions (ca). “Some months” means seven months. Nimittāni means ill omens.

### 1.14.3

**Перевод:**

**He saw a fearful change of time, with seasons appearing in the wrong order. He saw sinful men engaged in maintaining themselves by occupations which were characterized by anger, greed and cheating.**

**Комментарий:**

Time passed with the sequence of seasons being reversed. Men’s livelihood (vārtām) became most sinful (pāpīyasīm).

### 1.14.4

**Перевод:**

**He saw dealings that were filled with deception, and friendships which were mixed with cheating. There were quarrels between fathers, mothers, friends, brothers, husbands and wives.**

**Комментарий:**

Dealings were filled with great deceit (jihma-prāyam). Friendships were mixed with cheating (śāṭhyam) Kalkanam means quarrels and other conflicts between fathers and sons.

### 1.14.5

**Перевод:**

**Seeing the men’s sinful qualities filled with greed and the extremely inauspicious symptoms appearing according to time, he spoke to Bhīma.**

**Комментарий:**

The cause everywhere is time. His own time having arrived, seeing the nature (prakṛtim) of men, filled with greed, etc., he spoke to Bhīma (anujam).

### 1.14.6

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira said: I sent Arjuna to Dvārakā because I wanted him to see our friends there and to understand the activities of famous Kṛṣṇa.**

### 1.14.7

**Перевод:**

**O Bhīmasena! Seven months have passed. Your younger brother has not yet returned. I do not know the reason at all.**

### 1.14.8

**Перевод:**

**Has the time indicated by Nārada arrived, when the Lord desires to give up his form engaged in pastimes?**

**Комментарий:**

Nārada had hinted at the time in the last chapter. Has that time arrived (api) when the Lord desires to give up his place (aṅgam) of pastimes in the form of earth? Yasya pṛthivī śarīram: the earth is the Lord’s body. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad [3.7.3](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bau/3.7.3))

### 1.14.9

**Перевод:**

**From him, by his mercy we have attained wealth, kingdom, wives, life, family, citizens, victory over enemies, and the higher worlds.**

### 1.14.10

**Перевод:**

**Lion among men! See the ill omens in the sky, land and bodies indicating fearful situation close at hand which will bewilder our intelligence.**

**Комментарий:**

The omens indicate fear (bhayam śaṁsataḥ) not far off.

### 1.14.11

**Перевод:**

**O Bhīma! My left thigh, eye and arm are repeatedly twitching. There is trembling in my heart. Soon these omens will bring misfortune.**

**Комментарий:**

The left thigh and other limbs quiver. There is trembling of the heart. These signs will bring misfortune soon.

### 1.14.12

**Перевод:**

**Bhīma! A jackal spitting fire is howling at the rising sun. A fearless dog is looking at me and howling.**

**Комментарий:**

A jackal (śivā) is howling at the rising sun while vomiting fire from his mouth. A fearless (abhīruvat) dog (sārameyaḥ) looking at me is howling.

### 1.14.13

**Перевод:**

**O tiger among men! Auspicious animals are circling me counterclockwise and inauspicious animals are circling me clockwise. I see that my horses are crying.**

**Комментарий:**

Auspicious animals like the cow are going keeping me on the left (savyam). Inauspicious animals like the donkey are going keeping me on the right. I see that the horses (vāhān) are crying.

### 1.14.14

**Перевод:**

**This pigeon, a messenger of death, the owl which is making my mind tremble and the crow desire to empty the universe with their harsh cries.**

**Комментарий:**

Pratyulūkaḥ is a type of owl or a crow which is an enemy to the other owls. They desire to empty the universe by their harsh cries (kuhvānaiḥ).

### 1.14.15

**Перевод:**

**The air is hazy in all direction like a wall. The earth along with the mountains is trembling. There is sudden loud clamor with cloudless thunder.**

**Комментарий:**

The grey directions are like a wall, covering the world. There is tremendous sudden clamor (nirghātaḥ) along with cloudless thunder (stanayitnubhiḥ).

### 1.14.16

**Перевод:**

**The wind, spreading darkness with dust, blows harshly. And the clouds rain hideously as if spraying blood everywhere.**

**Комментарий:**

The clouds seem to rain blood (asṛk).

### 1.14.17

**Перевод:**

**See the sun with weak light fighting with other planets in the sky,and see the earth and heavens ablaze with the followers of Śiva along with other beings.**

**Комментарий:**

Graha-mardam means planets fighting. See the earth and heavens (rodasī), seeming to blaze, with Śiva’s attendants together with other living beings (sa-saṅkulaiḥ).

### 1.14.18

**Перевод:**

**The male and female rivers, the lakes and the mind of all beings are agitated. Fire does not burn with ghee. What does the future portend?**

**Комментарий:**

Again he speaks of the omens on the earth. The minds of living beings are disturbed.

### 1.14.19

**Перевод:**

**The calves do not drink milk and the cows do not give milk. The cows wail with tearful faces and the bulls do not enjoy the fields.**

**Комментарий:**

Na duhyanti as a reflexive verb (the cows do not milk themselves) is poetic license. It means that the cows do not give milk.

### 1.14.20

**Перевод:**

**The deities seem to be weeping, perspiring and moving about. The populated areas, villages, towns, gardens and hermitages are devoid of splendor and bliss. Why are they showing us such suffering?**

**Комментарий:**

Daivatāni means deities. Agham means suffering.

### 1.14.21

**Перевод:**

**It seems the earth, made inauspicious by these ominous signs, is devoid of the footsteps of the Lord endowed with glorious marks not seen in any one else.**

**Комментарий:**

By these great calamities, I consider (manye) certainly (nūnam) that the earth is devoid of the feet of the Lord whose marks like the thunderbolt and elephant goad do not exist in other persons (ananya-puruṣa-śrībhiḥ).

### 1.14.22

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇa! While the king was contemplating the matter with consideration of the ill omens, Arjuna arrived from Dvārakā.**

### 1.14.23-24

**Перевод:**

**Seeing lusterless Arjuna fallen at his feet, grieving like never before, with downcast face and tears flowing from his lotus eyes, with an agitated heart, Yudhiṣṭhira, remembering what Nārada has said, inquired from Arjuna in the presence of his friends.**

**Комментарий:**

Ab-bindūṇ means tears. Vicchāyam means without luster.

### 1.14.25

**Перевод:**

**Yudhiṣṭhira said: O Arjuna! Are our relatives, the Madhus, Bhojas, Daśārhas, Arhas, Sātvatas, Andhakas and Vṛṣṇīs well in Dvārakā?**

### 1.14.26

**Перевод:**

**Is our respected maternal grandfather Śūrawell? Are our maternal uncle Vasudeva and his younger brothers well?**

**Комментарий:**

He inquires, worried about troubles, from less specific to more specific relatives. Māriṣaḥ means respected. Mātula is Vasudeva.

### 1.14.27

**Перевод:**

**Are our seven aunts, Vasudeva’s wives, who are like sisters, headed by Devakī,and their sons and their wives well?**

**Комментарий:**

Svasāraḥ means mutual, indicating sisters.

### 1.14.28

**Перевод:**

**How are Ugrasena, who had an evil son, and his younger brother Devaka? How are Hṛdīka and his son Kṛtavarmā, Akrūra, Jayanta, Gada and Sāraṇa?**

**Комментарий:**

Āhuka is Ugrasena. His evil son was Kaṁsa. Ugrasena’s younger brother is Devaka. Hṛdīka’s son is Kṛtavarmā. Jayanta, Gada, Sāraṇaand Śatrujit (mentioned in the next verse) were Kṛṣṇa’s brothers (Vasudeva’s other sons).

### 1.14.29

**Перевод:**

**How are Śatrujit and others? Is Balarāma, Lord of the Sātvatas well?**

### 1.14.30

**Перевод:**

**Is Pradyumna, the mahāratha among all the Vṛṣṇis happy? Is Aniruddha, fierce in fighting, prospering?**

**Комментарий:**

Gambhīra-rayaḥ means “fierce in battle.”

### 1.14.31-33

**Перевод:**

**How are Suṣeṇa,Cārudeṣṇa,Jāmbavatī’s son Sāmba, and other prominent sons, and their sons such as Ṛṣabha? How are Śrutadeva, Uddhava and other attendants of Kṛṣṇa? How are the best of the Sātvatas headed by Nanda and Sunanda, protected by the arms of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma? Do your firm friends think of our welfare?**

**Комментарий:**

He mentions the chief of Kṛṣṇa’s sons. Sunanda and Nanda were the chief (śīrṣaṇyā) of the Sātvatas.

### 1.14.34

**Перевод:**

**Is Lord Govinda, who is kind to the brāhmaṇas and affectionate to the devotees, happily presiding over the Sudharmā hall surrounded by his friends in the city?**

**Комментарий:**

It is not proper at all to ask if Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord, whose svarūpa is auspicious, is happy. Therefore, he asks, “Is he happy in the assembly hall in Dvārakā?”

### 1.14.35

**Перевод:**

**Is the first person, Kṛṣṇa, with the help of Balarāma, residing happily in the ocean of the Yadu family for giving prema, liberation and prosperity to the people?**

**Комментарий:**

Maṅgalāya means “for giving prema to some.” Kṣemāya means “for giving liberation to a few.” Bhavāya means “for giving material prosperity to a few.” Ananta-sakhaḥ means “with the help of Balarāma.”

### 1.14.36

**Перевод:**

**The Yādavas, worshipped even by the devatās, play like the inhabitants of Vaikuṇṭha in their city which is protected by Kṛṣṇa’s strong arms.**

**Комментарий:**

Mahā-pauruṣikā means “conquerors with great prowess.” The word is formed according to the rule tena dīvyati khanati jayati jitam (Pāṇini 4.4.2). Iva is an ornament.

### 1.14.37

**Перевод:**

**Satyabhāmā and the other sixteen thousand queens, by serving his feet as their main activity, (through Kṛṣṇa) defeated in battle the devatās, and took the pārijāta tree which was being enjoyed by Indra’s wife Śacī.**

**Комментарий:**

They defeated the devatās (nirjitya tridaśān) by means of their husband Kṛṣṇa. Tad-āśiṣaḥ means Pārijāta and other enjoyable things. Vajrāyudha-vallabhā is Śacī.

### 1.14.38

**Перевод:**

**The fearless Yadu heroes, protected by the strength of his arms, constantly tread with their feet over the Sudharmā assembly hall which was taken by force even though the devatās deserved it.**

**Комментарий:**

Bāhu-daṇḍābhyudayānujīvinaḥ means “they who subsist on the strength (abhyudaya) of his arms.”

### 1.14.39

**Перевод:**

**Are you healthy? You appear to me to have lost your effulgence. Staying away so long, were you not given proper respect, or were you disrespected by your friends?**

**Комментарий:**

In six verses (39-44) he asks about the welfare of Arjuna who was not speaking. Anāmayam means health. Were you, who were staying there a long time (ciroṣitaḥ), without friends or more than that, disrespected by them?

### 1.14.40

**Перевод:**

**Were you struck by harsh words without affection? Did you fail to give what you had promised to someone who requested from you and then remained silent?**

**Комментарий:**

Were you not beaten (abhihataḥ) by words with harsh meaning (bhāvaiḥ)? Or did you fail to give what you promised to persons who were wanting (arthibhyaḥ) something with a desire (āśayā) to attain it and did you not say anything?

### 1.14.41

**Перевод:**

**Did you, giver of shelter, perhaps reject brāhmaṇas, children, cows, elders, invalids and women, and other living beings, who took shelter of you?**

**Комментарий:**

Śaraṇopasṛtam means surrendered. Sattvam means living beings.

### 1.14.42

**Перевод:**

**Did you perhaps approach an unworthy woman or did you approach a woman not properly attired? Were you defeated on the road by inferiors in strength or caste?**

**Комментарий:**

There are two sentences in the first line (the line should be split after agamyām). Asatkrṭām means “with contaminated clothing and other items.” Asamaiḥ means “persons lesser in strength.” Nottamaiḥ means “by those of inferior caste.”

### 1.14.43

**Перевод:**

**Did you perhaps take your meal without feeding elders and children who should be fed at the same time? Did you perform some horrendous, forbidden act?**

**Комментарий:**

Paryabhuṇkthāḥ means “Did you eat without feeding elders first?” Akṣamam means something which should not be done.

### 1.14.44

**Перевод:**

**Do you consider yourself empty in consciousness because of separation from your most dear friend? There can be no other reason for such devastation.**

**Комментарий:**

It is not possible that you have doubts concerning these things. Remembering the words of Nārada, the following is possible however. Do you think to yourself, “I am devoid of consciousness (hṛdayena), because of the absence of my dearest friend Kṛṣṇa at all times.” Otherwise you should not have affliction in your mind (ruk).

## Chapter Fifteen - The Pāṇḍavas Ascend to the Spiritual World

### 1.15.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: The friend of Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna, was thus questioned by his brother, the king, because he presented himself as an object of worry. He had become thin because of separation from Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

In the Fifteenth Chapter, hearing the lamentation of Arjuna and seeing the entrance of Kali, the king, becoming detached, gives the kingdom to his grandson and marches to death with his brothers. Arjuna became the object of doubt (vikalpitaḥ) for the king: was it because of this or was it because of that? Having that condition, subject to doubts, he has questioned.

### 1.15.2

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna, his lotus heart and face dried up because of grief, with dull complexion, remembering the Lord, could not reply.**

**Комментарий:**

The lotus of his heart and face had dried up from grief.

### 1.15.3-4

**Перевод:**

**Stopping with difficulty the tears and wiping his eyes with his hands, extremely pained by the increased longing of love due to separation, remembering the pure friendship with Kṛṣṇa, friendship mixed with servitude, and friendship mixed with parental feelings towards Kṛṣṇa, he spoke to the king with choked voice filled with tears.**

**Комментарий:**

Stopping the tears of grief (śucaḥ) in his eyes, he wiped away those tears which flowed by his hand. Because of Kṛṣṇa not being present (parokṣeṇa), he was in pain. Sakhyam is mutual dependence with affection. Maitrīm is sakhya mixed with dāsya. Sauhrḍam is sakhya mixed with vātsalya.

### 1.15.5

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna said: O King! My friend the Lord has cheated me. He has taken with him my great power which astonished the devatās.**

**Комментарий:**

Vañcitaḥ means “cheated.” Leaving me, taking the Yādavas, he disappeared. By him my great powers which astonished the devatās were taken away.

### 1.15.6

**Перевод:**

**Just as a dead person without life becomes repulsive, the world which is separated from Kṛṣṇa even for a moment appears repulsive.**

**Комментарий:**

This and the verses following till verse 13 are all connected with tenāham adya muṣitaḥ in verses 13. An example is given: a father, devoid of life (ukthena), is called a corpse. Eṣa refers to persons like a father.

### 1.15.7

**Перевод:**

**By taking shelter of him, I stole the power of the kings who had come to Drupada’s house for the svayaṁvara ceremony and were mad with lust, then pierced the fish with the strung bow and obtained Draupadī.**

**Комментарий:**

He remembers Kṛṣṇa’s gifts in ten verses (7-16). I pierced the moving fish and obtained Draupadī (kṛṣṇā).

### 1.15.8

**Перевод:**

**In his company, I conquered with my strength Indra and the devatās, gave the Khāṇḍava forest to Agni, obtained the assembly hall of wonderful construction made by Maya, and presented it to you as a gift at the Rājasūya sacrifice where kings from all directions had assembled.**

**Комментарий:**

U expresses astonishment. The Khāṇḍava forest belonged to Indra. I gave it (adām) to Agni. I obtained the hall made by Maya whom I saved from the burning forest. In that marvelous hall, the Rājasūya sacrifice (adhvare) was held.

### 1.15.9

**Перевод:**

**By his strength, Bhīma, endowed with the strength and enthusiasm of ten thousand elephants, killed Jarāsandha at whose feet kings surrendered for the purpose of the sacrifice, by which kings who had been taken prisoner for Jarāsandha’s sacrifice to Śiva were released, and brought gifts to your Rājasūya sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

He who had kings of his own nature at his feet was Jarāsandha. Tava anuja is Bhīma. Bhīma killed him. Without conquering him, the sacrifice could not be performed. Bhīma had the perseverance (sattva) and strength (vīryaḥ) of ten thousand elephants. Jarāsandha had gathered the kings in prison to sacrifice them to Bhairava-śiva. Because they were released, they brought tribute to the sacrifice.

### 1.15.10

**Перевод:**

**And Bhīma made widows of those whose rascal husbands in the assembly untied and pulled the hair bound up beautifully during the bathing ceremony at the Rājasūya sacrifice of your wife, whose tears fell on the feet of Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Your brother made widows of the wives, making them undo their hair in mourning(tat-striyo ’krṭa-hateśa-vimukta-keśāḥ). They were wives of rascals like Duḥśāsana by whom your wife’s hair, done up nicely for the bathing ceremony at the Rājasūya sacrifice, was untied (vikīrya) and pulled (spṛṣṭam). Yah refers to Bhīma from the previous verse. Her hair was most attractive (ślāghiṣṭa) because of the mahābhiṣeka performed at the sacrifice. From Draupadī’s face tears fell on the feet of Kṛṣṇa who appeared by her remembrance, when she offered respects to him. The phrase padayoḥ patitāśru-mukhyā follows the grammatical rule samastasyāsamastena nityāpekṣeṇa saṅgatiḥ: A word inside a compound can be considered connected with a word outside the compound if their meanings are unavoidably connected by a semantic expectation. (Harināmāmṛta-vyākaraṇa 1780, vṛtti)

### 1.15.11

**Перевод:**

**Kṛṣṇa, arriving at the forest and eating the remnants from the food pot, saved us from the scheme of our enemy in the form of Durvāsa who is difficult to overcome and who eats with ten thousand followers, because they felt completely satisfied while submerging themselves in the water.**

**Комментарий:**

Because of Durvāsa, whose visit was planned out by the enemy; who has a terrible curse; who eats at the head of a line of ten thousand disciples (yaḥ ayutāgra-bhuk), Kṛṣṇa saved us in the forest, after eating (upayujya) the remnants of food in the pot. Due to that, the group of sages bathing in the water felt satisfied up to the three worlds. The story is told in the Mahābhārata. Durvāsa, satisfied with Duryodhana’s hospitality, asked him to ask for a boon. Thinking in his mind that the Pāṇḍavas can be destroyed by the anger of Durvāsa, he said, “Yudhiṣṭhira is the head of our family. Therefore you should also accept his hospitality with your ten thousand disciples. But you should go to their house when Draupadī having eaten is not troubled by hunger.” When Durvāsa arrived, Yudhiṣṭhira with great respect invited him for food after Durvāsa had performed the noon bathing rituals. The sages submerged themselves in water for purifying themselves with agha-marṣaṇa. Then Draupadī being anxious thought of Kṛṣṇa, and he came there. Although having heard what happened, he said, “O Draupadī, I am hungry. Feed me.” In great shame she said, “O Lord! My food pot given by the sun god is inexhaustible until I eat. After feeding everyone, I have eaten. Thus there is no more food.” With insistence he made her bring the pot and eating the spinach and rice stuck to the edge of the pot, he said, “The soul of the universe, I am satisfied. Bring the sages to eat.” Because he dwells within the universe and was satisfied, Durvāsa, being too full, fled, fearing that they had prepared a meal that could not be eaten.

### 1.15.12

**Перевод:**

**By his power, Śiva along with Parvatī became astonished at my prowess in fighting and gave me his own weapon; others also gave me their weapons; and in this body I sat on half of Indra’s throne in his hall.**

**Комментарий:**

Śiva (śūla-pāṇiḥ) with Durgā (sa-girijaḥ) was astonished and gave me his own weapon pāśupata. Other devatās as well gave their weapons. I shared half the throne of Indra (mahad-āsanārdham).

### 1.15.13

**Перевод:**

**O descendent of Ājamīḍha! I have now been abandoned by that great person, by whose power, while I was staying in Svarga, the devatās along with Indra took shelter of my strong arms holding the Gāṇḍiva bow for killing the Nivātakavacas.**

**Комментарий:**

When I was playing in Indra-loka, the devatās with Indra took shelter of my strong arms for killing the Nivātakavaca demons (arāti). Yad-anubhāvitam here means “the arms had taken powers from Kṛṣṇa.” I have now been cheated (muṣitaḥ) by that excellent (bhūmnā) person.

### 1.15.14

**Перевод:**

**Having him as a relative, I alone crossed over the ocean of the Kuru’s strength, infinite in width, filled with formidable creatures, on my chariot, and took abundant wealth of cows and jeweled turbans, symbols of their power, from their head.**

**Комментарий:**

I, who had Kṛṣṇa as my relative (yad-bāndhavaḥ), alone, crossed the ocean of the Kuru army, to take back the cows of King Uttara. That army had no end, because of its density and broadness. That ocean was filled with formidable creatures (atārya-sattvam) such as Bhīṣma and Droṇa who were like timiṅgala fish in the ocean. I took wealth in the form of the cows and their turbans, symbols of their power (tejās-padam), from their heads, bewildering them with the mohana weapon.

### 1.15.15

**Перевод:**

**O great king! Situated in front of me as my charioteer, he stole by his glance the karma, minds, enthusiasm to fight, and ability to take up weapons of the mahārathas among the armies of Bhīṣma, Karṇa, Droṇa and Śalya decorated with an array of many royal chariots.**

**Комментарий:**

O great king (vibho)! In the armies of Bhīṣma and others decorated with groups of chariots of the abundant best kings acting as my charioteer in front of me, he took away (ārcchat) by his glances the life, minds, etc., of the leaders of the troops. By his inconceivable śakti he took away their prārabdha-karmas (āyuḥ). By his beauty he took away Bhīṣma’s mind. By the show of his abilities he took away their enthusiasm to fight in the form of mental sharpness (saha) and the sharpness of their senses in the form of holding weapons (ojaḥ).

### 1.15.16

**Перевод:**

**Because I was in the shelter of his arms the weapons of unfailing power released by Droṇa, Bhīṣma, Karṇa, Bhūriśravā, Suśarmā, Śalya, Jayadratha, Bāhlika and others did not touch me, just as the weapons of the demons did not touch Prahlāda.**

**Комментарий:**

The weapons used by (nirūpitāni) Droṇa and others did not touch me, who was established (praṇihitam) in the arms of Kṛṣṇa (yaddoḥṣu) by Kṛṣṇa himself, though they had unfailing greatness (amogha-mahimāni). Guru means Droṇa. Trigarta is Suśarmā. Śala is Śalya. Saindhava is Jayadratha. Bāhlika was the brother of Śantanu. An example of not being harmed by formidable weapons is Prahlāda (nṛhari-dāsam).

### 1.15.17

**Перевод:**

**I foolishly engaged as my charioteer the Lord, who gives life, whose feet are worshipped by the great souls aspiring for liberation, and by whose influence the enemies on chariots, losing concentration, could not strike me as I stood on the ground with thirsty horses.**

**Комментарий:**

By the feelings of lowness resulting from separation, Arjuna’s dāsya-bhāva appeared. Thus Arjuna sees that he was offensive to engage Kṛṣṇa as his charioteer and expresses regret in this verse. The Lord was engaged by me, a fool, as a charioteer (sautye). Great souls worship his feet for liberation (abhavāya). But I made him worship me. But listen to the mercy he showed me, who am such an offender. My horses were tired from lack of water when killing Jayadratha. I got down from the chariot and piercing the ground produced water. At that time the enemies could not attack me because by his influence their minds lost concentration.

### 1.15.18

**Перевод:**

**O King! On remembering Mādhava’s joking words, splendid with smiles endowed with depth and charm, our conversations, which touched the heart, while he addressed me as “Pārtha, Arjuna, friend, joy of Kurus,” my heart becomes attracted.**

**Комментарий:**

The words were beautiful with smiles which were deep (udāra) and attractive (rucira). The words touched my heart by their sweet syllables. Remembering those words, they attract my heart.

### 1.15.19

**Перевод:**

**Because of our intimacy in sleeping, sitting, walking, boasting and eating, I would scold him, saying, “O friend! You are truthful!” Being exalted, by his greatness he tolerated the offenses of me, a fool, just as a father tolerates a son or a friend tolerates a friend.**

**Комментарий:**

Vikatthana means self-glorification. Because of our oneness of heart (aiktyāt), I would scold him with sarcastic words such as “O friend you are truthful.” Though disrespected (vipralabhdaḥ), this great person (mahā) tolerated all my offenses (agham) because of his greatness (mahitayā). As previously, he manifests dāsya.

### 1.15.20

**Перевод:**

**O best of kings! Without the Lord, my dear friend, companion, my mind being vacant, like a weakling, I was defeated by criminal cowherds while protecting his queens on the road.**

**Комментарий:**

What you guessed—my defeat by low class people, also occurred. Without my friend, my mind became vacant (hṛdayena śūnyaḥ). On the road, while protecting the sixteen thousand queens (parigraham) of my friend, I was defeated by thieving cowherds as if I were a woman (abalā iva). All the powers were given to me by him. Without him, those powers disappeared. Here the clear meaning cannot be understood. The Lord enjoyed with his wives. (SB [10.59.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/59.43)) It is impossible that these forms of Lakṣmī could be touched by thieves. They would become invisible in their presence. The meaning is this. O saintly lady, we do not desire dominion over the earth, the sovereignty of the King of heaven, unlimited facility for enjoyment, mystic power, the position of Lord Brahmā, immortality or even attainment of the kingdom of God. (SB [10.83.41](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/83.41)) We desire the same contact with the Supreme Lord’s feet that the young women of Vraja, the cowherd boys and even the aborigine Pulinda women desire—the touch of the dust he leaves on the plants and grass as he tends his cows. (SB [10.83.43](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/83.43)) From this verse it is understood that the queens desired the attractive Kṛṣṇa with cowherd dress that the gopīs hankered for. Gopa means those who protected (pa) the earth and heavens (go). These persons, actually manifestations of Kṛṣṇa, took the queens away. The Lord took the form of these cowherd men and took away his dear queens from Arjuna. In other forms, they attained the status of Vraja gopīs. This was unknown to Arjuna. He reported what he perceived. This can be understood from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa. Vyāsa also spoke to Arjuna about this: Because of the curse of the sage Aṣṭāvakra, the best of women, becoming the wives of Kṛṣṇa, would be touched by the thieves. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.38.84) Long ago, the heavenly women praised Aṣṭāvakra. He blessed them by saying. “Viṣṇu will become your husband.” Because they laughed at him on seeing his crooked body, he cursed them, “You will be taken by the thieves.” Again, being merciful, he said that after the curse had ended, they would attain the Lord. Not being able to avoid the curse and the blessing, they experienced being taken by the thieves and attaining the Lord because of this, since Kṛṣṇa took the form of the thieves. It is also said: O Pāṇḍava! Do not lament at all. All of the queens, were taken from you by Kṛṣṇa, the master of all of them. (Viṣṇu Purāṇa 5.38.85) All his wives (sarvaṁ) were taken from you (upasaṁhṛtam) by Kṛṣṇa (tena). It should be understood that all those queens entered the consorts of the Lord in the spiritual world.

### 1.15.21

**Перевод:**

**I, a warrior, and the bow, the arrows, the chariot and the horses which made kings bow to me, have become powerless in a moment without the Lord. Everything is as fruitless as sacrificing ghee into ashes, or as immaterial as getting an illusory gift from a magician, or as perishable as sowing seeds in salty earth.**

**Комментарий:**

The reason for all this is separation from Kṛṣṇa and nothing else. All of these things which caused kings to bow to me, have lost their effect because Kṛṣṇa is absent. An example is given. Throwing ghee into ashes shows no results even using proper mantras. Receiving articles from a magician is useless. Sowing seeds in salty earth even with ploughing is useless.

### 1.15.22-23

**Перевод:**

**O King! Only four or five among our friends in Dvārakā remain. Drinking rice wine, by the curse of a brāhmaṇa they become bewildered, enraged in mind, and killed each other with handfuls of reeds as if not knowing each other.**

**Комментарий:**

Among the friends who fought with fistfuls of reeds only four or five remain. The cause was the curse of brāhmaṇas. Vāruṇī is wine made from rice.

### 1.15.24

**Перевод:**

**According to common vision only, the destruction of the Yadus occurred by the will of the Lord, because actually living beings themselves cause their own survival and destruction.**

**Комментарий:**

Why did some remain? The destruction of the Yadu family (etad) was the will of the Lord, according to common vision (prāyeṇa), but this is not the real truth, because among themselves (mithaḥ) living beings are the cause of destroying and protecting each other.

### 1.15.25

**Перевод:**

**Among the aquatics, the big eat the small and weak and those of equal strength or size can eat each other.**

**Комментарий:**

Among the fish (jalaukasām) the big eat the small, and the strong can eat an equally strong fish.

### 1.15.26

**Перевод:**

**The Lord in this way destroyed the Yadus, who became a burden on the earth, with strong Yadus killing other great Yadus.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord destroyed Yadus who were a burden to the earth (itarān) by the great powerful Yadus. He destroyed the portions of devatās who were a burden to the earth, but who had appeared among the Yadus by the order of Brahmā. Those Yādavas who were eternal associates were not a burden to the earth but were its ornaments, just as a young woman does not feel that her ornaments are a weight but are pleasurable. It is explained in the Eleventh Canto that they remain with the Lord in Dvārakā.

### 1.15.27

**Перевод:**

**When I remember the words of Govinda which were suitable to place, time and subject, and which calmed the pain in my heart, those words break my heart.**

**Комментарий:**

Describing the apparent destruction, he now remembers Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. When I remember those words of Govinda which were suitable to place, time and subject, they break (haranti) my heart.

### 1.15.28

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: When Arjuna contemplated the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa with deep affection, his intelligence, regaining steadiness, became calm.**

**Комментарий:**

Sūta speaks. Though his intelligence was full of pain due to separation, by attaining an appearance of the Lord from his continuous contemplation of the Lord, he became pacified, since his burning pain was extinguished. His intelligence became free of the contamination of unsteadiness (vimalā).

### 1.15.29

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna had his intelligence completely freed of all contaminations by intense bhakti which was completely filled with meditation on the feet of Vāsudeva.**

**Комментарий:**

Arjuna had intelligence (dhiṣaṇa) completely free (nirmathita) of all contaminations (kaṣāya). That was the condition of the portion of Indra who had entered Arjuna. Arjuna himself however is described in this way: Know that I am Arjuna and that Arjuna is I. (Mahābhārata) These are Kṛṣṇa’s words on his disappearance. It is not possible that Arjuna is the person described above.

### 1.15.30

**Перевод:**

**Arjuna again understood the knowledge sung by the Lord in the battlefield, which was not forgotten by the influence of time, action or darkness caused by separation from the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Remembering the Lord’s words before the battle of Kurukṣetra, Arjuna’s pain from separation subsided. What the Lord said Arjuna again understood. Concentrate your mind on me. Be my devotee. Worship me. Offer respects to me. Having absorbed your body and mind in me, taking shelter only of me, you will come to me. (BG [9.34](https://vedarama.com/en/books/bg/9.34)) O Arjuna! You should not be afraid at this time. After my disappearance you will attain me. That song was forgotten (āruddham) by many years of time (kāla), by actions of a king (karma), or by absorption in material enjoyment (tamasā). Devoid of the enjoyment of kingly actions, he now understood this even more (adhigamat).

### 1.15.31

**Перевод:**

**By the wealth of friendship with Kṛṣṇa he destroyed his lamentation. He vanquished all doubts about separation from the Lord. Since he was beyond the guṇas by his nature though difficult to perceive, and therefore was without a subtle body, he had no birth in the material world.**

**Комментарий:**

Thus he realized Kṛṣṇa. This is described. By direct meeting with his friend (brahma-sampattyā), lamentation vanished. His doubts about dualitywere destroyed. The doubt is that previously because of mutual friendship, there was oneness of heart between us while lying together, sitting together, walking together, boasting to each other, eating together, etc., but now there is separation (dvaita) and thus separation of hearts. Will Kṛṣṇa again bring me to the oneness of happy friendship? He cut the doubt by understanding that he would attain Kṛṣṇa. Arjuna unlike others did not previously experience saṁsāra. He is beyond the guṇas (nairguṇyāt), by his very nature (prakṛti) which is difficult to perceive (līna) since he is an avatāra of Nara and a friend of Kṛṣṇa. It is impossible for him to give up a subtle body since he did not have one. He is devoid of saṁsāra at all times.

### 1.15.32

**Перевод:**

**Hearing the arrangements of the Lord and the proper situation of the Yadu family, without others knowing, Yudhiṣṭhira fixed his mind on attaining Kṛṣṇa’s abode.**

**Комментарий:**

Hearing of the Lord’s arrangement (mārgam)--the complete situation in manifesting his disappearance from the material world (saṁsthām), Yudhiṣṭhira made up his mind to go on the path to Kṛṣṇa’s planet (svaḥ-pathāya). O best of kings! It is not surprising that those desiring to associate with the Lord, born in the Pāṇḍava family and devoted solely to Kṛṣṇa, immediately gave up the royal throne served by kings’ crowns. (SB [1.19.20](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/19.20)) Nibhṛtātmā means that their thinking was not revealed to others. Though only Yudhiṣṭhira is mentioned, the same applies to all the Pāṇḍavas.

### 1.15.33

**Перевод:**

**Hearing from Arjuna about the disappearance of the Yadus and the Lord’s attaining his abode, Kuntī became absorbed in the Lord with pure devotion, and disappeared from the pastimes in the world.**

**Комментарий:**

Hearing that the Yadus could not be seen (nāśam) and hearing the famous (tām) disappearance of Kṛṣṇa, she gave up moving in the material world (saṁsṛteḥ). She suddenly disappeared.

### 1.15.34

**Перевод:**

**Regarding both burdens equally, the Lord separated himself from that portion of the Yadus by which he removed the burden of the earth, like removing a thorn with another thorn.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the esoteric conclusion is presented to pacify Śaunaka and the sages who were in grief on hearing about the final condition of the Yadus. By that body consisting of the Yadus in the form of the devatās as ordered by Brahmā, he removed the burden of the earth (bhuvaḥ) which arises from the Lord. Padbhyāṁ bhumiḥ: earth arose from his feet. (Ṛg Veda 8.4.19) Singular is used to indicate a group. He removed the burden of demon kings from the earth which arose from himself, by some of the Yadus, like removing a thorn by using a the tip of a needle. The Lord (ajaḥ) then separated his body from that form. According to Amara-kośa, kaṇṭaka means the tip of a needle. Equally he disregarded the demons who were a burden to the earth and the Yādavas who were portions of devatās. One cannot object to this statement on the grounds that there is no mention of a special group of Yadus. If one does not distinguish the different types of Yadus then there would be contradiction to the statement sarvadā mat-priyā devī: O devī! The Yadus are eternally dear to me. (Padma Purāṇa)

### 1.15.35

**Перевод:**

**Just as the Lord maintains forms such as Matsya and gives them up, and just as a magician makes a show of giving up his body, the Lord made a show of giving up his body by which he relieved the burden of the earth.**

**Комментарий:**

Another example is given to explain the meaning. A skillful actor situated in his svarūpa, accepts forms of a fish, heron or crane, and then gives them up. Similarly, the Lord, situated in his svarūpa, gives up that form of aṁśas of devatā, the Yadus, by which he relieved the burden of the earth. The devatās, leaving him, went to Svarga. After fulfilling the responsibilities in their posts, they attain liberation. The jīvas holding posts in the universe remain as long as their term lasts (and later they attain liberation). (Brahma-sūtras [3.3.33](https://vedarama.com/en/books/vs/3.3.33)) This is an easy interpretation of the example of maintaining and destroying forms. Others explain as follows. A magician (naṭaḥ), situated in his svarūpa, by magic, shows his body being cut or burned. Similarly, the Lord, situated in his svarūpa, gives up his body by māyā, for separating himself from the world. They quote this: O King! You should understand that the Supreme Lord’s appearance and disappearance, which resemble those of embodied conditioned souls, are actually a show enacted by his illusory energy, just like the performance of an actor. After creating this illusion he entered into it, played within it for some time, and at last destroyed it, withdrawing from it by his own power. Thus he remains. (SB [11.31.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/31.11)) One should understand that those who explain that the Lord gives up his body are not skillful in deriving scriptural meaning since it is impossible for the Lord to give up that which is intrinsically part of himself (his svarūpa). Uddhava says: The Lord, having shown his form, the center of attraction for all eyes, then took that form and disappeared from person who had not performed austerities but had attained the Lord’s mercy, and were continually craving a vision of his form. (SB [3.2.11](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/2.11))

### 1.15.36

**Перевод:**

**When Lord Mukunda, whose topics are worthy of hearing, left this earth by means of his spiritual body, from that day onwards Kali, the cause of inauspiciousness, entered those whose intelligence was sleeping.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse clarifies the condition of his giving up his body. When Mukunda gave up the earth by his body (sva-tanvā), from that time (tad āhaḥ) Kali entered into thieves, persons whose intelligence is sleeping (aprati-buddhi-cetasām). Sva-tanvā does not mean “He gave the earth along with his body.” That is a wrong interpretation, because of the rule upapada-vibhakteḥ kāraka-vibhaktir balīyasī: instrumental meaning of inflection is stronger than the meaning of the first member of a compound.

### 1.15.37

**Перевод:**

**Wise Yudhiṣṭhira, seeing the wheel of irreligion with greed, lying, dishonesty, and violence in towns, road, houses and body, dressed himself suitably to leave.**

**Комментарий:**

Tat parisarpaṇam means entrance of Kali. Paryadhāt means he put on the appropriate clothing for traveling.

### 1.15.38

**Перевод:**

**The King enthroned in Hastināpura his grandson Parīkṣit who had observed the rules suitable for kings and had qualities equal to his own as master of the lands surrounded by the ocean.**

**Комментарий:**

He enthroned as king, Parīkṣit who had observed rules proper for a king (vinayinam) and was very equal (susamam) in qualities to himself. Toya-nīvyāḥ means “of the land belted by the oceans.”

### 1.15.39

**Перевод:**

**He enthroned Vajra as the king of Śūrasena in Mathurā and then being capable, performing pṛajāpatya sacrifice, placed within himself the fires.**

**Комментарий:**

Vajra was the son of Aniruddha. Nirūpya means “performing.” Apibat means “he fixed within himself.” Īśvaraḥ means “capable.”

### 1.15.40

**Перевод:**

**Giving up all clothing and bracelets, he was free of thoughts of “I” and “mine” and the unlimited bonds of attraction.**

### 1.15.41

**Перевод:**

**He offered the voice and other senses into the mind, the mind into the prāṇa, the prāṇa into apāna, apāna along with excretion into death, and death into the body.**

**Комментарий:**

Yudhiṣṭhira’s efforts to produce indifference, etc., is described. Vācam (speaking) represents all the senses. He offered (juhāva) all the senses into the mind, their controller. Juhoti means to give. He offered the mind into the prāṇa because the mind has prāṇa as its controller. He offered prāṇa to apāna (itare) since apāna attracts prāṇa. Apāna is in charge of excretion (utsarga). He offered the apāna and excretion into death, the presiding deity of apāna. In offering voice and other senses and mind and prāṇa, it should be understood that he also offered the actions, speaking, thinking, etc. He offered death into the combination of the five gross elements, the body (pañcatve) since they are related to death.

### 1.15.42

**Перевод:**

**He offered the body into the three guṇas and the three guṇas into prakṛti. He offered everything into the antaryāmī and offered the antaryāmī into indestructible Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

He offered the body of five elements into the three guṇas (tritve), a portion of prakṛti in the form of individual (vyasti). He offered the three into one aggregate, a portion of prakṛti. He offered all that into the antaryāmī (ātmani). “Everything arising from your śakti of māyā is nothing to me.” He offered antaryāmī into Kṛṣṇa (brahmaṇi), Svayam Bhagavān. Elswhere there is joining together of ātmā and Brahman as one. Just as he offered Parīkṣit his kingdom, he offered the senses, etc., into their suitable controller and became free of worries. This conception entered into the Lord’s associates like Yudhiṣṭhira who were aṁśas of devatās such as Dharma, but not eternal associates Yudhiṣṭhira, etc. The devatās in the material world took shelter of the associates of the Lord. This is understood from Padma Purāṇa.

### 1.15.43

**Перевод:**

**He wore torn cloth, did not eat, stopped speaking, shaved his head, and showed himself as a dumb or insane person or a ghost.**

**Комментарий:**

Two and a half verses describe his definite position with the Lord in both cases. Baddha-vāk means not speaking.

### 1.15.44

**Перевод:**

**Not waiting for his brothers, not hearing anything as if deaf, he left the house. Meditating on Kṛṣṇa in the heart, he went in the northern direction where great souls had previously gone, from which one does not return.**

**Комментарий:**

He did not wait for his brothers (anavekṣamāṇaḥ). Param brahman means Kṛṣṇa. He went north to meditate on Kṛṣṇa, from which he did not return.

### 1.15.45

**Перевод:**

**Seeing that Kali, the friend of irreligion, had influenced the citizens on earth, all his brothers decided to follow him.**

**Комментарий:**

They decided, “Let us also take up meditation on Kṛṣṇa that will make us attain Kṛṣṇa.” They saw the citizens touched by Kali, friend of adharma.

### 1.15.46

**Перевод:**

**They who had performed all duties such as dharma and artha as it should be done, understanding the endless nature of those duties, meditated on the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa with their minds.**

**Комментарий:**

They performed everything such as dharma (sarvārthāḥ) as it should be done (sādhu-kṛta-sarvārthā). Understanding the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa (Vaikuṇṭha) to be superior to that (atyantikam), they meditated on those feet.

### 1.15.47-48

**Перевод:**

**Having pure intelligence, with minds solely on the Lord, whose portion is Nārāyaṇa, by profuse bhakti with meditation on Kṛṣṇa, they attained the goal, unattainable by the materialists, the pure abode of the Lord, with their pure bodies.**

**Комментарий:**

They had intelligence devoid of desire for power and material happiness (viśuddha). They were fixed only in happiness of service to the Lord. They attained their destination at Badarikāśrama, at the most excellent (pare) feet of Nārāyaṇa. The destination is described. They attained the dwelling place of eternal associates purified of contamination like Uddhava (vidhūtakalmaṣāsthānam) where there was the Sudharmā hall, called Kuśasthālī (Dvārakā). How did they attain it? They attained it in their same spotless bodies (virajenātmanā) devoid of their devatā expansions such as Dharma, Marut or Indra. They did not give up their bodies.

### 1.15.49

**Перевод:**

**Vidura also, by absorbing his mind in Kṛṣṇa, gave up his body at Prabhāsa and went to his abode escorted by pitṛs.**

**Комментарий:**

Vidura gave up his body. He was a form of Dharmarāja, not an associate. He went to his abode with the pitṛs who came to take him. Sva-kṣayam means his place of jurisdiction. He also has a form as an associate of the Lord. However, concentrating on Kṛṣṇa (tat cittaḥ), the associate attains Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.15.50

**Перевод:**
