**Draupadī, understanding that her husbands had become indifferent to her, concentrated her mind on Vāsudeva and attained him.**

**Комментарий:**

Draupadī along with Subhadrā (indicated by ca) attained Kṛṣṇa. Since it is not mentioned that she gave up her body, in her very body she attained the Lord (since she was an eternal associate).

### 1.15.51

**Перевод:**

**He who hears with faith about the departure of the Pāṇḍavas, dear to the Lord, as described herein, after attaining pure blessings, will attain bhakti with liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

Iti samprayāṇam means the departure as herein described and not otherwise. Siddhim means liberation.

## Chapter Sixteen - Dharma and Earth talk in the presence of Parīkṣit

### 1.16.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: O brāhmaṇa! Just as those who knew about his birth had described him, Parīkṣit, the great devotee, possessing qualities of a great person, ruled the earth under the guidance of the best brāhmaṇas.**

**Комментарий:**

In the sixteenth chapter the victory tour of Parīkṣit, the question by Dharma to Earth, and Earth’s lamentation about separation from Kṛṣṇa are described. By instructions (śikṣayā) of the best brāhmaṇas he ruled. Sūtyām means birth. Just as foretold by astrologers (samādiśan) at his birth, Parīkṣit had qualities of a great person like Yudhiṣṭhira.

### 1.16.2

**Перевод:**

**He married Irāvatī, the daughter of King Uttara, and bore four sons starting with Janamejaya.**

### 1.16.3

**Перевод:**

**Accepting Kṛpa as his guru and giving profuse donations, he performed three horse sacrifices on the banks of the Gaṅgā, in which the devatās became visible.**

**Комментарий:**

Ājahāra means performed. Śāradvata is Kṛpa.

### 1.16.4

**Перевод:**

**One time, while touring his kingdom, the hero valiantly punished Kali, a śūdra dressed as a king, who was kicking a cow and bull.**

### 1.16.5-8

**Перевод:**

**Śaunaka said: Why did the king while touring punish Kali but not kill him? The person dressed as a king who was kicking the cow was a very low śūdra. O great soul! Please tell us if it concerns Kṛṣṇa, or concerns his devotees who lick the honey from his lotus feet. O Sūta! For those men who desire Kṛṣṇa, what is use of material talks which are a waste of life for short-lived humanity? The Lord as death has been called here to this sacrifice of animals. As long as he is here, no one will die. Therefore, men should drink the words containing sweet pastimes of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Why did he punish him but not kill him (nijagrāha)? If the punishment of Kali is related to topics of Kṛṣṇa (kṛṣṇakathāśaryam) or of his devotees (satām), please tell us. If they are not related to Kṛṣṇa what is the use of the discussion since they are a useless destruction of life? “How can we hear lengthy talks about Kṛṣṇa if we are perishable?” Time (bhagavān) has been called to this place (iha) by us for killing animals in sacrifice (śāmitra-karmaṇi). Then what happens? No one dies. Then what should men do? They should drink the nectar of the Lord’s pastimes.

### 1.16.9

**Перевод:**

**The lives of those who are lazy, foolish and short lived, are wasted at night in sleeping and in the day by useless actions.**

**Комментарий:**

Then what? Anything other than hearing about the Lord is useless.

### 1.16.10

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: When Parīkṣit, expert in battle, was staying in Kurujāṅgala inspecting his kingdom, hearing the rather unpleasant news that Kali had entered, he took up his bow.**

**Комментарий:**

Hearing the rather unpleasant news that Kali had entered in Kurujāṅgala, protected (vartite) by his troops (cakra), Parīkṣit took up his bow (śarāsanam). It was somewhat pleasant also, since he was skillful. Śauṇḍi means skillful.

### 1.16.11

**Перевод:**

**He had gone out from his city to gather tribute with his troops along with chariots, horses, and elephants on a chariot with lion flag, pulled by black horses and well decorated.**

**Комментарий:**

Situated on his chariot, surrounded by troops, he left the city for conquest.

### 1.16.12

**Перевод:**

**Conquering Bhadrāśva, Ketumāla, Bhārata, the northern Kuru provinces, and other countries such as Kimpuruṣa, he received taxes.**

**Комментарий:**

He conquered Bhadrāśva and other varṣas touching the sea, in the east, west, south and north. Ilāvṛta surrounded Meru. North are Ramyaka and Hiraṇmaya varṣas. To the south of Meru are Harivarṣa and Kimpuruṣa-varṣa (north of Bhārata-varṣa) and Uttarakuru (part of Bhārata-varṣa).

### 1.16.13-15

**Перевод:**

**In those places, hearing the glories of his ancestors being sung which indicated glory to Kṛṣṇa, his deliverance from the brahmāstra of Aśvatthāmā, and the great devotion and affection of the Pāṇḍavas and Vṛṣṇis for Kṛṣṇa, highly satisfied and very generous, his eyes filled with affection, he gave those glorifiers great wealth and cloth.**

**Комментарий:**

He heard the glories of his ancestors such as Yudhiṣṭhira and himself, which indicated the glory of Kṛṣṇa. How could that glorification indicate Kṛṣṇa’s greatness? He was saved from the brahmāstra. Satisfied with the glorifiers (tebhyaḥ), he gave them wealth.

### 1.16.16

**Перевод:**

**Hearing through their praises that Kṛṣṇa acted as charioteer, member of the assembly, servant, friend and messenger of the dear Pāṇḍavas and that Viṣṇu was respected by the whole world, he expressed devotion to the lotus feet of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Moreover, hearing the activities such as being the charioteer for the dear Pāṇḍavas, and hearing that the whole world offered respects to Kṛṣṇa, Parīkṣit showed devotion to the lotus feet of Lord. Kṛṣṇa acted as follower (pāraṣadam) by presiding over the assembly. He acted as servant by following their orders. He stayed up at night with sword in hand protecting them (vīrāsana).

### 1.16.17

**Перевод:**

**Hear from me the most astonishing event that suddenly occurred while he was following this procedure of the previous kings daily.**

**Комментарий:**

An extra anu should be added. Thus every day (anu aham) he followed the procedure (anuvartamānasya).

### 1.16.18

**Перевод:**

**Dharma, moving about on one leg, seeing a cow with no effulgence, with tears in her eyes as if she had lost her calf, inquired from her.**

**Комментарий:**

Dharma in the form of a bull asked questions to the cow who had lost her effulgence (vicchāyām) and was without her calf (vivatsām).

### 1.16.19

**Перевод:**

**Dharma said: Auspicious cow! Are you well? You appear pale and your face is slightly withered. I think that you are in great anxiety. O mother! Are you lamenting for a friend in a distant land?**

**Комментарий:**

Kaccit indicates a question. O auspicious one! Is your body in good health (anāmayam)? Though externally you appear healthy, I see pain in the heart, internal malady in you. Why have you lost your effulgence? I have understood this by the signs in your slightly pale face. Thinking of a cause, he asks in five verses (20-24).

### 1.16.20

**Перевод:**

**Do you lament because I am standing on only one leg? Or are you lamenting for yourself being enjoyed by sinners? Or are you lamenting because the devatās have been deprived of their share of the sacrifices? Or are you lamenting for the citizens without rain?**

**Комментарий:**

Do you lament that I am deficient of three legs, standing on only one? Are you lamenting because you are being enjoyed by śūdras (vṛṣalaiḥ)? Bhokṣyamāṇaṁ is in the masculine gender modifying ātmā. Or do you lament that the devatās are deprived of their share of the sacrifice? Or (uta svit) do you lament the citizens suffering from lack of rain.

### 1.16.21

**Перевод:**

**O earth! Are you lamenting because the women are not protected by the husbands, children are not protected by parents and moreover suffer at the hands of merciless people? Are you lamenting about learned words being spoken in brāhmaṇa families with sinful conduct? Are you lamenting the best of brāhmaṇas serving kings with no respect for brāhmaṇas?**

**Комментарий:**

Are women are not protected by their husbands? The children are not protected by the parents. Moreover, they are suffering because of persons merciless like Rākṣasas. Are you lamenting apparent learned words (devīm vācam) in brāhmaṇa families of bad conduct? Are you lamenting those of noble family born in royal dynasties which have no devotion to brāhmaṇas?

### 1.16.22

**Перевод:**

**Are you lamenting about degraded kṣatriyas influenced by Kali, or Text 23\] Dharma and Earth talk 359 countries ruled negligently by them? Are you lamenting because people are engaged in eating, drinking, dressing, bathing, and sexual relationships without regard for restrictions?**

**Комментарий:**

Do you lament about degraded kṣatriyas influenced (upasṛṣṭān) by Kali or countries made lifeless (avaropitāni) by them? Do you lament about people inclined to eating, drinking, dressing, bathing and sex (vyavāya), etc., haphazardly with no regard for rules and prohibitions?

### 1.16.23

**Перевод:**

**O mother! O Earth! Are you lamenting on remembering the activities — which make a mockery of liberation by their sweetness — of the Lord who appeared to relieve your heavy burden and then disappeared, leaving you alone?**

**Комментарий:**

O mother, O Earth (dharitri)! Remembering the activities of the Lord who has disappeared, do you lament being left by him? He appeared (kṛtāvatārasya) to relieve (avatāra) your burden. Those activities were taken shelter of (vilambitāni) by liberation. Or the activities made liberation a laughing-stock because of their sweetness. The syllables ḍa and la can be exchanged and thus the word becomes viḍambitāni (mocking).

### 1.16.24

**Перевод:**

**O Earth! Please tell me the cause for your anxiety by which you have become extremely thin. O mother! Has time which is strongest of the strong, stolen your good fortune worshipped by the devatās?**

**Комментарий:**

Vikarśitāsi means “you have been made extremely thin.” Has your good fortune been stolen by time?

### 1.16.25-30

**Перевод:**

**Earth said: You know all the dharma about which you are asking me. I am lamenting the population which is now influenced by the sinful glance of Kali, and which is devoid of the Lord, the abode of Lakṣmī, the abode of qualities, by whose power you existed with four legs which gave benefit to the people, and in whom reside the following eternal, great qualities, which do not disappear at any time: truthfulness, purity, compassion, control of anger, generosity, self-satisfaction, straightforwardness, control of mind, control of external senses, following his caste role during pastimes, neutrality to all, tolerance, indifference to material enjoyment, consideration of scripture, omniscience, distaste for material enjoyment, control of others, determination in battle, influence, skill, defining social duties, independence, expertise in arts, beauty, lack of confusion, tenderness, boldness, modesty, being good-natured, sharpness of mind, sharpness of the knowledge senses and action senses, the abode of enjoyment, imperturbability, steadiness, faith, fame, respectability, lack of pride, and as well, other qualities desirable for those who want greatness.**

**Комментарий:**

O Dharma! you know all about what you are asking me. “Still it should be heard from your mouth” You know the dharma by whose protection you exist with four legs of mercy, truth, austerity and cleanliness. Present tense is used since the condition is near the present according to the sūtra: vartamānasāmīpye vartamānavad vā (Pāṇini 3.3.131). The sentence continues till verse 31. Satyam means speaking about things as they are. Śaucam means purity or purifying or a pure state of mind, being pleased with bhakti alone with disregard for excellence of material objects. Dayā means unmotivated desire to remove others’ suffering. By that there is protecting those who have surrendered, and being friendly to devotees. Kṣānti means having control of the mind even when angry. Tyāga means giving to those who ask. Santoṣa means full of one’s own bliss. Ārjavam means mind, body and words are one. Śama means control of the mind, by which one has firm vows. Dama means control of the external senses. Tapas means acting according to one’s sva-dharma such as kṣatriya during pastimes. Sāmyam means devoid of thinking one is an enemy and another is a friend or not making distinctions between those who surrender, but who have differing birth and qualities. Titikṣa is tolerance when others offend you. Uparati is indifference to attaining material enjoyment. Śrutam means judging by scripture. Jñānam means omniscience, having direct experience of everything and gratitude, etc. Virakti means absence of taste for material enjoyment. Aiśvaryam is control of others. Śauryam is determination in battle. Tejas is being able to overpower others. Balam is śakti to preserve everything. Smṛti means searching out what should be done as duty. Svātantryam is non-dependence on others. Kauśalam is skill in activities. Kānti is beauty, in which all limbs are properly placed. Dhairyam is not breaking promises. Mārdavam is being tender or not tolerating separation from devotees. Prāgalbhyam is extreme boldness. Praśraya is mildness. Śīla means good natured. A great person has intense affection for those in front of him even though they are slow witted. Sahas is sharpness of mind. Ojas is sharpness of knowledge senses. Balam is sharpness of action senses. Bhaga is being the shelter of enjoyment. Gāmbhiryam means imperturbed by devotee’s offenses or the revealers of those offenses. Sthairyam is not being fickle or having only one object of affection. Āstikyam is faith in scriptures and its established meaning. Kīrti is fame because of good qualities. Māna is being worthy of worship by all. Anahaṅkṛti is absence of pride. These qualites and others not stated, are eternal. Other qualities include being true to one’s word and respecting brāhmaṇas. These great qualities are eternal—existing over all time in the Lord. Each one is complete. They are praised (prārthyāḥ) by those who desire greatness. Praising those qualities, they attain greatness. Even at pralaya the qualities do not disappear because they are under the Lord’s shelter and because they are intimately related to the Lord, like heat is related to the sun. The people are now devoid of the Lord, the abode of good qualities (guṇa-pātreṇa) and are afflicted by (īkṣitam) sinful Kali. \]

### 1.16.31

**Перевод:**

**O best of the devatās! I lament for myself and you, and for the devatās, pitṛs, sages, devotees and all the varṇas and āśramas.**

### 1.16.32

**Перевод:**

**Lakṣmī, desiring whose glance Brahmā and others, surrendered to the Lord for a long time, perform great penance, has left her abode of the lotus forest and with great attachment worships the beauty of Kṛṣṇa’s feet.**

**Комментарий:**

I cannot tolerate separation from the Lord. This is expressed in four verses (33-36). Though Brahmā and others have surrendered to the Lord for a long time (bahu-titham), being devotees with material desires, they desire the glance of Lakṣmī.

### 1.16.33

**Перевод:**

**Being ornamented with the footprints of the Lord marked with lotus, thunderbolt, goad, flag, I surpassed the three worlds in beauty. After I attained glory from the Lord, he left me who had become proud, even after the glory had dissipated.**

**Комментарий:**

I was ornamented with the footprints marked (ketaiḥ) with the flag (ketu) lotus, goad and thunderbolt. Attaining vibhūtis from Kṛṣṇa (tataḥ), surpassing the three worlds, I became beautiful. After that, with the destruction of the vibhūti, when I became proud thinking that Vaikuṇṭha could not equal me, he gave me up and disappeared.

### 1.16.34

**Перевод:**

**The independent Lord destroyed my great burden of a hundred akṣauhiṇīs of those who were previously of demonic dynasty of kings. By his prowess he made you whole when you were suffering with loss of your legs. He appeared with a beautiful form in the Yadu family.**

**Комментарий:**

He destroyed my heavy burden. By prowess in himself, he made you whole with your four legs (sampādayan), who were miserable with deficient legs. He manifested a beautiful form in the Yadu dynasty.

### 1.16.35

**Перевод:**

**Who can tolerate separation from that supreme person whose foot prints made my hair stand on end constantly, while he took away the pride and self-control of his queens who were proud by his loving glances, pleasant smiles and sweet speech?**

**Комментарий:**

Who could tolerate separation from the Lord? He took away the steadiness of the queens like Satyabhāmā (mandu-māninīnām) along with their pride (samānam) by his glances of prema. He made my hairs stand on end (romotsavaḥ). I was decorated (viṭaṅkitāyāḥ) with his feet. I constantly had the good fortune of the touch of his feet, among all the dear women. How can I tolerate separation from him now?

### 1.16.36

*tayor evaṁ kathayatoḥ pṛthivī-dharmayos tadā \| parīkṣin nāma rājarṣiḥ prāptaḥ prācīṁ sarasvatīm \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**While Earth and Dharma were conversing, the king named Parīkṣit came to the Sarasvatī River flowing east.**

**Комментарий:**

Tayoḥ means Earth and Dharma. Prācīm here means “flowing east.”

## Chapter Seventeen - The Punishment of Kali

### 1.17.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: At Kurukṣetra the king saw the śūdra dressed as a king beating an unprotected cow and a bull with a stick held in his hand.**

**Комментарий:**

In the seventeenth chapter the questions and answers between Parīkṣit and Dharma, and the punishment and mercy received by Kali are described. Hanyamānām means “beating.” The śūdra wore the markings of a king like crown, etc., which broke the custom followed in Satya, Treta and Dvāpara yugas. It indicated his independent nature.

### 1.17.2

**Перевод:**

**The śūdra was beating the bull, white like a water lily, fearful, trembling, weak and standing on one leg, who appeared to be urinating.**

**Комментарий:**

Mehantam iva means “as if urinating.” This means that Dharma, standing on one leg, was at every moment flowing away. Trembling of the bull indicated that the bull did not oppose the actions of Kali.

### 1.17.3

**Перевод:**

**He saw the cow, giver of ghee for sacrifice, without a calf, suffering greatly, as she was being kicked by the śūdra. The thin cow, with tears in her eyes, desired the share of sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

Dharma-dughām means giver of sacrificial ingredients such as ghee, milk, etc. She (Earth) was without a calf (vivatsām) because the grains had been destroyed. She had tears in her eyes because of the destruction of dharma. She was thin (kṣāmām) because sacrifices were not performed. She desired the share of sacrifice (yavasam).

### 1.17.4

**Перевод:**

**Situated in his chariot, with his bow poised, in a voice deep as thunder, he questioned the śūdra dressed in gold.**

**Комментарий:**

Kārtasvara means gold. He was dressed in gold. He was on his chariot. He had his bow ready, worried that the śūdra Kali would try to flee.

### 1.17.5

**Перевод:**

**Who are you, having power? You attack the weak with force in the world under my protection. Like an actor, you appear to be a king by dress, but by actions you are a śūdra.**

**Комментарий:**

“Who are you to commit violence to the weak in my presence?” “I am a king.” “Since I am the king, how can you be the king?” “I am king because I am wearing the dress of a king and am acting like one.” “No! You are a śūdra (advijaḥ) by your actions.” A real actor imitates the actions of another as truly as possible.

### 1.17.6

**Перевод:**

**Since Kṛṣṇa has departed with Arjuna, you are in pitiable position. By beating innocent creatures in a solitary place, you deserve to be killed.**

**Комментарий:**

“Just as you are king of your country, I am king of this time. Thus you do not have power over me.” The king answers in this verse. “Till Kṛṣṇa departed out of sight with Arjuna, where were you?” “I was present, but I did not reveal myself because of fear of them. But without them, being independent, I fear no one today.” “You are worthy of lamentation. You desire to die today.”

### 1.17.7

**Перевод:**

**You are white like a water lily, and move around on one leg without the other legs in the form of a bull. Are you some devatā coming to afflict us by such an appearance?**

**Комментарий:**

Let that offense be. I will think for a moment. The king then inquired from the bull. You are white like a lotus root. Who are you? He then suggests a possibility. Moving on one leg, devoid of three legs, are you some devatā who has come to give us pain? I have not seen a bull suffering like you.

### 1.17.8

**Перевод:**

**Except for your tears, the tears of living beings have never fallen on the earth which was ruled by the Kuru kings and well protected by arms of punishment.**

**Комментарий:**

“While you are the king, we have now become miserable.” The king then speaks with pride mixed with regret. Except for your tears, tears have not fallen in the land which was well protected (parirambhite) by arms of punishment.

### 1.17.9

**Перевод:**

**O son of Surabhī! Do not lament! Do not fear the śūdra! O mother! Do not weep! Since I am here as the punisher of sinners, you will have good fortune.**

**Комментарий:**

He then spoke consoling words to the bull who was pointing out the weeping cow who was just like him, saying “What you say is false”. O son of Surabhī! Do not lament (śucaḥ for śocaḥ). Do not fear the śūdra. He then speaks to the cow. O mother! Do no cry. While I am living as the punisher (mayi śāstari) of the wicked, you will have auspiciousness. I will kill him now.

### 1.17.10-11

**Перевод:**

**O cow! The inattentive ruler in whose state all the citizens suffer from criminals loses his fame, lifespan, fortune and progress. This is the highest duty of the kings, to punish those who cause suffering to others. Therefore, I will kill that sinful killer.**

**Комментарий:**

“But if you kill him for our happiness, then you make us implicated in killing.” He replies. O chaste cow! The ruler loses good fortune (bhagaḥ) and next life promotion (gatiḥ) when the citizens suffer. I must kill him (enam) for my benefit. There is no fault for you in that. Enam means the śūdra who is afflicting you.

### 1.17.12

**Перевод:**

**Son of Surabhī! You have four legs. Who has cut off three of your legs? There should never be such a condition in the state ruled by kings following Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Avṛścat means cut. A suffering animal like you should not exist in the state ruled by a king following Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.17.13

**Перевод:**

**O bull! May you, who is innocent and of pure character, have good fortune! Tell me who has disfigured you, who has spoiled the fame of the Pāṇḍavas.**

**Комментарий:**

Tell me the criminal who is spoiling the fame of the Pāṇḍavas. Who is the person who disfigured you by cutting off your legs?

### 1.17.14

**Перевод:**

**He who commits violence against the innocent must certainly fear me in all ways. By punishing the sinful, the innocent will be peaceful.**

**Комментарий:**

“If I say, he will fear you. What is the use in that?” He who harms the innocent will fear me by all means.

### 1.17.15

**Перевод:**

**I will cut off the arms along with the armbands of the person who without regard for rules commits violence against the innocent beings, even if he is a devatā.**

**Комментарий:**

“What happens if there is someone stronger that you?” Even if the person is a devatā, I will cut off his arms at the root—with the armbands (sāṅgadam). Actually, there is no person among men and devatās who is as strong as me.

### 1.17.16

**Перевод:**

**The highest duty of the king is to protect those who follow the rules given to them in the scriptures, and to punish the sinful who deviate from the scriptures when there is no emergency.**

**Комментарий:**

“What is the use in punishing one person and showing mercy to another person?” This verse answers. He punishes those who are fixed in adharma, deviating from the path of the Vedas when there is no emergency. He punishes according to scriptures.

### 1.17.17

**Перевод:**

**Dharma said: These words which make one fearless of suffering are suitable for you, descendent of the Pāṇḍavas. Being attracted to their qualities of prema, Kṛṣṇa engaged in playing dice and other activities.**

**Комментарий:**

Kṛṣṇa was attracted to all the Pāṇḍavas’ qualities which were filled with prema (guṇa-gaṇaiḥ). Because Kṛṣṇa is controlled only by prema, and since you are the son of Arjuna fixed in Kṛṣṇa, nothing is impossible for you.

### 1.17.18

**Перевод:**

**O best of men! I do not know the person who is the cause of my suffering, since I am bewildered by different statements of scripture.**

**Комментарий:**

He answers the question “Who has cut off your legs?” I do not know the person (yataḥ) from whom the causes of suffering (kleśa-bījāni) arise. I suffer from the śūdra but the seed of that suffering is something else. I do not know that. “But you know scriptures. Why do you not know?” I am bewildered by different scriptural statements.

### 1.17.19

**Перевод:**

**Some say the self is the cause of suffering. Others say planets are the cause of suffering. Others say karma is the cause. Others say the svabhāva is the cause.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the different opinions are described. Some, atheists, who cover up (vasanā) the difference (vikalpa) between jīva and the Lord, the followers of Kapila, say the self gives suffering to the self. Their philosophy is that the ātmā attached to prakṛti suffers. By discrimination, giving up prakṛti one no longer suffers. Others, astrologers, say that fate is the cause. Their philosophy is that cruel planets cause suffering. Auspicious planets, worshipped, take away suffering. Others fixed in karma say karmas are the cause. Their philosophy is that suffering arises from inauspicious karmas. From auspicious karmas one attains happiness of Svarga. Others, the Buddhists, say that svabhāva is the master. The principle is that effects do not arise from a cause. Their philosophy is that when actions are performed to destroy suffering, the actions come to an end even without destroying the suffering and even after repeatedly uniting, offspring are not born whereas living entities are spontaneously born from sweat. Thus suffering and its absence arise from svabhāva.

### 1.17.20

**Перевод:**

**And among these, some ascertain that happiness and distress come from that which is beyond reason and perception, O sage among kings! Consider the proper truth by your intelligence.**

**Комментарий:**

Among these, some followers of the Vedas, who are convinced of the Lord, determine that suffering and its destruction arise from the Lord, who cannot be determined completely (anirdeśyāt) and whose plan is difficult to understand because of his inconceivable śakti (apratarkyāt), after hearing about jīva following its karma. eṣa u evāsādhu karmakārayati tam yam adho ninīyate He makes him do bad actions who he wants to lead to hell. (Kauśitaki Upaniṣad 3.8) O king of the sages! Please consider with your intelligence this without obstruction.

### 1.17.21

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: O best of the brāhmaṇas! When Dharma, the bull, spoke in this manner, the king, free from illusion by putting his mind in meditation, answered.**

**Комментарий:**

When Dharma spoke in this way, King Parīkṣit (samrāṭ), by a mind absorbed in the five causes of suffering mentioned (samāhitena), spoke to Dharma. Then he became free of all doubts (vikhedaḥ). The following should be said. In the contact with and separation from prakṛti, the Lord (as puruṣa) and jīva (the doer) are not the cause of suffering and its destruction since even after leaving prakṛti and again after contacting prakṛti, just like before the contact, the two are always present as powerful entities. Planets are not the cause since the planets are not independent and depend on the wheel of time. Karma is not the cause of suffering since it is destroyed in a moment. Svabhāva is not the cause since it is insentient. Therefore, the Lord is the cause. His plan is difficult to understand since he is beyond logic. Bhīṣma says na cāsya karhicit rājan pumān veda vidhisitam: O King, man cannot know the Lord’s plan. Is Dharma’s discussion of suffering for increasing remembrance? Or is it for spreading the glory of how I, his servant, will punish Kali? Dharma does not have any sin to undergo suffering. If so, he could not have his position in control of dharma.

### 1.17.22

**Перевод:**

**The king said: O knower of dharma! You speak dharma (by not telling me the identity of your aggressor), because the person who points out the sinner attains the same place as the sinner. You are dharma in the form of a bull.**

**Комментарий:**

He clarifies what he has understood. Though knowing the aggressor, one should not say. I have understood that you are Dharma, because you speak about dharma. “Why do I not speak? “He who hints at the sinner attains hell, the place of the sinner, what to speak of the person who speaks directly.”

### 1.17.23

**Перевод:**

**It is certain that the course of the Lord’s māyā is beyond the thought and words of the living entities.**

**Комментарий:**

You have said everything (athavā). The movement of the Lord’s māyā (deva-māyāyāḥ) is beyond the mind and words of living beings. It is apratarkya and anirdeśya. Who can understand or explain why the Lord, by his śakti of māyā, gives happiness and suffering in the form of maintenance and destruction to the living beings?

### 1.17.24

**Перевод:**

**In Satya-yuga you had complete dharma of four portions with four elements: austerity, cleanliness, mercy and truth. Three portions are broken by three portions of irreligion: pride, association with women, and intoxication.**

**Комментарий:**

I know all your fortune and misfortune which you have not told me. Please hear. Two verses (24-25) describe this. Three portions of your feet – austerity, cleanliness and mercy are broken by three parts of adharma: pride (smaya), association with women (saṅga) and intoxication (madaiḥ).

### 1.17.25

**Перевод:**

**O Dharma! Now in Kali-yuga truth remains prominent on the one remaining portion of dharma. But Kali, irreligion, increasing by untruth, desires to devour even truth.**

**Комментарий:**

O Dharma! Now, in Kali-yuga, of your four parts of dharma, only truth remains, since you maintain yourself to some degree by truth. Or by that truth, a person can accomplish you (dharma). Kali (adharmaḥ), increasing with untruth, desires to destroy that one leg. According to the twelfth canto the following can be established. In Satya-yuga, dharma was complete with four legs. In Treta, gradually one fourth of the four legs of austerity, cleanliness and mercy, decreases by pride, association with women, intoxication and lying. In Dvāpara, they decrease by half and in Kali they remain only in one quarter amount.Items are sometimes named because of their prominence. Truth is named here because it is now more prominent (though the other three factors of dharma are also present).

### 1.17.26

**Перевод:**

**The cow is earth. The Lord relieved her of a great burden. She became most auspicious in all ways by the auspicious touch of his feet.**

**Комментарий:**

The earth, her great burden of demons removed by Kṛṣṇa (bhagavatā), became auspicious (kṛta-kautukā) by the placement of his beautiful feet.

### 1.17.27

*śocaty aśru-kalā sādhvī durbhagevojjhitādhunā\| abrahmaṇyā nṛpa-vyājāḥ śūdrā bhokṣyanti mām iti \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**With tears in her eyes, the chaste Earth filled with misfortune, being abandoned by the Lord now, laments, “Śūdras dressed as kings with no respect for brāhmaṇas enjoy me.”**

**Комментарий:**

Now, being abandoned by the Lord, the unfortunate (durbhagā) earth, weeping with tears, laments, “Śūdras enjoy me.”

### 1.17.28

**Перевод:**

**Pacifying Dharma and Earth in this way, Parīkṣit took his sharp sword for killing Kali, the cause of adharma.**

**Комментарий:**

He took his sword in order to kill Kali (kalaye).

### 1.17.29

**Перевод:**

**Seeing the king intending to kill him he gave up the markings of a king in great fear and bowed his head to Parīkṣit’s feet.**

**Комментарий:**

Understanding the king’s intention (abhipretya), Kali gave up his marks of a king such as his crown.

### 1.17.30

**Перевод:**

**The praiseworthy warrior, worthy of taking shelter, affectionate to the helpless, did not kill him out of his mercy. He spoke to him with a slight smile.**

**Комментарий:**

The king, worthy of praise, did not kill (ślokyaḥ). The king considered, “Since he has surrendered, I cannot kill him. Since he is wicked, if I will kill him, taking shelter of adharma arising from killing him, he will enter me and will not die.” He slightly smiled, because he had not completely given up his anger. “This plan has been made by the Lord. Today you have been protected from my hand, though I want to kill you.”

### 1.17.31

**Перевод:**

**The king said: Coming with folded hands in front of the sustainer of Arjuna’s glory, you should have no fear at all. But you, friend of irreligion, cannot stay in my kingdom at all.**

**Комментарий:**

Contemplating his goal, he spoke. You cannot stay by any degree in this place because you are the friend of adharma.

### 1.17.32

**Перевод:**

**When you become situated in kings, all sorts of irreligion follow: greed, untruth, theft, vulgarity, giving up dharma, poverty, deceit, quarrel and pride.**

**Комментарий:**

All sorts of adharma follow you who exist in the bodies of kings (nara-deva). Anārya means wicked. Aṁhah means sin in the form of giving up one’s sva-dharma. Jyeṣṭhā means poverty. Māyā means deceit.

### 1.17.33

**Перевод:**

**You cannot stay in Brahmāvarta where dharma and truth suitably reside, and where knowers of sacrifice worship Kṛṣṇa with sacrifice.**

**Комментарий:**

You cannot stay in Brahmāvarta, suitable for living with dharma and truth. Yajñeśvara (lord of sacrifice) is Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.17.34

**Перевод:**

**You cannot reside where the Lord, Bhagavān, who is the soul of the devatās, who exists inside and outside like the air, being worshipped, distributes to the worshipper,material and spiritual prosperity—what is useful to all moving and non-moving beings.**

**Комментарий:**

“But the devatās should also be worshipped, not just Bhagavān.” He is the inspiration of those who are worshipped (devatās) (ijyātmtamūrtiḥ). He distributes unfailing enjoyment to our subjects, moving and non-moving beings. He is perceived internally and externally like air. If you exist here, this will no longer be the situation.

### 1.17.35

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Directed in this way by Parīkṣit, trembling Kali spoke to Parīkṣit who had his sword raised, appearing like Yama ready to kill him.**

**Комментарий:**

Parīkṣit had his sword raised (udyatāsim). Daṇḍa-pāṇim means Yama.

### 1.17.36

**Перевод:**

**Kali said: O king of the whole earth! Wherever I will live, following your order, I will see only you with bow and arrow in your hand.**

**Комментарий:**

O king of the whole earth! If I leave Brahmāvarta, where will I reside? There also I see you with bow and arrow in your hand. You, controller of the whole earth, will give pain to me, your citizen.

### 1.17.37

**Перевод:**

**Best of upholders of dharma! You should designate for me that place where I can reside permanently abiding by your order.**

**Комментарий:**

“Say what I should do.” Give me a place where I can stay without moving (niyataḥ).

### 1.17.38

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: He then gave to Kali who had made the request places where there were four types of irreligion: gambling, wine, unmarried women and slaughter of animals.**

**Комментарий:**

Pānam means drinking wine and other things. Striyaḥ means unchaste women. Sūnāḥ is unprescribed violence. With violence to living entities comes all adharmas. Those who commit violence to living entities have no austerity, cleanliness, mercy or truthful speaking.

### 1.17.39

**Перевод:**

**When implored again, the king gave the place where there was gold. In that place there will be untruthfulness, intoxication, illicit sex, pride, and enmity.**

**Комментарий:**

“O King! Having heard this news, no one will perform gambling, drinking, illicit sex or animal killing. What you gave is not giving anything.” The king gave wealth represented by gold (jāta-rūpam) as the place for Kali, who begged again. Since you have sheltered by me, who maintain my fame, trust that this place, being given, will not be taken away. If you do not act according to my order, you will be killed easily. In persons with wealth these adharmas will appear. Anṛta means lying. Mada means intoxication due to drinking liquor. Kāma means association with women. Rajas is pride. These are the four adharmas. The fifth is enmity (vairam). Mada and kāma in neuter gender is poetic license.

### 1.17.40

**Перевод:**

**Kali, propagator of irreligion, following the order of the king, took up residence in these five places given by the son of Uttarā.**

**Комментарий:**

Amūni pañca sthānāni should actually be in locative case.

### 1.17.41

**Перевод:**

**The person who desires his own benefit, and particularly the religious king, the protector of people and the teacher, should not indulge in these five items.**

**Комментарий:**

Bubhūṣuḥ means “desiring his own benefit.” Loka-pati means protector of people. Guru is a teacher. They particularly should not indulge in these sins. “Not serving gold or one’s wife” means not being attached to them. Others’ wives must be given up completely.

### 1.17.42

**Перевод:**

**The king restored the three destroyed legs of the bull: austerity, cleanliness and mercy. Comforting the earth, he made her prosperous.**

**Комментарий:**

Punishing Kali, he restored (pratisandadha) the legs of the bull.

### 1.17.43-44

**Перевод:**

**He is presently sitting on the throne given by his grandfather King Yudhiṣṭhira who desired to go to the forest. This sage among kings, who is greatly fortunate, famous, ruling the world, shining with the wealth of a Kuru king, is now residing in Hastināpura.**

**Комментарий:**

Because of his influence, you can perform sacrifice here. He is present on his throne given by Yudhiṣṭhira (pitāmahena). Adhyāste means “he is presently protecting the kingdom.” The present tense is used because of nearness to the present (Parīkṣit had already disappeared).

### 1.17.45

**Перевод:**

**This king, the son of Abhimanyu, who, by protecting the earth, has allowed you to be consecrated for performing sacrifice, had such authority.**

**Комментарий:**

Satrāya means for performing sacrifice. This is different from the sacrifice which Balarāma saw. There has never been and never will be a person like Parīkṣit, who saw Kṛṣṇa in the womb and who punished Kali.

## Chapter Eighteen - The Brāhmaṇa’s Curse

### 1.18.1-3

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Parīkṣit, who, being burned up by the weapon of Aśvatthāmā, did not die in his mother’s womb because of the mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa, performer of astonishing actions; who, because he had taken shelter of the Lord, did not become bewildered with great fear from the life threatening snake called Takṣaka called by the curse of the brāhmaṇa; who became the student of Śukadeva, and giving up all material association and being situated in realization of the Lord, gave up his body on the bank of the Gaṅgā.**

**Комментарий:**

In the eighteenth chapter, it is described that the thirsty king places a snake around the neck of the sage, and, returning home, is cursed by the sage’s son. Sūta speaks in summary of the astonishing story of Parīkṣit’s life from birth till attaining the Lord to Śaunaka and other sages who were very astonished at hearing his punishment of Kali. 388 Vaiṣṇavānandinī \[Ch.18 Becoming the pupil of Śukadeva (vaiyāsakeḥ śiṣyaḥ), giving up material association, he gave up his body on the bank of the Gaṅgā. He was in a condition (saṁsthitiḥ) where he realized (vijñāta) Kṛṣṇa (ajita).

### 1.18.4

**Перевод:**

**Those who remember the lotus feet of the Lord; those who relish the nectar of pastimes concerning the Lord; and those whose very lives are the Lord do not have confusion even at the time of death.**

**Комментарий:**

This is not astonishing. This verse gives the reason. Uttamaślokavārtānām means “of those whose cause of living is the Lord.”

### 1.18.5

**Перевод:**

**As long as Abhimanyu’s son, the great lord, was the king of the earth, Kali, though he had entered everywhere, could not influence anyone.**

**Комментарий:**

After Parīkṣit punished Kali, what was his situation? This verse describes the condition.

### 1.18.6

**Перевод:**

**On the day that the Lord left the earth, at that very instant, Kali, the source of irreligion, entered the world.**

**Комментарий:**

This verse describes Kali’s entrance. Gām means earth.

### 1.18.7

**Перевод:**

**The king did not kill Kali. He was like a bee, enjoying the honey— for at this time he understood that auspicious acts bear results quickly, whereas austerity and yoga to not bear results quickly.**

**Комментарий:**

“What was the condition of Parīkṣit after punishing Kali?” He did not kill Kali because he was like a bee (sāraṅgaḥ), taking the essence. The essence is described. In Kali (yat) auspicious acts like worship of the Lord, predominated by kīrtana quickly yield results and other actions like austerity and yoga (itarāṇi) do not give results. People engaged in material work do not heed the auspicious actions of your worship, found in Pañcarātra scriptures made by you, and authorized by you as the correct form of worship. I offer my respects to the Lord who as powerful time quickly destroys the aspiration to live for these nondevotees. (SB [3.9.17](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/9.17))

### 1.18.8

**Перевод:**

**Kali was an attentive tiger among inattentive men. But what could powerful Kali do to the innocent people since he was afraid of the devotees?**

**Комментарий:**

The king had another intention. Was there a disturbance to the foolish people by Kali? There was none because Kali feared those who were discerning in nature. Kali was a vigilant tiger among the careless.

### 1.18.9

**Перевод:**

**I have described to you what you had asked concerning pure Parīkṣit, which is related to the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa.**

### 1.18.10

**Перевод:**

**Those who desire their own benefit should hear those topics concerning the qualities and activities of the Lord who performed the greatest actions.**

**Комментарий:**

Bubhūṣubhiḥ means “by those who desire their own good.” To do anything other than this is to waste one’s life.

### 1.18.11

**Перевод:**

**The sages said: O auspicious Sūta! Live for innumerable years, since you narrate to us, who are mortals, the shining glories of Kṛṣṇa!**

**Комментарий:**

Live for uncountable years (śāśvatīḥ samāḥ). Accusative case is used to denote full duration of time according to the rule kālādhvanor atyanta-saṁyoga (Pāṇini 2.3.5) You narrate the glories of Kṛṣṇa which destroy ignorance (viśadam) and destroy death (amṛtam) for mortals.

### 1.18.12

**Перевод:**

**You let us, blackened by the smoke at this uncertain sacrifice, drink the intoxicating nectar of the lotus feet of Govinda.**

**Комментарий:**

At this performance of sacrifice (karmaṇi) which is not credible (anāśvāse) because the result cannot be guaranteed due to many irregularities (whereas the results of bhakti are certain), you let us, whose eyes and other limbs are discolored by the smoke from the sacrifice, drink the nectar. The genitive case here indicates accusative case. That one should have faith in bhakti is shown. The results of bhakti are directly attained. They drink the nectar. It is intoxicating (madhu) honey (āsavam) since it gives us something unexperienced elsewhere and we experience the taste at every moment.

### 1.18.13

**Перевод:**

**Let us not compare even a particle of devotee association to Svarga or liberation, what to speak of any blessings in this world.**

**Комментарий:**

The great glory of associating with devotees is described. Imperative form of the verb (tulayāma) is used to indicate supposition. We cannot compare a moment of association of devotees, which showers the sweetness of topics about Kṛṣṇa to the results of karma – Svarga or liberation with predominance of happiness and power – the result of jñāna. We cannot even imagine it. No one compares water or buttermilk to a sweet drink mixed with camphor, pepper and cardamom. That being so, what can one say about blessings from mortals, such as kingdom? In this world if bhakti were impossible from even a particle of association of devotees, who are the cause of bhakti, then in the rarity of prolonged association, its result – bhakti – would be established as extremely rare. Because this fact is established by many there is no doubt at all. Thus the plural (tulayāma) is used. Association with devotees (bhagavatsaṅgī-saṅgasya) is stronger than association with the Lord. Contact with a heated iron is more intense that contact with fire. That is indicated by the word saṅgi-saṅga.

### 1.18.14

**Перевод:**

**What knower of rasa could be satisfied with the topics of the Lord who is the sole, supreme shelter of the greatest devotees? Even those who are masters of yoga and the devatās headed by Brahmā and Śiva, cannot find an end to the spiritual qualities of the Lord who is without material qualities.**

**Комментарий:**

“From devotee association one attains a taste for the rasa of Kṛṣṇa’s topics. That taste was attained by you.” What knower of rasa could be satisfied with topics of Kṛṣṇa? He cannot be satisfied. Kṛṣṇa is the sole shelter of the great devotees like Nārada and others. They take shelter of him definitely as the supreme (parāyaṇasya). He is the lord of all beings and has an eternal, most attractive form. The taste is not attained just by association one time, since the Lord is the cause of unlimited qualities and the topics are unlimited. Masters of yoga cannot find the end of his qualities related to his svarūpa, such as his power over all, his omniscience, his affection, his friendship with devotees, all devoid of any touch of māyā (aguṇasya). Thus one should pray for association of devotees always.

### 1.18.15

**Перевод:**

**O learned one! You are the one who prominently serves the Lord among us! Please describe in detail to us who desire to hear the great, pure and transcendental activities of the Lord who is the supreme shelter of the greatest devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

Therefore (tat), among us, you are the chief devotee, accepting the Lord as master, being devoted solely to him. O giver (udāra)! Udāra means a giver according to Amara-kośa. Distribute the pure pastimes of the Lord to us who desire to hear them (śuśrūṣatām). Udāra can also mean great. The rest is clear.

### 1.18.16

**Перевод:**

**Parīkṣit, the great devotee, with pure intelligence, attained liberation, in the form of the feet of the Lord having a flag marked with Garuḍa, through knowledge spoken by Śukadeva.**

**Комментарий:**

Stories of the devotees mixed with stories of the Lord, tasty like sweet yogurt, take away all suffering. Please speak of them to us. Parīkṣit attained the feet of Kṛṣṇa who had a flag marked with Garuḍa by the great pastimes of the Lord (yena) by knowledge taught by Śukadeva. Jñāna here means that by which the Lord is realized. The feet are described. They are called apavarga since they destroy māyā. This clarifies the previous request: We desire to hear about the birth, activities, passing away and destination of this highly intelligent devotee, which Śukadeva narrated to you. Please tell us, full of faith, if you desire to tell this. (SB [1.12.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/12.2))

### 1.18.17

**Перевод:**

**Please tell us clearly the mostpure story of Parīkṣit containing unlimited pastimes, which is devoted to astonishing bhakti and which is attractive to the devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

Please tell us the story of Parīkṣit as it is (asaṁvṛtārtham), in which astonishing bhakti-yoga is situated and which is attractive to the devotees.

### 1.18.18

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: Born of mixed castes, we have certainly made our birth successful today by following after Śukadeva, for glorification of great devotees quickly destroys the mental pains arising from low birth.**

**Комментарий:**

Respected by great sages, he praises his good fortune to have the opportunity to explain the Bhāgavatam. Aho indicates astonishment. Ha indicates bliss. Though we are born of mixed caste (viloma-jātāḥ), today our births have become (āsmaḥ) successful by following you, wise in knowledge (vṛddhānuvṛttyā), because this collection of conversations (abhidhāna-yogaḥ) with great devotees like you, even though in common language, destroys mental affliction (ādhim) caused by birth in a low family.

### 1.18.19

**Перевод:**

**What doubt is there about this for one who chants the name of the unlimited Lord, who is the shelter of the greatest devotees, who has unlimited power, and who is called unlimited because he bestows his unlimited qualities to the devotee?**

**Комментарий:**

“But how can this narration destroy the prārabdha-karma which causes the bad birth?” The śakti to destroy prārabdha-karma of devotees is given by the Lord. Those talks destroy bad birth for the person who chants the name of Kṛṣṇa in the assembly of great devotees. What more should be considered? Kṛṣṇa’s power is explained. He is Bhagavān, full of six opulences. He is all-pervading (anantaḥ). He is called Ananta because his unlimited qualities are given to the devotees (mahad-guṇatvāt). Because of giving to the devotees his power to destroy prārabdha of the devotee, my karma of bad birth has been destroyed now by devotees’ association.

### 1.18.20

**Перевод:**

**Enough with delineating the Lord, who has no equal or superior in qualities! Ignoring Brahmā and others who pray to her, even Lakṣmī, serves the dust of the feet of this Lord who has no material desire.**

**Комментарий:**

Since the Lord’s śakti is unlimited, no one can describe him, but only to some extent. Lakṣmī (vibhūtiḥ), attracted to the qualities of the Lord, although having no desires, giving up Brahmā and others (itarān) who pray to her, serves (juṣate) the dust of his feet. He has no equal or superior (sāmyānatiśayanasya). No one is equal to him, what to speak of being superior to him.

### 1.18.21

**Перевод:**

**Who can be called by the name Bhagavān except Mukunda whose toe-nail water purifies the universe along with Śiva and becomes arghya for Brahmā?**

**Комментарий:**

Since he is the Supreme Lord, Lakṣmī, giving up all others, serves only him. The water coming from his toenail arghya, offered by Brahmā, purifies the world including Śiva (seśam). Who in this world except Mukunda has the name Bhagavān? He is the Supreme Lord since Lakṣmī, Brahmā and Śiva, the best living entities serve his feet.

### 1.18.22

**Перевод:**

**The devotees who are attracted to Kṛṣṇa, immediately giving up strong attachment to body and other material objects, attain the highest stage of life, parama-haṁsa, in which lack of hatred and absorption in Kṛṣṇa are the natural qualities.**

**Комментарий:**

Thus the sober devotees worship him. Quickly giving up, by the strength of knowledge, association with bodies attained over beginningless time (ūḍham), the sober devotees, attracted to him, attain the fourth stage of life (antyam), parama-haṁsa, in which non-violence, etc., are the natural qualities.

### 1.18.23

**Перевод:**

**You are like many suns! I will speak the knowledge requested by you according to what I have understood. Just as birds fly high in the sky only according to their ability, so the wise speak the pastimes of the Lord only according to their limited ability.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way, praising his good fortune and speaking of the Lord’s powers, Sūta answers Śaunaka’s question. O Śaunaka and others like suns (aryamaṇah)! I will speak what was asked by you, according to how much I have understood. Just as birds fly in the sky according to their ability and not to the topmost height of the sky, even those who are wise speak the Lord’s pastimes (gatim) according to their intelligence (samam).

### 1.18.24

**Перевод:**

**One day while taking his bow and hunting in the forest, after pursuing animals, he became tired, hungry and very thirsty.**

### 1.18.25

**Перевод:**

**Not seeing water anywhere, he entered the hermitage of a sage, and saw the peaceful sage sitting with closed eyes.**

**Комментарий:**

Acakṣāṇaḥ means “not seeing.” The sage was Śamīka.

### 1.18.26

**Перевод:**

**The sage had withdrawn his senses, life air, mind and intelligence after controlling them. Having surpassed the three states, he had entered into trance and realized Brahman. He did not move at all.**

**Комментарий:**

He had surpassed the three states of waking, sleeping and deep sleep and realized (prāptam) the fourth state, satyam (param bhūtam), Brahman.

### 1.18.27

**Перевод:**

**Parīkṣit, having a dry palate, requested water from that the sage covered with unkempt, matted locks of hair and dressed in deer skin.**

**Комментарий:**

He was covered with the skin of the ruru, a type of antelope (rauravena). His palate dry, Parīkṣit asked for water.

### 1.18.28

**Перевод:**

**Thinking that he had been disrespected by not being offered a grass seat, arghya or friendly words, the king became angry.**

**Комментарий:**

Not being shown a sitting place of grass, water or even shown a place to stand, not receiving kind words or arghya, thinking that the sage had done nothing to welcome him and was disrespecting him, he became angry.

### 1.18.29

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇa! Afflicted by hunger and thirst, he suddenly became angry and spiteful towards the brāhmaṇa as never before.**

**Комментарий:**

Matsaraḥ means to not tolerate another person’s excellence. Spiteful to the brāhmaṇa, he became angry, though he had never shown anger to a brāhmaṇa previously. The meaning is this. This wicked act was not possible for Parīkṣit. But for preaching Bhāgavatam, for spreading the fame of Śukadeva, and for bringing the dear devotee to the Lord, the Lord made this arrangement. Parīkṣit himself explains this: For me, whose impure action was the sin of disrespecting an innocent brāhmaṇa, and whose heart was very attached to family life, the Lord has come in the form of the brāhmaṇa’s curse, which has become the cause of detachment from material life. By the Lord’s presence the attached person quickly becomes fearless. (SB [1.19.14](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/19.14))

### 1.18.30

**Перевод:**

**In anger he placed a dead snake on the shoulder of the sage using the tip of his bow, left the hermitage and returned to his city.**

**Комментарий:**

Gatāsum means dead.

### 1.18.31

**Перевод:**

**Was he actually closing his eyes in a state of withdrawing his senses? Or was he feigning trance, perhaps because a fallen kṣatriya had come?**

**Комментарий:**

After the king had thrown the dead snake on the sage he began to reflect. Has the sage withdrawn all his senses (nibhṛtāśeṣakaraṇaḥ)? Did he close his eyes on seeing me, a fallen kṣatriya? Is it false samādhi? Having thrown the snake, he wanted to know the truth.

### 1.18.32

**Перевод:**

**The sage’s powerful son, playing with other young boys, hearing that the king had offended his father, spoke the following words to them.**

**Комментарий:**

His son was Śṛṅgī. He heard that his father had been disrespected (agham prāpitam).

### 1.18.33

**Перевод:**

**This is the sin of kings who are like fat crows! It is a sin committed by servants or door-keepers, who are like dogs, acting against their masters.**

**Комментарий:**

Pālānām means kings. Pīvnām means fat. Bali-bhujām means crows.

### 1.18.34

**Перевод:**

**The fallen kṣatriya is designated as a dog by the brāhmaṇas. How can he, guarding the door, enter the house and eat the food in the pots?**

**Комментарий:**

The fallen kṣatriya is classed as a dog (dvāra-pālaḥ) by the brāhmaṇas. How can he enter the house and eat the ghee, etc., in the pots?

### 1.18.35

**Перевод:**

**Since Lord Kṛṣṇa, the punisher of those who deviate from the path, has departed, I will punish this breaker of rules. See my power!**

**Комментарий:**

Though Kṛṣṇa, punisher of the deviants, is not visible, I, his follower, will punish him.

### 1.18.36

**Перевод:**

**Saying this to his friends who were sages’ sons, eyes red with anger, performing ācamana with water from the Kauśikī River, he released a thunderbolt of words.**

**Комментарий:**

He spoke to his friends. He performed ācamana using Kauśikī water. The sandhi in the phrase kauśikyāḥ apaḥ is poetic license.

### 1.18.37

*iti laṅghita-maryādaṁ takṣakaḥ saptame ’hani \| dhakṣyatisma kulāṅgāraṁ codito me tata-druham \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By my order Takṣaka will burn to ashes this black sheep of his family, offender of my father, and a trespasser of boundaries.**

**Комментарий:**

Because he placed a dead snake on the body of my father, that snake, becoming Takṣaka, will burn to ashes (dhakṣayti) the king, the black coals of his family. The word sma completes the meter.

### 1.18.38

**Перевод:**

**Returning to the hermitage, the boy, seeing his father with the snake around his neck, began crying loudly, afflicted with suffering.**

**Комментарий:**

Gale should actually be part of the compound without a declensional ending.

### 1.18.39-40

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇa! The descendent of Aṅgirasa, hearing the wailing of his son, eventually opened his eyes and saw the dead snake on his shoulder. Casting off the snake, he asked, “O son! Why are you crying? Who has wronged you?” Having been asked, his son explained everything.**

**Комментарий:**

He cast (visṛjya) the snake away.

### 1.18.41

**Перевод:**

**Hearing that Parīkṣit had been undeservedly cursed, the sage was not pleased with his son. “Oh fool! You have committed a great sin. For a small offense you have given great punishment.”**

**Комментарий:**

The curse was not suitable (atad-arham). The sage spoke words to express his disapproval. Aho bata indicates lamentation. O fool! You have committed a great sin, since for a small offense (droha) you have given great punishment (damaḥ).

### 1.18.42

**Перевод:**

**O fool! By this king’s intolerable power the citizens, protected Text 43\] The Brāhmaṇa’s Curse 405 and without any fear, enjoy prosperity. He is equal to Viṣṇu in fame. You cannot consider him to be the same as ordinary men.**

**Комментарий:**

O fool (apakva-buddhe)! This king was previously called Viṣṇu (parākhyam). He is absorbed in Viṣṇu. You should not see him as an ordinary man, since by his power the citizens are protected.

### 1.18.43

**Перевод:**

**O child! When this king, representative of Viṣṇu, disappears, the people, suffering from thieves, will be destroyed in an instant like unprotected sheep.**

**Комментарий:**

Alakṣyamāṇe means disappearing. Avivarūthavat means “like a herd of sheep.”

### 1.18.44

**Перевод:**

**The sin, though it is unrelated to us, arising from theft of wealth from people without a protector, will affect us at that time. The crowd of thieves will kill each other, curse each other and steal animals, women and wealth from each other.**

**Комментарий:**

The wealth of the people who are without a protector (naṣṭa- nāthasya) will be stolen. Know that, because of this theft, sin will arise for us, since we are its cause. The sin is not directly related to us (ananvayam). The sin is shown. The thieves will kill each other, curse each other, and steal from each other (vṛñjate) animals, women and wealth.

### 1.18.45

**Перевод:**

**Proper conduct of men, prescribed through actions of varṇa and āśrama which are described in the Vedas will be destroyed. Mixed castes will arise for people like the dogs and monkeys absorbed in money and women.**

**Комментарий:**

Ārya-dharmaḥ means proper conduct.

### 1.18.46

**Перевод:**

**King Parīkṣit, the protector of dharma, the renowned emperor, a sage among kings, a very great devotee of the Lord, performer of horse sacrifices, suffering from hunger, thirst and fatigue, does not deserve our curse.**

**Комментарий:**

Having said that he should not be cursed simply because he is the king, the sage now explains other reasons why he should not be cursed in three lines.

### 1.18.47

**Перевод:**

**The Lord, the soul of all beings, should forgive the sin committed to your innocent devotee by this boy of immature intelligence.**

**Комментарий:**

If Kṛṣṇa, whose plans are unfathomable, forgives this great sin to the devotee, we will be delivered. There is no other solution. The Lord is in the hearts of his servants (bhṛtyeṣu) and us (sarvātmāḥ).

### 1.18.48

**Перевод:**

**Though the devotees are capable of counteracting, they do not react when scolded, cheated, cursed, insulted or beaten.**

**Комментарий:**

Parīkṣit could counter curse and make the first curse ineffective. This did not happen, because Parīkṣit was a great devotee. Though criticized (tiraskṛtā), cheated (vipralabdhā), insulted (avajñātāḥ), and beaten (hatās), the devotees do not take counter measures against those who criticize, etc.

### 1.18.49

**Перевод:**

**The great sage was sorry for the sin committed by his son, and did not think at all that the king had committed an offense when he (the sage) was treated badly.**

**Комментарий:**

He did not think it an offense when the king treated him badly (viprakṛtaḥ).

### 1.18.50

**Перевод:**

**Generally the devotees in this world, on experiencing distress and happiness by others’ actions, do not become agitated and do not rejoice, since they do not take shelter of material happiness and distress.**

**Комментарий:**

This is reasonable. Dvandveṣu means in happiness and distress. The jīva (ātmā) does not take shelter of dualities (aguṇāśrayaḥ).

## Chapter Nineteen - The Arrival of Śukadeva

### 1.19.1

**Перевод:**

**Sūta said: The king, reflecting that his act was reprehensible, became very depressed. I have performed a deplorable act like an uncivilized person towards an innocent brāhmaṇa with great power.**

**Комментарий:**

In the nineteenth chapter, Parīkṣit repents for his act, fasts till death, and the sages arrive and finally Śukadeva arrives. His thoughts when he returned home are described in two and half verses (1-3). I have done an act (karma) of putting the snake on sage’s neck, which is to be criticized (garhyam). He became depressed.

### 1.19.2

**Перевод:**

**Because of my disrespect for this godly person it is certain that unpreventable misfortune will come very soon. Let that be direct atonement for my sin in full force, so that I will not commit such an act again.**

**Комментарий:**

Because of my act of disrespecting a brāhmaṇa which is disrespect for the Lord (deva-helanāt) unavoidable (duratyayaṁ) suffering (vyasanam) will come to me now (nāti-dīrghāt). Let it be direct (addhā), not affecting my sons, etc. Let the atonement for the sin be full (kāma) – so that I will not commit the sin again.

### 1.19.3

**Перевод:**

**Let the fire of an angry brāhmaṇa family burn up my kingdom, strength, and wealth today, since I am so sinful! May I never again think sinfully of giving suffering to brāhmaṇas, devatās or cows!**

**Комментарий:**

Thinking of the suffering, he prays for atonement before that. Let fire in the form of the anger of the brāhmaṇas burn my kingdom, strength and wealth. Being most unfortunate, may I not think sinfully of disrespecting the brāhmaṇas. etc.

### 1.19.4

**Перевод:**

**As he was thus thinking, he heard about how he would die from the bite of Takṣaka because of the curse issued by the sage’s son. He considered it beneficial that soon the fire of Takṣaka would be the cause of detachment from material life.**

**Комментарий:**

Thinking thus, the king heard that the sage’s son had said that in seven days he would die from Takṣaka. The king had heard from a student sent by Śamīka, “O King! My son, a boy, cursed you. I scolded him. But since the curse is unavoidable, you should deliberate on methods for attaining the next life.” When the messenger had departed, the king wanted to go there to beg forgiveness for his offense, but considering that the sage would be embarrassed, and also because he did not desire negation of the curse, he did not go. He considered the fiery poison of Takṣaka beneficial because it would quickly (na cireṇa) produce detachment from all his attachments. “Forgetting merciful Kṛṣṇa who protected me in the womb from the fire of the brahmāstra, I have been attached to the kingdom. What misfortune for me.” Giving up everything, he would worship the Lord.

### 1.19.5

**Перевод:**

**Having previously concluded that both were to be rejected — giving up this world and Svarga — and thinking that service to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet was the highest goal, he sat down on the bank of the Gaṅgā with a vow of not eating.**

**Комментарий:**

After that (athaḥ) giving up both worlds (imam amum) which he had previously considered useless, after the curse, he sat down with a vow of not eating on the Gaṅgā. He knew that service to Kṛṣṇa’s feet was the topmost human goal.

### 1.19.6

**Перевод:**

**Who at the point of death would not serve the Gaṅgā who, carrying water made excellent by the dust of Kṛṣṇa’s feet mixed with beautiful tulasī, purifies the worlds along with their leaders inside and outside, above and below?**

**Комментарий:**

“Why did he choose the Gaṅgā?” She carries (netrī) most excellent (abhyadhika) water. She purifies the planets along with their protectors, above and below, internally and externally (ubhayatra). Who while dying would not serve her? All persons for their auspiciousness will serve her.

### 1.19.7

**Перевод:**

**Making this decision to sit without eating on the bank of the Gaṅgā, Parīkṣit, without thoughts of other processes or deities, peaceful, and free of all material attachments, concentrated on the feet of Mukunda.**

**Комментарий:**

Deciding (vyavacchidya) to sit and fast to death, he was fixed on Kṛṣṇa (ananya-bhāvaḥ).

### 1.19.8

**Перевод:**

**Noble-minded sages who purify the world came along with their disciples to see Parīkṣit there on the pretext of visiting a holy place. The devotees, being holy places personified, actually purify the holy places.**

**Комментарий:**

The sages came to see Parīkṣit, not to bathe, since they were already pure. “But such sages are seen to visit holy places.” This is only a pretext (prāyena apadeśaiḥ). They had understood that seeing Parīkṣit, a great devotee, was superior to seeing the holy places. Understanding suddenly by their omniscience that the Bhāgavatam would be recited, they came with great, astonishing bliss.

### 1.19.9-10

*atrir vasiṣṭhaś cyavanaḥ śaradvān ariṣṭanemir bhṛgur aṅgirāś ca \| parāśaro gādhi-suto ’tha rāma utathya indrapramadedhmavāhau\|\| medhātithir devala ārṣṭiṣeṇo bhāradvājo gautamaḥ pippalādaḥ \| maitreya aurvaḥ kavaṣaḥ kumbhayonir dvaipāyano bhagavān nāradaś ca \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Atri, Vasiṣṭha, Cyavana, Śaradvān, Ariṣṭanemi, Bhṛgu, Aṅgirās, Parāśara, Viśvamitra, Paraśurāma, Utathya, Indrapramada, Idhmavāha, Medhātithi, Devala, Ārṣṭiṣeṇa, Bhāradvāja, Gautama, Pippalāda, Maitreya, Aurava, Kavaṣa, Agastya, Vyāsa and Nārada came there.**

**Комментарий:**

Gādhisuta is Viśvāmitra. Rāma is Paraśurāma, son of Jamadagni. Indrapramada and Idhmavāha were also there.

### 1.19.11

**Перевод:**

**Other exalted sages among the devatās, sages of great penance, sages among kings and Aruṇa and others also came. Welcoming the best of sages, the king bowed his head.**

**Комментарий:**

Arṣeya-pravān means the best among the gotras of sages.

### 1.19.12

**Перевод:**

**When they were all comfortably seated, standing in front of them, with pure heart and folded hands, he again offered respects and inquired from them concerning what he wanted to do.**

**Комментарий:**

He offered respects again in order to inform them of his plan. He had a pure heart (vivikta-cetaḥ). Abhigṛhīta-pāṇiḥ means “with folded hands.”

### 1.19.13

**Перевод:**

**The king said: Oh! We are most fortunate among kings since we have been qualified for mercy of the great sages. The family of kings is generally cast off at a great distance from the foot washing place of the brāhmaṇas, since they are involved in impure activities.**

**Комментарий:**

He describes the sages’ mercy on himself. Among kings we are most fortunate because we have qualities for receiving the mercy of great souls. Mercy of great souls is rare for kings because the royal family is rejected far away from even the foot washing water of brāhmaṇas. Smṛti says dūrād ucchiṣṭa-viṇ-mūtra-pādāmbhāṁsi samutsṛjet: one should keep at a distance leftover food, excrement, urine and foot water. (Yājñavalkya-smṛti 1.154) They cannot remain near the brāhmaṇas because their condemned activities are thoroughly impure. The king speaks in this way only out of humility, since many statements show that the kings interacted with brāhmaṇas.

### 1.19.14

**Перевод:**

**For me, whose impure action was the sin of disrespecting an innocent brāhmaṇa, and whose heart was very attached to family life, the Lord has come in the form of the brāhmaṇa’s curse, which has become the cause of detachment from material life. The person attached to the Lord quickly becomes fearless.**

**Комментарий:**

He explains his wicked act. Because of putting a snake around a brāhmaṇa’s neck, full of sin, I was cursed by a brāhmaṇa. Kṛṣṇa, to show that he delivers the most fallen, has come in front of me with your coming. This is my inference. The person attached to the Lord quickly attains fearlessness.

### 1.19.15

**Перевод:**

**The brāhmaṇas and Gaṅgā-devī should know that I am surrendered and have dedicated my heart to the Lord. Let the snake released by the brāhmaṇa, even if it is an imposter, bite me. Please sing topics concerning the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

He prays to the sages in two verses (15-16). O brāhmaṇas! The sages should know that I am surrendered. Gaṅgā-devī should also know this. Kuhakaḥ means false.

### 1.19.16

**Перевод:**

**On the other hand, in whatever birth I receive, may I have rati for the unlimited Lord, excellent association with his devotees who take shelter of him and friendship with all living beings! I offer respects to the brāhmaṇas.**

**Комментарий:**

Punaś ca means “on the other hand.” In whatever birth I take, may I have rati for the Lord and excellent association (prasaṅgaḥ) with his devotees, and friendship with all living entities! After praying for these three, he then offers his respects.

### 1.19.17

**Перевод:**

**Having decided in this way, the wise king, entrusting the country to his son, sat down facing north on kuśa grass with tips facing east, on the right bank of the river.**

**Комментарий:**

Deciding in this way, the king gave his son Janamejaya the responsibility of the kingdom and remained there (āste) on the bank of the Gaṅgā (samudra-patnyāḥ).

### 1.19.18

**Перевод:**

**When the best of the kings sat fasting, the devatās in the sky showered the earth with flowers in praise and continually drums sounded in joy.**

**Комментарий:**

They praised (praśasya) his fast till death. Vyakiran means that they made a shower. The drums sounded by themselves.

### 1.19.19

**Перевод:**

**Because the great sages gathered there had the quality and ability to give mercy to the citizens, they approved of his decision by praising it, and said that he has the qualities of the Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Praising the devatās’ worship of the king, the sages approved his decision. The devotees (santaḥ) said that he had qualities like those of the Lord. The sages had strength (sāra) and the quality of giving mercy to citizens.

### 1.19.20

**Перевод:**

**O best of kings! It is not surprising that those desiring to associate with the Lord, born in the Pāṇḍava family and devoted solely to Kṛṣṇa, immediately gave up the royal throne served by kings’ crowns.**

**Комментарий:**

Yudhiṣṭhira and his family (bhavatsu), descendants of Bhārata, who followed Kṛṣṇa.

### 1.19.21

**Перевод:**

**We will all remain here until the king, the best of the devotees, gives up his body and attains the supreme planet which is without grief and passion.**

**Комментарий:**

Seeing the resolve of the king, they spoke to the king to support him and themselves, consulting among themselves.

### 1.19.22

**Перевод:**

**Hearing the words of the sages, which were impartial, flowing with nectar, profound in meaning and true, he then spoke with a desire to hear about the activities of the Lord after offering respect to the sages.**

**Комментарий:**

Samam means “impartial.” This is expressed when they said (in the previous verse), “We will remain here.” The words were flowing with honey when they praised him as the best of the devotees. The words were profound because they mentioned the planet devoid of passion (not directly saying the Lord’ planet). The words were true because they said he would attain the planet of the Lord. Hearing these four types of statements, he spoke to them.

### 1.19.23

**Перевод:**

**You who have gathered here from many places are full of knowledge like the Vedas situated in Satya-loka, above the three worlds. And you have no goal except to give mercy to others in this world and in the next. Actually, that is your very nature.**

**Комментарий:**

You are like the personified Vedas who are situated in Satya-loka (tri-pṛṣṭhe), above the three worlds. Having spoken of their great knowledge, he speaks of their great mercy. You have no goal except to give mercy to others. Is that their goal? No, it is their nature.

### 1.19.24

**Перевод:**

**O brāhmaṇas! I thus ask about your mercy. That should be determined. Since I have developed faith in your mercy, please consider and tell me in common agreement what is the pure activity, though there are many duties to be performed and many duties for those who are dying.**

**Комментарий:**

I ask about your mercy—what form will I take? I ask what should I do. This should be asked in order that I develop concentration (pṛcchyam). I will develop faith in that (viśrabhya). In the subject of what should be done in karma, austerity and yoga, etc. (iti krṭyatāyām) decide that activity which should be done in all conditions and particularly for those dying and which is free from violence (śuddham). Inform me what should be done after determining it in agreement.

### 1.19.25

**Перевод:**

**The great son of Vyāsa, Śukadeva, wandering the earth as he pleased, ignoring everyone, without indications of any āśrama, satisfied with his own attainment, wearing discarded cloth, and surrounded by children, arrived at that time.**

**Комментарий:**

After not agreeing on what to teach him, they waited for Śukadeva’s arrival. “If Śukadeva, knowing the essence of everything comes, he should instruct him and we will become most blissful hearing that.” As they thought that, Śukadeva arrived. “From birth Parīkṣit was devoted to Kṛṣṇa alone, and having given up the kingdom, and fasting till death, is most qualified to hear the topics of Kṛṣṇa. I will have him hear the great Purāṇa, showering the sweetness of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes that I studied.” Determining this, he arrived on his own. He was without desire (anapekṣaḥ). He did not desire the opinions of other sages because they were insignificant, since he was always satisfied with direct realization by attaining Kṛṣṇa, his master (nijalābha-tuṣṭaḥ). He did not have signs of any āśrama. His appearance was not understood by people (avadhūtaḥ).

### 1.19.26

**Перевод:**

**He was sixteen years old, with tender feet, hands, thighs, arms, shoulders, and forehead. His face had beautiful, long eyes, raised nose, symmetrical ears, and shapely eyebrows. His throat was as beautiful as a conch.**

**Комментарий:**

Three verses (26-28) are connected. He was sixteen. His feet, hands, thighs, arms, shoulders, forehead and limbs were soft. His face had beautiful, long eyes and raised nose. His ears were equal, not irregularly long or short. His eyebrows were beautiful. This was the appearance of his face. His tender throat was beautiful like a conch with three lines.

### 1.19.27

**Перевод:**

**His collar bone was well covered. His chest was broad and high. His navel was like a whirlpool. His abdomen was beautiful, indented with three horizontal lines. The directions were his clothing. His hair was scattered on his face. His arms reached his knees. His complexion was that of Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

His collar bone (jatrum) below the throat was covered because it was fleshy. His chest was broad and raised. His navel was like a whirlpool. His belly was attractive with three lines. His complexion was equal to that of Kṛṣṇa, best among the devas (su amara) since Kṛṣṇa shone in his heart. He was similar to Kṛṣṇa in form by his great beauty.

### 1.19.28

**Перевод:**

**He was dark in complexion, attractive to women with his pleasant smile, his bodily features and his attractive youthfulness. Though his splendor was covered, the sages, knowing the symptoms of a great person, rose from their seats to welcome him.**

**Комментарий:**

His beauty is described. He was attractive to women by his natural beauty, by his fixed (sadā), attractive, new youth and his smile. Seeing him, though his splendor was covered, the sages stood up.

### 1.19.29

**Перевод:**

**When his guest arrived, Parīkṣit worshipped him with bowed head. The ignorant women and children disappeared. In the worship, Śukadeva sat on a huge seat.**

**Комментарий:**

Parīkṣit (viṣṇurātaḥ) worshipped him while bowing with his head to Śukadeva (atithaye), also called Brahma-rāta. The women, who thought he was directly Cupid, and the children who thought he was crazy stopped following him. They fled out of fear of the sages. He was worshipped by the sages with respect, embraces and asking about his welfare. He worshipped the sages also by offering respects, embracing them and asking about their welfare. Nārada, Parāśara, Vyāsa and others, with tears and choked voices, smelled his head and asked about his welfare. He offered respects to them. Requested by them, by their order he sat on huge seat suitable for a teacher. The sandhi in sopaviveśa is according to the rule so’ci lope cet pāda-pūraṇam (Pāṇini 6.1.134).

### 1.19.30

**Перевод:**

**The greatest among the great, Śukadeva, seated there, surrounded by the crowd of sages among brāhmaṇas, sages among kings and sages among devatās shone like the moon surrounded by groups of planets, constellations, and stars.**

**Комментарий:**

The glory of him sitting there is described. The planets (graha) are Venus, etc. The constellations (rkṣa) are Aśvinī, etc. Tāra refers to other luminaries. Śukadeva was more excellent than Nārada and other great sages since he was devoted solely to Nanda’s son.

### 1.19.31

**Перевод:**

**The devotee king, approaching the sage peacefully sitting with unlimited knowledge, offered his respects with his head, folded his hands, again offered respects and then inquired with sweet words.**

**Комментарий:**
